## v1
Source: docs/v1/ at bd0c224688d9. 53 phases.
The v1 notes folder is history, not documentation of the app as it exists today; phase notes are kept as originally written, not edited to track later changes.
Result sets are stored columnar: one array per column plus a shared row count.
A hand-edited or stale-shape row fails loudly instead of propagating undefineds into the UI.
The SQL console is gated on the connection's caps.sql rather than shown unconditionally.
A document body past 64 KB falls back to its raw text rather than a tree, with a truncated badge.
Committing an edit in the editable grid stages a pending cell edit (§8.14) rather than writing immediately.
DocumentTree.vue was moved to views/shared/document/ so both places can mount it.
A folder with no children shows "No items" rather than an empty list indistinguishable from a still-loading one.
A lint override now enforces §11's layering rule at lint time instead of by review alone.
ClickHouse's testcontainers module hardcodes a ulimit override the sandbox cannot satisfy, so the subclass NoUlimitClickHouseContainer in tests/ipc/clickhouse/container.ts clears hostConfig.Ulimits in its constructor.
The ClickHouse backend fixture freezes one non-determinism per D6: a materialized view's auto-generated .inner_id.<uuid> backing-table name, a fresh random UUID per container.
tests/db/ holds exhaustive adapter-edge coverage, while the backend half of tests/ipc/ is one linear fixture-capturing scenario per adapter; the tiers test different layers, so similar assertions are not duplicates.
redis's truncated field is undefined at the adapter level and false at the IPC level, because tree-service.ts normalizes what the adapter omits.
Building @confluentinc/kafka-javascript always tries to vendor-build zlib/libcurl/libcrypto/zstd from source; setting the CKJS_LINKING environment variable to dynamic skips this and links against system dev packages.
The Kafka backend test drives the real tree with topics ungrouped, each consumer group as its own root-level node, and "Consumer groups" as a frontend-only folder over it.
A Kafka read fans across both partitions and interleaves them by arrival rather than key or offset order, so the fixture is sorted by key before writing.
The Kafka consumer group's coordinator host:port is non-deterministic (fresh Docker hostname and host-mapped port each run) and is frozen per D6.
The Wails spike phase requires the user's explicit sign-off before any implementation begins; the word "spike" does not exempt it from CLAUDE.md's Opus-plans-then-sign-off-then-implement loop.
Most Wails claims in the plan are best-current-knowledge for a spike stage to confirm, because wails.io and v3.wails.io are blocked by the environment's egress proxy and the official docs were never read.
Wails has no first-class sidecar/external-binary mechanism, so spawning the Node child and bundling the vendored runtime are both hand-rolled.
Wails v3's default binding transport is a fetch() to a local /wails/runtime endpoint with a JSON.stringify body, a full JSON round-trip; the runtime source also exposes a setTransport() override worth investigating for bulk data.
No first-party WebDriver story for Wails surfaced; discussion #4205 recommends pointing Playwright at wails dev's localhost:34115, which is not shell-integration coverage.
The E2E decision carries over unchanged from the deleted Tauri plan: the UI suite moves fully to Playwright's isolated webkit tier, permanently giving up native menu/window/lifecycle coverage, real-IPC-bridge correctness, real Keychain coverage and src/main/security.ts's navigation-lock posture.
In the Wails architecture, settings, tabs, layout and op log live in the Go process, so losing the engine means "reconnect your databases" (engine-host.ts's no-auto-respawn policy) rather than the app being unable to save anything.
In the Wails architecture Keychain access becomes ordinary in-process Go code, with no privileged cross-process surface, resolving the credential seam rather than relocating it.
Wails being a smaller, less mature project than Tauri is an accepted trade-off given the user's explicit Go-over-Rust preference; the spike does not relitigate it.
The Wails spike row supersedes the former "P51 Tauri + Node sidecar migration spike"; P20's own row and its out-of-scope verdict are unchanged.

### v1 P0 Foundations
v1 P0 foundations use Bun, Biome, TS7 and electron-vite as the toolchain.
v1 P0 includes a three-process skeleton that communicates via MessagePort.
v1 P0 includes SQLite storage with migrations.
v1 P0 includes dark theme tokens and codicons.
v1 P0 workbench shell includes panels, status bar toggles, and a settings dialog with fonts.
v1 P0 includes a Playwright harness that launches and screenshots the app.
All other v1 work builds on the P0 foundations.

### v1 P1 Connections & tree
v1 P1 (Connections & tree) covers connection CRUD (fields + URI), colors, adapter interface, PostgreSQL adapter, connect/disconnect, lazy cached tree, green dot, tree context menus, filters + panel search, operations panel, L1 metadata cache, and a Testcontainers harness for Postgres.
v1 P1 is the first end-to-end vertical slice; it proves the adapter shape before five more adapters are written.

### v1 P2 Tabular data view
v1 P2 tabular data view scope: virtualized grid, tabs (same table opened N times), pagination with page sizes, count-all, projection, sort, stop button, search toolbar, L2/L3 caches and prefetch.
v1 P2 filter toolbar covers WHERE and ORDER BY input plus history and saved filters.
v1 P2 adds a MariaDB adapter as a second SQL engine, because it cheaply validates the database abstraction.

### v1 P3 Cell editor
The v1 P3 deliverable is a cell editor: a CodeMirror panel with format autodetect, manual format override, and beautify (indented or compact). It depends on grid selection.

### v1 P4 DDL tabs
In v1 P4, the DDL tabs are a read-only DDL view built on a model that is ready to become editable.

### v1 P5 Mutations
v1 P5 (Mutations) covers add/delete row, cell editing, pending-change set, exact-command preview, commit/rollback, read-only guard, and cache invalidation.
v1 P5 (Mutations) depends on the grid, cell editor, preview, and op log.

### v1 P5.5 Query console
Phase v1 P5.5 is the query console: a console tab that runs a statement or all statements, shows results in the grid, and supports saved queries.
Phase v1 P5.5 (query console) needs the grid and the op log, and is independent of the remaining adapters.

### v1 P6 Interaction completeness
Phase v1 P6 is 'Interaction completeness': the full right-click matrix, copy/paste rows, and keyboard shortcuts.

### v1 P7 PK/FK navigation
Phase v1 P7 covers PK/FK navigation: an FK metadata graph, cell buttons, and filtered tabs.

### v1 P8 MongoDB
v1 P8 delivers a MongoDB adapter and a document view with expand/collapse, edit and delete.
MongoDB is the first non-tabular shape; v1 P8 validates the page-kind union.

### v1 P9 Redis
Phase v1 P9 covers Redis via an adapter plus a key/value view, as the second non-tabular shape.

### v1 P10 Kafka + SQS
v1 plan phase P10 covers Kafka and SQS adapters plus a stream view.
Kafka and SQS (v1 P10) have the most divergent semantics and benefit from everything in the earlier phases.

### v1 P11 Pre-connect scripts
Pre-connect scripts are a per-connection optional shell command (e.g. port-forward) that runs before connecting.
A connection is marked disconnected if its pre-connect script process exits while the connection is in use.
Pre-connect scripts are configured in the connection dialog UI.
Pre-connect scripts cut across every adapter, so they land once the adapter surface is complete, ahead of the final hardening pass.

### v1 P12 Hardening
Phase v1 P12 (Hardening) covers a memory/perf pass against the §2 budgets, cache tuning, cold-start time, and unsigned packaging.
Measure performance only once the surface is complete, because nothing should still be changing under it.

### v1 P13 Nonfunctional checks
Phase P13 (v1) is the nonfunctional-checks phase: sweep the whole codebase for memory leaks, storage leaks, inefficient or redundant DB interaction, and insufficient caching, and fix everything found.
P12 measured against budgets and only pulled the levers its numbers justified, deliberately leaving several items open for a dedicated sweep, listed in docs/PERF.md §4. P13 closes them before the surface is described in docs.

### v1 P14 Docs
The v1 P14 docs phase covers descriptions in every expected in-repo location plus the main repository README, including full functionality, install and dev-setup instructions.
Docs are written once the app's behavior and nonfunctional characteristics are final, so nothing documented needs revisiting.

### v1 P15 GH tooling
The GH tooling phase (P15) covers a pre-commit hook, GitHub Actions CI that runs on macOS only, a tag-triggered unsigned macOS binary build, and verified auto-update configuration.
The GH tooling phase (P15) is scheduled last, because CI and release tooling should target a finished, documented build rather than a moving one.

### v1 P16 Misc fixes
P16 adds an explicit per-connection preconnect-mode checkbox that overrides P11's settle-window auto-detection.
P16 adds a connection-kind icon picker and a click-to-open connection-error popover.
P16 gives scripts/demo-dbs/ full six-engine coverage.
P16 fixes two font-family bugs and adds testcontainers preset packages for Postgres, MariaDB and Redis.
P16 is not a planned deliverable. It is a batch of user-directed fixes found after P15 shipped, grouped into one phase rather than reopening P1, P9, P10, P11 and P14.

### v1 P17 S3
The S3 adapter's object browser tree mirrors redis's namespace-tree shape, using '/' as delimiter instead of ':' and bucket/prefix/object node kinds instead of database/namespace/key.
An S3 object reuses the keyvalue page kind: its metadata (ContentType, Size, LastModified, ETag, StorageClass, user Metadata) plus a Body field/value row matches the flat listing a redis hash key renders, so KeyValueView.vue needed no new view.
S3 support is read-only browsing in this phase, with no insert, update or delete of buckets or objects.
tests/db/s3.spec.ts tests the S3 adapter against a LocalStack container, mirroring the structure of sqs.spec.ts.

### v1 P18 Autocomplete
Autocomplete is scoped to the four surfaces with a real free-text identifier grammar (SQL's WHERE/ORDER BY, Mongo's filter/SORT) plus the query console.
Redis, Kafka, SQS and S3 get no autocomplete, because they have no free-text identifier surface (local-page search, a structured multiselect, or nothing), per docs/plans/P18-autocomplete.md's D1.
AutocompleteField.vue owns its own input rather than wrapping TextField.vue, because a wrapper can't safely intercept Enter across two components (see plan §1).
SQL column completions are inserted dialect-quoted only when quoting is needed.
Mongo field completions insert as 'field: ', include _id, and use a curated $-operator vocabulary.
The shared CodeMirror host has undo/redo restored via history()/historyKeymap, folded into the existing read-only compartment.
The console completion popup has no typing debounce (activateOnTypingDelay/interactionDelay: 0), and Tab, not Enter, accepts a completion so Enter stays a newline in the multi-line console.
In the query console, SQL keywords, types and builtins complete in uppercase via sql()'s upperCaseKeywords.
Mongo console completion offers collection names after db., the ten supported shell methods after db.<collection>., and $-operators.
Redis console completion offers command names on the first token only.
The Mongo and Redis consoles each have their own StreamLanguage highlighting mode instead of SQL's.
Inline lexical/shape-level diagnostics per engine come from a lintSource prop/compartment using @codemirror/lint, with no gutter or panel, only an underline and a themed hover tooltip.
Diagnostics reuse each adapter's own error wording so a diagnostic can never contradict what running the statement would do.

### v1 P19 Tree reorganization + generic object-definition view
The tree shows tables first, ungrouped; every other object kind (functions, sequences, etc.) is grouped into per-kind folders by default.
Tables no longer expand to show columns in the tree; columns are shown in the definition view instead.
A renderer-only GROUPED_KINDS table (view/matview/sequence/function, with MariaDB's function folder labelled "Routines") splits an already-fetched child list into ungrouped tables/collections plus collapsed per-kind folders; expanding a folder issues no IPC call and no op-log row.
Tables, views, matviews and collections are tree leaves; both SQL adapters' children() return [] at that depth, and the DB specs' column assertions moved to describe().
The DDL feature was renamed to "definition" end to end (Caps.ddl→Caps.definition, adapter method, IPC channel, tab kind, L1 cache key, views/ddl/→views/definition/) to fit non-SQL connections.
A legacy 'ddl'-row coercion on read means upgrading doesn't drop open tabs or blank the Operations panel.
ObjectDefinition gained language, constraints and documentSchema fields.
Postgres fills ObjectDefinition constraints from the pg_constraint query it already runs for its DDL text, with zero extra round trips.
MariaDB fills constraints from one added information_schema.TABLE_CONSTRAINTS query, run on the definition path only.
The definition tab is one view with a Structure/Source Segmented toggle (not the mockup's four-way split); Structure is the default and Source shows raw SQL text.
Structure stacks Columns/Indexes/Constraints sections for SQL, or Indexes/Validation for Mongo, with count badges, fed by a second, independently-cached describe() load.
The tree's former column-row context menu (Copy name/Add to projection/Sort by) was relocated into the definition view's Columns section.
Mongo's definition() sources Source from the collection's creation-options document (EJSON-relaxed) and Validation from its $jsonSchema validator, rendered as a field table when it is one, else raw JSON, with an empty state when there is none.
The P19 implementation plan is in docs/plans/P19-tree-reorg-definition-view.md.

### v1 P20 Electrobun migration spike
The Electrobun migration spike (P20) is out of scope and will not be done; it was superseded by a direct Electron memory-footprint pass (see docs/PERF.md).
Electrobun has no E2E testing path: its webview is WKWebView, which Apple exposes no WebDriver/CDP endpoint for, so _electron.launch() in tests/ui/*.spec.ts has no equivalent and Cypress hits the same wall.
The only working Electrobun E2E alternative, Appium mac2-driver/XCUITest, drives the OS accessibility tree rather than the DOM, so for this app's grid-heavy cell-level UI it would mean rewriting the whole suite's interaction model with no CSS-selector equivalent.
Electrobun's RPC bridge only copies payloads and has no port-transfer primitive (no Transferable/ArrayBuffer/structuredClone in rpc.ts), so it cannot support SPEC §4's MessageChannelMain design where the engine subprocess gets a port straight into the renderer.
Under Electrobun, bulk pages would move onto the same event loop as the native menu and window chrome, undermining the interaction budgets in docs/PERF.md §2.1 rather than merely needing re-measurement.
Electrobun is pre-1.0 (its default JSC-based main-process runtime, Cottontail, is v0.5.0), so API compatibility under either mainProcess option is unverified beyond docs.
Electrobun's benefit (lower baseline memory/idle footprint) was judged not to clear the cost given the E2E and bulk-data dealbreakers.
Run each branch's perf tests (budgets, perf, memory, startup specs under tests/ui) multiple times, because they have real run-to-run variability and a single sample can't show a real difference (docs/PERF.md §2.1).
Get the user's explicit sign-off before implementing any spike phase, including the Wails migration spike, because the word "spike" does not exempt it from CLAUDE.md's Opus-plans-then-sign-off-then-implement loop.
Wails has no first-class sidecar/external-binary mechanism, so spawning the Node child and bundling the vendored runtime are both hand-rolled (discussion #3021 was answered only with go:embed).
Wails v3's default binding transport is a fetch() to a local /wails/runtime endpoint with a JSON.stringify body (a full JSON round-trip), though a setTransport() override exists worth investigating for bulk data.
No first-party WebDriver story exists for Wails; discussion #4205 suggests pointing Playwright at wails dev's localhost:34115, which is not shell-integration coverage.
In the Wails plan the UI suite moves fully to Playwright's isolated webkit tier, permanently giving up native menu/window/lifecycle coverage, real-IPC-bridge correctness, real Keychain coverage and src/main/security.ts's navigation-lock posture.
In the Wails architecture, settings/tabs/layout/op log live in the Go process, so losing the engine means reconnecting databases (engine-host.ts's no-auto-respawn policy) rather than the app being unable to save.
In the Wails architecture Keychain access becomes ordinary in-process Go code, with no privileged cross-process surface.
Wails being smaller and less mature than Tauri is an accepted trade-off given the user's explicit Go-over-Rust preference, and the spike does not relitigate it.
The Wails plan row supersedes the former "P51 Tauri + Node sidecar migration spike"; P20's own row and out-of-scope verdict are unchanged.

### v1 P21 Menu shortcut hints
Every context-menu item that has a keyboard shortcut prints it alongside its label, VS Code style, muted and right-aligned.
A single shared binding table (src/shared/shortcuts.ts) is the one source of truth, feeding the native menu bar's accelerators, the context menus' displayed keys, and the local DOM-scoped keydown handlers, so a printed key and the key that runs cannot drift.
The P21 audit covered all 104 context-menu rows across 21 builders and surfaced 5 bindings that already worked but were never shown: the grid's Cmd/Ctrl+C over cell/row/column selections, its Enter-to-edit, and the tab strip's Cmd/Ctrl+W.
P21 adds 9 new bindings following VS Code conventions: F2 rename, Delete/⌘⌫ delete, Ctrl/Cmd+C copy, Ctrl/Cmd+N new, Enter open, Shift+Alt+C copy path, and Ctrl/Cmd+D duplicate.
The new P21 shortcuts are scoped only to the project tree and the SQL grid, the two surfaces that already have focus and a selection.
The P21 audit could be exhaustive because the right-click matrix (P6) and every view that feeds it are complete; the binding table must exist before shortcuts can be shown or remapped.
The P21 plan is documented at docs/plans/P21-menu-shortcut-hints.md.

### v1 P22 App-owned tooltips
The app draws all hover hints itself: the native title attribute is removed from src/renderer and replaced by a v-tooltip directive plus one AppTooltip.vue singleton mounted beside ContextMenu.vue.
AppTooltip.vue shares the same Teleport/fixed/.p-float chrome as every other floating surface in the app.
The tooltip opens after 400 ms (the app's existing hover constant, the lint delay in CodeMirrorHost.vue) and re-arms without the pause when moving between adjacent controls.
The tooltip hides on leave, click, key, scroll and blur, and is pointer-events: none so it can never intercept the press it describes.
The hovered control is resolved via one document-level rAF-coalesced pointermove plus elementFromPoint, not per-element mouseenter, because Blink dispatches no pointer events on a disabled control and many hints explain a disabled state.
The v-tooltip directive mirrors the hint into aria-label where the control has no accessible name, and wires aria-describedby while shown; focusin opens the tooltip for keyboard users.
main.ts registers v-tooltip globally, and App.vue installs and tears down the single document-level listener set.
The tooltip text is exposed in the data-kira-tip attribute, and UI tests read it from there instead of from title.
The remaining title= occurrences in the tree are component props (DialogFrame's and SavedListMenu's own title prop), never directive targets.
tests/ui/tooltips.spec.ts covers open-delay timing, a disabled control's hint, a hint inside an already-open popover, pointer-events: none, and the aria-describedby and auto-aria-label guarantees.

### v1 P23 Kafka tree reshape + stream-engine definitions
A Kafka topic is a leaf in the tree and no longer expands into partition rows, the way P19 made SQL tables leaves.
At the Kafka cluster root, topics show first and ungrouped, and only the auxiliary Consumer groups kind is foldered, by adding one row to P19's renderer-only GROUPED_KINDS table (no adapter change, no new NodeKind, no path segment, no IPC on expand).
caps.definition is true for Kafka and SQS; partition data is relocated into the definition view, not deleted.
ObjectDefinition has a generic sections list of name/value/detail rows, rendered by PropertiesSection.vue inside P19's existing definition view.
A Kafka topic definition shows partitions (id, leader, replicas, ISR) from metadata the tree already fetched, plus describeConfigs topic configuration with sensitive values masked.
A Kafka consumer group definition shows its state, members and committed offsets; previously consumer groups had no view.
An SQS queue definition shows its full GetQueueAttributes set.
The definition view no longer hard-requires describe(): it uses Promise.allSettled and meta may stay null. Its Open-in-console button is gated on caps.sql.
children() on a Kafka topic path is deliberately left intact, because the stream view's partition multiselect is a live second consumer of it.
Redis stays definition: false permanently, because key type/TTL/memory are already on every key/value page.
S3 stays definition: false as a named follow-up, because a bucket's properties need five SDK calls that a single-bucket IAM policy routinely denies.
Decision D2: topics stay ungrouped at the root, matching P19's SQL rule, rather than the plan's initial default of foldering the whole root; resolved after user feedback.
SQS is in scope for P23 (decision D9, the plan's default).
kafka/definition.ts and sqs/definition.ts degrade to a notes line rather than failing the tab when describeConfigs (Kafka) is denied.
tests/ui/definition.spec.ts passes unchanged as D8's regression guard that Postgres/MariaDB/Mongo definition tabs are unaffected.

### v1 P24 Search filter, cell editor fixes, expressive date picker, design cohesion
The grid's search toolbar has a filter toggle that narrows the grid to only the matched rows, client-side and reversibly, with its own "no matching rows" empty state.
Timestamp formats in the cell editor use a translate pane, on the same footing as the hex/base64 decoded-text pane, instead of the native datetime-local picker.
The timestamp translate pane has an editable field and an app-owned calendar picker (local/UTC toggle, month grid, time controls, a "now" shortcut). It syncs with the raw value in both directions in real time and re-encodes into the value's exact original shape.
P24 fixed cell-editor bugs: a stale disabled-reset tooltip, no keyboard way to abandon an edit, a truncated value that could be silently staged as a full-value write, and statusLine disagreeing with the buffer while typing.
The P24 design-cohesion sweep fixed sixteen findings (F1–F16): one icon size, one type scale, SegmentedControl and definition-section primitives replacing five sets of hand-rolled duplicates, and previously dead .is-invalid CSS states.
formatBytes is a single shared helper used from four places, replacing three divergent implementations.
The grid's view header shows the same kind/count/read-write/PK facts that every sibling view shows.
The P24 plan is in docs/plans/P24-search-filter-celleditor-dates-design.md; its §5 names the new scenarios added to tests/ui/data-view.spec.ts and cell-editor.spec.ts.

### v1 P25 Credential encryption at rest (Keychain)
Connection secrets (passwords, tokens, URIs with embedded credentials) move from plaintext in the SQLite connections table to OS Keychain-backed encryption, macOS Keychain via safeStorage first.
The storage layer is shaped so a Linux/Windows credential-store backend is a later adapter, not a rewrite.
P25 credential encryption must account for the Playwright harness, which drives a real headless Electron process and therefore a real, ask-free Keychain path in CI.
P25 is security hardening independent of other v1 features, deferred past the functional phases so it lands against a settled connection-record shape rather than one still changing under P17–P24.
secret-cipher.ts is the only file importing safeStorage, and it is probed once after app.whenReady().
Stored passwords are a kira:v1:<base64> envelope, and a pre-P25 plaintext row is upgraded on next launch.
duplicate() copies the stored ciphertext raw rather than decrypting it.
Decrypt/encrypt failures surface as real messages (reveal()'s {password, error}, the dialog's inline save error) instead of unhandled rejections.
The connection dialog's credential note reflects the platform's actual backend.
On Linux development, setting KIRA_INSECURE_SECRETS=1 enables an insecure development-fallback path for secrets, covered by tests/ui/secrets.spec.ts.
secrets.spec.ts scenario 1 deliberately asserts available === true and backend === 'keychain' rather than skipping, as D15's 'fail loudly, not silently' CI guard, so a macOS runner with a locked or absent default keychain produces a red build.
P25 was implemented with zero edits to any existing spec, per F12.

### v1 P26 Cell editor moves inside the data view
Before P26, the cell editor panel was a workbench-global singleton mounted once outside any per-tab tree, so switching tabs could leave it rendering the previous tab's cell.
The cell editor is now owned by each data-shaped view, mounted and torn down with the view like other per-tab state, so a tab switch cannot leave it stale; a view kind can opt out.
P26 was done before P27 reshapes the Mongo document view so P27 need not work around the cell-editor ownership issue; Mongo's document view has little use for the cell editor in its current form.
state/cellSelection.ts moved from one global slot to a per-tab-id record.
CellEditorDock.vue is the only mount point of the cell editor, added to every data-shaped view (grid, documents, key/value, stream, console); the shell mount and CellEditorPanel.vue were deleted.
The unmount-time selection clears that guarded the old shared slot were removed, so a backgrounded tab keeps its cell selection on switch-back.
A dirty cell editor buffer now stages on unmount, not just on blur, so a keyboard-driven tab switch cannot silently drop an in-flight edit.
Mongo's DocumentView.vue mounts the cell editor dock unchanged, per P26's boundary with P27.
P26 is implemented per docs/v1/plans/P26-cell-editor-in-data-view.md.

### v1 P27 Mongo document view redesign + ObjectId support + render perf
In the Mongo document view, a document's collapsed preview shows only its primary key by default, and all documents are expanded to just their first layer of keys.
Each Mongo document row has edit, expand/collapse and delete actions.
The cell editor panel (P26) is hidden by default for Mongo, and the document view no longer publishes to it; JSON beautify, byte count and revert live in the expanded document's own edit area.
The cell editor and the Mongo document row's edit area share one dirty/beautify/bytes/revert implementation via views/shared/useEditBuffer.ts and EditBufferActions.vue; beautify.ts was hoisted to the renderer root.
views/documents/ejson.ts recognises the closed EJSON v2 wrapper set by shape, without importing bson, and renders BSON values in Mongo shell form.
engine/adapters/mongo/literal.ts provides resolveEjsonWrappers and parseDocumentLiteral, so the filter box, mutate.ts and the document editor all accept shell constructors and extended JSON in the same document.
VirtualList.vue has an additive rowHeights prop (prefix-sum offsets, binary search) that backs documentRows.ts's exact per-row height model; DocumentTree.vue uses a flattened, CodeMirror-free read path, and DocumentView.vue's list uses this virtualizer.
The filter bar and Mongo console both offer the six BSON constructors as completions, and Copy _id and Copy document use the shell form.
P27 groups the preview reshape and the ObjectId work because they touch the same document-rendering code path; the render perf fix must be verified against the new reshape, not the code it replaces.
The ObjectId representation (bare string versus tagged {"$oid": ...}) was to be chosen after surveying how Compass and Studio 3T represent it.

### v1 P28 Connections panel: sticky group headers + checkbox filter
Scrolling the connections panel keeps the currently-open connection's group header pinned at the top until scroll passes into the next connection's group.
The connections panel filter is an expandable checkbox tree (by kind/tag and the tree's own grouping) instead of a regex/text match over the flat list.
tests/ui/support/tree.ts is the single band-aware copy of the Playwright tree helpers, replacing duplicated/flaky helpers across twenty files.
VirtualList.vue has an additive scrollstate emit and a zero-height #sticky overlay slot.
project/stickyBand.ts computes the pinned connection/database/schema band, capped at three rows and clamped to the viewport, purely from the flat row array and scroll offset.
ProjectTree.vue renders the sticky band as real TreeRows (sticky prop, distinct testid, tabindex=-1), so pinned rows are fully interactive.
grouping.ts labelForKind() is the single source of every node kind's display label, per connection kind.
The glob/regex rule list (connection_filters, shared/domain/connection-filter.ts) was replaced by a set of exclusions: TreeVisibility in shared/domain/tree-filter.ts, stored in connection_tree_filters (migration 0005).
Nothing ticked off in the filter is ever hidden, including an object fetched for the first time after the filter was saved.
project/filterTree.ts derives the filter dialog's kind rows, tri-state object rows and live consequence count from the same cached nodes the tree renders from, with no new IPC call.
FiltersDialog.vue is a two-section checkbox UI that opens focused on the row it was invoked from, with ancestors pre-expanded.

### v1 P29 Grid/cell-view scroll rendering gap
DataGrid.vue is the only two-axis-virtualized surface; every CodeMirror host runs EditorView.lineWrapping, so the cell editor has no horizontal scroll.
In DataGrid.vue, rowRange/colRange are derived through four primitive computeds so a sub-boundary scroll invalidates nothing downstream; before, they invalidated on every scroll pixel.
columns.ts's visibleColumnRange applies symmetric pixel overscan, capped per side; before it had zero column-axis overscan while the row axis had 560px.
displayCell/cellNavEntry results are computed once per render in renderRows's RowVM/CellVM, and the template reads fields only; before, the template re-evaluated them on every render (~126 calls per rendered cell).
cellNavEntry is gated on a navColumns precheck plus a narrow per-row value map, instead of building a whole-row snapshot before knowing whether a column has a nav affordance.
page.ts's decode cache prunes only the rows that left the window, instead of clearing entirely on every row boundary crossed during a fling.
.grid-row has contain: layout, added as a low-risk, low-cost change; its measured effect could not be isolated.
budgets.spec.ts includes horizontal/wide-table scroll measurements and overscan-coverage invariants using the app.scroll_grid fixture in tests/ui/support/pg.ts (60 columns x 5000 rows); they self-skip without a Docker daemon.
The P29 refactor is regression-guarded by the Docker-gated suite: data-view, mutations, interaction, tabs, leaks, cell-editor and perf.spec.ts.
P29 is specified in docs/v1/plans/P29-scroll-render-gap.md; its goal is to close the scroll blank gap by fixing virtualization/paint timing rather than masking it with a spinner.

### v1 P31 Cross-cutting polish and bug-fix batch
P31 batches sixteen small unrelated fixes (following P16's precedent) because none is large enough to be its own phase and several touch the same surfaces; batching needs one round of test:ui regression coverage instead of sixteen.
caps.describe is false for kafka, sqs, redis and s3, so the definition view's second load never fires for an adapter that cannot serve describe.
In CodeMirrorHost.vue the completion keymap is wrapped in Prec.highest so Arrow Up/Down reach the autocomplete popup; before, defaultKeymap's cursor-move won by array order.
The workbench has a 6px inset on three sides (left, right, top) from the window edge, and the tab strip scrolls horizontally (wheel included) once its tabs overflow.
renderer/fonts.ts checks font availability by canvas measurement, comparing a candidate family against a guaranteed-bogus probe, because document.fonts.check() returns true for anything; an unavailable font is reported honestly.
A font change re-measures every grid's column widths instead of reusing the old widths.
The grid, documents, keyvalue and stream in-page search toolbars share filter-toggle state via views/shared/searchFilter.ts, and restart their scan when the underlying page is replaced (pageVersion.n).
The Mongo documents view and the stream view have match highlighting and empty states, matching the grid and key-value views.
DateTimePicker.vue lives in views/shared/ and is used for the stream view's since-timestamp field, which validates on apply instead of silently discarding an unparseable value.
The grid gutter has a red pending-delete rail that is mutually exclusive with the yellow edit rail.
Delete or ⌘⌫ and the cell context menu's Delete row both work from a cell or range selection, not only a row selection.
The header sort chevron is a 13px codicon pinned to the cell's right edge; the redundant sort-direction text is dropped from the header label.
The Columns/Fields buttons show a plain indicator dot instead of an "N / M" text badge; the counts remain in the tooltip.
The preview command panel puts a blank line between staged statements.
typeGlossary.ts covers every SQL type family plus Mongo's BSON bsonType spellings, and the grid header tooltip includes the type description.
The project tree's twisty has no tooltip (it was redundant) but keeps its aria-label.
The "no colour" swatch in the connection color picker is a diagonal-slash mark, because a hollow ring read as a 13th hue.
The three saved/recent filter menus show full-text tooltips on every entry.
workbench.spec.ts's font-availability scenario is a real executable regression guard that is not Docker-gated; it caught two bugs in fonts.ts and a TextField/:invalid-prop snap-back bug in SettingsDialog.vue, all fixed.
The tests/db/ suites and tests/ui specs kafka, sqs, mongo, redis, data-view, tabs and tooltips are Docker-gated and self-skip without a Docker daemon, so they need a real run in CI or macOS/Colima, including tooltips.spec.ts's D25/D27 assertions.

### v1 P32 Kafka client migration + skip unnecessary group-join
The Kafka adapter uses @confluentinc/kafka-javascript pinned at 1.10.0 (Kafka 4-compatible) as a production dependency; kafkajs is removed from package.json, src and tests.
@confluentinc/kafka-javascript is a native, Electron-ABI-specific module built by scripts/native-electron-build.sh (marker + cache + electron-rebuild), wired as predev/pretest:ui/pretest:db:kafka/prepackage:mac instead of electron-builder's own rebuild step.
The Kafka adapter's client.ts, index.ts and produce.ts use the compat KafkaJS API behind one shared RdConfig.
Kafka errors.ts classifies errors by librdkafka numeric codes instead of kafkajs error names.
Kafka catalog.ts and definition.ts handle the array-shaped fetchTopicMetadata and numeric group-state/type enums, which are resolved to names and never shown as a bare digit.
A Kafka topic's Configuration section is permanently empty with a note, because the new client has no describeConfigs call.
The Kafka browse consumer (read.ts) uses assign() with explicit start offsets and a bounded poll loop and never calls subscribe(), so browsing never joins a consumer group.
The browse consumer's group.id stays a required-but-never-joined constant (kira-studio-browse) rather than a per-browse UUID.
The Kafka adapter suite runs under ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 electron (test:db:kafka, tests/electron-db/kafka.spec.ts on node:test) instead of Bun, because Bun cannot load this driver at any ABI.
The Kafka seed fixture (tests/db/fixtures/0005_kafka_seed.ts) runs the broker's own CLI inside the test container instead of a JS client.
The Kafka test broker moved from cp-kafka:7.6.1 to 8.0.7 (Apache Kafka 4.0), the compatibility target of P32.
Kafka test scenarios 17–20 prove group-less browsing: no kira-studio-browse group appears in listGroups(), it has no committed offsets, a timestamp filter still seeks via fetchTopicOffsetsByTimestamp, and an oversized start offset is refused rather than silently truncated.
P32 is sequenced after P27's non-tabular-view lessons and P31's Kafka fixes so the client swap does not chase two moving targets in the same adapter.
The Kafka native build's blocker was the native ABI rebuild, not the image pull; confluentinc/cp-kafka pulls fine via mirror.gcr.io.
@confluentinc/kafka-javascript's build always tries to vendor-build zlib/libcurl/libcrypto/zstd from source (util/configure.js hardcodes --install-deps --source-deps-only), regardless of system packages.
Setting the CKJS_LINKING environment variable to dynamic makes util/configure.js skip the vendored build and link against the system's dev packages, avoiding source hosts blocked by the sandbox proxy (zlib.net, curl.se).
bun run test:db:kafka (21 cases) runs against a real broker; tests/ipc/kafka/kafka.backend.spec.ts uses real fixtures covering the tree, partition-filter popover children(), an offsetWindow stream read, and topic/consumer-group definitions.
In the Kafka tree, topics are ungrouped, each consumer group is its own root-level node, and 'Consumer groups' is a frontend-only folder over them, following D15's mysql/clickhouse precedent.
A Kafka read fans across both partitions and interleaves them by arrival, not key/offset order, so fixtures are sorted by key before being written.
The Kafka consumer group's coordinator host:port is non-deterministic (fresh Docker container hostname and host-mapped port each run) and is frozen in fixtures per D6.
tests/ui/kafka.spec.ts and tests/ui/support/kafka.ts were deleted once the backend fixture tests and UI half were both verified.

### v1 P33 S3: upload, download, delete, bounded edit
S3 objects support download, upload, delete, and edit; edit is allowed only where the object is small enough to render/parse.
Above a size threshold an S3 object is neither parsed nor rendered, following the grid's large-value-truncation precedent (§8.6), to avoid a stalled frame or runaway parse.
S3 mutation was deferred in P17 S3, which shipped read-only browsing only; P33 is that deferred work, reusing established mutation/editing patterns.
caps.fileTransfer is a new capability flag, true only for S3; for S3, canInsert, canUpdate, canDelete and writable are all true.
s3/mutate.ts uses the same sentinel-through-MutationRowOp technique as redis/mutate.ts, with _key plus $file/$contentType for an upload.
S3 insert refuses an existing key via a HeadObject probe, because S3 has no conditional-create.
An S3 edit's PutObject preserves every other HeadObject attribute (D11).
s3/transfer.ts is the only file in the S3 adapter that imports node:fs.
downloadObject streams to a .kira-partial-* temp file in the destination's own directory, renamed into place on success and unlinked on any failure or cancellation.
The S3 preview ceiling is 4 MB (OBJECT_BODY_PREVIEW_BYTES), with no Body row at all above it; this deliberately reverses the old 32 MB truncate-and-show (OQ1) since Download now serves large objects.
A separate 1 MB limit, OBJECT_BODY_EDIT_BYTES, gates S3 Edit; Edit also refuses a truncated or non-UTF-8 body, each with a tooltip naming the real reason.
main/ipc/files.ts provides one engine-neutral kira:files:chooseSave/chooseOpen IPC domain for native dialogs, with names basename()'d because an S3 key routinely contains /.
KeyValueView.vue's Download button is never blocked by read-only mode (D18).
KeyValueView.vue reuses its existing editOpen ref to swap in the inline ObjectBodyEditor.vue band for an S3 object, instead of a second popover model.
state/objectStore.ts lives in renderer/state/, not views/keyvalue/, so project/menus.ts can reach it; it holds upload-dialog state and the download/upload/delete flows driving UploadObjectDialog.vue.
The S3 demo seed (scripts/demo-dbs/s3/seed.sh) has a size/type ladder (empty/small/medium/over-edit-limit/over-preview-limit/binary), a >1000-object bulk/ prefix exercising ListObjectsV2's continuation loop, and a third kira-uploads-bucket for the empty-bucket-upload case.

### v1 P34 MySQL adapter
The MySQL adapter lives in engine/adapters/mysql/ and follows the fixed internal shape (index.ts, client.ts, query.ts, definition.ts, read.ts) that postgres/ and mariadb/ already establish.
MySQL was added as the last SQL adapter because new-engine work is cheapest once adapter-shape decisions have been exercised twice, and MySQL's dialect is closer to MariaDB's than any other engine, making it the lowest-risk addition.
P34 adds no new dependency: the MariaDB adapter's npm driver mariadb is a genuine dual MariaDB/MySQL client and is reused for MySQL.
engine/adapters/mysql-family/ is a shared core extracted byte-for-byte from mariadb/; its profile.ts defines a 3-field MysqlFamilyProfile (kind, serverLabel, applyEngineOptions), and a per-engine caps literal is passed separately.
mariadb/ keeps index.ts, caps.ts and client.ts with the real MariaDB profile and reduces query.ts, read.ts and definition.ts to re-exports of mysql-family; mysql/ mirrors that shape with the MySQL profile.
mapError in mysql-family/query.ts (renamed from mapMariaError) maps MySQL 8 caching_sha2_password handshake failures to E_AUTH: errno 45044 over a plaintext connection, and errno 45063 against a self-signed cert under sslmode=verify-full. The message names both remedies, sslmode=require or allowPublicKeyRetrieval=true.
shared/domain/connection.ts has the 'mysql' connection kind with DEFAULT_PORT 3306.
views/shared/sqlIdent.ts defines a SqlDialect = 'postgres' | 'mysql' family type, where mariadb and mysql share one dialect, plus sqlDialectFor(). It replaced twelve renderer call sites' hand-rolled kind checks, whose silent undefined fallback for an unhandled kind would have emitted invalid double-quoted identifiers for MySQL.
MySQL gets a real (non-empty) mark in EngineIcon.vue and _icons.html. ConnectionDialog.vue and grouping.ts gained the MySQL tile, an accent colour and the "Routines" folder label, which is shared with MariaDB.
tests/db/mysql.spec.ts ports mariadb.spec.ts's 33 scenarios 1:1 with four engine-difference adjustments: server version, no sequence kind, a wider composite_pk row-estimate band for MySQL's statistics cache, and view-not-sequence children. It adds five MySQL-specific scenarios, for 38 in total.
The five MySQL-specific test scenarios are two auth scenarios against never-authenticated users kira_nocache and kira_pubkey, native-JSON classification, absent integer display widths, and the big nested_json array surviving its WITH RECURSIVE seed port.
The MySQL fixture 0008_mysql_seed.sql and Testcontainers harness support/mysql.ts are byte-for-byte ports of the MariaDB ones. They substitute CHAR(36) for MariaDB's UUID type and a digits-cross-join numbers table for its SEQUENCE-based big_rows insert, because MySQL has neither.
tests/ui/mysql.spec.ts is a small deliberate subset of tests/ui/mariadb.spec.ts. Its load-bearing assertion is the dialect seam: a grid row's Filter by this value comes back backtick-quoted and narrows the grid.
scripts/demo-dbs/ has an eighth service, mysql:8.4, on host port 3307 because 3306 is already used by MariaDB. It has its own byte-parallel init.sql and seed.sql.
Several MySQL-specific findings in the P34 plan (the TLS path, the statistics-cache band, the JSON decode) were reasoned from driver/server source and are flagged in docs/v1/plans/P34-mysql-adapter.md as verify-on-container.

### v1 P35 SQLite adapter
The SQLite adapter lives in engine/adapters/sqlite/ and uses node:sqlite with no new dependency, because the app already relies on it for its own storage (main/storage/db.ts) and it ships in the engine's runtime (Electron/Node 22.5+ or Bun 1.4+, SQLite 3.53+).
The SQLite adapter stands alone with no shared 'family' pattern, because SQLite shares neither wire protocol nor driver with any other engine (P34's shared-driver reason does not apply).
shared/domain/connection.ts has the 'sqlite' kind and a FILE_KINDS set; in fields mode the database field holds the absolute file path, host/port are unused, and there is deliberately no DEFAULT_PORT entry.
In the SQLite adapter, every prepared statement touching real user data (never catalog/pragma queries) must use setReadBigInts(true), because an integer outside Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER would otherwise crash the whole page with ERR_OUT_OF_RANGE.
The SQLite value-to-text codec (toCellText) switches on the value's own JS type, not the column's declared type, because SQLite is dynamically typed.
SQLite keyset paging falls back from primary key to a unique index to the table's implicit rowid; rowid is never surfaced as a page column nor used as mutation identity.
MariaDB/MySQL/Postgres adapters degrade to offset paging where SQLite can use rowid as a stable keyset tiebreaker.
The SQLite adapter sets caps.cancel = false, because node:sqlite has no sqlite3_interrupt and its API is synchronous, so a running statement blocks the event loop and an AbortSignal cannot arrive while it runs.
SQLite mutate() wraps a batch in BEGIN IMMEDIATE/COMMIT/ROLLBACK rather than a deferred BEGIN, so a contended file fails before any row changes.
SQLite definition() returns sqlite_master.sql verbatim with constraints composed from pragmas; CHECK constraints have no catalog, so they are named as absent with a note pointing to the Source text.
ConnectionDialog.vue shows an isFileStyle-gated Database file field with a Browse… button instead of host/port/credentials for SQLite; kira:files:chooseOpen accepts an optional filters payload for this.
SqlDialect has 'sqlite' as a genuine third member, not an alias of 'postgres', wired through languages.ts's CodeMirror dialect and quoteIdent's double-quote default.
tests/db/sqlite.spec.ts ports mysql.spec.ts's 33 scenarios with SQLite adjustments plus seven SQLite-specific scenarios, including int64/BigInt fidelity, the file-is-not-modified guard (the phase's central promise), rowid keyset paging, a locked database surfacing E_TIMEOUT, and refusal of multi-statement input.
tests/db/support/sqlite.ts is a temp-file fixture (mkdtemp, seeded via node:sqlite, a single WITH RECURSIVE CTE for the 1M-row big_rows table), not a container, gated by sqliteAvailable()/SQLITE_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE rather than Docker.
tests/ui/sqlite.spec.ts is the first DB-backed UI spec that runs unconditionally (no Docker gate), and its load-bearing assertion mirrors the MySQL spec's backtick one but with double quotes.
scripts/demo-dbs/sqlite/seed.ts builds the SQLite demo database directly with node:sqlite, with no compose service, using the same e-commerce model as the other relational engines.
In catalog.ts, getReadTarget() must not treat a view as a rowid table, because PRAGMA table_list reports wr: 0 for views too; doing so crashed keyset-fallback reads on views with 'no such column: rowid'.

### v1 P36 ClickHouse adapter
The ClickHouse adapter lives in engine/adapters/clickhouse/ as a standalone eighth SQL-family adapter with ten files (index, client, query, catalog, read, mutate, console, definition, caps, errors) and no shared family, because ClickHouse shares neither wire protocol nor driver with any other adapter.
ClickHouse's OLAP conventions fit the fixed SQL-adapter shape once row identity is treated honestly; no divergent shape was needed.
The adapter uses @clickhouse/client@1, a plain JS HTTP client needing no native build.
The @clickhouse/client HTTP interface has no per-request database override; database comes only from client-construction config, so every statement relies on the connection's construction-time default database plus fully-qualified db.table identifiers (D19).
ClickHouse caps set canUpdate and canDelete permanently false and canInsert true, because a MergeTree PRIMARY KEY is a sparse index over parts, not a unique row key, so no addressable row exists for update/delete.
Never surface system.columns.is_in_primary_key as a PK badge in ClickHouse; toColumnMeta() hardcodes isPrimaryKey: false, because a badge would claim uniqueness the engine lacks.
ClickHouse mutate.ts insert-only assertColumnsKnown deliberately does not pre-block MATERIALIZED/ALIAS generated columns (D28); an explicit mutate() against one surfaces ClickHouse's native rejection verbatim (Adapter rule 4), while the renderer's insert paths gate them via a ColumnDescriptor.generated flag.
The ColumnDescriptor.generated flag is threaded through every adapter's read.ts/console.ts and the grid's add-row, paste and duplicate-as-insert paths.
ClickHouse definition() composes Engine, Sorting key, Primary key, Partition key, Total rows and Comment from system.tables, plus create_table_query verbatim minus its trailing semicolon, and two fixed notes on the sparse-index and no-FK facts.
ClickHouse is a fourth SqlDialect in views/shared/sqlIdent.ts and editor/languages.ts: backtick-quoted, backslashEscapes true, with its own keyword and type lists.
DataGrid, DataToolbar and gridMenu use a canDeleteRows computed to gate the −row button and delete menu by caps, separate from the edit-permission check, with a tooltip naming the real reason.
typeGlossary CLICKHOUSE_DESCRIPTIONS matches case-sensitively in PascalCase against un-lowercased type text, because ClickHouse Int8 and Postgres/MySQL int8 mean unrelated things.
unwrapClickHouseWrappers() recursively strips Nullable(...)/LowCardinality(...) before the generic single-level paren regex, which otherwise mangled Nullable(LowCardinality(String)).
tests/db/clickhouse.spec.ts has 45 scenarios using a ClickHouse testcontainer (@testcontainers/clickhouse@12.1.0) with fixture 0010_clickhouse_seed.sql; it proves duplicate-key rows via ReplacingMergeTree and cancels via KILL QUERY WHERE query_id = ... SYNC sent as a second HTTP request with a self-chosen query_id.
scripts/demo-dbs/ has a tenth service, clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.3 on host port 8124, with its own init.sql and seed.sql re-expressing the e-commerce model in MergeTree terms via numbers() and a data_types_demo row showing ClickHouse's type family.

### v1 P37 RabbitMQ adapter
RabbitMQ fits the existing stream view (P10) on SQS's half (no addressable position, 'batch' pagination) once message data and topology data are kept apart, so it needs no new page kind.
RabbitMQ message routing key, headers and body map onto the five fixed stream columns, while exchanges, bindings and policies move into the definition view, as P23 did for a Kafka topic's partitions.
The RabbitMQ adapter adds no new dependency and uses the rabbitmq_management plugin's HTTP API via the platform's global fetch, because AMQP 0-9-1 cannot enumerate queues, exchanges or bindings; an AMQP client was rejected as a second protocol with no enumeration benefit.
The adapter lives in engine/adapters/rabbitmq/ (index, caps, client, query, catalog, read, mutate, definition, errors; no console.ts because caps.sql is false).
Every RabbitMQ HTTP call goes through query.ts's single request()/requestAll() pair, so the default-vhost %2F encoding, per-request AbortSignal, Authorization header and op-log command text are each written once.
The RabbitMQ adapter's canUpdate and canDelete are permanently false, because a RabbitMQ message has no broker-assigned identity and AMQP has no per-message update or delete.
RabbitMQ mutate() accepts only insert, a publish through an exchange defaulting to the default exchange with the queue's name as routing key; a publish the broker accepts but routes nowhere ({"routed":false}) is reported as an error.
RabbitMQ reads are poll-on-demand with ackmode reject_requeue_true so nothing is removed (messages return marked redelivered), clamped to 500 messages (the management API's page-size ceiling).
RabbitMQ reads request truncation one byte over the budget, so an oversized payload the broker doesn't fully truncate is still marked truncated by the page builder's own logic.
RabbitMQ connect() issues exactly one request (GET /api/overview) and gives distinct messages for wrong credential, no management plugin, and pointing at AMQP port 5672 instead of management port 15672; an amqp:// URI is refused.
A RabbitMQ virtual host reuses the existing database NodeKind with a per-connection-kind label override rendering it "Virtual host"; exchange is the one new NodeKind, a definition-only leaf foldered under an "Exchanges" heading like Kafka consumer groups in P23.
The nameless default exchange is filtered out of every listing, because its blank name cannot survive the app's path/tab-title plumbing.
tests/db/rabbitmq.spec.ts (27 scenarios) runs against a RabbitMQ testcontainer using @testcontainers/rabbitmq@12.1.0, with its wait strategy overridden from the AMQP-era log line to an HTTP probe of the management port; it includes a P13 tripwire of exactly two GETs per vhost expansion regardless of object count.
A RabbitMQ stream-type queue's basic.get refusal is reclassified from a generic query error to E_UNSUPPORTED, and the credential never reaches op_log.command text.
tests/ui/rabbitmq.spec.ts is a small Docker-gated subset (no local-file equivalent for a broker) asserting that the queue tab never auto-loads with a requeue-worded warning, Poll shows the routing key in the key column, and no Delete-message button appears.
scripts/demo-dbs/ has an eleventh service, rabbitmq:4.3.5-management-alpine, on host port 15672 with the AMQP port deliberately not published, seeded by a host-side seed.sh using curl against localhost:15672 because the image has no bulk-publish CLI.

### v1 P39 Modularity and cleanliness
P39 (Modularity and cleanliness) is a user-directed post-v1 phase, not a SPEC §10 deliverable, that reorganizes structure with zero behavior change; nothing in it touches themes (P38 was skipped by user direction).
P39 runs as three iterations per CLAUDE.md's multi-pass convention; each is its own Opus-plan/Sonnet-implement cycle against the tree the previous iteration left, with its own plan file (P39-modularity-and-cleanliness.md, -iter2.md, -iter3.md).
P39 was split into rounds rather than one larger plan because structure debt accumulated over 38 phases faster than a single pass could safely find and fix.
P39 iteration 1 enforced SPEC §11's layering: views/* no longer imports workbench/* or a sibling views/*.
P39 iteration 1 collapsed five near-duplicate data-view modules (page store, chunked scanner, find-widget toolbar, page-size table, load-error classifier) into shared implementations under views/shared/.
After P39 iteration 1, every adapter except RabbitMQ uses one shared errors.ts/mapError.
resolveProjection and safeInt were hoisted into sql-text.ts, and a single unsupported()/noQueryConsole() pair replaced twenty capability-stub throws.
The four data-view folders share one naming scheme: page.ts, search.ts, menu.ts, mutations.ts.
shared/port.ts, layout.ts, settings.ts and shortcuts.ts were moved to match SPEC §11's own tree.
Every data-testid stayed unchanged during P39 iteration 1.
In state/tabs.ts, six patchers differ: three skip the save on a no-op patch and three don't. A naive merge would change behavior, so the difference is kept as an explicit skipUnchanged flag per caller; which behavior is the bug is deferred to the functionality-review phase (P43).
The functionality-review phase is P43, renumbered from P40 when P40/P41 (console UX, Redis/S3 tree navigation) were inserted, then from P42 when a console/grid/cell-editor polish batch was inserted ahead of it.

### v1 P40 Query console UX and cell-editor read-only mode
Each console result set has a stable key from resultPageKey(tabId, seq) in views/console/state.ts, using the tab's own monotonic nextSeq and never an array index, so closing one result set never re-keys its siblings.
The query console shows one result set at a time behind a .p-tab-chip strip (console-result-strip, console-result-tab, console-result-close), replacing the old stacked-panel-per-statement layout.
The fixed 260px-height .result-panel was removed and the active console grid is flex: 1, like the data grid's .grid-area, which fixed the empty band below the last row when the cell editor is closed.
A console toolbar toggle (console-new-result-toggle) decides whether a run's result is appended as a new result set or replaces the current one; it is persisted per tab as consoleTabStateSchema.newResultSet, default false, so already-saved tabs restore unchanged.
views/console/search.ts is the fourth createPageSearch call site, after grid, documents and keyvalue.
activePage(tabId) in console state.ts is the single place that resolves which of a tab's result sets is active, so the two shared cleanup handlers in views/shared/page/ need no per-result-set key scheme.
setActiveResult bumps pageVersion in resultPages.ts so an open find re-scans the newly active result.
CellEditorDock and CellEditorView have an optional readOnly prop, default false, so the other four mounts are unchanged. When true (viewer mode) there is no reason chip (data-read-only="true", no data-read-only-reason) and no UUID-generate, modified, byte-badge, Beautify or Revert.
The read-only cell editor omits the reason chip's data-read-only-reason because no write was ever on offer to refuse; the hidden affordances either did nothing meaningful on a console cell or misrepresented why.
ConsoleResultGrid.vue's header dropped the p-badge data-type badge for the same hover tooltip (name, type, glossary description) as DataGrid.vue's header cell, plus row-hover and tabular-nums parity.
P43 iteration 1 fixed two defects found during P40: stream and documents mounted the cell editor with no write path (F14), and resultPages.ts's setVisibleWindow had no caller (F22), so the console's decode cache was never pruned like the grid's.
P40 is a user-directed, post-v1 phase, not a SPEC §10 deliverable, implemented per docs/v1/plans/P40-query-console-ux-and-readonly-cell-editor.md.

### v1 P41 Redis/S3 tree navigation and the sticky header fix
The sticky ancestor-band bug was caused by DOM order in theme/primitives/VirtualList.vue, not by stickyBand.ts geometry: the sticky slot rendered after the header/top-spacer, so the position: sticky; top: 0 box never engaged from flow position 0. Fixed by moving the slot to be the first child.
The caps.keyBrowser boolean is true only for redis and s3 and marks an engine whose top-level container hides an unbounded, arbitrarily-nested key/object space.
The connection tree keeps only top-level containers (S3 buckets, Redis databases) as leaves: redis/catalog.ts database node and s3/catalog.ts bucket node have hasChildren: false, and key/object navigation moved to a dedicated Browse panel.
project/grouping.ts isLeafKind, project/state/tree.ts revealPath and project/filterTree.ts use the keyBrowser flag so a keyBrowser container is treated as a leaf like a table/topic.
A 'browse' tab kind (shared/domain/tabs.ts, views/browse/{state,BrowseView.vue,menu.ts}) shows exactly one level at a time, with Up/breadcrumb navigation and a client-side substring filter.
Browse tab state.levelPath persists which level a reactivated tab resumes at, per the §8.4 identity rule (unchanged).
Container/key/object row menus were moved verbatim from the tree's now-deleted namespaceMenu/prefixMenu/keyMenu/objectMenu into the Browse panel.
uploadMenuItem lives in state/objectStore.ts so the tree's bucket row and the Browse panel's container rows share one upload entry point without crossing the project/ → views/ layering rule.
state/viewCommands.ts's leaf-registry pattern was extended with registerBrowseInvalidate/browseInvalidate for Browse invalidation.
The entry points into a Browse tab are ProjectTree.vue's onOpen and project/menus.ts's browseMenuItem, which is first in the menu ahead of Refresh.
redis.spec.ts, s3.spec.ts and memory.spec.ts open and drive a Browse tab instead of tree-based namespace/prefix/key/object navigation, and assert that no prefix row exists in the tree.
tests/ui/sqlite.spec.ts has inline-height sticky-band assertions covering the sticky header fix.
UploadObjectDialog.vue's containerPrefix computed captured only the container's immediate local segment (pathTail), so uploading via an S3 prefix nested 2+ levels deep dropped ancestor segments from the prefilled key; fixed in P43.

### v1 P42 Console, grid and cell-editor polish batch
The query console's newResultSet setting defaults to true, so a run opens a new result tab by default; an explicit saved 'off' from before this phase still restores off, because an explicit preference outranks a changed default.
Query console result tabs have a right-click menu with Close, Close others and Close to the right; the result-chip strip uses --kira-h-sm height with wheel-to-horizontal scrolling shared with the main tab strip via renderer/wheelScroll.ts.
The document view files (ejson.ts, documentRows.ts, DocumentTree.vue) live in views/shared/document/, and rows.ts's dependency on documents/page.ts is inverted into a registered row source.
The Mongo console result uses the shared document view, collapsed by default (unlike the data tab's expanded default, since a console run is usually a shape-skimming glance at many documents), with no edit/delete affordance.
lintMongoConsole checks arguments with the shell-literal parser tryParseShellText (exported from ejson.ts), not JSON.parse, because Mongo statement grammar allows ObjectId(…)/ISODate(…) constructors that a plain JSON parser would reject.
Word wrap is a single appearanceSettingsSchema.wordWrap setting (default true, matching prior hard-coded behavior), applied in CodeMirrorHost.vue through a fifth Compartment, replacing per-component hard-coding.
The grid supports drag-to-select (mousedown, mouseenter while held, mouseup, with auto-scroll at the viewport edge); its corner cell selects everything as one range selection, never a row selection, which would be an O(rows) Array.includes inside the render loop.
Two adjacent selected grid cells share one border at their common edge using four independent box-shadow custom properties, rather than each drawing a full outline.
The numeric stepper's chevrons are fixed via the icon prop rather than a CSS override, because CodiconIcon's inline font-size style beats an external rule.
Every scroll container's corner square is transparent instead of Chromium's opaque white default.
workbench/state/tooltip.ts accepts a structured { title, meta?, body? } as well as a plain string; the grid/console-result column header tooltip is the first caller, and the plain-text data-kira-tip mirror is unchanged.
The cell editor's format picker drops uuid and url formats because both were inert on selection; detectUuid's regex remains as a guard inside detectBase64 so a real UUID is not mis-detected as base64.
The iso8601 format label is "Time (ISO…)", and the format list is reordered common-first into three groups.
The cell editor's validate.ts checks the buffer against the effective format (JSON/XML balance, CSV column-count consistency, SQL lint error, parseable timestamp, decodable base64/hex shape) and shows a named "invalid" chip.
The cell editor's format picker is an app-drawn ContextMenu instead of a native select, because a native option sits outside elementFromPoint's reach and per-row hover explanations (FORMAT_HELP, also used by the trigger's tooltip) need it; arrow-key navigation of the native control was lost.
The cell editor's generators panel has four entries (UUID v4, ULID, a random hex token, "Now") and is never format-gated.
EditBufferActions has a showBytes prop (default true); the cell editor passes false so the byte figure is not duplicated with the status badge.
DateTimePicker.vue's month/year label is a button cycling three views (days, a 3×4 month grid, a 4×4 16-year block); prev/next arrows page the active view, and picking a month or year moves only the calendar view, never the selected value.
CONNECTION_COLOR_CHOICES offers eight swatches (none, red, amber, green, cyan, blue, magenta, grey) at a 42° minimum OKLCH hue gap, versus the full palette's worst gap of 25.6° (blue↔indigo).
connectionColorSchema stays whole, because narrowing it would make a connection saved with a retired colour silently vanish on next launch rather than merely stop being offered.
runChunkedScan takes an optional priority window scanned in its own animation frame first, then runs the ordinary ascending pass from row 0 rebuilding the match list from scratch, so the published order stays strictly ascending.
Every scan tick publishes into searchState marked pending, so the filter toggle (matchedRows()) waits for a completed scan before hiding rows, avoiding hide-then-unhide flicker.
views/shared/page/visibleRows.ts carries each tab's on-screen row window to the scanner, reported by DataGrid.vue's bounds watch and a VirtualList.vue visible-range emit forwarded by DocumentView.vue and ConsoleResultGrid.vue.
KeyValueView.vue renders every loaded row without virtualization, so it has no visible window to report and keeps ascending-only scan priority while still getting progressive publication.

### v1 P43 Functionality review
P43 is a user-directed, post-v1 functionality review phase (not a SPEC §10 deliverable), run as three iterations; unlike P39 it may change behavior.
S3 upload through a nested prefix keeps every ancestor segment, not just the immediate parent, via containerPrefix in UploadObjectDialog.vue.
ConsoleResultGrid.vue's decoded-cell cache prunes as it scrolls, using resultPages.ts's setVisibleWindow, which is now wired to a caller.
The stream and document cell-editor docks no longer offer a write path that silently did nothing.
A NUL byte in a ClickHouse identifier is rejected instead of reaching the query.
A failed commit's server error reaches the grid, and every immediate mutation surfaces its own failure the same way.
A filter change invalidates the tab's stale row count.
A disconnect returns every open tab to Reconnect & load, not just the tab that noticed.
A committed mutation reloads every other tab open on the same target; a key/object mutation refreshes an open Browse tab; a failed mutation still invalidates the target's cached pages.
Mongo's offset-cursor skip guard fires only on a genuine page jump (offset > 0), not on page one's { offset: 0 }.
Redis list pagination no longer clamps to the 500-row LIST_WINDOW, so a requested page size is honoured.
Kafka's readTopic clamps a window whose high watermark falls inside an uncommitted transaction gap.
useRunState in state/runState.ts prefers a still-running op over a newer finished one, so the toolbar ring does not go dark mid-query.
metadata-cache.ts has a per-connection MAX_ROWS_PER_CONNECTION eviction pass.
TreeChildren's wire shape is { nodes, truncated? } across every adapter, engine/control.ts, both protocol layers and the renderer's Browse view, so a round-capped listing reports truncation.
The cell editor's selection clears on every page change across console, keyvalue and stream views, not just the grid.
A truncated cell value never reports as broken JSON.
A generated ULID's timestamp half decodes to the real date; toCrockford's trailing-pad encoder was wrong for a 50-bit left-padded field.
A closed console result set releases its parsed documents and expansion state instead of leaking them.
A superseded search scan never republishes stale matches, and an open inline grid editor no longer acts as a drag-select handle.
Docker-free bun:test specs over a bun:sqlite + drizzle-orm/sqlite-proxy harness (tests/db/metadata-cache.spec.ts, tests/db/run-state.spec.ts) verify the metadata-cache eviction and run-state behavior.
A truncated children refresh drops the stale complete-looking cache row it couldn't replace, rather than serving it without a truncation strip.
views/browse/state.ts has a per-tab supersession guard like other views' load(), so a slow Redis/S3 listing cannot land after the user navigated elsewhere.
Refresh on a cursor-paged Redis key (hash/set/zset/stream) returns the pager to page one instead of drifting forward.
The find toolbar keeps the match the user navigated to mid-scan once the scan finishes, rather than snapping back to the first hit.
DocumentTree.vue scrolls sideways so long values in expanded documents are reachable, using the same chrome-less idiom as TabStrip.vue and ConsoleView.vue.
Every ContextMenu.vue menu, including the cell editor's format picker, has roving keyboard navigation.
Whole-row or whole-column grid selections draw their own end caps instead of leaving the outline open.
Caps has an optional maxPageSize so the stream page-size picker doesn't offer RabbitMQ sizes a basic.get poll can never serve.

### v1 P44 Unit tests
Phase P44 is a user-directed, post-v1 phase (not a SPEC §10 deliverable) that adds unit tests sparingly: only where there is enough logic to justify one, and prefer a unit test over existing Playwright UI coverage when both could apply.
tests/db/ is out of scope for new unit tests: no file is added to it, moved out of it, or edited in it.
Put every new unit spec in tests/unit/, which has its own tsconfig.json, a typecheck:unit script folded into bun run typecheck, and a test:unit script, mirroring tests/db/.
The two Docker-free specs metadata-cache.spec.ts and run-state.spec.ts from P43 iteration 3 remain in tests/db/; moving them (a git mv plus two prose citations) is handed to P45, whose remit includes the repo folder structure.
P44 added five unit specs: shared/domain/sql-split.ts lexical regimes, engine/adapters/sql-text.ts keyset-eligibility/page-token logic, views/shared/page/scan.ts scheduler, redis/s3 catalog truncated flag, and the load supersession/cursor-strategy reload in views/browse/state.ts and views/keyvalue/state.ts.
The scan.ts frame-driven scheduler is tested with a queue-based fake requestAnimationFrame that hands out frames one at a time, because a settled-end-state Playwright test cannot observe the state between two frames.
The redis/s3 catalog truncated flag is tested with hand-written fake clients across all 200/20 round-cap rounds, avoiding seeding hundreds of thousands of keys in a live assertion.
The load supersession guard in views/browse/state.ts and the cursor-strategy reload in views/keyvalue/state.ts are tested with manually resolved promises that force the older-lands-after-newer interleaving, which Playwright cannot force on demand.
If a defect surfaces while writing a unit spec, write it up and hand it to §8; do not fix it under a test: commit.
The Testcontainers-backed suites are the only prior coverage of sql-text.ts and sql-split.ts paths and only walk the eligible-and-correct path.

### v1 P45 Docs cleanup
SPEC.md and its plans/ are retired as v1 history, not a living spec; what is still true and durable lives in ARCHITECTURE.md, which CLAUDE.md points to and which points out to PACKAGING.md and PERF.md.
SPEC.md and docs/v1/plans/ were not moved, because 56 in-repo citations name docs/v1/plans/ by path (six in live src/ and tests/ comments); moving would dangle them and force retro-editing frozen history (D57/D58).
ARCHITECTURE.md, PACKAGING.md and PERF.md live in docs/, and design/ lives in docs/design/, so docs/*.md is living documentation; docs/v1/ holds only SPEC.md, plans/ and an archive-banner README.md as the v1 record.
ARCHITECTURE.md is authoritative over SPEC.md; SPEC.md's front matter was rewritten to invert authority to ARCHITECTURE.md (D60/D61).
ARCHITECTURE.md absorbed the process model, storage, caching, the §5.1 per-engine mapping table, the stack and §2's invariants, pointing to docs/PERF.md §1 for budget numbers instead of duplicating the table (D62).
ARCHITECTURE.md has a ~55-line UI architecture section distilled rather than copying SPEC §8's 604 UI lines verbatim (D63); it covers view-kind-by-page-kind selection, tab-identity rule, session-restore reconnect gate, staged-vs-immediate write model, per-view mounted cell editor panel, Browse panel's one-level model, and shared find/chunked-scan machinery.
Test layout rule (D66): tests/unit/ needs nothing external and finishes in about a second, while tests/db/ and tests/electron-db/ need a real external resource; tests/ has four directories.
preconnect.spec.ts stays in tests/db/ because it spawns real OS processes and measured 23.0s of a 24.1s total; metadata-cache.spec.ts and run-state.spec.ts moved to tests/unit/.
Bun's shared module registry makes specs' inline window stubs race for which one wins for the whole test run, so a shared tests/unit/support/window.ts is used instead.
The real v1 implemented range is P0–P37; P38 was skipped by user direction.

### v1 P46 Disable unnecessary Chromium/Electron features
Phase P46 (post-v1, user-directed, not a SPEC §10 deliverable) audits the app's Electron/Chromium surface and disables capabilities the app does not use.
Renderer hardening lives in src/main/security.ts (rendererWebPreferences, hardenSession, hardenWindow); its only electron import is import type, so it unit-tests without mocking.
Every P46 disable is backed by a reproduction against the shipped renderer, not an inference from Electron's hardening checklist.
Before hardening, window.open to a remote URL returned a live handle (window count 1→2), and location.href to a remote origin navigated the real app away and destroyed the workbench; the meta CSP does not govern top-level navigation.
Without a permission handler, every Chromium permission (notifications, geolocation, media, device APIs) was granted by default.
With devTools: true in a packaged build, openDevTools() stayed reachable even though menu.ts's dev gating already closed the keyboard shortcut path.
Spellchecker was enabled on a stock window, so it would spellcheck the connection dialog's password field when the eye toggle reveals it; it is now disabled.
autocomplete="off" is set on the shared TextField.vue component, which about sixty call sites share.
electron-builder.yml sets three Electron fuses to false (runAsNode, enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable, enableNodeCliInspectArguments) to close a Node inspector on the main process in packaged builds.
Never make the permission handler deny-all; allowlist only clipboard-read and clipboard-sanitized-write, shared by the request and check handlers, because deny-all broke navigator.clipboard (grid paste and the 38 copyText call sites in clipboard.ts).
Never turn off grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges, because it bricks the app: the renderer's script type=module crossorigin loaded over file:// is CORS-refused without it.
scripts/verify-packaging.sh has static checks S6/S7 guarding that the three safe fuses are set and that grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges stays untouched.
Background throttling stays on, because nothing on the quit-flush or run-state-ticker paths depends on a hidden window's timers running unthrottled.
Hardware acceleration stays on because the grid's scroll budget depends on GPU compositing.
Electron disableDialogs/safeDialogs are not enabled, because window.confirm() gates six destructive actions and replacing them is a UI change for a future phase.
No --disable-features command-line switch list was added, because such a list is fail-open and untestable, and the capabilities it would target either don't exist in the app or have a first-class, assertable Electron API.
Hardening is tested by tests/ui/hardening.spec.ts (Docker-free, ten scenarios) plus tests/unit/security.spec.ts and menu.spec.ts.

### v1 P47 `@tanstack/vue-virtual` spike
P47 is a post-v1 spike, not a SPEC §10 deliverable, gated by an evidence-based go/no-go decision (D14): replace DataGrid.vue's hand-rolled row/column virtualization with @tanstack/vue-virtual's row/column virtualizers.
Swapping the virtualization library risks silently regressing the P29 invariants: overscan coverage, zero-mutation sub-row scrolls, and DOM-cell bounds.
On the row axis, ceil(560 / 28) = 20 overscan rows reproduces the old fixed overscan-row count exactly, bridging P29's 560px pixel-budget overscan.
The column axis bypasses the library's own overscan and uses visibleColumnRange's expansion loop unchanged as a custom rangeExtractor (columnRangeExtractor in columns.ts), so the same pixel budget and per-side cap apply.
TanStack's default observeElementRect measures the scroll element's border-box size, which does not subtract a visible scrollbar's thickness (unlike clientWidth/clientHeight). On scroll_grid this ~12-15px discrepancy caused 65 DOM mutations on a 4px sub-row scroll, failing budgets.spec.ts's zero-mutation assertion.
DataGrid.vue uses a custom observeElementRect (observeScrollElementRect) that measures clientWidth/clientHeight via its own ResizeObserver instead of TanStack's border-box default.
The D14 verdict for the @tanstack/vue-virtual spike is go: all five no-go criteria were clean, and columns.ts loses visibleColumnRange's 43-line binary-search-plus-expansion-loop for the smaller columnRangeExtractor seam.
The migration improved all three D13 work-p50 metrics: big_rows vertical 2.4→2.2ms, scroll_grid horizontal 6.3→6.2ms, scroll_grid vertical/wide-table 6.2→5.1ms (see docs/PERF.md §2.1).
columnRangeExtractor has a dedicated unit spec, tests/unit/column-range.spec.ts, with 7 cases covering both caps, both edge clamps, single-column, narrower-than-viewport, zero-column, and uniform and variable widths (D16).
budgets.spec.ts and perf.spec.ts needed no edits for the TanStack migration (D17).

### v1 P48 Cross-view reusability audit
P48 is a user-directed post-v1 cross-view reusability audit-and-refactor (not a SPEC §10 deliverable) of the six data-view kinds, their ViewChrome toolbars and the backend engine adapters; it extracts shared configurable primitives only where duplication is real, not merging things that merely look alike.
P48 was motivated by the user report that a fix or improvement made in one data view isn't reflected in its siblings, because apparently shared components were often two independently maintained ones.
P48 held zero-behavior-change discipline (D0) except two commits: countOpId ignores a superseded row-count response on document/key-value/stream tabs (ported from grid/state.ts), and the data grid's own 1px chrome border was dropped when it adopted ViewChrome.
All data-testids were preserved in P48 except document-prev/document-next, renamed to document-pager-prev/document-pager-next with the shared Pager.vue.
errors.ts has throwIfCancelled, which re-checks cancellation after an await (the mid-flight sibling of assertNotCancelled, which runs before a call starts); it replaced 26 duplicated copies across eight adapters.
