abort.ts's withAbortRace implements the callback-style abort/settle race shared by the postgres and mysql-family adapters, replacing six copies.
shared/protocol/page.ts's unpagedPosition replaced ten copies of the unpaged-position literal.
sql-text.ts holds the SQL read path's shared keyset planning (assertKeysetSupported, resolveFetchColumns, buildScanOrderBy, buildKeysetPosition for postgres/mysql/sqlite) and count/filter logic (whereClause, parseCountValue), plus primaryKeyFromIndexes/resolveKeyShape moved from postgres's and mysql-family's catalog modules.
requireConnected is the shared connected-handle guard in the adapters, replacing ten copies.
createRuntimeStore provides setActionError, toggleSearchOpen and setSearchOpen, replacing five per-view copies.
viewOp.ts provides beginOp/applyLoadFailure, used by the load paths of grid, documents, keyvalue, stream and console; browse keeps its own loadSeq-based supersession (D18).
views/shared/page/store.ts is the single page-cache implementation behind all five page modules.
views/shared/immediateMutation.ts backs the ten immediate-mutation writers of documents, keyvalue and stream.
views/shared/page/search.ts's createMatchIndex and scan.ts's emptyScan/tabularRowScanner/keyValueRowScanner are the shared find-toolbar match index and row scanners for all views.
DataView.vue (the SQL grid) mounts ViewChrome like every other view, with DataToolbar/FilterToolbar targeting ViewChrome's named slots; its hand-rolled ViewHeader/.p-toolbar-rail was deleted.
views/shared/page/Pager.vue is the pager shared by the grid and the document list.
views/shared/document/DocumentRow.vue carries the Mongo document row head, shared by DocumentView.vue and ConsoleResultGrid.vue, each supplying its own #actions/#body slot content.
views/shared/page/columns.ts exports columnHeaderTooltip, GUTTER_WIDTH and DEFAULT_COLUMN_WIDTH, shared by DataGrid.vue and ConsoleResultGrid.vue; the CSS gutter width is the --gutter-width custom property.
biome.json's views/<kind>/* sibling-import restriction group now includes browse, the seventh view folder, which had been uncovered since P41.

### v1 P49 Vue/Node ecosystem library spike
P49 spike concluded no Vue/Node ecosystem library is worth adopting; P47's @tanstack/vue-virtual adoption for the SQL grid stays.
@tanstack/vue-virtual is not extended to VirtualList.vue's other six call sites, because every one is single-axis and TanStack's demonstrated win was specifically the column axis.
VirtualList.vue has three features with no library equivalent: a precomputed rowHeights prefix sum, in-flow sticky slots, and a pixel-inset scrollToIndex.
Pinia, TanStack Query, TanStack Form, VueUse, vue-router, a third-party component kit, drizzle-kit and lru-cache were each rejected for architecture-specific reasons.
TanStack Query is rejected because the cache already lives in the engine process, every read is a logged operation, cancellation is server-side, and a cursor-paged page has no stable key.
@floating-ui/vue is the one library marked 'revisit later' by the P49 spike.
ConsoleResultGrid.vue now shares DataGrid.vue's columnRangeExtractor, GUTTER_WIDTH and DEFAULT_COLUMN_WIDTH behind a second TanStack column virtualizer, while its rows stay on VirtualList.vue; previously it rendered every column of every visible row with no cap.
ConsoleResultGrid keeps rows on VirtualList.vue because nothing on the row side showed a symptom (the conservative half of D4's open question).
KeyValueView.vue and StreamView.vue now mount VirtualList.vue; before, they rendered every loaded row directly with no virtualization, up to 10,000 rows.
OVERSCAN_PX, MAX_OVERSCAN_COLUMNS and observeScrollElementRect live in views/shared/page/columns.ts so both grids' column axes share them and avoid a second copy.
theme/anchoredPosition.ts is one pure function parametrized by a 'menu'/'callout' strategy that replaces the hand-rolled overlay positioners in PopoverPanel.vue, AppTooltip.vue and ErrorPopover.vue, which had drifted apart and shipped a bug.
ContextMenu.vue is left untouched by the anchoredPosition refactor because it is mouse-anchored, a deliberately different positioning model.
tests/ui/sqlite.spec.ts has a scenario showing console column virtualization: a 200-column single-row query renders well under 200 DOM cells, including after scrolling to the far right where the last column's value is visible.
tests/ui/redis.spec.ts checks the status line's loaded-count instead of a DOM node count for a 30-row list, since that exceeds VirtualList's default overscan; a new test bounds DOM nodes for a 5000-field Redis hash's 100-row page.

### v1 P50 UI test suite split: IPC-boundary backend tests and mocked-IPC frontend tests
The tests/ui/ per-adapter Playwright specs are split at the IPC boundary into two tiers: only 2–3 specs (sqlite, mongo, s3) stay as full end-to-end coverage; other adapters get a backend test and a frontend test.
A backend test drives the real IPC control-channel handler against a real (Testcontainers or file-backed) data store with no Electron renderer; a frontend test drives the real rendered UI in Playwright/Electron with the IPC layer mocked, asserting rendering/interaction only.
The frontend test's mock payload and the backend test's checked fixture must live in the same file in the same folder, so a backend assertion fails first if the backend stops producing a shape the frontend mocks.
The IPC backend tier is its own suite (tests/ipc/**/*.backend.spec.ts, run by bun run test:ipc:be) and does not join tests/db/, a repo convention.
The IPC backend files must stay out of tests/db/ because bun run test:db is a directory glob that Bun would try to run those files under and fail.
tests/db/ covers the adapter against a real container, while tests/ipc/ covers the four layers above the adapter: control channel, data-op dispatch, tree/metadata cache, port framing.
Each tests/ipc adapter folder holds three files sharing one name: <adapter>.backend.spec.ts, <adapter>.frontend.spec.ts, <adapter>.fixture.ts; colocation of both halves and the fixture is the binding rule, not a directory split.
window.kira is deeply frozen and non-configurable, so renderer-side IPC mocking is impossible; the control channel is mocked by swapping ipcMain handlers in the main process via ElectronApplication.evaluate.
The bulk-data port is mocked by posting a fresh MessageChannel's port1 through the same {__kira:'port'} door production code already listens on for reload/reconnect.
The control mock excludes a request's tabId from its arg-matching key, because it is a per-tab UUID generated by the renderer at open time and cannot be reproduced from a fixture; the port mock likewise ignores opId/tabId.
Real containers in this sandbox are pulled via mirror.gcr.io, a Docker Hub read-through mirror not blocked by the network policy, unlike Docker Hub directly or quay.io; this is recorded in CLAUDE.md.
tests/ipc/support/harness.spec.ts proves the IPC test harness itself without Docker, using a temp-file SQLite fixture.
In the MySQL split, the "Routines" folder is a frontend-only grouping over real leaf-kind nodes; the backend has no "folder" node kind at all.
The kept full-stack anchors sqlite.spec.ts, mongo.spec.ts and s3.spec.ts each carry a header comment naming what they are now the sole proof of.
tests/db/ is exhaustive adapter-edge coverage, while the tests/ipc backend half is one linear fixture-capturing scenario per adapter, so similar-looking assertions mostly test different layers.
For redis, truncated is asserted undefined at the adapter level and false at the IPC level, both correct, because tree-service.ts normalizes what the adapter omits.
Duplicate assertions found by the db-vs-ipc review were removed from the tests/ipc backend specs only (rabbitmq default-exchange and poll routing-key; sqs empty-queue count), never from tests/db/ (P50 D1).

### v1 P51 Wails (Go) shell + Go app core + Node engine child process, migration spike (Superseded for implementation by P52)
P51 planned a migration spike moving the app off Electron onto Wails (Go) with a partial backend migration; it is superseded for implementation by P52 and never went past the plan.
In the P51 architecture, Wails/Go is the native shell and absorbs all of src/main (IPC handlers, SQLite storage, settings/layout/tabs/saved-queries/filters/connections persistence, op log, pre-connect scripts, tree service, connection state, credential encryption, logging).
In the P51 architecture, src/engine (119 files, 14,743 lines, eleven DB adapters, cache, scheduler) stays unchanged in TypeScript and runs as a plain Node.js child process spawned from Go's os/exec, as src/main/engine-host.ts forks it from Electron today.
P51 vendors a real Node runtime binary in the bundle, with the engine's .js and node_modules shipped as loose files beside it.
The user ruled out Rust/Tauri in favor of Go, and ruled out any non-Node JS runtime or single-file compilation (Bun, Deno, Electrobun's Cottontail, pkg, Node SEA, bun build --compile).
Install size is explicitly not a concern, which licenses vendoring a real Node runtime and removes the self-extract-then-dlopen packaging/hardened-runtime risk of the Tauri plan.
Backward compatibility with existing kira.sqlite rows is not required, so the Go-side storage/settings schema is defined fresh without zod-.default()-style compatibility machinery.
The motivation for the migration is docs/PERF.md §2.2's ~620MB Chromium/Electron baseline overhead against a 350MB memory budget, which no app-level lever can reach; tests/e2e/memory.spec.ts was removed rather than kept red (commit d23121e).
The P51 spike's deliverable is a written report covering the renderer-Go bridge, Go-Node engine transport, bundling/signing, engine supervision, Keychain seam, E2E testing, a real memory measurement, per-module disposition, and a go/no-go recommendation.
The Go-Node engine transport would frame the existing transport-agnostic PortRequest/PortResponse/PortEvent protocol over stdio pipes or a Unix domain socket; this is new code on both sides and the one change src/engine needs.
Incrementally migrating individual DB adapters from the Node engine to native Go is a natural future direction (the per-kind adapters/registry.ts loader map suits it) but is not designed in P51.
P51 required the user's explicit sign-off before implementation, since a spike is not exempt from CLAUDE.md's Opus-plans-then-sign-off-then-implement loop.
Wails has no first-class sidecar/external-binary mechanism (discussion #3021 is answered only with go:embed), so spawning the Node child and bundling the vendored runtime are hand-rolled.
Wails v3's default binding transport is a fetch() to a local /wails/runtime endpoint with a JSON.stringify body (a full JSON round-trip), but its runtime source exposes a setTransport() override worth investigating for bulk data.
No first-party WebDriver story surfaced for Wails; discussion #4205 suggests pointing Playwright at wails dev's localhost:34115, which is not shell-integration coverage.
The E2E decision is that the UI suite moves fully to Playwright's isolated webkit tier, permanently giving up native menu/window/lifecycle coverage, real-IPC-bridge correctness, real Keychain coverage and src/main/security.ts's navigation-lock posture.
In the Wails plan, losing the engine means reconnecting databases (engine-host.ts's existing no-auto-respawn policy), since settings/tabs/layout/op log live in the Go process.
In the Wails plan, Keychain access becomes ordinary in-process Go code replacing safeStorage, with no privileged cross-process surface.
Wails being smaller and less mature than Tauri is an accepted trade-off given the user's Go-over-Rust preference and is not relitigated by the spike.
P51 supersedes the former "P51 Tauri + Node sidecar migration spike"; P20's row and its out-of-scope verdict are unchanged.

### v1 P52-P57 Wails/Go migration, implemented
The Electron shell was replaced with a native Wails v3 (Go) shell, absorbing all of src/main into Go; src/engine (eleven DB/queue/object-store adapters in TypeScript) stays as designed.
The TypeScript engine runs as a genuine Node.js child process under a real vendored Node runtime shipped beside the app, not Electron's and not the system one.
The bundled engine runs correctly under a bare Node with no load-bearing Electron coupling.
The credential cipher is AES-256-GCM under the app's own key with a kira:v2: envelope, replacing Chromium safeStorage's AES-128-CBC; kira:v1: data is deliberately not migrated (fresh-database cutover).
The Wails build uses the bundle identifier com.kirathecat.kira-studio-shell during P52-P56, deliberately distinguishable from the Electron build for the coexistence window.
The Go storage repos layer (P53) matches the prior Drizzle-backed TypeScript repos one-for-one, and ./internal/... needs nothing but the Go toolchain.
The Go engine-host speaks the engine's stdio-framed wire protocol, replacing Electron's parentPort; stdout is the frame channel, not a log sink, so src/engine was fixed accordingly.
P54 fixed three Go concurrency bugs: a writeFrame/stdin mutex deadlock, a cmd.Wait() race with the stdout reader, and a shared channel that split push events between subscribers instead of delivering each to all.
The migration depends on a pinned-wails3-version discipline, established in P55.
P56 used a deferred-emitter/deferred-dialogs pattern for Wails' construction-order constraints, and a bridge-level argument-validation convention.
P56 switched resolveEngine() to the real bundled engine, which exposed that the renderer's engine-status logic still expected Electron's MessagePort handoff, leaving the status pill stuck on "connecting".
The renderer bridge (src/renderer/bridge/{control,port}.ts) uses Wails' generated bindings for the control plane and one named JSONStream for the data plane, replacing contextBridge/MessagePort.
JSONStream's JSON round-trip silently dropped TextColumnChunk's TypedArrays; this was fixed with reviveChunks.
bridge/stream.go forwards bulk-data bytes verbatim with no parse or copy, an architectural invariant.
The test tier was rebuilt: tests/ui/ has 36 specs against real WebKit with both wire planes mocked; tests/e2e-real/ has 2 specs against a real -tags server Go binary; tests/e2e/ was retired.
tests/electron-db/ was folded into tests/db/kafka.spec.ts, running under the vendored Node.
Retiring tests/e2e/ permanently lost MongoDB's own anchor value coverage; hardening.spec.ts and startup.spec.ts were deleted for having no Wails analogue.
A DataGrid.vue performance bug was fixed: an unmemoized v-tooltip binding rewrote every header cell's DOM attributes on every scroll tick.
Go tests were cut from 60 to 23 files under a stricter bar of advanced/complex/deeply-nested logic only, now written into the testing conventions.
M6 deleted src/main/, src/preload/ and src/engine/index.ts; M7 removed Electron from the build, including electron.vite.config.ts, electron-builder.yml and scripts/native-electron-build.sh.
The shipping bundle identity must be set in CFBundleExecutable and in AppProcessSet's process-matching needle, in addition to other places; sign-bundle.sh and verify-packaging.sh were rewritten for the real Wails bundle layout.
The Go↔Node engine stdio protocol forces JSON.stringify of bulk typed-array data, causing a measured memory/CPU regression versus Electron: roughly 11x text inflation and 48x transient heap inflation for a 100KB chunk; not fixed in P57.
AppProcessSet scanned the whole system process table once per search needle instead of once per measurement tick; this Go performance bug was fixed.
Open items going into P58/P59: the Kafka native-module packaging gap, dead KiraApi/AppInfo interfaces in src/shared/protocol/ipc.ts (the one remaining non-comment "electron" reference in src/), and CI workflow files pending a workflow-scoped push.

### v1 P58 DB adapters in Go, Node engine sidecar removed
P58 migrated the DB adapters from the Node engine sidecar (src/engine) to native Go, called in-process, and removed the Node engine subprocess, vendored Node runtime and stdio/JSON transport entirely.
P58 had six sub-phases: P58a (Go substrate + Postgres, checkpoint C1), P58b (MySQL/MariaDB/SQLite/ClickHouse), P58c (MongoDB/Redis), P58d (SQS/S3), P58e (Kafka, checkpoint C2), P58f (cutover deleting src/engine, enginehost and the vendored Node runtime).
The parent plan for P58 is docs/v1/plans/P58-go-native-adapters.md, amended in place per sub-phase findings; CLAUDE.md holds per-sub-phase implementation findings.
The P58 Deliverable column was left as originally written per the folder's never-retro-edit rule (docs/v1/README.md), though it omits sqlite and wrongly lists rabbitmq.
sqlite migrated to native Go in P58b, and rabbitmq was never migrated because it had already been dropped as a supported connection kind before P58.
The ten kinds migrated to native Go are postgres, mariadb, mysql, sqlite, clickhouse, mongodb, redis, kafka, sqs and s3; nativeKinds reached ten of ten at P58e's M9.3 commit.
Go driver choices: pgx/v5 (postgres), go-sql-driver/mysql (mariadb/mysql), modernc.org/sqlite (sqlite), hand-rolled net/http client (clickhouse), mongo-driver/v2, go-redis/v9, franz-go+kadm (kafka), aws-sdk-go-v2 (sqs/s3).
modernc.org/sqlite is pure Go with no cgo and is also used for the app's own storage layer, closing the last cgo dependency in the product's own code.
Checkpoint C1 (after P58a M5) proved one native Postgres connection served end to end while the Node child still served the other nine kinds.
Checkpoint C2 (before P58f M10) proved all ten kinds passed a full manual pass with the Node child spawned but idle, confirmed by a request-scoped warning counter reading zero.
The migration loses MySQL's allowPublicKeyRetrieval, because go-sql-driver/mysql has no equivalent, a narrow security-posture loss.
Native migration gains: Kafka regains DescribeConfigs/DescribeCluster (lost since P32), SQLite gets real server-side cancellation, MongoDB document view keeps the on-disk type tag of whole-number doubles, and a packaged build can connect to Kafka for the first time.
P58f M10 deleted src/engine (110 files, 13,637 lines), enginehost, enginetest, the vendored Node runtime, scripts/vendor-node.sh and all packaging steps referencing them.
tests/db/ survives as a shared fixture corpus rather than a spec suite.
The backend half of tests/ipc/ moved to a Go fixture generator (shell/internal/ipcfixture) that still writes the same *.fixture.ts modules read by frontend specs, preserving the anti-drift guarantee.
The Kafka native-module packaging gap left by P57 was closed in P58e, then became moot once P58f deleted the Node engine.
<!-- /chapter v1 -->

<!-- chapter v1.1 -->
## v1.1
Source: docs/v1.1/ at bd0c224688d9. 22 phases.
v1 shipped, with its spec at docs/v1/SPEC.md, closed out through P58.
The v1.1 folder uses a fresh phase numbering (P1, P2, …) instead of continuing v1's, because v1's numbering was specific to that spec's scope.
Real-bridge tests use the tests/e2e-real arrangement, driven by injected JS rather than Playwright, so the renderer is a genuine WKWebView rather than Chromium.
DataGrid.vue and columns.ts were reverted to the pre-P47 hand-rolled rowRange/colRange.
P22 D5, on F8–F11, declined embedder-level configuration of Wails v3.0.0-beta.16 and declined the one reachable escape hatch (NativeWindow() into Wails' private ObjC class).

### v1.1 P1 Cutover closeout + dependency/script/folder audit
The P58f cutover deleted src/engine, enginehost and the vendored Node runtime; it was implemented under the P58 row of docs/v1/SPEC.md before the v1.1 chapter opened.
v1.1 P1 audits every dependency (package.json, shell/go.mod) and every script (scripts/*, the scripts block in package.json) against what the post-cutover tree uses, and removes anything stale.
tsgo no longer does anything meaningful now that the toolchain has moved on, so v1.1 P1 removes it.
The repository is now a single Go backend plus a single Vue frontend, replacing the old two-backend/two-framework (Electron+Node engine) shape. v1.1 P1 removes or folds in stale folders and structural constructs left from that shape.
v1.1 P1 sweeps for dead weight beyond what P58f touched, because starting the new chapter on a clean baseline is cheaper than carrying stale tooling or an outdated folder layout into every later phase.
v1.1 phases are numbered from P1 again rather than continuing v1's numbering.

### v1.1 P2 Code review, three rounds
The v1.1 P2 code review follows the CLAUDE.md code-review process: three parallel Opus subagents (architecture/structure/maintainability/security; functional correctness/business logic; performance/resource efficiency) report findings only, and Sonnet fixes every finding.
The v1.1 P2 review runs three full rounds against the tree as it stands after P1; each round starts from what the previous round's fixes actually changed.
P2 is a hardening pass over the Wails/Go rewrite and the P1 cleanup, done before building further on top of it.
P2 uses three review rounds instead of v1's five, because P1 already narrows the tree the review runs against.

### v1.1 P3 Folder structure, scripts, and dependency-tooling cleanup
The v1.1 P3 phase restructures the repo folder layout to match a standard Wails app, leaving room to grow into a monorepo hosting more Wails apps plus parts shared between their backends and frontends.
P3 checks whether tests/db/, the shared DB fixture corpus that docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's Testing section calls not a suite of its own, is still in the right place under a monorepo-shaped tree.
bun run dev is broken because shell/build/Taskfile.yml's stock Wails scaffold defaults PACKAGE_MANAGER to npm and its dev:frontend and install:frontend:deps tasks run inside shell/frontend, which has no package.json.
The real frontend lives at the repo root rather than in shell/frontend.
Dependency installation is inconsistent between npm and bun across the Taskfiles and scripts; P3 standardizes it on bun.
P3 audits and fixes every dev/build/install script, and removes or folds in folders and files that no longer serve a purpose under the new layout.
The repo's folder shape drifted through several structural rewrites (Electron+Node engine to Wails+Go) without a dedicated pass; P3 is done right after P2 because restructuring before more feature work is cheaper than restructuring twice.

### v1.1 P4 FE↔BE data transfer protocol
The renderer and the Go backend exchange data through the hand-built adapterhost/bridge streaming frame protocol over Wails' in-process bridge; it was designed for one process pair on one machine.
The frontend and backend may later be split and communicate over a real network instead of Wails' in-process bridge, so the protocol should be efficient and network-portable.
The team decided to settle the efficient, network-portable protocol shape now, because discovering a needed redesign after more code depends on single-machine assumptions costs more.
A standardized transport/serialization protocol (e.g. gRPC, HTTP/2+protobuf) should be adopted over the hand-built framing if it is genuinely a better fit; keeping the hand-built approach is not the default.

### v1.1 P5 RAM usage
The v1.1 P5 task is a RAM usage audit: find where the app's own RAM usage can be reduced and fix what is found.
For the v1.1 P5 RAM audit, the frontend (Vue/WebView side) is the main place to look for improvement, not the Go backend.
docs/PERF.md records a real, measured memory story, but the app has never had a dedicated sweep for RAM used unnecessarily.

### v1.1 P6 Vue Vapor mode
v1.1 phase P6 evaluates Vue's Vapor mode (compiled, no-virtual-DOM rendering) against the app's own component tree and adopts it wherever it genuinely helps.
Vapor mode is evaluated after the backend rewrite became stable, because its tradeoffs differ from the rendering the app was built against and the team wants a real evaluation, not an unexamined assumption either way.

### v1.1 P7 CPU/memory status readout
The app's CPU/memory status-bar readout lives in internal/metrics; it is suspected to be inefficient and incorrect, with CPU reading far higher than expected.
The app is Mac-first; Activity Monitor's approach to process CPU/memory accounting is the reference model for the status-bar readout.
Users report they do not trust the status bar's CPU/memory numbers, on the one platform the app ships on.

### v1.1 P8 Multi-window correctness
The app must work correctly with two or more windows open at once; multi-window is a real usage pattern to support cleanly, not an edge case.

### v1.1 P9 Row coloring settings
A settings toggle lets users disable the data grid's row coloring; when off, every row renders white/plain.
The data grid no longer applies a distinct color to string-typed cell values; strings render in the plain text color like every other type.

### v1.1 P10 Ship the app as a DMG (Done)
The Taskfile tasks darwin:package:dmg and create:dmg in apps/kira-studio/build/darwin/Taskfile.yml parameterize the background image, volume icon, file icon, and window sizing.
P10 wires darwin:package:dmg/create:dmg into package.json's packaging script and the release workflow so they produce a real, signed .dmg, replacing the zipped .app.
The DMG was deferred from P4 as a deliberate scope decision, not a missing capability; the Taskfile pipeline existed and worked but was never wired into what ships.

### v1.1 P11 FlatBuffers data-plane wire format (Done)
The data plane's responses and events use a FlatBuffers binary frame instead of the JSON+base64 envelope; requests stay JSON text.
The FlatBuffers frame is file-identified with "KIF1"; its [uint] offset/truncated vectors are decoded as typed-array views over the received bytes (zero-copy).
P11 re-measured the P4 F10 fixtures on the new codec and recorded the result in docs/PERF.md.
P4 declined a binary envelope until a stated bar was cleared; P11 reversed this because FlatBuffers cleared it: 2.7 KB gzip bundle, near-zero wire overhead at real page sizes, and the whole frontend decode pass disappearing.
FlatBuffers superseded P4's own proposal of a hand-built binary envelope, which was not built.

### v1.1 P12 Code review, round three
P12 (v1.1) is the third CLAUDE.md-process code review round, originally scoped as part of P2, using the same three-subagent structure and run against the tree after P3-P10 land, with findings fixed the same way.
P12 runs as two iterations per CLAUDE.md's multiple-passes rule: each iteration gets its own plan-doc file, and the second runs against what the first iteration's fixes changed, not batched into one pass. The second catches what the first round's fixes introduce.
P2 was capped at two rounds so the third round (P12) could catch whatever P3-P10's work introduces, rather than reviewing code that further substantial work would immediately change.

### v1.1 P13 Query console format button
Phase v1.1 P13 adds a Format button to every query console: each SQL dialect's console (Postgres, MySQL/MariaDB, SQLite, ClickHouse) and any non-SQL query/command surface the adapters expose (Mongo, Redis, Kafka, etc.). It reformats the query in place with a real formatter.

### v1.1 P14 Confirm-before-reveal for saved credentials
Show a confirmation step before revealing a saved password or secret (e.g. in a connection's edit form); never display it immediately.
Prefer the operating system's own authentication prompt (macOS LocalAuthentication: Touch ID / system password) over a bespoke in-app modal to confirm revealing a saved secret, because the app is Mac-first.
Use an in-app confirmation to reveal a saved secret only where OS-level auth genuinely isn't a good fit.

### v1.1 P15 Fake data generator
Planned feature v1.1 P15 (fake data generator): integrate a Faker-style fake-data library and a UI to generate a user-specified number of realistic fake rows into the currently viewed table/collection, respecting column types/schema wherever the connection's adapter exposes them.
No fake data generation capability exists in the current tree.

### v1.1 P16 DB adapter min/max version compatibility suite
For each database/queue adapter, the oldest and newest server version its Go client library actually supports must be determined.
An on-demand compatibility suite runs each adapter's existing conformance tests against both the oldest and newest supported server image for its kind, instead of one pinned version; it is not part of the regular CI/test run.
The connection-add UI must show a note per connection kind stating its minimum supported server version.
Developing against one pinned test-fixture server version never catches version-boundary bugs, which is why the min/max version suite is built.

### v1.1 P17 Settings panel apply-on-save, with revert-to-default
The settings panel is to be reworked so changes are staged locally and only take effect when the user clicks Save, instead of applying live as each control changes. Live-apply was reported as confusing.
The settings panel is to get a "Revert to Defaults" button that resets every setting to its default value.

### v1.1 P18 SQL language server, EXPLAIN analysis, and auto-explain
The SQL language server provides conventional language-server features (completions, diagnostics, hovers) for the SQL dialects the app supports.
The SQL language server is driven purely by user-supplied DDL, with no live database connection and no schema introspection over a real connection.
An Explain button runs the database's own EXPLAIN (or dialect equivalent) on the current query, parses the output, and presents it readably, calling out issues such as missing indexes, full scans and expensive operations rather than a raw plan dump.
A configurable threshold defines an expensive query; it flags an Explain result and decides when auto-explain surfaces a warning.
Each connection's edit form has a toggle to enable auto-explain, which automatically runs Explain on every SELECT query issued on that connection.

### v1.1 P19 Dependency and runtime version bump
Phase P19 bumps every tracked dependency to the latest stable release: root package.json and workspace packages, shell/go.mod, the Go toolchain version, pinned Node/Bun runtime versions, and any other pinned toolchain version.
P19 includes fixing whatever the bump breaks: compile errors, failing tests, deprecated APIs, and config-format changes the new versions expect.
P19 is scheduled after P5-P10's feature and perf work is stable, because bumping earlier would mean chasing a moving target while that work is still landing.
P19 is scheduled before P12's review rounds and P21's docs sweep so both run against the final, up-to-date dependency set rather than one about to go stale.

### v1.1 P20 Scripts and dev/package workflow overhaul, second pass
Phase v1.1 P20 is a full audit and cleanup of every dev/build/install/package script in the repo: scripts/*, every Taskfile (apps/kira-studio/build/*/Taskfile.yml and others), and the scripts block of package.json.
P20 targets bun run dev not working, packaging not working, and something not regenerated correctly; the Wails bindings-generation step is the prime suspect after P19's Go/toolchain bump.
A Wails bindings regeneration with wrong flags can silently break the tree underneath it, as shown in P3's history.
P20 must collapse duplicated script logic into a single shared implementation; git config is invoked roughly three times across what should be one script.
P20 is a second pass of P3's script discipline because P13-P19 kept touching scripts (including P16's new scripts/db-compat.sh and P19's toolchain bump) with no dedicated pass over the area.
P20 must land after P19, because P19's dependency/toolchain bump likely causes the breakage (stale bindings-generation step, toolchain-version mismatch) and fixing scripts earlier would target a moving target.
P20 must land before P21's closing docs sweep so P21 can document a dev/package workflow that works.

### v1.1 P21 Docs refresh
Phase P21 is the v1.1 chapter's closing docs-refresh phase, modeled on v1's docs/v1/plans/P14-docs.md, because documentation drift compounds with every phase that lands without a dedicated pass.
P21 sweeps docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/PERF.md, docs/PACKAGING.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md's "Known open items", and any other doc that docs/v1/README.md's discipline points at, for drift against the tree after P1-P20.
P21 fixes outdated architecture descriptions, superseded perf numbers, packaging steps that P10 changed, and version and dependency mentions that P19 superseded.

### v1.1 P22 WebView scroll performance
The data grid's fast-scroll rendering lag (rows take a moment to catch up) has had no prior investigation in this repo and needs root-cause work, likely in the virtualizer's overscan/windowing, paint scheduling, or how fast the rendered row window catches up at high scroll velocity.
docs/v1.1/WEBVIEW-SCROLL-MEMORY.md investigated WKWebView memory growth during sustained scroll on real macOS hardware and concluded there is no frontend-code fix; the growth is WebKit's own CoreAnimation tile-pooling behavior.
The WKWebView memory investigation ruled out the virtualizer, CSS containment, sticky positioning, painted content width, and scroller architecture as causes.
Do not re-test contain: layout, sticky-to-absolute conversions, @tanstack/vue-virtual swaps, top/left vs transform, or painted-content-width for WKWebView scroll memory, because they were already measured and ruled out on real hardware.
Measure WKWebView memory with proc_pid_rusage, not vmmap.
The two untried avenues for the WKWebView memory problem are embedder-level WKWebViewConfiguration/WKPreferences tuning (needs Go/Wails-shell-side work; unknown whether Wails v3 exposes a usable surface) and reducing the default window size.
The default window size is hardcoded to 1280×800 in apps/kira-studio/internal/shell/window.go, and the memory doc measured window size as directly scaling the memory cost.
<!-- /chapter v1.1 -->

<!-- chapter v1.2 -->
## v1.2
Source: docs/v1.2/ at bd0c224688d9. 30 phases.
The v1.2 folder uses a fresh phase numbering (P1, P2, …) rather than continuing v1.1's, because v1.1's numbering was specific to that spec's scope.
The Adapter interface gained SchemaColumns, with model.RelationColumns/ValidateRelationColumns and the Caps.SchemaColumns flag; the flag is true for the five SQL kinds and false for mongo/redis/kafka/sqs/s3, which return adapters.Unsupported.
Each SQL adapter's listSchemaColumns is a widened form of its existing per-object column query (schema-wide WHERE, no relname/TABLE_NAME filter), never a loop over relations.
Postgres and mysqlfamily fold IsPrimaryKey into the schema-columns query; sqlite reuses relevantTables plus one pragma_table_xinfo join; clickhouse uses one schema-wide system.columns query with IsPrimaryKey always false per its D18/D23 rule.
tree.Service.SchemaColumns uses a fourth metadata_cache kind (columns) that shares the container's existing (connection_id, path) row instead of one row per relation, to respect F8's 200-row budget; it is cache-aside with requireConnected after the cache check, like Children/Describe/Definition.
The frontend state/schemaColumns.ts ensureSchemaColumns is idempotent, single-flight and fire-and-forget; it is wired into ConsoleView.vue on mount and container-path change, and invalidated by the onConnectionMetadataInvalidated broadcast.
SQL completion source precedence is document DDL (wins wholesale), then cached schema columns, then tree relations, then undefined; there is never a per-table merge.
effectiveSchema (state/schemaColumns.ts) is the DdlSchema-shaped projection that lint and hover read via ConsoleView.vue's ddlSchema computed, so completion, diagnostics and hover cannot disagree about what the console knows.
P19 D15's "Fill from connection" button and D16's no-schema hint strip were deleted; the Schema (DDL) dialog, saveDdl/ensureDdl/parseDdl and the connection-row menu entry remain, now as a deliberate override rather than the only schema supply.
mongoFilterCandidates/mongoSortCandidates live in views/shared/mongoFieldSample.ts because views/console/** may not import views/documents/**.
The Mongo console offers a fourth completion position: a bare identifier inside a db.<collection>.<method>(…) call offers that collection's sampled field names, merged with the BSON-constructor list; DocumentView.vue pushes the sample on page load and the console only reads it, never fetches.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's Caching section records the fourth metadata_cache kind and hands two open items to P24.
P22c deliberately has no staleness/TTL policy for the columns cache kind (P24's question), no cross-schema completion (OQ-3), and no indexes/foreign keys in the SchemaColumns payload (OQ-2).
Per-adapter SchemaColumns conformance tests assert DataType/IsPrimaryKey byte-identical to Describe's answer for the same column, run against real containers for postgres/mariadb/mysql/sqlite/clickhouse.

### v1.2 P1 Shared UI shell: title bar, left panel, and tab/content area, modularized
v1.2 P1 builds a custom title bar, using the app background color rather than OS chrome, that hosts the two top-level mode tabs, Studio and Http.
v1.2 P1 generalizes Studio's left-hand tree panel and bottom tabbed content area into components parameterized by what they show, so a collections tree left panel and HTTP request/response tabs can use the same panel and tab-strip machinery.
The shared-shell refactor must not change Studio's own behavior.
v1.2 P1 adds no new user-facing HTTP functionality; it is purely structural work every later phase depends on.
The shared shell is built first, before HTTP-specific features, because most of Http mode's chrome already exists in Studio; building it twice would mean two panel implementations and two places for the same bug. Later phases build on the real shared shell, not a placeholder.

### v1.2 P2 HTTP request/response core
v1.2 P2 delivers the minimum viable Http tab: a request builder (method, URL, query params, headers, body) that sends real HTTP requests.
The Http tab response viewer shows status, headers and body, with JSON syntax highlighting, a beautify action, and status-code hint text alongside the status.
v1.2 P2 is the smallest end-to-end slice of the Http feature (request in, response out, readable); later phases add capability on top of this working core rather than building toward one.

### v1.2 P3 Request body payload formats
The request body editor has modes: raw (plain text only), code (JavaScript/JSON/HTML/XML, syntax-highlighted, JSON/XML beautifiable), x-www-form-urlencoded, form-data (including real file fields), and binary (one local file as the whole body).
GraphQL body support shipped initially for Postman parity but was later removed outright, not deprecated.
Raw's five-language sub-selector was split into plain-text raw and a new top-level code mode, so body modes no longer map 1:1 onto Postman's body.mode.
P4 must build a real translation between the app's body modes and Postman's body.mode at the collection import/export boundary.
Payload formats (P3) were sequenced right after the core rather than folded into P2, because collections (P4) and curl generation (P5) must serialize/parse the full payload-format model, and getting it right first avoids revisiting their serialization.

### v1.2 P4 Collections: SQLite storage, real Postman-format import/export, left-panel tree
Collections are stored in the app's existing SQLite database (kira.sqlite, which also holds connections, tabs and settings), not as loose Postman-format files on disk. The internal representation (normalized folder/request schema, serialized JSON column, or a mix) is left to the phase's plan.
SQLite was chosen for collections because it matches how all other app state persists, adds no new storage paradigm and avoids a premature filesystem-sync problem; Postman fidelity is a property of conversion at the import/export boundary, not of the storage engine.
P4 has no git-based or edit-the-file-directly workflow: no filesystem watching and no external-edit detection; this is explicitly deferred.
Import must read a real Postman Collection v2.1 JSON file and reproduce its structure faithfully in SQLite.
Export must write a real, valid Postman Collection v2.1 JSON file from the stored data, so collections round-trip through the app.
Collections are shown in the left panel as a folder/request tree, built on P1's modularized panel.
Collections are the organizing structure for history, curl and the timeline, which all operate on a saved request, so a request needs a place to live first.
The body mode vocabulary is this app's own rather than Postman's body.mode verbatim.

### v1.2 P5 Collection variables and environments
Collection variables are scoped to a collection; environments are separate named, switchable sets of variables layered on top of collection variables.
Variables and environments resolve via {{name}} substitution wherever a request references one, including URL, headers and body.
Each variable or environment entry can be marked a secret with a checkbox; a secret's value is masked inline in the list and shown only after a system authentication prompt.
Secret reveal reuses the existing v1.1 P14 fingerprint/Touch ID reveal mechanism instead of rebuilding it.
Both variables and environments keep a history of prior values, not just the current one, and both support reordering.
P5 is sequenced right after collections (P4) because collection variables are scoped to a collection and need one to exist.
P5 is sequenced before curl generation (now P7) because a generated curl command must substitute real values for any {{variable}} reference to be runnable.
P5 is sequenced before response history, the raw inspector and the timeline, because none of those are meaningful until requests can use variables.

### v1.2 P6 Faker-backed dynamic values
v1.2 P6 adds Faker-backed dynamic values in requests: a {{$...}} variable form (random name, email, UUID, timestamp, and so on), in the style of Postman/Bruno.
Dynamic {{$...}} values are resolved via @faker-js/faker fresh on every send, not stored.
@faker-js/faker is already a dependency, adopted in v1.1's P15 fake-data-generator phase.
P6 follows P5 because it extends P5's {{...}} substitution engine, resolving a generator function in place of a stored variable, so no second substitution mechanism is needed.

### v1.2 P7 curl parse and generate
v1.2 P7 lets a user paste a curl command to populate a request (method, URL, headers, body, auth).
v1.2 P7 generates an equivalent curl command from the current request's state.
In generated curl commands, any {{variable}} or {{$dynamic}} reference is resolved to its real value, because curl has no notion of either.
P7 (curl parse and generate) depends on P3's full payload-format model, because curl's -d/--data/-F flags map onto exactly those body modes.
P7 depends on the P5/P6 substitution engine existing, so a generated curl command is runnable as-is.
P7 benefits from P4 existing, because a parsed curl command is naturally saved into a collection.

### v1.2 P8 Response history
Response history (v1.2 P8) persists past responses per request, not just the most recent; they are browsable and comparable against each other.
A response history entry is a saved snapshot of exactly what P2's response viewer renders.
Response history is sequenced after the core response viewer and after collections exist, because history is scoped per saved request.

### v1.2 P9 Raw request/response inspector and raw request editor
v1.2 P9 is a raw request/response inspector and raw request editor: it shows the exact bytes sent and received, not the structured/parsed model P2's builder presents, and lets users author and edit a request at the raw level.
v1.2 P9 is an advanced-debugging capability on top of the structured request/response model established by P2-P3.
v1.2 P9 needs a real request/response cycle to inspect, so it is sequenced after the core rather than before it.

### v1.2 P10 Request timeline
The request timeline shows the full chronological sequence of everything that happened while a request ran, not only the final response's own timing.
In the request timeline, each redirect hop is its own step with its own detail (method, URL, status, response headers), not folded into a single number.
Within each hop, the timeline shows the phase breakdown (DNS resolution, connection, TLS handshake, wait/TTFB, content download) rather than one elapsed-time figure. A reused connection is shown as reused, not as an instant/zero one.
Phase data comes from net/http/httptrace.ClientTrace callbacks hooked on the request's context, the standard low-risk mechanism, rather than a parser or protocol implementation of our own.
Go's built-in redirect-following client reuses the original request's context across every hop, so one httptrace installed up front fires once per hop.
internal/httpclient/client.go's existing checkRedirect/[]RedirectHop context-threading, added for P2/P3's redirect-count/final-URL reporting, is the bucketing technique P10 extends with per-hop timing at each checkRedirect call boundary.
P10 needs real instrumentation of the HTTP client's own request lifecycle, which only exists once P2's core request path is built.
P10 is sequenced after the raw inspector because both surface detail about the same underlying request execution.

### v1.2 P11 gRPC support
The v1.2 P11 phase adds gRPC support: browsing services and methods (via reflection or a supplied .proto file) and issuing unary and streaming gRPC calls, hosted through the same shell.
gRPC is the one non-HTTP protocol in scope for this chapter; WebSocket was dropped.
gRPC support is sequenced last among the feature phases so it lands on a shell and request/response/variables/history/timeline model already proven by every earlier phase.

### v1.2 P12 Studio/Http(→Api) modularization & separability audit
Phase P12 (v1.2) is a structural pass on the Studio/Http boundary, not a feature phase: no new user-facing behavior beyond the Http → Api rename.
P12 extracts the Api-facing frontend code into its own genuine Bun workspace package(s) (e.g. packages/api-core, packages/api-ui), not a tidier directory inside apps/kira-studio; exact naming and split are left to P12's plan.
The root workspaces config gains packages/* in P12.
The package split (rather than a directory-only move) follows v1.3's Git module precedent, so the two modules end up structurally consistent.
P12 splits any test file that grew to cover both modules' cases into one file per module, folder-separated, even where the runner command still runs both suites together.
P12 audits internal/bridge and the remaining Go side for coupling that would block splitting Http into a standalone app later.
P12 renames the module Http → Api in user-facing text (the mode tab and other text naming the mode) and in code wherever an identifier, file or directory names the module rather than the HTTP protocol.
internal/httpclient and other genuinely protocol-shaped names are not renamed in P12.
The rename to Api is done because gRPC (P11) makes the mode more than an HTTP client, and it goes in the same pass because it touches the same files the separability audit is already moving.
P12 is the phase every other phase should minimize work for; doing the separation once, deliberately, after the module is feature-complete is cheaper than enforcing perfect separation through ten prior phases or leaving the coupling indefinitely.

### v1.2 P13 Api module UI check and improvement
P13 is a UI check and improvement pass over every Api-mode surface (request builder, response viewer, collections tree, dialogs, empty/error states), checked against the app's design system rather than each earlier phase's ad hoc styling.
The design system reference for P13 is docs/design/kira-design-system, primitives.css, and the shared token scale.
P13 makes Api mode consistent with Studio mode's spacing, type, color and icon conventions, and tightens it to be dense enough for a power tool, not sparse for its own sake.
P13 cleans up leftover placeholder or rough-edge styling from earlier phases.
P13 adds no new functionality; it is a visual/interaction-consistency pass, not a feature phase.
P13 is sequenced after modularization and the rename (P12) so it polishes the module under its final name and directory shape rather than styling files P12 would move.
P13 is sequenced before the code review (P14) so the review reads the UI in its finished, polished state rather than styling about to change again.

### v1.2 P14 Api module code review (2 rounds)
v1.2 P14 is two rounds of dedicated code review scoped to the Api module only (renamed from Http in P12): its separated directories plus its facing halves of internal/httpclient, internal/postman, and internal/bridge.
The P14 Api module review applies the same discipline as v1.1's P12 chapter-wide review (docs/v1.1/SPEC.md), focused on this chapter's own module.
P14 is sequenced last, after modularization, the rename, and the UI pass, so the review sees the module under its final name and final separated, polished shape, not code P12/P13 would move, rename, or restyle.

### v1.2 P15 (new) Request builder UX pass — part 1: layout, primitives and body mode
P15 part 1 is the presentation half of the request builder UX pass, covering eight of twelve user-reported items; the editor-behaviour half is P15b.
The response panel (chrome and pane switcher included) is present from the moment a request tab opens, not only after a first send.
A status code's meaning is shown on its own always-visible line, not a truncatable caption plus a hover.
The Studio/Api mode-switch buttons are sized up to the app's own tab metrics and now render the icon that modes.ts has declared unused since P1.
The .p-input class is inline-flex, so a TextField is about 20 characters wide wherever placed unless its call site says otherwise; this is the root cause of both the narrow table fields and the URL field never growing.
A new shared Checkbox primitive matching the app's design system replaces all fourteen raw input type=checkbox call sites app-wide, Studio included (nine are in Studio). It is shared app-wide because primitives already live in the shared theme layer; AppButton, TextField and SegmentedControl are precedent.
JSON is promoted to its own top-level body mode as a UI-level promotion over unchanged code+codeLanguage storage, not a new wire/Go/Postman vocabulary and not a data migration.
A request's tab shows a body-present indicator via a new optional badge() member on the tab-kind registry, which answers what P4 D15 declined.
The toolbar is reflowed so Save sits with the request's name in the view head and the URL field takes the row.
P15 is sequenced after P12/P13 so it refines a surface already settled into its final module shape and design-system pass, not files about to be renamed or restyled.
P15 is split from P15b because the halves fail differently, P15b opens with a packages/api-core foundation commit unrelated to these eight fixes, and this half touches Studio (nine checkbox call sites and the title bar), so one review pass carries that risk.
The plan for P15 is in plans/P15-request-builder-ux.md.

### v1.2 P15b Request builder UX pass — part 2: input and editor behaviour
v1.2 P15b request builder UX pass part 2 changes land in three files: AutocompleteField.vue, CodeMirrorHost.vue and FieldRowsTable.vue.
P15b covers four items: header-name autocomplete over a curated well-known request-header vocabulary; {{variable}} colouring (resolved / secret-deferred / unresolved) with hover and variable-name autocomplete in URL, header values and body editor; auto-closing bracket/quote pairs; arrow-key navigation across row-based request tables.
The {{variable}} hover shows the resolved value but never a secret's value.
A secret's plaintext deliberately never reaches the renderer.
The {{variable}} work extends AutocompleteField.vue's overlay approach (a real native input with a read-only CodeMirror painted behind it) rather than replacing the fields with editors, because page.fill() across five spec files depends on them staying real inputs.
CodeMirrorHost gains one rangeHighlights seam for colour that comes from data rather than from a grammar.
The variable hover uses a field-local panel on theme/floatingPosition.ts's existing pointReference, because the overlay is pointer-events: none and the app's tooltip singleton anchors per element, not per token.
Resolved variable values need no new pipeline: mergedValuesAndSecrets and resolve already run per keystroke for the unresolved-count chip.
Auto-close brackets/quotes is enabled only in the request body editor and the plain fields, because editor/wrapSelection.ts declines closeBrackets() due to the console's unterminated-string lint.
P15b is implemented: all seven commits (N1-N7) landed on claude/feature-v1-2, and the plan plans/P15b-request-builder-editor-behavior.md is fully checked off.

### v1.2 P16 (new) SQL grid polish, cross-cutting dropdown/input consistency, and Api search
P16 SQL grid work (pager/nav arrows repositioned, per-column minimum width, consistent overscroll) is Studio-only; dropdown closed-state height and input padding/contrast fixes are cross-cutting design-system changes for Studio and Api.
P16 adds a search affordance to the Api request/response viewer and to the module's other list-shaped surfaces (headers, variables, and similar).
The P16 pager moves into ViewChrome's existing #toolbar-end group, last after the pending-changes controls, rather than a new slot; the RunState reflow that would jitter it is fixed at the primitive.
The SQL grid per-column minimum width is header-aware, not a raised flat floor, because the header cell spends 42-58px on padding, the 14px sort control and an optional PK/FK badge that no width computation accounted for.
The SQL grid disables overscroll with overscroll-behavior: none on every .slick-viewport, because only 'disabled' is expressible in CSS; this deliberately re-opens the earlier reverted 'disable overscroll entirely' instruction, and a one-word revert path is recorded.
The P16 plan is in plans/P16-sql-grid-consistency-search.md; it was sequenced after P15 by the one-phase-at-a-time discipline, not because it depends on P15's output.
P16 was implemented in sixteen commits (Q1-Q16) plus a follow-up correcting a header-chrome constant that was 8px short, caught by the new D4 case in tests/ui.

### v1.2 P17 (new) Variable/environment management overhaul, Faker scoping, and value pipes
P17 scope: method select restyled and colour-coded per HTTP method; Description field on variable/environment entries; editing a variable set or environment opens as its own tab; environments can be cloned.
P17 adds a bulk-edit mode for variables and environments via a .env-format text editor (KEY=VALUE lines), applied as a diff against the existing set through a parse/serialize/reconcile design.
P17 adds a single panel, reachable from any request tab, showing the active environment's and the collection's variables together instead of two separate dialogs.
substitute.ts's doc comment states "no expression language" as a deliberate design choice (D1/D17); P5 secret masking, P6 per-occurrence dynamic-value freshness and P9/P10/P14 masking fixes rely on substitution staying a simple one-pass find-{{-find-}} scan. Pipes deliberately reopen this decision.
Pipes reopen only the name read (text.slice(open+2, close).trim()), not the {{/}} scan. Parsing is all-or-nothing: every segment after the first must be a known transform, else the whole text is the name, so existing inputs are unaffected.
The pinned Go/TS substitution corpus passing unedited is the guard that the pipe grammar change is inert for existing inputs.
apivars.Resolver records the exact text it wrote per distinct (name, pipeline) pair, not name → plaintext, so secretReplacer masks base64/upper/URL-encoded forms, one entry per spelling; a request using both {{secret}} and {{secret | base64}} masks both.
The rendered form reaches the resolver through a callback (onResolved) rather than a field on Reference, because Reference is a struct whose purpose is to be reported.
Resolver.Used() returns UsedSecret{Name, Rendered, Placeholder} fed by an onResolved callback, and every rendered/piped form is masked via a length-sorted strings.Replacer.
A transformed secret (e.g. base64-encoded) is as sensitive as its plaintext, so masking must register the transformed form; omitting it reopens the encoded-secret gap found in P14 round 2.
A pipe applies after per-occurrence dynamic-value generation, leaving P6 D3 freshness and D7 short-circuit untouched.
Initial pipe transforms are base64 encode/decode, upper/lower case, and URL encode/decode, usable on variables, environments and dynamic values.
Faker re-scoping is additive with no migration: fake.<faker module>.<method> (vocabulary from Studio's fakeData/recipes.ts) sits beside Postman's $name spellings, which stay as the Postman import/export contract. The Faker catalogue also gains autocomplete.
Tests tests/ui/http-pipes.spec.ts cover: a piped secret never resolves client-side; the Raw pane, Timeline hop and persisted history entry show the masked reference; both spellings of one secret mask as one distinct secret.
P17 is implemented in commits R1-R14 on claude/feature-v1-2, with the plan in plans/P17-variable-environment-overhaul.md.

### v1.2 P18 (new) History correctness, retention, gRPC parity, mode-button alignment, and environment colour
The history store is api/state/history.ts (P12 D12's shared factory createHistoryStore), not in packages/api-core.
Stale history list root cause: createHistoryStore's lazy branch set stale but nothing read it, so a send with the History pane closed left the list frozen. Fix: ensureLoaded became ensureFresh with stale as a second precondition, and ensure() always returns the reactive proxy.
History retention was capped at 20 per scope since P8 D6/P11 D11 in both tables; P18 raised both caps to 30 (HISTORY_PER_SCOPE_LIMIT and GRPC_HISTORY_PER_SCOPE_LIMIT), oldest evicted first, pinned equal to the Go constants by a parity test.
A truncated history list shows the note "only the last 30 are kept" at the bottom.
gRPC history stored its request message with no per-entry cap, which broke the invariant its global byte sweep depends on (one oversized message emptied grpc_call_history). P18 gave the request message a 256 KiB cap, proven by a Go test.
Response bodies in history are persisted up to maxHistoryBodyBytes in internal/storage/repos/response_history.go.
HTTP history stored the request and its truncation flag but nothing rendered either; RawExchangePane.vue now reads them, reconstructing both documents from the four stage-1 fields of a snapshot via the generateRawRequestFromStored adapter, with no new storage.
gRPC request view parity (GrpcRequestView.vue) added {{variable}} colouring/hover/completion (variableCompletion.ts moved to api/state/), a grpc-timeout-style metadata vocabulary, a response pane that is a panel from tab-open, grpcCodeHint() giving non-OK codes their own meaning line, and Save/target field in P15 D7/D4's shape.
The Studio/Api mode tab icon is boxed and its text labelled (workbench/TitleBar.vue), because an unboxed icon made the icon-to-label gap differ between the two tabs by ~1.6px, against the app's law that icons never float unboxed next to text. A geometric test covers it.
Environments have a user-assigned colour from Studio's own palette, none by default (LAW 07: one palette). ColorPicker was promoted to theme/primitives/ and domain/color.ts is the shared home.
Environment colour is shown via an app-drawn EnvironmentSelect, the toolbar rail and head dot of both request views, the environment's own tab, the manage dialog and the variables overview panel; history rows and the tab strip are deliberately excluded.

### v1.2 P19 (new) Connection dialog sizing, Mongo console fixes, query-result selection, and SQL tooling regression
ConnectionDialog.vue is one static 620×520 size for both steps and every tab/sub-tab, so the dialog does not resize when switching tabs.
A geometry test in connection-dialog-tabs.spec.ts records the dialog box across every step/tab/sub-tab/engine-family combination, asserts it never moves, and asserts no pane's scrollHeight exceeds its clientHeight.
The Mongo filter/order pair is in the collection view (views/documents/DocumentView.vue), not the query console, which has no filter or order input at all.
The Mongo collection filter box looked inert because it was the only filter surface with no prefix label, and its typed value is painted through the syntax overlay in chrome-like greys and pale blues; DocumentView.vue now gives it a FILTER label.
FilterToolbar.vue had three wiring gaps, fixed on both Mongo boxes: no no-op guard on blur, no @escape revert, no resync watcher. An idle blur now issues zero new data:read/data:count calls and the exact count survives.
views/shared/clipboardFormats.ts and views/shared/slick/selection.ts live in views/shared/ because biome.json makes views/console/** importing views/grid/** a lint error.
The console's tabular result uses a SlickHybridSelectionModel supporting row, column and range selection, with ⌘C and a context menu (views/console/resultMenu.ts) mirroring views/grid/menu.ts's vocabulary.
Document/key-value console rows have a menu with "Copy as JSON" and "Copy all as JSON"; the Mongo collection view's row menu has the same item.
The SQL formatter itself is not broken (sql-formatter 15.8.2 formats a twenty-case battery correctly); it read as broken because of three P13 issues: all-or-nothing failure on one unparseable fragment, caret jumping to offset 0 (OQ-2), and keywordCase 'preserve' making an already-formatted document a silent no-op.
formatConsoleText is per-statement, returning {text, ok, failures}, so an unparseable fragment (e.g. \dt after a good SELECT) does not block its neighbours; a warn strip names the failed statement and a note strip says "Already formatted." for a byte-identical no-op.
The caret survives a reformat by statement index via CodeMirrorHost's opt-in keepSelectionOnExternalSync/setCursor props, default off so other hosts are unchanged; this closes v1.1 P13 OQ-2.
SQL completion source precedence is: user DDL document schema (wins wholesale), then cached schema columns, then table names from the project tree's cache via consoleRelationNames (same technique as mongoCollectionNames), then undefined; sources are never merged per table.
The 'Fill from connection' button/progress state and the no-schema hint strip were removed; the Schema (DDL) dialog, saveDdl/ensureDdl/parseDdl and the connection-row menu entry remain, as a deliberate override rather than the only schema supply.
A new --kira-state-on token (#4ec9b0, ~168° hue) replaces --kira-accent (#0078d4) for the "filter/sort/projection applied" indicator, because the accent is within 8° of the #569cd6/#9cdcfe query-text highlight blues and that hue region is used by no syntax token or other state colour.
--kira-state-on is applied to .ph-active, IconButton's .has-indicator dot and StreamView's partition button; the SlickGrid sort chevron deliberately stays on neutral --kira-fg/--kira-fg-muted.
The Adapter interface has a SchemaColumns method and Caps.SchemaColumns flag, true for the five SQL kinds (postgres, mysql, mariadb, sqlite, clickhouse) and false for mongo/redis/kafka/sqs/s3, which return adapters.Unsupported for it.
Each SQL adapter's listSchemaColumns is a widened form of its existing per-object column query (schema-wide WHERE, no relname/TABLE_NAME filter), never a loop over relations.
Postgres and mysqlfamily SchemaColumns fold IsPrimaryKey into the same query; clickhouse SchemaColumns always reports IsPrimaryKey false per its D18/D23 rule.
tree.Service.SchemaColumns uses a fourth metadata_cache kind (columns) that shares the container's existing (connection_id, path) row instead of one row per relation, to stay within F8's 200-row budget.
SchemaColumns follows the same cache-aside shape as Children/Describe/Definition, with requireConnected checked after the cache check, so cached results are served while disconnected.
The frontend schema-columns state (state/schemaColumns.ts, ensureSchemaColumns) is idempotent, single-flight and fire-and-forget; it is invalidated by the onConnectionMetadataInvalidated broadcast, and ConsoleView.vue triggers it on mount and on container-path change.
namespaceFromCached (ddl.ts) projects cached RelationColumns into lang-sql's namespace shape byte-for-byte like toSqlNamespace.
effectiveSchema (state/schemaColumns.ts) is the DdlSchema-shaped projection that lint and hover read via ConsoleView.vue's ddlSchema computed, so completion, diagnostics and hover agree on what the console knows.
mongoFilterCandidates/mongoSortCandidates live in views/shared/mongoFieldSample.ts because views/console/** may not import views/documents/**.
The Mongo console offers a completion position for a bare identifier inside a db.<collection>.<method>(…) call, giving that collection's sampled field names merged with the BSON-constructor list; DocumentView.vue pushes the sample on page load and the console only reads it, never fetches.
The new schema-columns cache kind has no staleness/TTL policy (P24's question), there is no cross-schema completion (OQ-3), and SchemaColumns' payload has no indexes or foreign keys (OQ-2).

### v1.2 P20 (new) README feature overview
README.md presents what the app does today as a short feature-by-feature summary in brief mentions, not implementation detail; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md stays the source of truth for how it is built.
The README intro and Status section name both workbenches: Studio (shipped through v1.1) and Api (in development as the v1.2 chapter).
The README has a Studio features section (the prior feature list, retitled unchanged) and an Api features section covering the request builder, collections, environments/variables, curl import/export, response history, request timeline and gRPC.
The Api request builder supports body modes raw, syntax-highlighted code, form-urlencoded, multipart form-data and binary file.
Api environments/variables support colour and Faker.
Api response history is capped at 30 entries.
Api has no dedicated Basic/Bearer/API-key/OAuth2 auth UI; auth is set via the Headers panel, matching curl parity.
Check README feature claims against the frontend source; do not assume them from the SPEC's phase titles, because a claimed auth UI turned out not to exist.
The README Documentation and top-level-layout sections link docs/v1.2/ as the live chapter and docs/v1.1/ as the reference for the completed chapter.

### v1.2 P21 (new) Code review, three rounds — whole application
P21 is a three-round code review of the entire application (Studio and Api, every phase up to P20), a from-scratch review of the current tree, not a diff. Earlier reviews were module-scoped (P14, Api only); P21 is sequenced last so it reads the final shipped state.
Each review round uses three parallel Opus analysis agents (architecture/structure/maintainability/security, functional correctness/business logic, performance/resource efficiency) that report findings only, then one sequential Sonnet agent fixes everything found.
MySQL/MariaDB URI query parameters no longer splice into a connect-time SET statement.
sslmode=require verifies the server certificate on Redis/MongoDB (matching Kafka), with an explicit verify-none escape hatch, and rediss:// enables TLS.
An imported Postman collection's file reference is never carried into a live, sendable path, and curl import (-F name=@path, a lone --data-binary @path, -T path) likewise no longer resolves a live local file path, to prevent file exfiltration.
Response headers are masked for secrets the same as the request half; a successful gRPC call's StatusMessage/headers/trailers are masked before the renderer or kira.db sees them.
All transient revealed-secret maps (variables, history popover, Copy-as-curl) re-mask on the same 5-minute auth grace expiry, not only when their dialog closes.
internal/ipcerr lives outside internal/bridge, and a go list -deps test guards the layering; the layering test enumerates every internal/* package rather than a hand-maintained list.
The database file is named kira.db (not kira.sqlite) since the Go port; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, README and PACKAGING were corrected.
A failed collection import rolls back the collection it partially created, and postman.Parse's whole-file read is bounded.
safeRun's recover() preserves a panicked *adapters.Error's own code instead of always folding to E_INTERNAL.
StreamView.vue's compose dialog and Delete button read Caps instead of connection.kind; DocumentView and StreamView also gate writes on the connection's ReadOnly flag as well as adapter Caps.
connections.Update reconnects a live connection when its destination or ReadOnly flag changes, so the adapter, cache and server-side enforcement do not stay pointed at the old destination.
kira:v2 ciphertext does not bind additional authenticated data to its row; this is an accepted design constraint because binding it would break SecretStore.copy()'s duplication path.
sslmode vocabulary differs across the six adapters ("six adapters, six vocabularies"); this duplication and libpq's allow semantics remain an open separate refactor, while Kafka verify-none/insecure, ClickHouse prefer and Postgres verify-ca are now accepted.
MySQL/MariaDB TLS ServerName uses net.SplitHostPort (fixing IPv6 hosts), and its process-global TLS config registry no longer leaks one entry per connection id.
Concurrent Test connection presses across multi-window no longer share one Preconnect key, so they cannot kill each other's pre-connect script; a pre-connect script's fallback PATH no longer leads with an empty (.) element.
internal/storage/model reuses internal/httpclient types; this coupling is a known open refactor.
Generated SQL literals are dialect-aware (backslash escaping), and dollar-quoting in the SQL splitter/linter is Postgres-only.
The {{name | transform}} pipe vocabulary (base64/urldecode/case-mapping) is byte-identical between Go and TypeScript.
The column projection cannot be reduced to zero columns, and hiding one column of a composite primary key fails loudly naming the hidden column rather than reaching an opaque server-side refusal; a blanket primary-key lock on Hide column was wrong because it broke tested composite-PK behaviour.
Duplicate row(s) no longer stages a truncated cell as real data, and Commit fails loudly instead of silently no-opping on an unaddressable row.
A server-streaming gRPC call's terminal event can no longer be dropped by a network-vs-event-channel race, and a streaming call's history row is recorded before its terminal event fires.
Both history tables cap the whole snapshot rather than field by field, and both history repos gate the byte-budget sweep on a cheap aggregate; the response-history stale flag is sequenced against a concurrent send.
applyLoadFailure sets a real status on disconnect for every view, not just console.
SQS never polls automatically; all reload paths (mutation self-reload, sibling-tab fan-out, project-tree double-click reuse) go through one guarded reload().
Deleting a folder or collection purges every cascade-deleted descendant's cached request, avoiding a live-looking Save button on an orphaned tab.
Copy-as-curl emits a form-data text row's own Content-Type.
A stream/Kafka view's "N total" is scoped to the active partition/offset/timestamp filter rather than the whole topic.
A failed tabsSave no longer permanently blocks a later, identical-looking save.
MariaDB's fake-data generator respects signed integer display widths and mysql/ClickHouse temporal formats.
The row context menu builds clipboard snapshots lazily, and resolveColumnOrder is memoized and no longer O(cols²); the console's document parse cache is pruned to the rendered window.
An oversized page is rejected by its own measured size before being encoded.
Never spread into Array.push in the project tree row builder, because it hard-fails past ~65k rows.
Known deferred items: duplicated protocol-neutral field-walk in internal/apivars and bridge/grpc.go; nested-block-comment splitting edge case; DocumentView's per-toggle full-page height recompute and ConsoleResultGrid's full materialization of every document row (only cache pruning was fixed).
budgets.spec.ts's scroll p50 threshold is a documented pre-existing flake in the test:ui suite, passing in isolation.
The gRPC live-message buffer is trimmed to exactly its cap, keeping the most recent 10,000 messages; trimming to 90% of the cap was reverted because it broke the D15/D17 contract.
The gRPC live-stream buffer uses markRaw so incoming messages are not deep-reactivated.
MySQL/MariaDB connections serialize concurrent operations against the same pinned connection, matching Postgres's existing fix.
The Postgres, MySQL/MariaDB and Redis connection sets use dial single-flight and a lock-free LRU-eviction split, so eviction does not block operations and concurrent dials of the same cold database do not race or leak connections.
variables.Upsert purges a variable's plaintext-value history when the variable becomes a secret, so earlier plaintext revisions are not readable through the history endpoint.
oplog's Stop() blocks until its own consumer goroutine has exited, closing a shutdown race.
connections.Update and Insert route a changed secret through the secret store transactionally, as a fresh connection does, so a rotated password is not left under the connection's old field.
httpclient redirect handling strips caller-supplied headers, including auth tokens, before following a cross-host redirect.
Collection export writes its file atomically, so a failed write cannot leave a half-written file in place of the original.
Postman export reduces a local absolute file path to its base name instead of writing it into the exported collection.
SQLite DSN parsing rejects URI metacharacters that could smuggle extra file: query parameters past the app's validation.
Grid and console clipboard TSV export neutralizes a leading formula-trigger character (=, +, -, @, tab, CR), as CSV export already did (CWE-1236).
The Tree, Connections and Collections services have inconsistent context.Context threading; this was left unfixed because it is a large, invasive refactor across every call site with no single active bug behind it.
The fake-data generator clamps every generator's output to its declared column bounds, so finance.amount, binary.hex and others cannot emit out-of-bounds values.
The curl importer/generator round-trips --data-urlencode verbatim and applies the existing ;-in-value guard to -F.
The URL query codec treats a bare + and an escaped %2B symmetrically on round trip.
ClickHouse computeOrderBySql falls back to a deterministic all-columns ORDER BY when no better ordering is available.
enginecache's stale-flip path uses ByteLru.Update() rather than a Set-based re-insert, which lost the entry's place in LRU eviction order.
The paged find box budgets its per-frame scan by cell count rather than row count and debounces query input at 150 ms, the same as the tree/collections search boxes.
The HTTP response find bar's findRanges is memoized by (doc, query).
The browse view's loaded node list is markRaw'd rather than deep-reactive, matching the project tree, since it can hold up to 200,000 entries.
TabsRepo.Save upserts and prunes rows instead of deleting and reinserting every row for the window on each save.
Read pendingChanges per cell in SlickGrid's staged-edit extractor, not at build time, because the extractor is not rebuilt on every staging change; hoisting the read broke staged-edit rendering.
rangeHighlightPlugin's full-document rescan per keystroke was deliberately not changed: it is a CodeMirror ViewPlugin with no unit-test seam and a correctness risk around viewport-boundary widening.
BrowseView.vue's filter-input debounce was skipped because it has no Playwright coverage to verify a script-setup refactor.
The gRPC response pane's per-batch row-height remap was left unchanged because it lives in VirtualList.vue, shared across grid/documents/browse/console/gRPC, so changes carry a large blast radius.

### v1.2 P22 (new) Sixth user-driven batch — Api/Studio polish, global sizing tokens, search sweep, and schema-aware SQL tooling
SQL/Mongo table and column completion must be driven automatically by the schema metadata the app already fetches and caches from the live connection, with no manual step.
The language layer must never connect to the real database itself for completion; cached metadata is enough. The DDL/LSP design was a misunderstanding.
P19's "Fill from connection" button, which staged cached schema metadata into a "Schema (DDL)" document the user must open and click, was the wrong shape for schema-aware completion.
Control height is exposed as a small set of global CSS custom properties so raising one token raises inputs, dropdowns and other form controls together.
Search affordances in all Studio and Api panels that can support search must use the app's one shared search component, uniformly.
Every autocomplete dropdown in the app (SQL/Mongo completion, header names, variable names, faker functions) renders through one visually uniform popup component/style.
--kira-state-on (active-filter/order-by indicator, introduced by P19) is #e0a33c (gold/amber), replacing the teal.
The .p-method class has no tinted background anywhere and keeps only its text colour; the filter: brightness() hover workaround the fill needed was deleted. Studio has no method/kind chip with a background fill (its tree renders an EngineIcon), so the change lands entirely in Api.
Header value autocomplete offers full canned values for headers like Content-Type and a prefix-only vocabulary (Bearer, Basic, Digest, etc.) for Authorization.
Every configurable row in the request builder (headers, params, form-data fields, other row-shaped fields) gets a Description field, as P17 added to variables/environments.
The collections panel has an Environments category at the bottom, alongside rather than inside the folder/request tree, collapsed by default. Clicking an environment opens it in a new tab via the same openVariableSetTab call as EnvironmentsDialog's row click, and the dialog is untouched.
The Mongo document/console copy action is a three-format submenu (Shell / Canonical Extended JSON / Relaxed Extended JSON) for a single document and a whole result set, matching the SQL grid's Copy row(s) idiom. Shell mode is mongosh-style, e.g. ObjectId("...")/ISODate("..."); canonicalToRelaxed in views/shared/document/ejson.ts is new.
On app close, the app remembers which top-level module (Studio or Api) was active per window and reopens into that module.
Adding a new row to a row-insertable table should scroll the new row into view. FieldRowsTable, MetadataTable and VariableSetView scroll their newly appeared trailing blank row into view on growth, never on a shrink.
PanelSplitter has an opt-in divider prop that draws a box-shadow hairline at rest. It is applied to HttpRequestView, GrpcRequestView and CellEditorDock (which dropped its local border-top workaround), and not to WorkbenchShell's two splitters, whose grid gap already reads as a groove.
