The response status code is an always-visible inline line in both response panes; its tooltip was removed on purpose. HTTP's history list, timeline and diff dialog, and gRPC's call history, now carry v-tooltip="statusHint(...)" like the two response panes; the inline line is never tooltip-only.
P22 is split into three plans: P22 (shared tokens/primitives/shell, must land first), P22b (per-surface Api and Studio polish) and P22c (schema-aware completion).
P22 part 1 provides control-height roles, a disabled-primary contrast fix and a shared completion-popup block, which parts 2 and 3 consume.
P22 part 3 is the only part touching Go, the wire and the SQLite schema.
Repo convention: recorded batches of reported items are written as one durable batch before their plan exists, and phases are sequenced one at a time (P22 after P21).
When planning for re-checked regression items, root-cause each against the real current tree; never assume either the user's report or the prior phase's 'Implemented' note is right.
The 'no live DB connection from the language layer' constraint stands; P22c revisits P19's DDL-document completion design against cached metadata instead.
The control-height token scale --kira-h-xs/sm/md/lg already exists and all form primitives read it. The --kira-control-h/-lg/-sm, --kira-control-inline-h and --kira-viewhead-h role layer is a pure alias over it, so rendered heights stay unchanged (22/22/26/18px at the 12px default), except .p-view-head.
A chrome-bar token family (--kira-bar-h and friends) splits .p-toolbar (34px) from .p-view-head (28px), a likelier cause of the reported height inconsistency than any control. .p-view-head changed from 28 to 34px to match .p-toolbar, both now using --kira-bar-h.
The page-number input being taller than its neighbours is not reproducible from the stylesheet: every control in that row is --kira-h-sm under one box-sizing: border-box, so measure before any fix.
The pager sat about 76px from the grid's right edge, not ~8px as P16 claimed, because P16's D2 rendered RunState after #toolbar-end with a permanent 7ch reservation. In ViewChrome, RunState is now placed ahead of #toolbar-end, closing the gap in both the data grid and DocumentView.
The console lacked the sel-t/-r/-b/-l selection perimeter layer that P19's port deliberately dropped; fill already matched. The console's result grid now uses the same layer as the SQL data grid, and computeSelEdgeHashes lives in views/shared/slick/selectionEdges.ts because of the biome.json cross-import boundary.
A .p-btn:disabled cascade defect rendered every disabled primary button at a measured 1.13:1 contrast. A disabled primary button (.p-btn.primary and .p-iconbtn.is-primary) is now dimmed with a uniform 0.45 opacity.
The search-affordance sweep covers the five surfaces that have no search plus the one with a non-shared search, since Api request panel search and gRPC search are partly shipped.
There are two row-add idioms: a trailing blank row in four tables, and the SQL grid's staged-insert button.
SQL Format used out.join(';\n\n'), which silently deleted the document's final semicolon on every press, because splitSqlStatements excludes the trailing ; from each statement. formatConsoleText was fixed.
The tree's cached metadata does not contain columns (P19 D5 made a relation a leaf); the SQLite-backed metadata_cache holds ObjectMeta.Columns and is readable with no live connection.
P22c adds a one-round-trip-per-container SchemaColumns adapter capability, cached as a fourth kind on the container's existing cache row (zero net rows against the 200-row cap), and deletes P19's 'Fill from connection' button and no-schema hint strip.
The mode-tab guard in mode-switch.spec.ts measures real ink by screenshotting each tab's icon/label and diffing against its sampled background, not by box-level assertion.
The mode-tab icon renders at native 16px (was 13px in a 16px box) and shares one line-height with the label. A residual per-glyph side-bearing difference (inset 4px→3px) remains and is documented, not chased.
The --kira-icon-optical-y escape-hatch token is wired but unused, left at default 0px, so a later optical fix has one place to land.
One .p-completion* block lives in primitives.css, and its metrics (including per-kind icon glyphs) are mirrored onto CodeMirror's tooltip DOM in editor/theme.ts, so plain-field and console completion popups share one visual spec.
The status-bar CPU/memory tooltip shows only its numbers. The one load-bearing explanatory sentence moved into docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's CPU-convention paragraph.
Each window has a persisted mode column (migration 0014). NormalizeMode maps an unknown value to 'studio', and repos/windows.go GetMode/SetMode mirror SetBounds.
The frontend derives boot mode from windowsEnsure's answer, replacing the old heuristic that used whichever restored tab was marked active.
Per-window bounds persistence is entirely Go-side and native-event-driven, with no frontend IPC counterpart. setMode therefore debounces its own windowsSetMode write like patchLayout in state/layout.ts, and never writes synchronously on the click.
Mode switching must write nothing synchronously (F20 invariant); the "mode switching writes nothing" test case proves it.
The --kira-control-h value was deliberately not raised (OQ-1): the mechanism exists, but picking a new number is a human judgement at the app.
The codicon glyph set is not replaced with inline SVG (OQ-3).
--kira-bar-h's meaning was introduced by a concurrent commit and is not redefined by P22.
packages/api-core has headerValueCompletions: a whole-value vocabulary for Content-Type/Accept etc. and a prefix vocabulary (Bearer , Basic ) for Authorization. It composes with the {{variable}} source and is wired into the value cell as a function of the row's name.
Save sits in the #head-trailing slot of HttpRequestView and GrpcRequestView, so its position does not shift with the dirty mark or an unresolved-variable chip.
A resolved {{variable}} uses its own --kira-var-resolved token, fixing a collision with --kira-syntax-property; the hue was freed up by vacating it in D9.
variableHoverSource and variableCompletionSource are wired onto the HTTP raw/code body editors and the gRPC message editor, which previously had VariableSupport range-highlighting only.
A description field exists on HTTP headers, urlencoded and form-data fields and gRPC metadata rows, behind a per-tab fieldDescriptions toggle that is off by default. Query params have no persisted row of their own (the URL is authoritative, a structural constraint), so they use a paramDescriptions side-car map reconciled on every URL edit.
FieldRowsTable, MetadataTable, VariableRow and VariablesOverviewPanel share one CSS Grid template instead of per-field flex values, so a secret row's extra affordance does not misalign columns. An inheritAttrs: false wrapper fixes the environment/variable views' inputs not filling their width.
The gRPC method select uses the same wrapper + :deep(.p-select) idiom as the target field, so it does not shrink to its widest option. The gRPC toolbar-width test asserts that target field and method select grow together on resize, since both are flex: 1.
OperationsPanel's op log already had a working always-visible filter matching command/kind/error; the only gap, the connection's own name, was added to its existing predicate instead of adding a second search box.
BrowseView's always-visible filter field was converted to the toggle-plus-PanelSearchBox idiom, with rt.filter, filteredNodes, the 'N of M' caption and every data-testid unchanged.
keywordCase 'preserve' is not reversed, because of the ClickHouse hazard from P13 D4.
Descriptions (header/param descriptions) never reach the wire in either protocol, in any format; internal/httpclient.Header has only Name/Value fields.
CollectionsPanel's initVariables() call raced EnvironmentSelect's on a fresh Api-mode mount, double-firing variablesListEnvironments; this was fixed.
resultMenu's 'Copy all > Shell mode' must produce one compact line per document; never map each document through the always-pretty-printing toShellText.
The Adapter interface has SchemaColumns with Caps.SchemaColumns true for the five SQL kinds and false for mongo/redis/kafka/sqs/s3, which return adapters.Unsupported.
Adapters implement listSchemaColumns as a schema-wide widened form of their per-object column query, never a loop over relations; postgres and mysqlfamily fold IsPrimaryKey into the same query. ClickHouse always reports IsPrimaryKey false, per its D18/D23 rule.
tree.Service.SchemaColumns uses a fourth metadata_cache kind 'columns' sharing the container's existing (connection_id, path) row rather than one row per relation, because of F8's 200-row budget; it uses cache-aside with requireConnected after the cache check.
Schema column cache in the studio (state/schemaColumns.ts ensureSchemaColumns) is idempotent, single-flight and fire-and-forget, and is invalidated by the onConnectionMetadataInvalidated broadcast.
SQL console completion precedence is document schema (wins wholesale), then cached schema columns, then tree relations, then undefined, never a per-table merge.
Lint and hover read the schema through effectiveSchema, a DdlSchema-shaped projection, so completion, diagnostics and hover cannot disagree about what the console knows.
The Schema (DDL) dialog remains as a deliberate override rather than the only schema supply.
mongoFilterCandidates/mongoSortCandidates live in views/shared/mongoFieldSample.ts because views/console/** may not import views/documents/**.
The Mongo console offers the collection's sampled field names for a bare identifier inside a db.<collection>.<method>(…) call, merged with the BSON-constructor list; DocumentView pushes the sample on page load and the console never fetches it.
The new schema-columns cache kind has no staleness/TTL policy (left to P24), there is no cross-schema completion (OQ-3), and the SchemaColumns payload has no indexes or foreign keys (OQ-2).

### v1.2 P23 (new) SQLite storage unbounded-growth audit
The P23 audit read all nineteen schema tables and found seventeen already bounded; the two unbounded ones were op_log and filter_history, each with a count cap but no byte cap.
api_response_history is bounded by 256 KiB per body, 30 entries per scope, a 128 MiB table budget and a launch orphan sweep.
grpc_call_history is bounded by 64 KiB per message, 100 messages per entry, 30 per scope, a 32 MiB budget and an orphan sweep.
metadata_cache is capped at 4 MiB per row and 200 rows per connection, and is dropped whole on every reconnect.
api_variable_history trims to 20 entries per variable.
The tabs table is rewritten per window on every save and cascades on delete when the window closes; connections, collections and variables grow only when a person creates something, so none is an unbounded-growth gap.
There is no metrics/telemetry storage: internal/metrics is an in-memory ticker with no table.
There is no on-disk cache alongside kira.db; ~/.kira-studio/ holds only the database, its -wal/-shm files, and logs/, which internal/logging/sweep.go ages out at 30 days.
Before P23, op_log's command column stored an entire pasted console document verbatim, so large scripts re-run many times bypassed the 20,000-row cap in bytes.
Before P23, filter_history was capped at 20 rows per (connection_id, path), with no bound on the number of paths, no byte cap and no liveness oracle.
op_log now truncates command to 64 KiB and error to 8 KiB in OpsRepo.Finish, and sets a command_truncated flag, because the Operations panel's Re-run executes command and must not run a partial script.
The op_log 64 KiB command cap derives from grpc_call_history's per-message figure and from being the largest value keeping the table under twice its budget between two 500-op prunes.
Migration 0015_p23_op_log_bytes.sql adds op_log stored_bytes and command_truncated columns, backfills bytes using CAST(... AS BLOB) so non-ASCII counts bytes rather than characters, purges rows already over the combined cap, and creates the op_log_bytes covering index.
op_log has a 32 MiB table-wide byte budget enforced as a third pass in Prune, using the op_log_bytes covering index; the sweep removes oldest first and never empties the table.
The Operations panel (OperationsPanel.vue) disables Re-run, but not Copy command, on a row whose command was truncated.
filter_history is capped at 4,000 rows per connection (200 paths per connection × 20 per path, matching metadata_cache) and 4 KiB of text per row.
Before P23 nothing returned freed pages to the filesystem (no VACUUM or auto_vacuum), so kira.db was a permanent high-water mark.
buildDSN sets _auto_vacuum=INCREMENTAL (for new databases) and journal_size_limit(4194304) (4 MiB WAL limit); repos.Maintenance.Reclaim runs an incremental_vacuum at startup when the freelist exceeds 16 MiB, wired in main.go beside the two SweepOrphans calls.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md records the growth-bound table and the standard a new table must meet; it also corrected doc drift where connection_filters is really connection_tree_filters.
Because OpRecord gains commandTruncated (a bridge-visible type shape change), bindings must be regenerated with wails3 task common:generate:bindings and checked against a real backend (-tags server build plus tests/e2e-real/sqlite-real.spec.ts).

### v1.2 P24 (new) DB metadata cache staleness audit
Intended metadata cache behaviour: connecting after idle refreshes cached metadata; navigating the already-loaded tree serves from cache and must not re-fetch; only an explicit user refresh or staleness (written before the connection was last established) triggers a re-fetch.
Before P24, a successful connect deleted every metadata_cache row for that connection (connections/service.go:639), a wipe not a refresh, so every node, table and console opened afterwards was a cold server fetch; this caused the over-fetching.
Navigating a live tree does not re-fetch: expand() early-returns on the renderer's own copy (project/state/tree.ts:164); the grid's describe, the definition view's definition and ensureSchemaColumns are each once-per-tab and read through the cache; nothing polls and nothing auto-connects.
Before P24, explicit Refresh re-fetched only one kind at one path, leaving the other three kinds on the same (connection_id, path) row untouched, and a connection's Refresh re-listed only its root instead of all rows for that connection as P1 §6c's eviction table specifies.
Before P24, TreeService.Invalidate had zero frontend callers, and Refresh on a table called Children on a leaf (a real engine op and op-log row returning an unrendered empty list) while describe/definition/columns payloads stayed stale.
Before P24 the metadata cache had no staleness concept: fetched_at was read only by the eviction ORDER BY, etag was never written non-NULL, and a cache hit won at any age.
Decision: cache staleness is not a TTL, because a TTL would re-fetch mid-session (violating no-refetch on navigation) and miss a reconnect inside its window; stale means written before the connection was most recently established.
Staleness uses ConnectionState.Since as the epoch and a per-kind timestamp stored under a reserved fetchedAt key inside payload_json, with no migration, new column or new bridge method.
metadata_cache.Get returns a per-kind fetchedAt map, and tree.Service.getCached bypasses (never drops) a payload written before the connection's own Since.
A successful connect no longer calls DropConnection in attemptConnect; it keeps cache rows, still emits metadataInvalidated, and rows are re-read lazily per opened path, remaining available for the disconnected/launch read (P1 D10, P22c F7).
Per-kind timestamps are required because a row-level fetched_at fails the per-kind hazard case; only the per-kind map passes it.
Every tree Refresh routes through treeInvalidate: refresh, refreshConnection and refreshObject drop the whole row (or every row for a connection) before re-reading, and menus.ts sends a leaf's Refresh to refreshObject so it no longer calls Children on a table/collection.
dropSchemaColumns clears the console's completion cache on every explicit refresh, not only on reconnect.
P24 extended tests/ui/tree.spec.ts with container- and leaf-refresh assertions, checked via control.log() rather than opsCount() where more precise.
budgets.spec.ts is a documented pre-existing flake in the full test:ui suite (cross-file worker contention; scroll p50 threshold); it passes when re-run alone.
The Adapter interface has a SchemaColumns method and Caps.SchemaColumns flag, true for the five SQL kinds (postgres, mysql, mariadb, sqlite, clickhouse) and false for mongo/redis/kafka/sqs/s3, which return adapters.Unsupported for it.
Each SQL adapter's listSchemaColumns is a widened form of its existing per-object column query (schema-wide WHERE, no relname/TABLE_NAME filter), never a loop over relations.
In SchemaColumns, postgres and mysqlfamily fold IsPrimaryKey into the same query; clickhouse always reports IsPrimaryKey false per its D18/D23 rule.
tree.Service.SchemaColumns uses a fourth metadata_cache kind, columns, sharing the container's existing (connection_id, path) row instead of one row per relation, to respect F8's 200-row budget; it is cache-aside with requireConnected after the cache check, like Children/Describe/Definition.
The frontend schema-columns cache (state/schemaColumns.ts ensureSchemaColumns) is idempotent, single-flight and fire-and-forget, and is invalidated by the onConnectionMetadataInvalidated broadcast that project/state/tree.ts also subscribes to.
SQL completion source precedence is document DDL (wins wholesale) → cached schema columns → tree relations → undefined, never a per-table merge.
namespaceFromCached (ddl.ts) projects cached RelationColumns into lang-sql's namespace shape byte-for-byte like toSqlNamespace.
effectiveSchema (state/schemaColumns.ts) is the DdlSchema-shaped projection that lint and hover read via ConsoleView.vue's ddlSchema computed, so completion, diagnostics and hover agree about what the console knows.
The 'Fill from connection' button/progress state (P19 D15) and the no-schema hint strip (D16) were removed; the Schema (DDL) dialog, saveDdl/ensureDdl/parseDdl and the connection-row menu entry remain, now framed as a deliberate override rather than the only schema supply.
mongoFilterCandidates/mongoSortCandidates live in views/shared/mongoFieldSample.ts because views/console/** may not import views/documents/**.
The Mongo console offers a completion for a bare identifier inside a db.<collection>.<method>(…) call: that collection's sampled field names, pushed by DocumentView.vue on page load and never fetched by the console, merged with the BSON-constructor list.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's Caching section records the fourth metadata_cache kind and hands two open items to P24.
P22c deliberately has no staleness/TTL policy for the new cache kind (left to P24), no cross-schema completion (OQ-3), and no indexes/foreign keys in the SchemaColumns payload (OQ-2).

### v1.2 P25 (new) DataGrip connection import
P25 adds DataGrip connection import: the user picks a local JetBrains DataGrip project folder with a file picker, and the import reads connections and saved passwords from files already on disk without any interactive DataGrip/JetBrains login.
A DataGrip project's data sources live in .idea/dataSources.xml (component DataSourceManagerImpl, one data-source element per source with driver-ref, jdbc-driver and jdbc-url) plus .idea/dataSources.local.xml (component dataSourceStorageLocal), correlated by uuid.
dataSources.local.xml carries user-name, secret-storage and a database-info dbms hint for each data source; neither XML file holds the password, except for a URL-only source whose jdbc-url contains user:password@.
DataGrip stores passwords in the IntelliJ Platform PasswordSafe under the service name 'IntelliJ Platform DB — <uuid>' (em dash), where the key is the data source uuid, per generateServiceName(subsystem, key) in CredentialAttributes.kt.
The 'DB' subsystem string appears in no JetBrains source because the database tooling is closed; it rests on four agreeing third-party reimplementations.
The secret-storage values master_key, memory and forget map to DataGrip's Forever, Until restart and Never by inference from real files, and are used only to word a miss.
PasswordSafe has three backends; the KEEPASS file store is not the default anywhere. The default is KEYCHAIN: the macOS Keychain on macOS and the freedesktop Secret Service over libsecret on Linux.
The DataGrip import reads the macOS Keychain only. A project configured with PROVIDER=KEEPASS gets a named credential-store-unsupported refusal instead of a file-store retry.
The c.pwd/c.kdbx KeePass file-store fallback (kdbx.go, mainkey.go, the gokeepasslib dependency, and the testdata/passwordsafe fixtures and generator) was removed because the user asked only for macOS Keychain; it came from misreading the user's description of the credential store internals.
Linux Secret Service (needs libsecret over cgo, deliberately absent from this repo's Go) and Windows DPAPI are refused by name; every format uncertainty must fail loudly with a specific 'unsupported DataGrip/IDE version' message rather than yield a silently wrong password.
No Go library exists for the IntelliJ credential-store glue, and the only complete implementation is AGPL-3.0 Swift, which cannot be vendored into this MIT repo; DBeaver's DataGrip-import request is closed unimplemented.
The implementation is in internal/datagrip (project XML parse/correlate, engine cascade, macOS Keychain query, Scan/Apply), with generated test fixtures in internal/datagrip/testdata/gen.
The project-folder picker is bridge.Dialogs.OpenDirectory / FilesService.ChooseFolder, and bridge.DataGripService.Scan/.Import send only the project path across the bridge.
The Studio import dialog is DataGripImportDialog.vue, reachable from ProjectPanel.vue's header and StudioStart.vue's first-run screen, with tests in tests/ui/datagrip-import.spec.ts.
DataGrip import is a distinct self-contained capability, not a change to an existing import path (Postman import is collections, not connections), and is its own phase because of its reverse-engineering risk, with no dependency on P22-P24.

### v1.2 P26 (new) Separate interface font from data font
P26 splits the single --kira-font-family token (theme/tokens.css, Menlo, monospace, applied to body in theme/base.css) into a customizable data font (--kira-font-data) and a fixed interface font (--kira-font-ui).
The data font stays monospace, is one of P28's fifteen FONT_CHOICES, and is customizable via the Settings dialog's font dropdown; it applies to surfaces rendering database/API content (grids, document/key-value views, CodeMirror editors, console).
The interface font for UI chrome (panels, dialogs, buttons, menus, labels, tabs) is not customizable (no Settings entry) and is fixed to VS Code's UI font stack: -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "Segoe WPC", "Segoe UI", system-ui, "Ubuntu", "Droid Sans", sans-serif.
The interface font stack must include system-ui, which VS Code's own comment calls load-bearing on Linux; the originally quoted string omitted it. The stack is the union of VS Code's per-platform rules, exported as DEFAULT_FONT_FAMILY and published as --vscode-font-family.
VS Code's editor font (--vscode-editor-font-family) stays monospace and user-configurable; it is the wrong precedent for the interface font and must not be copied.
The split is a reversion: the design system primitives.css was ported from has two type roles (--font-ui and --font-mono) governed by LAW 08: mono is for data (strings from a database); if the app wrote it, it is UI.
P26 supersedes v1 SPEC §8.2's sentence that the whole app uses one font family and size; call sites are classified by LAW 08.
The .p-input.ui class in primitives.css hard-coded a sans stack outside any token; the plan folds that literal into the interface-font token instead of keeping a second definition.
Inventory: 43 --kira-font-family hits, 38 real consumers across 25 files, plus body, plus .p-completion which needed a declaration. 36 stay data; 3 move to chrome (cell editor's format select, its generate menu, the grid's multi-sort order badge).
Changing body to the interface font carries every chrome surface lacking its own declaration, because Tailwind's preflight gives form controls font: inherit.
--kira-font-size is deliberately left unsplit: the type scale is not cleanly role-split (--kira-t-md equals --kira-font-size with mixed consumers), P22 D1 coupled --kira-t-xs/-sm/-lg to that setting on purpose, and Row height already governs tree, grid and lists.
The Settings dialog needs no copy change (it already says "Data font" / "Data font size"), and fonts.ts's availability probe needs no change because it is only called on appearance.fontFamily and the fixed stack ends in sans-serif.
No Settings entry for the interface font is built, even though the design-system artboard draws one.
The .p-completion popup has an explicit --kira-font-data declaration, keeping it consistent with the CodeMirror completion popup unified by P22 D8; a fontFamily parity assertion in autocomplete.spec.ts guards it.
font-roles.spec.ts asserts that data and chrome surfaces resolve different font tokens.
If mode-switch.spec.ts's P22 D5/D6 ink measurement breaks from a font change, fix it with --kira-icon-optical-y, never by relaxing the bound.
mode-switch.spec.ts passed unchanged after the font split, so --kira-icon-optical-y stays undeclared.
P26 was implemented in six commits (T1-T6), introducing --kira-font-data and --kira-font-ui and renaming the 36 data sites.
The split was requested because one font served two jobs, making the P28 font dropdown appear to change button/label text when users only meant to change table data rendering.

### v1.2 P27 (new) Test-suite speed
Image pull is not the dominant cost of the Go test tier once container images are cached.
The repo has no go test -p pin anywhere, and CLAUDE.md prescribes no -p 1, so nothing in the Go tier is serialized by a -p setting.
internal/ipcfixture was the slowest Go package because it starts six containers one after another and tears them down serially in TestMain.
t.Parallel() cannot speed up ipcfixture because it panics against the t.Setenv("KIRA_HOME", …) call in ipcfixture/harness.go.
A Prewarm/StopConcurrently seam over testsupport's mutex-guarded fixture memo starts and stops containers concurrently. It is called from the TestMains of ipcfixture, adapters/kafka and adapters/mysqlfamily, with no production-code change.
The testcontainers Kafka module's log-based wait fires before the advertised listener serves, so the seed's first request can be closed mid-ApiVersions when brokers start concurrently. A client.Ping poll (waitKafkaBrokerReady) before seedKafka fixes it.
IsDockerAvailable is memoized with sync.Once, because the docker info probe had 144 call sites at about 60 ms each.
The Go tier go test ./apps/kira-studio/internal/... dropped from about 95.8 s (102.5 s in the implementation worktree) to about 58 s, a reduction of about 43%. The Kafka fix produced zero failures.
For bun run test:ui, raising Playwright workers alone breaks the wall-clock budgets in budgets.spec.ts (12 ms scroll budget), which measured 15 ms at 4 workers and 22 ms at 3, because the spec shares the box with other WebKit instances.
Wall-clock-budget specs (budgets.spec.ts, perf.spec.ts and the two '150 ms sandbox gate' tests in slick-grid.spec.ts) run in a serial ui-timing Playwright project with dependencies on ui. The rest run in the ui project with workers '100%'. The slick-grid tests are selected by title via grep/grepInvert.
The top-level Playwright workers setting in playwright.config.ts is the real worker-pool size; a project's own workers can only narrow it. It was moved from '50%' to '100%'.
bun run test:ui went from 351 s to about 269-275 s with 245/245 passing. The ipc-frontend tier went from 25.9 s to 10.5 s with workers '100%'. typecheck runs its four independent projects concurrently, saving about 30%.
Declined: go test -p 8, because the default is already GOMAXPROCS and a literal would cut parallelism on bigger machines. Also declined: caching bun run build:test (0.7% of test:ui) and replacing the 59 waitForTimeout sites (mostly deliberate frame pacing).
Container reuse across test runs was declined because the adapter suites mutate data and run DDL, so a reused container would carry the previous run's writes into the next run.
scripts/setup.sh already caches its bindings step: the first run took 4.43 s and the second 0.135 s with the bindings step skipped.
grpcclient's startEchoServer race was fixed in the test with a real grpc_reflection_v1 ServerReflectionInfo round-trip readiness gate. An EOF failure still occurs in roughly 30-40% of sampled runs in the sandbox, and reproduces under CPU stress with no Docker involved. Fully closing it would need a client-side retry in internal/grpcclient's production dial path, which is out of this phase's scope.
internal/shell's TestSecondShouldQuitReturnsTrue can flake under load because its 1 s internal handshake timeout is exceeded; it is attributed to environmental noise.
P27 deleted, skipped and loosened no tests or assertions, and swapped no runner. Environment-noise flakes were reported rather than masked with longer timeouts or looser bounds.

### v1.2 P28 (new) Seventh user-driven batch — Api/Studio polish, floating-surface hardening, the menu bar, and the window's webvi...
In Api, the HTTP/gRPC status-code explanation becomes the status chip's tooltip instead of a permanent line, reversing D11/P18 D13.
HttpRequestView and GrpcRequestView test only isDynamicName for the unresolved chip, so all 57 FAKE_NAMES ({{fake.*}} references) are wrongly flagged as unknown; P28 stops counting them into the unresolved chip.
Postman $name aliases leave autocomplete and the reference dialog and survive only as an import-time rewrite through the exhaustive ALIAS_TO_FAKE table.
Pasting a curl command into the Api URL field routes through the existing P7 parser.
A single find bar with the data views' own case/word/regex toggles sits above both the Api request and response panels.
In Api, the field-name column joins the variable-hover surface, the Variables button uses the variable-group icon instead of symbol-variable, and every Api dropdown anchors left.
ViewChrome can omit run controls that a variables tab has no use for, and environment name/description fields get real labels.
Environment management moves out of EnvironmentsDialog into a real tab, the sidebar environments category is removed (reversing P22b D8), and the server-environment icon replaces the gear.
The Studio console grid gets the SQL data view's live cell-range drag highlight; the data view already has it and only the console lacks the onCellRangeSelecting subscription.
In Studio, a right-click paints the column selection it already makes and no longer lets the following interaction collapse a multi-row selection; the toolbar delete appeared to do nothing because it read a selection the menu had already destroyed, behind an enablement check that never consulted one.
Studio sort control loses 10px of flex chrome, and initial column width gets its own 96px floor separate from the 64px resize floor.
The in-cell FK/PK button reserves padding again instead of overlaying the text, reversing item 12 of P22b's coordinator round.
The Studio pager returns to DataToolbar, as a targeted revert of commit d2892f49.
Item 9 (Mongo's plain copy) is deliberately not implemented: every Mongo copy affordance is an explicitly labelled format (Shell mode / Canonical Extended JSON / Relaxed Extended JSON), two of three preserve types, and there is no Mongo copy button anywhere, so no format is rewritten on a guess.
The console's rowAsJsonMenu has exactly one call site, the Redis branch its comment says it is for, so it is not reachable for a Mongo result (contradicting the plan's F9).
floatingPosition gains size() so an over-tall menu scrolls instead of clipping, PopoverPanel and the autocomplete popup teleport out of ancestor stacking/clip contexts, and the five bare z-index literals become one ordered token ladder.
Both import buttons and a New Request action move into the native menu bar, and --kira-statusbar-h shrinks from 26px to 20px.
A closing window navigates its webview to about:blank before the native close, so the WebContent process holds an empty document rather than the app's whole heap; this is implemented entirely in internal/shell/closeflush.go and main.go is not touched.
Wails v3 beta.16 exposes no window-destroy API; macosWebviewWindow.destroy() is dead code and byte-identical to close().
P28 is a user-driven batch from real use, not a code-review round, because CLAUDE.md reserves that name for the three-parallel-agent process; it is sequenced after P27 since it reverses recent P22b decisions.

### v1.2 P29 (new) Bind secret ciphertext to its kind (AAD)
AES-256-GCM AAD in internal/secrets/cipher.go is bound to the secret's kind (scope: connection, variable, variable-history), so a ciphertext only decrypts under the scope it was encrypted for.
Before P29, Seal/Open carried no AAD, so an attacker with raw write access to kira.db (but not the OS Keychain) could move a connections.password ciphertext into api_variables.secret_value and have a variable reveal expose the connection credential.
The secret envelope prefix is bumped from kira:v2: to kira:v3: to mark the AAD format change.
AAD is kind-scoped, not row-scoped, so SecretStore.copy() (ConnectionsRepo.InsertDuplicateWithSecret, VariablesRepo.DuplicateEnvironment) can keep copying ciphertext via raw SQL subquery with no decrypt/re-encrypt and no cipher/keychain dependency at duplicate time.
No backward compatibility: kira:v1: and kira:v2: ciphertexts fail decrypt with a re-enter error once kira:v3: ships; stored connection passwords and API-variable secrets must be re-entered once, with no migration, dual-read or grace period.
The kira:v1: envelope (legacy Chromium safeStorage) already fails decrypt with a "re-enter it" error.
The two internal/connections/service.go Encrypt sites are Create and Update; Duplicate never touches the cipher and copies the raw ciphertext column via InsertDuplicateWithSecret.
internal/storage/repos/variables.go has five Encrypt/Decrypt sites: encryptFor, valueChanged, RevealValue, RevealHistoryValue, mergeSecrets.
VariablesRepo.recordHistory re-encrypts under ScopeVariableHistory using the plaintext callers already hold (valueChanged's decrypted oldPlain) instead of copying the live ciphertext, because a raw copy from api_variables.secret_value to api_variable_history.secret_value is cross-kind and would be unreadable and allow laundering a stolen secret.
internal/secrets/scope.go declares Scope as a named string type with three constants and a valid() check invoked by both Encrypt and Decrypt before any crypto, so a scope typo yields a loud E_SECRET_STORE error.
Cipher API is Encrypt(scope, plain) and Decrypt(scope, stored); all nine production call sites plus recordHistory's new one use it.
The wrong-envelope error message in cipher.go names kira:v3: and reads sensibly for variable reveals as well as connections; decryptFailure's separate message is unchanged.
internal/storage/repos/secret_scope_test.go tests cross-kind ciphertext moves against the app's own tables in both directions.
The no-AAD design was deliberately left open in cipher.go's comment (P21 round 2 finding 10) because a scope string threaded through every call site was out of scope then; dropping v1/v2 compatibility removed the reason for deferral.
<!-- /chapter v1.2 -->

<!-- chapter v1.3 -->
## v1.3
Source: docs/v1.3/ at bd0c224688d9. 34 phases.
The v1.3 chapter is the git subsystem, a third top-level subsystem beside studio and api. It ships headless: the git backend runs inside Kira Studio and the frontend is a separately-installed VS Code extension.
The v1.3 git chapter uses fresh phase numbering (G1, G2, …), not a continuation of v1.2's.
v1.2 shipped the Api module, specified in docs/v1.2/SPEC.md.
The branch origin/claude/feature-v1-3 is a superseded attempt at the git chapter and is not built on.
Real GitHub requests run through gh api rather than reading gh's stored credential out from under it.
The diff is produced by an argv-only, no-shell invocation, not a hand-rolled Go diff algorithm.
branch.resolvePr exists and is wired into G11's reaper rather than duplicating it; the exact reaper shape is G11's own design, with a seam G24 can hook rather than rebuild.
The Git module is documented in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (Git module section) and docs/PERF.md §2.13.

### v1.3 G1
The v1.3 G1 goal comprises a socket listener, framing, handshake, a pairing broker, a trust store and a Connected editors pane.
v1.3 G1 is proven end-to-end on app.init and repo.open alone.

### v1.3 G2

### v1.3 G3
Milestone v1.3 G3 covers porcelain parsers with a golden corpus, cat-file --batch, a paged log session, commit store/interner/packing, and graph.stream over FlatBuffers, so the graph renders end to end.

### v1.3 G4
Milestone v1.3 G4 covers commit detail, file tree, diff, blob reads and line-mapped "Go to file", plus the seven host-capability methods wired client-side.

### v1.3 G5
Milestone v1.3 G5 covers refs/tags/branches, checkout with the Go pre-flight engine, revert, linked-worktree detection, an in-progress banner, and an undo slot.

### v1.3 G6

### v1.3 G7
Release v1.3 group G7 covers remote operations: fetch, push, decomposed pull, force-with-lease, protected branches, askpass broker with credential relay, and auto-fetch.

### v1.3 G8
v1.3 G8 covers multi-client hardening: two-windows/two-repos matrix, disconnect teardown, revoke-while-connected, stale-socket recovery, and perf re-baseline against the new transport.

### v1.3 G9
Phase G9 (v1.3) switches the repo watcher from fsnotify's kqueue backend to FSEvents, matching VS Code (@parcel/watcher) and Zed (the notify crate's macOS backend).
fsnotify's kqueue backend needs one open file descriptor per watched directory, including every directory under commonDir/refs (per G2/G3).
FSEvents watches a whole directory tree recursively through one event stream, which eliminates the per-path fd cost entirely instead of only measuring how close it gets to kern.maxfilesperproc.
G8's fd-cost question (F10) was still unresolved after six phases and was promoted to a ship-blocker; it exists only because of kqueue's per-directory fd cost, which G9 removes.
G9 requires a new macOS-only dependency (e.g. github.com/fsnotify/fsevents), a rewrite of internal/gitclient/watcher.go, and re-verification of every existing watcher test under the new backend.
The FSEvents switch is its own phase (G9) rather than folded into G2 or G8, because it is not a small fix.
G9 is sequenced right before Ship, so the packaged extension never ships with the fd-cost risk open.

### v1.3 G10
G10 ships the extension: a vsce package step producing a real .vsix, and DMG bundling where the scripts/sign-bundle.sh/package script copies it into the app bundle at a known runtime path.
G10 adds an Install VS Code Integration button in the Connected editors pane (G1) that runs code --install-extension <path> with argv only and no shell, matching every other spawn in this chapter. It falls back to "reveal in Finder" when the code CLI isn't on PATH.
G10 includes the non-packaging part of upstream's P13: an SCM title-bar button and a status-bar item that open the existing webview; they are entry points, not a redesign of the webview.
G10 includes a command-palette audit that wires a command for every mutating operation existing by then: checkout/revert/branch/tag from G5 and fetch/push/pull from G7.
G10 was moved up from last on 2026-09-07 so the extension is installable and testable sooner, right after hardening; G11 onward are post-ship follow-ups.
Each phase from G11 onward must register its own command-palette command when it lands, because the G10 palette audit is not repeated.
G10 supersedes upstream P13 (Ship), using DMG bundling instead of Marketplace/OpenVSX distribution.

### v1.3 G11
G11 incremental review stores per-file last-reviewed state as a compressed blob snapshot, not just a commit sha, because rewritten history (rebase/squash/amend) can't be diffed by sha alone.
G11 incremental review uses an ordinary git diff when the last-reviewed commit is still an ancestor of HEAD; if history was rewritten, it diffs the stored blob against current content directly.
G11 supports partial/range-level reviewed-vs-unreviewed marking within a file, not just a whole-file toggle.
G11 review state lives in its own SQLite file, review.db, and is TTL-purged after 14 days idle.
G11's reaper shape is its own design, with a seam G24 can hook rather than rebuild. G24 adds the eager purge on PR close (not built in G11), using branch.resolvePr wired into G11's reaper rather than duplicating it.

### v1.3 G12
Phase v1.3 G12 is a connectivity, pairing and review-UI hardening batch of real bugs/UX issues found by using the shipped G10 extension against Kira Studio.
Revoking a connected editor's pairing token does not make a subsequent re-pairing appear in the Connected editors list; it is a stale-list bug, not just a revoke bug.
Pairing appears to require a fresh connect/approval per repo rather than once per VS Code installation, although G1's trust store already keys on per-editor identity (§3.3); G12 will remove the per-repo prompt if nothing genuinely requires it.
Kira Studio should be foregrounded when its pairing dialog appears, using the platform's window-activation API; where that is blocked (no accessibility permission, background launch), it falls back to a system notification.
Connected editors is to be split into its own Settings tab, separate from the git settings it is currently bundled with.
The G3 commit graph does not render at all, a regression; the cause lies somewhere in FlatBuffers framing, graph.stream or the Vue renderer, and it must be root-caused and fixed with no workaround.
The VS Code extension should default to the workspace's own repo by wiring repo.open to the workspace root automatically, falling back to a picker only when no repo is found there; currently it prompts to open a folder even when a git repo is already open.
The extension needs a persistent status affordance distinguishing connecting, connected, actively loading and disconnected-or-broken, sourced from rpcstream's own connection lifecycle rather than inferred.
The review sidebar's diff opens only in VS Code's native diff view and is never rendered inside the webview; its filter and list/tree toggle move into one panel-level toolbar instead of being repeated per commit.
The review sidebar, including G11's new Files pane, is to be restyled to match Kira Studio's visual design by reusing Kira Studio's existing components/tokens rather than the extension's ad hoc styling.
The extension must ship with Kira Studio's own app icon (not a placeholder) and a short extension README.md.
The review sidebar's "open file" action is broken and must be fixed, and every button in that panel is to be converted from text labels to icons for consistency with the rest of the UI.
Triage of which G12 items are genuine regressions versus always-broken, and which touch the G1/G2/G3 backend versus only the extension/webview, happens at the phase's own Opus planning pass.

### v1.3 G13
G13 (v1.3) adds inline AI review comments: flat file+line/range annotations on top of G11's review session, with no threading.
Review comments show in a centralized list ordered by file then line, formatted as plain text to copy into an AI chat.
AI review comments are deliberately just a copy-paste workflow for now, not a live AI integration.
A clear-all action resets a review session's comments.

### v1.3 G14
v1.3 G14 treats the graph rendering nothing as a core-functionality regression: loading returns zero commits even though G12's handshake/boot fix was verified and the panel and its load control work.
The zero-commit graph bug is distinct from the handshake: suspected causes are the data path (graph.stream/logSession/gitstore) or G12's auto-open-repo logic (wrong repo resolved, unborn/detached/wrong-ref starting point, or a swallowed error reported as an empty result).
Root-cause the graph bug against a real repo, not by re-running G12's container-only repro.
The pairing/connect prompt should become a real system-level notification/prompt as its primary mechanism, not window focus with a notification fallback (G12 item 3), so it surfaces above whatever the user is looking at.
The VS Code status-bar connection item is too narrow; less-critical detail should move out of it (e.g. a hover tooltip).
The file tree's indentation should be reduced because it wastes horizontal space.
Font size should follow VS Code's own setting rather than a fixed Kira Studio scale, correcting G12 D14's structure-only restyle; it should track the host like color already does.
The branch review UI is to be redesigned, modeled on GitLens rather than its current design.
A "Go to file" button is to be added to VS Code's native diff editor (where G12 moved every review diff), left of the next/previous-change buttons as in GitLens, reusing G4's line-mapped "Go to file" capability.
The diff "Go to file" button must work for non-checked-out/historical file content too, landing the cursor at the corresponding line.
An "Open in graph" button is to be added to the same diff toolbar, jumping to the commit in the graph view, modeled on GitLens.
G14 is sequenced immediately after G13 and ahead of G15's range-marking feature, because item 1 is a core-functionality regression, not a polish item.

### v1.3 G15
G12 moves the review sidebar's diffs out of the webview into VS Code's own diff view (editor.openRangeDiff), which drops G11's range-level "mark reviewed" capability.
G12 accepts the loss of range-level marking as an interim gap and restores only whole-file marking, so G15 can rebuild range marking against the real editor surface.
G15 adds range/hunk-level review marking inside VS Code's native diff editor, modeled on the built-in Git extension's hunk-staging gutter UI, using gutter decorations and/or per-hunk CodeLens actions on the diff's right-hand document.
G15 wires hunk actions to G11's review.mark RPC using its existing ranges parameter instead of a whole-file call; that parameter is already shipped but unused client-side after G12.
G15 touches only apps/kira-studio-vscode/src: no Go changes, no packages/git-ui, no review.db migration, no CONTRACT_VERSION bump, because G11 already put the range machinery on the wire.
G15's decoration lifecycle, selection-to-LineRange mapping, and re-open-after-new-commits behavior are left to the phase's own Opus planning pass, the same placeholder-then-design approach as G25/G26/G28.
G15 depends on G11 and G12 and has status new.

### v1.3 G16
The webview layout collapse (panel about 75px tall showing 6 of 2004 rows) occurs because neither the document emitted by apps/kira-studio-vscode/src/html.ts nor the bundled webview CSS gives html, body or #app an actual height.
In packages/git-ui, .kv-app (App.vue) and .kv-review-view (ReviewView.vue) both set height: 100%, which resolves to auto because the ancestor chain has no height.
The layout collapse is a real app bug, not a test-harness or theme artifact: it reproduces identically with zero --vscode-* variables injected and with two full theme sets.
DOM/accessibility-tree checks (aria-rowcount matching the real commit count) passed despite the visual collapse, so DOM-shape checks cannot catch this regression.
G16 fix: establish real height from html/body down to #app/.kv-app/.kv-review-view in both webview roots (graph view and review view). Candidates: a rule in html.ts's emitted document, or a rule in packages/git-ui's shared CSS fixing every host at once (which also needs #app/mount() sizing resolved).
VS Code's default body { padding: 0 20px } is never reset in the webviews, silently eating 40px of width from every panel; G16 fixes this.
LoadMoreButton.vue renders a dead "Load the last 0" button when a single page covers all of history, because the exhaustion signal on a chunk is wrong.
The main source of the wrong exhaustion signal is internal/gitsession/walk.go, whose cache-replay path hardcodes Exhausted: false on every replayed chunk.
logsession/session.go has a narrower exhaustion defect, only reachable when the commit count is an exact multiple of the page size.
Fix the exhaustion bugs at their source (walk.go and session.go), not client-side.
G16 must add a guard asserting a real rendered box height, not DOM shape, because no current test tier catches this class of layout regression.
stash.list's pre-existing unhandled-RPC issue is not part of G16; it is tracked at G14 F14 and handed to G17, which owns stash server-side.

### v1.3 G17
Item G17 in v1.3 is stash plus merge-tree pop prediction, wired into G5's blocked-checkout resolution.

### v1.3 G18
G18 moves per-viewer graph and stash display settings out of VS Code's contributes.configuration into an in-app settings dialog opened from the graph view, configurable per repo.
The server-owned settings protectedBranches, fetch.autoInterval and git.path stay where they are and are not in scope for the G18 move.
G18 was triggered by G17's plan flagging a decision on whether to wire the inert kiraVersion.stash.showInGraph setting into the graph view, which would need a CONTRACT_VERSION bump.
Per-viewer display settings move out of apps/kira-studio-vscode/package.json contributes.configuration and app.init's VS-Code-sourced settings snapshot into new per-repo server-side storage plus a dialog in packages/git-ui.
G18 is not designed yet; the full design happens at its own Opus planning pass, using the same placeholder-then-design approach as G25/G26/G28.
Candidate keys to move in G18 from packages/git-core/src/settings/schema.ts are graph.pageSize, graph.scope, stash.showInGraph and stash.includeUntracked, plus any other per-viewer setting found at plan time.
G11's review.db, its own per-repo SQLite file, is one plausible prior-art pattern for G18's per-repo storage, but not a mandate.
G18's design must cover the new or replacing RPC surface and migration/back-compat for values already set in VS Code's settings.json.

### v1.3 G19
Phase G19 (v1.3) is a batch of 15 Git graph, review panel and file-tree UI fixes found by the user's hands-on use of the shipped app, sequenced right after G18.
G19's row fixes only scope and sequencing; the full design and exact fix for each item come from the phase's own Opus planning pass, the same placeholder-then-design approach as G12/G14.
G19 Git Graph: checked-out branches get a visual indicator, and the timestamp column is widened because it is too narrow.
G19 Git Graph: rebuild the toolbar and right-click/context menu from Kira Studio's existing design-system components, because the current elements don't match the rest of the app.
G19 Git Graph commit detail panel: the commit message is truncated to a few lines and expands only on click, with the tree of the commit's changes shown below it the same way the review panel shows it.
G19 Review bar: the two compared branches are stacked, with a button to swap base and compared.
G19 Review bar: the filter bar is hidden behind a search button, and every copy button is removed from the panel, with that functionality moved into a right-click context-menu action.
Known bug in the review panel: "see all commit changes" picks up only the last file in the commit instead of all files; G19 fixes it.
G19: when a diff is open and the user navigates to a virtual file (one not on disk at that point, e.g. only at a historical revision), the file's tab gets a lock icon to show it doesn't really exist.
Known bug in the review panel: expanding a commit and clicking a file inside it collapses the commit; expand state must not be lost on a file click.
G19: the review panel must retain its state across close/reopen, gets a "back" button, and is renamed "Kira Version."
G19 file tree/file list (shared across graph detail pane, review panel and elsewhere): use the same font as the rest of the UI instead of the current apparently monospaced font.
G19 file tree: show file-type icons from the codicons set (already a packages/git-ui dependency) matched to extension/type, keeping the modified/deleted/added status indicators but small and secondary beside the file icon.
G19: added/deleted line-count numbers in diff stats are abbreviated with K/M/B suffixes (e.g. 1200 becomes 1.2K).

### v1.3 G20
Phase v1.3 G20 migrates every tooltip and viewport-anchored floating surface across all frontend modules onto Floating UI collision-aware positioning (flip/shift/size), matching the existing precedent in apps/kira-studio/frontend.
packages/git-ui uses 57 plain native HTML title/:title attributes across 19 files as tooltips, which are unstyled and inconsistent with the rest of the app.
apps/kira-studio/frontend's v-tooltip mechanism (workbench/state/tooltip.ts plus theme/floatingPosition.ts, built on @floating-ui/dom) renders position: fixed, teleported to body so ancestor overflow: hidden cannot clip it, and uses the top-of-stack --kira-z-tooltip token.
apps/kira-studio/frontend has a second, bespoke tooltip system: the request panel's JSON/variable-value hover preview (RequestBodyPane.vue variableHoverSource via editor/hover.ts buildHoverSource) uses CodeMirror's native hoverTooltip, bypassing tooltip.ts and AppTooltip.vue.
The CodeMirror hover tooltip is not teleported to body because CodeMirrorHost.vue never configures parent: document.body. It stays inside two overflow: hidden ancestors (.cm-host, .request-pane) and reproducibly renders behind the request panel's toolbar.
CodeMirror's tooltip carries a hardcoded z-index: 500 that is not coordinated with the app's --kira-z-tooltip token.
apps/kira-studio/frontend is not exempt from G20: it needs its own fix for at least the CodeMirror-native tooltip path.
The G20 audit must check every tooltip-producing mechanism in every module (native title, v-tooltip, CodeMirror hoverTooltip, and any other bespoke hover/preview), because a second mechanism already slipped past the first survey.
In packages/git-ui, RowContextMenu.vue hand-rolls a post-mount viewport clamp via getBoundingClientRect and window.innerWidth/innerHeight instead of flip/shift middleware.
In packages/git-ui, App.vue's .kv-branch-force-delete--floating confirmation popup has no clamping and uses raw click-point left/top.
Seven packages/git-ui dropdowns/popovers (BranchPicker, PullStrategyPicker, RepoPicker, review/BaseSelector .kv-base-panel, AppToolbar .kv-push-menu, SearchBox/SearchResults suggestion and error popups) use position: absolute; top: calc(100% + Npx); left: 0 with no viewport awareness.
apps/kira-studio/frontend's ContextMenu.vue, PopoverPanel.vue, AutocompleteField.vue and ErrorPopover.vue are already Floating-UI-based and need no migration.
@floating-ui/dom is a root package.json dependency but not packages/git-ui's own; G20 adds it to packages/git-ui.
G20 depends on G19, which builds packages/kira-ui, a host-agnostic shared component package including KuiContextMenu. G20's RowContextMenu.vue and dropdown migration should build on it if it exists, otherwise coordinate with it rather than duplicate it.

### v1.3 G21
In both the git graph panel and the review panel, build all elements (dialogs, tooltips, buttons, search bars, right-click menus) from Kira Studio's own component library.
In the git graph, give branch/tag labels the same color as the graph line (lane) they represent.
Git graph branch lines' beginnings are not properly connected to the origin and hang disconnected; to be fixed.
The commit SHA column is removed from the git graph; the commit details panel shows one short SHA, and clicking it copies the full SHA, so the separate copy button is removed.
The "show Kira in status bar" option is removed from VS Code entirely, including the setting itself from contributes.configuration, not left inert.
Show file icons from codicons based on the actual file extension, not a coarse category.
Show file status (added/deleted/modified etc.) as just a colored letter, with no chip/badge styling.
Reuse a single shared file-diff-tree component in both the review panel and the git graph panel; do not duplicate implementations.
Both diff file trees (review and git graph) open changes in VS Code's own editor, not the extension's embedded panels.
Single-click on a file in a diff tree opens a diff as a VS Code preview tab (replaced by the next single-click open); only a double-click keeps it permanently open.
G19's D8 added {preview: false} to vscode.diff calls so bulk 'all commit changes' opens land each file in its own persistent tab; bulk multi-file opens keep {preview: false}, while a single click on one file omits it or uses {preview: true}.
G21 items 1 (checked-out HEAD shown in graph), 6 (wider date column) and 8 (all-commit-changes shows all files) duplicate G19's D1, D2 and D8; re-verify against current code and fix only a real remaining problem or regression.
G20's scope is floating/positioned surfaces (tooltips, context menus, dropdowns, popovers), while G21 item 2 is broader (every dialog, button, search bar); whichever phase is implemented second must account for the other's coverage to avoid duplication or gaps.

### v1.3 G22
v1.3 G22 completed reset (3 modes), cherry-pick, and the undo slot.

### v1.3 G23
G23 in v1.3 covers Search: a Go tail scan and a client matcher, with regex-dialect reconciliation (§8).

### v1.3 G24
G24 GitHub PR links detect a GitHub remote from origin and authenticate via the local gh CLI on the Kira Studio backend, with REST PR lookup through gh api. This replaces upstream's VS Code-built-in-provider design, which this chapter can no longer use.
PR links resolve per-commit, not per-branch-tip (decided 2026-09-07), a deliberate departure from most clients including upstream's branch-tip-only design.
The commit graph shows a small indicator on any commit that belongs to a PR, resolved by commit membership rather than whether the branch is checked out, so it works in detached HEAD, mid-branch commits, and non-checked-out branches.
Clicking a commit surfaces the PR link in the existing commit-detail pane (G4's).
PR lookup uses GitHub's "list pull requests associated with a commit" REST endpoint via gh api, resolved lazily per selected commit rather than for every visible graph row, because a bulk per-row check risks the rate-limit budget; the exact mechanism is left to the phase's planning pass.
Upstream's branch-tip badges (branch-picker rows, message-column ref badges opened via ExternalOpener) are kept alongside the new graph indicator, because they answer a different question: whether a branch has an open PR versus whether a specific commit is part of one.
G24 includes a kiraVersion.github.enabled setting, PR number/title matching added to search's ref scope, and wiring G11's eager PR-closed purge into its reaper since branch.resolvePr now exists.
GitHub auth is requested on first use only, never at activation.

### v1.3 G25
v1.3 G25 adds worktree support: git worktree create/list/switch/remove, building on G5's linked-worktree detection.
v1.3 G25 includes a user-configurable prepare script that runs after creating a worktree, with visible progress feedback.

### v1.3 G26
G26 (v1.3) is stacked branches: a sequence of dependent branches, each based on the previous and typically one PR per branch, with restacking (rebasing each branch onto its updated parent when an earlier branch changes) and stack navigation.
G26 stacked branches has no upstream design to port and is not designed yet; the full design is deferred to the phase's own Opus planning pass, using the same placeholder-then-design approach as G25.
G26's design must settle how the stack relationship is tracked, because git has no native concept of a stack.
G26's design must settle the restack algorithm and its conflict/undo story against G5's pre-flight engine and undo slot, and how stacks render against G6's branch review and G24's per-commit PR indicator.

### v1.3 G27
APFS returns filenames in NFD (decomposed) form, while git stores paths as the bytes the committer's platform produced, usually NFC (composed).
A non-ASCII filename can byte-differ between what gitclient/porcelain's parsers (status, diff-tree, for-each-ref) report and what the watcher reports for the same file, breaking any path comparison, map lookup or cache key that assumes byte-equality.
NFC vs NFD path normalization mismatch is a real, well-documented bug class that other git tools have hit.
Decision: normalize paths consistently at ingestion, from git output and from the filesystem alike, rather than patching each comparison site ad hoc.
The G27 audit must cover every path comparison/lookup across gitclient/porcelain, the watcher, gitsession's caches, and the wire contract.
Before assuming the NFC/NFD issue is unaddressed, check whether upstream's TypeScript ever hit it, because Node on macOS gets the same NFD-from-filesystem behavior.

### v1.3 G28
G28 (v1.3) adds auto-stash on checkout: uncommitted changes never block a branch switch; they are stashed automatically and tagged with their origin branch, instead of requiring a manual stash first.
G28 cross-branch apply: the stash list shows each entry's origin branch, and a stash from a different branch can be applied to the currently checked-out branch via a right-click action.
Merge-tree pop prediction from G17 also applies to cross-branch stash apply, because a cross-branch apply is the case most likely to conflict.
G28 auto-detach on worktree conflict: checking out a branch already checked out in another linked worktree fails with git's hard error "already used by worktree"; instead, HEAD is detached at that branch's commit rather than blocking the user.
G28 adds a global/unscoped stash: a separate bucket for entries not tied to any one branch and meant to be reapplied repeatedly (e.g. a WIP experiment or personal local tweak), distinct from auto-stash.
Git's native stash stack is a single ordered pop-once list with no room for keeping and reusing an entry, so the global stash almost certainly does not use it.
G28 has no upstream design to port; the full design (auto-stash trigger point in G5's checkout pre-flight, cross-apply RPC and conflict-prediction reuse, multi-apply stash storage) is deferred to the phase's own Opus planning pass, the same placeholder-then-design approach as G24/G26.
G28 builds on G17's basic stash and depends on G5 and G17.

### v1.3 G29
Every pre-window log.Fatalf failure site in the Go shell's main.go (config.EnsureLayout, logging.Init, storage.Open, repos.New, the settings read, window list/create) reaches only a log file; no window is created and the user sees nothing, so the app silently fails to launch.
G29 requires each boot failure in main.go to be surfaced as a native OS-level message shown before any window exists, not as a UI panel that depends on a window having opened.
A boot failure occurring after a repository has already opened inside an existing window is handled by G14 D3, not by G29.
The schema_version-newer-than-binary refusal in storage.Open prompted G29, which was then generalized to all pre-window boot failures.
The equivalent refusal in internal/gitreview/migrate.go (review.db schema newer than the binary knows) fires mid-session inside an already-open window as an RPC-level error, not a boot blocker; it is out of scope for G29 unless investigation finds it silently swallowed too.

### v1.3 G33
Phase G33 is a docs-only update scoped to the root README.md and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, plus CLAUDE.md notes; it produces no new code.
The root README.md was silent on the git module: Studio and Api each get a mention, git does not.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md must describe the git module as a real, shipped subsystem (transport, session model, package list), not as absent or a plan.
docs/v1.3/plans/G<N>-*.md files are per-phase historical records, not living docs, and are left as-is by G33.

### v1.3 G34
Phase G34 (v1.3) targets Kira Studio visual-language parity for packages/git-ui: the graph and review panels' main toolbar and all context/right-click menus use bespoke layout, density and styling instead of packages/kira-ui's Kui* components. The user requested it because proportions and design differ from Kira Studio.
The graph/review UX-fixes batch (docs/v1.3/plans/graph-review-ux-fixes.md §7) treated Kira Studio parity as a deliberate non-goal and documented the density split between the .kv-toolbar/density.css workbench scale and the .kv-skin-kira/kira-structure.css scale (G12 D14/G21 D11).
Unifying the two density scales piecemeal, rather than as its own phase, would make the work harder, not easier.
G34 planning must decide which toolbar/menu elements become real Kui* components and which stay bespoke; SearchBox.vue's ARIA combobox already set a precedent for staying bespoke.
G34 planning must determine whether and how the packages/git-ui webview can consume packages/kira-ui's real tokens/CSS given VS Code webview isolation and CSP, including the first-paint budget cost per docs/PERF.md, and how to reconcile the toolbar's and file trees' two density scales without breaking either surface.

### v1.3 G30
Phase G30 is code review round 1 of 3, applying CLAUDE.md's "Code review" convention to the whole v1.3 chapter, not to one phase.
G30 review scope: all of internal/git*, internal/bridge/rpcstream, the migrated apps/kira-studio-vscode extension, packages/git-* wiring, and every design decision in the v1.3 plan file.
Each G30 review round runs three Opus subagents in parallel, one per dimension (architecture/structure/maintainability/security; functional correctness/business logic; performance/resource efficiency), and they report findings only.
After the reviewers report, one sequential Sonnet subagent fixes every finding and commits each fix individually; no findings document survives the round once fixed.
When a review round finds nothing real, say so; never manufacture a finding.
G30 was moved to the very end of the v1.3 chapter (2026-09-09) so review runs last and covers the chapter's final state, including G33's docs edits and G34's new frontend code. It originally sat right after G29, ahead of G33 (docs) and G34 (styling).
Only G30's row position moved; its identifier stays G30, so earlier phase plan docs that point at "G30-G32" as the review rounds remain correct.
G30 depends on G1–G29, G33 and G34.

### v1.3 G31
G31 is code review round 2 of 3: the same three-dimension cycle as G30, rerun independently over the chapter's current state (after G30 fixes) as a fresh look at the whole chapter, not verification of G30's findings.
Review rounds run independently because a fix landed in one round can itself introduce new problems.
A first review pass over 20+ phases of code from as many separate implementing subagents rarely catches everything in one sweep, so review is repeated in rounds.

### v1.3 G32
Phase G32 of v1.3 is code review round 3 of 3, repeating the same review cycle over the chapter's state after G31's fixes.
If G32's review finds nothing real across all dimensions, the chapter's code is closed out; if it finds something, it is fixed the same way as in the prior two rounds.
The review round count is fixed at three and is not extended on the fly, even if round 3 finds something, per CLAUDE.md's "repeat... for as many rounds as asked".
G32 is the true last phase of v1.3, after which the chapter is done.
<!-- /chapter v1.3 -->

<!-- chapter v1.4 -->
## v1.4
Source: docs/v1.4/ at bd0c224688d9. 7 phases.
v1.3 shipped the git subsystem, headless behind a VS Code extension (spec at docs/v1.3/SPEC.md).
v1.4 has no single headline subsystem; it is a set of independent reliability, tooling and feature phases across existing modules.
v1.4 uses fresh phase numbering (P1, P2, …) instead of continuing v1.3's, returning to the P prefix used by v1.1 and v1.2. v1.3 used the G prefix because its chapter was a single subsystem; v1.4 is not, so it does not use G.

### v1.4 P1 Test-suite speed
The v1.4 P1 phase goal is to measure and then cut the wall-clock cost of every test suite in the repo and merge genuine duplication.
The test-speed phase lands first in v1.4 because every later phase pays the suite cost on every loop.
No test-suite duration baseline exists: docs/PERF.md tracks app-level budgets and has never recorded suite duration, so establishing a baseline is the first step.
The Go suite (bun run test:go = go test ./...) has 1596 Test* functions across 259 _test.go files and no t.Parallel() anywhere, so packages run concurrently but tests within a package run serially.
Container fixtures in internal/adapters/testsupport use one container per test binary, memoized by fixture.go and started concurrently by Prewarm.
internal/ipcfixture starts mariadb, mysql, clickhouse, redis, sqs and kafka a second time beside internal/adapters/*'s own six containers in the same ./... run, because a test binary is a process.
Running go test with -race over the full tree takes real minutes, per docs/v1.3/SPEC.md's "Full verification scope" note.
tests/ui/ has 248 tests in 47 spec files, using real WebKit against the real built bundle with fullyParallel; the ui-timing project (4 wall-clock tests, workers: 1) declares dependencies ['ui'], so it waits for all 248 tests before starting.
tests/ui/slick-grid.spec.ts alone takes about 110s, per a comment in playwright.config.ts.
The Bun suites comprise 73 specs in tests/unit/ plus 45 in-source *.test.ts files under packages/ and apps/kira-studio-vscode/.
Other suites: tests/ipc/ has 6 frontend specs, tests/e2e-real/ has 4 specs/6 tests, and apps/kira-studio-vscode/tests/ has 2 Playwright projects.
In .github/workflows/ci.yml, three of four jobs need the checks job, so nothing starts until lint, typecheck, build, test:go and verify:packaging all finish.
CI runs bun run test:go twice: on macOS in checks without Docker and on ubuntu in container-tests with Docker; only darwin && cgo files make the macOS run more than a subset.
scripts/setup.sh regenerates bindings in three separate CI jobs.
test:ipc:fe, test:webview, test:e2e-real and test:matrix run in no CI job.
Merging test coverage is bounded: CLAUDE.md exempts internal/adapters/*/*_test.go from the sparse-unit-test bar and keeps them per-capability, and the P25 general/complete split stays; only genuine duplication there may be pruned.
The 73 + 45 TypeScript specs versus tests/ui/'s 248 tests are the likelier overlap for merging coverage.
The CLAUDE.md rule "implement the whole plan, then test once and fix what's found" exists because the expensive suites are expensive; it is a workaround for what this phase attacks.

### v1.4 P2 Api: import collections from Postman's own local storage
Postman import is file-based: menubar item Import Postman Collection… emits ChannelImportPostman, App.vue switches to Api mode and opens a file chooser, and CollectionsService.Import receives only a path, never the bytes.
Postman import parses the chosen .json with postman.Parse and writes it to kira.db via CollectionsRepo.ImportTree, preserving origin_json for round-trip fidelity (v1.2 P4).
internal/datagrip (v1.2 P25) is prior art for reading another tool's state directly: it locates a JetBrains config directory under the user's home (configdir.go), parses it with Scan, and sends only a path across the bridge.
v1.4 P2 (import Postman collections from Postman desktop's local LevelDB store) was dropped after planning; its plan doc and partial internal/postmanstore implementation were reverted.
Postman's local records are not JSON but Chromium SerializedScriptValue-encoded JS objects, so reading them needs the IndexedDB-over-LevelDB key/value coding scheme, a subset of Blink/V8's value serializer, and a Postman Collection Format v1 to v2.1 conversion.
The LevelDB import was dropped because its complexity (a few hundred lines estimated even when narrowed to plain-data tags) was judged too much for the value of one menubar import action.
go.mod has no LevelDB dependency (github.com/syndtr/goleveldb and github.com/cions/leveldb-cli were added then removed by the revert).

### v1.4 P3 SQL formatting for the remaining SQL editors
The app keeps sql-formatter (15.8.2, already a dependency) as its SQL formatting library rather than swapping it.
The query console Format button splits statements with the same splitter Run all uses and calls formatDialect per statement (views/console/format.ts), via lazy chunk views/console/sqlFormatterEntry.ts (~37 KB gzip, tree-shaken to the five dialects the app speaks).
CodeMirror indentService/indentOnInput over @codemirror/lang-sql was declined as formatter because it only re-indents lines and never re-wraps or normalizes a statement; indentation is not formatting.
Prettier with prettier-plugin-sql was declined because it adds a second formatter runtime whose default backend is sql-formatter, so it adds nothing.
project/SchemaDialog.vue (the per-connection DDL document) mounts CodeMirrorHost with :autocomplete="false", registers no view.format command and has no Format affordance, although the console, gRPC beautify, the menubar Format entry and the global Shift+Alt+F chord exist.
The P3 deliverable is Format coverage for SQL editors that still lack it, starting with SchemaDialog.vue, not a library swap; other SQL-bearing editors in the same state should be audited. This gap remains open.
Bug fix: Format right after typing, then Cmd+Z, wiped the whole query because CodeMirrorHost's external-sync dispatch lacked an isolateHistory annotation, so history()'s newGroupDelay merged it into the typing's undo group.
A fix for the console Format style complaint (sql-formatter's clause-per-line output even for trivial queries) was implemented then reverted at the user's explicit request because it was made without sign-off.
P3 pairs with P4: both close gaps on the same editors, so one pass over SchemaDialog.vue serves both.

### v1.4 P4 SQL editor autocomplete from cached table/column metadata
The data-view filter autocomplete is in views/grid/filterCompletion.ts; FilterToolbar.vue's WHERE/ORDER BY boxes read runtime[tabId].meta.columns, the L1-cached kira:tree:describe result for the relation the tab is open on. These columns are authoritative because they include columns the current projection hides.
The data-view filter autocomplete falls back to the loaded page's own columns when describe() errored, quotes each identifier dialect-correctly only when it needs it, and renders through theme/primitives/completion, not CodeMirror.
The SQL editor's autocomplete lives in views/console/completion.ts and views/console/sqlLanguageService.ts, layered as DDL document first, then cached columns, then bare tree relation names, over @codemirror/lang-sql's schemaCompletionSource/keywordCompletionSource.
state/schemaColumns.ts holds the SQL editor's column cache, filled from control.treeSchemaColumns on the view's own lifecycle hook, and dropped on reconnect and on an explicit tree Refresh.
Never fill the schema column cache from a CompletionSource or on a keystroke; fill it only from the view's lifecycle hook.
The schema column cache is keyed by containerPathFor, which walks the console tab's path back to its last database:/schema: segment.
A console opened at the connection root has path === '' (OperationsPanel.vue's new-console action passes that); openConsoleTab substitutes a real path only for a Postgres connection with a session-remembered Set as default. A root-opened console is a normal case, not an edge case.
For a root-opened console, containerPathFor returns null, so ensureSchemaColumns never fires and cachedRelationsFor returns []; the console falls through to lang-sql's keyword source with no table or column names.
v1.4 P4 decision: give a root-opened console the same cached completion as a container-scoped one, give SchemaDialog.vue's DDL editor completion (P3 touches the same component), and merge the two candidate builders over the same cached column metadata where they genuinely overlap.
Decision: extend the existing completion mechanism rather than build a fresh second completion path, because closing the gap costs far less and a fresh implementation would leave a second path behind.

### v1.4 P5 Git extension: status-bar blame widget
The v1.4 P5 phase adds a git status-bar blame widget showing the commit that last touched the active editor's current line (author, age, summary), with click-through to that commit.
The extension already creates and owns one vscode.StatusBarItem (G10 D16, apps/kira-studio-vscode/src/extension.ts), carrying connection state and G12 D10's debounced in-flight indicator, so status-bar lifecycle, theming and disposal are already solved.
commit.detail (G4) already renders a commit and is the natural click target for the blame widget.
commit.resolvePr (G24) answers which PR a commit belongs to, per-commit rather than per-branch-tip, which is the shape a blame line needs.
A new git-ipc method lands in the surface made of packages/git-ipc's contract plus internal/gitrpc's handler table.
No blame path existed anywhere before this phase: a tree-wide search for blame across apps/, packages/ and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md returns one unrelated hit.
The phase adds a real git blame run through internal/gitclient's streaming runner.
The blame porcelain parser sits beside gitclient/porcelain's existing parsers and is guarded by the committed golden-byte corpus, because it is a case of several interacting rules that CLAUDE.md's testing bar targets.
The new blame contract method requires a CONTRACT_VERSION bump; the version is 33 today.
The extension-side line tracking (active editor, cursor line, invalidation on edit, on save and on repo.changed) has no analogue in the extension today.
Whether blame is computed per-line on demand or per-file incrementally, and how a dirty buffer's line numbers map back to committed lines, are left to the phase's own planning pass.
P5 is a standalone phase because it builds only on infrastructure shipped in v1.3 and is the first git capability needing a genuinely new git command rather than another view over the existing log/graph pipeline.

### v1.4 P6 Visual regression testing across pages and modules
No pixel comparison exists in the repo today; tests/ui/ (248 tests, real WebKit, real built bundle, both wire planes mocked) asserts DOM, geometry and computed styles.
The only screenshot in tests/ui/ is mode-switch.spec.ts's ink measurement (P22 D5), which decodes a PNG and counts ink, with no baseline image.
apps/kira-studio-vscode/tests/layout asserts real getBoundingClientRect() box heights, the answer G16 landed after a shipped build rendered a graph panel visually collapsed to ~75px while its aria-rowcount stayed correct and every check passed.
--kira-font-ui is a system-font stack (-apple-system, Segoe UI, Ubuntu, …) that resolves to a different real face on a macOS dev machine than on the Linux CI runner, so a baseline from one machine is not assertable on another until fonts are pinned.
--kira-font-data is user-customizable by design (P26 D3).
tests/ui/ runs WebKit (WKWebView on macOS, WebKitGTK on Linux) while the extension tier runs Chromium, so two rasterizers are already in play.
