## v1.8
Source: docs/v1.8/ at bd0c224688d9. 25 phases.
The v1.8 chapter holds misc fixes and polish across two existing modules: the API client (incognito mode, input sizing) and the git module (graph rendering/perf, PR integration, diff viewing, code review UX, settings placement, branches/stashes UX, blame, worktrees).
The v1.8 chapter continues P numbering (P71+) rather than taking a fresh letter, because these are unrelated phases rather than one subsystem, as in v1.1, v1.2, v1.4 and v1.6.
v1.7 shipped the database MCP server as one cohesive chapter, specified in docs/v1.7/SPEC.md (M1-M8).
Worktree creation/switching work reuses WorktreeList.vue's existing create/switch machinery rather than adding a new dialog.

### v1.8 P71 API module: incognito mode per tab, 4-row auto-growing inputs (Landed)
The API module has no browser-local storage; apps/kira-studio/frontend/src/state/tabs.ts and api/state/* all write through the bridge to Go/SQLite.
Incognito mode is a per-tab toggle: when on, nothing from that tab's session persists after the tab closes (no history entry, no env/variable writes, no collection changes, no saved request); rendering, running requests and viewing responses work normally; the tab shows a visual incognito indicator.
Incognito is a frontend-only per-tab flag (state/tabIncognito.ts) that filters every tab snapshot before tabsSave.
The Go RPC args HttpSendArgs and GrpcCallArgs have an Incognito bool field that guards response-history recording and internal/oplog persistence; a running incognito op shows live in the Operations panel but is never written.
An incognito tab's active environment is a per-tab in-memory override and does not touch the shared active-env row.
Raw-text inputs auto-grow up to 4 rows before switching to internal scroll, via a CSS grid-replica trick (.p-input.is-grow in primitives.css) behind an opt-in grow prop on TextField.vue and AutocompleteField.vue.
The grow prop is used on params, headers, form-data and gRPC metadata cells, and deliberately not on URL/target fields, Monaco body editors, form-data's content-type cell, or VariableRow's password-type value cell.

### v1.8 P72 Git graph panel: remove repo-switch dropdown, rendering/perf/layout fixes, settings relocation
RepoPicker.vue and its AppToolbar.vue mount point were deleted because Kira Studio's left sidebar and the VS Code extension's one-repo-per-window model already make the repo-switch dropdown redundant.
The git graph's reload on tab focus was fixed by keeping RepoGraphView.vue mounted across tab switches with KeepAlive, instead of tearing down and rebuilding the whole layout.
Checkout misalignment of the git graph was fixed by keeping the graph layout on screen across a refresh instead of resetting it to zero lanes mid-stream.
Scroll flicker in the git graph was fixed by rebuilding row heights when PR badges resolve, calling invalidateRowHeights() alongside invalidateAllRows().
Widening minRowBuffer was found unnecessary once the row-height invalidation fix landed.
ResizeObserver fires reliably under KeepAlive on both Chromium and WebKit (confirmed via a standalone repro), so the fallback MountHandle.refresh() method was not added.
Branch/tag label sizing in the git graph reads off the shared --kv-* type scale so labels match adjacent commit-message text.
The dateFormat and kiraVersion.log.level repo settings moved app-wide, as appearance.dateFormat and advanced.gitLogLevel respectively.
The log.level key remains a source:'repo' schema entry because VS Code's RepoSettingsDialog.vue is its only editing surface there, host-conditional via the MountOptions.host seam; only its instanceWide cross-repo-collapse special case was deleted.
Settings relocation sits last within the git-module row so it does not relocate a setting whose look/appearance behavior the same row may have just changed; P74/P75 depend on stable graph navigation.

### v1.8 P73 Tab-strip icons and markdown reading-view font
TabKindDef.icon is widened to string | { filePath }; only the repo-file tab kind returns { filePath: tab.path }, which TabStrip.vue resolves through the same fileIconStyle() the repo file tree uses, so a file's tab icon matches its tree icon.
The repo-diff tab kind keeps the git-compare icon because it is the only glance-level diff signal given tab-title truncation.
The orphaned repoFileIcon() and its monacoLanguageFor import were deleted.
The repo-map MCP server has a confirmed false negative on non-call reads, so orphaned code is verified by grep; the issue is documented in docs/v1.8/mcp-repo-map-issues.md.
The markdown reading-view font was small not because of its base size (already tied to --kira-font-size, matching Monaco) but because fenced code inherited the :deep(code) --kira-t-sm step-down, and Tailwind preflight zeroes heading font-size with .md-reading never restoring it.
Fenced code in the markdown reading view is sized by a more-specific :deep(pre code) rule at --kira-t-md, overriding the :deep(code) --kira-t-sm step-down.
Markdown reading-view headings h1-h6 use an em-based scale (1.6/1.4/1.2/1.05/1) so they track the Appearance font-size setting rather than a literal size or --kira-t-xl.
repo-workspace.spec.ts's per-language-icon test compares a tab's .tab-file-icon mask-image to its tree row's; markdown-reading.spec.ts's Source/Reading test compares a fenced block's computed pre code font-size to a Monaco .view-line's.

### v1.8 P74 Commit detail, PR integration and diff viewing
The commit-detail 'Show more' defect was clipping: the expanded .kv-detail-pane-meta region was hard-clipped at max-height 50%, with scrollHeight exceeding clientHeight at both 300px and 800px app heights.
The commit detail Refs row gates on the reactive prop detail.decoration.length, not the non-reactive decorationEl.childNodes.length.
The expanded commit-meta region uses flex: 0 1 auto, min-height min(220px, 60%), max-height 70% and overflow auto, so it is bounded and scrollable rather than unbounded.
commit-meta-clamp.spec.ts asserts that after expansion clientHeight grows past the collapsed cap and overflowY is auto, not scrollHeight <= clientHeight, because the harness fixture is deliberately tall.
PR links in BranchPicker.vue, StackList.vue stack badges and refBadges.ts (SlickGrid formatter badge) are buttons calling pr.browserUrl / GitHubService.OpenPullRequestURL, never raw <a href>, so PRs open in the external browser.
The pr.browserUrl change bumped the git RPC contract version from 35 to 36.
Desktop already opened one tab per changed file for 'open commit changes' (hostHandlers.ts:265-291); the defect was pinned: true making every tab permanent.
previewIdByWorkspace was widened to previewIdsByWorkspace (a cohort); every changed-file tab but the first opens with pinned: false and previewCohort set.
Go to file reuses mapLineAcrossDiff verbatim for the live-file drift line remap; repo-file tabs carry an optional rev and are titled with the short rev when set.
Go to file is wired from FileTree.vue's row menu (gated on sha being available) and RepoDiffView.vue's repo.goToFileFromDiff command.
RepoDiffView.vue has no toast/announce channel, so its go-to-file unavailable/failure case falls back to console.warn, a known gap.
tabs.spec.ts is exclusively the Postgres/DB tab system and repo-workspace.spec.ts's gitStreamMock has no graph.stream or commit.detail support, so the tests went into repo-tab-slots.spec.ts plus new repo-file-tab-revision.spec.ts and repo-go-to-file-handler.spec.ts.
rebuildAncestry has an optional budget parameter (default unchanged) so budget-cutoff tests can use a 5-row fixture instead of 50,000 rows.
wireConformance.test.ts does not exist in the repo.
A contract version bump requires regenerating packages/git-ipc/testdata/graphChunkFrame.{bin,json} (via gitsock TestFixtures_CaptureGraphChunkFrame with KIRA_GIT_FIXTURES=write), renaming TestContractVersion_IsNN in internal/gitrpc/stash_test.go, and classifying new methods in internal/bridge/gitstream.go's allowlist.
internal/bridge/gitstream.go's allowlist must classify every gitrpc method; TestGitrpcDispatch_EveryMethodIsClassified catches a missing one, a security-relevant gap.
internal/grpcclient's reflection test is a known flake under full parallel go test runs; it passes standalone.

### v1.8 P75 Review tab: fix navigation, then UX polish
The collapsed-row "Open all changes" failure in the review tab happened because row-action bundles were built per expansion (inside expand()), so a never-expanded ReviewCommitRow had no bundle and read undefined.
The row-action bundle lives at session scope (ReviewSessionState.rowActions), set in setTarget, cleared in clearTarget and reused by expand(), so it is available before a row is expanded.
"Open in graph" was broken because the anchor was a command:kiraVersion.openCommitInGraph URI, a VS Code webview mechanism with no handler in Kira Studio's Wails WebView.
Contract request graph.revealCommit ({repoId, sha} -> {revealed}) is answered locally by both hosts: desktop in hostHandlers.ts (reusing the blame-reveal stash/activate sequence), VS Code in proxyHandlers.ts via graphProvider.runUiAction('revealCommit', …).
Contract version was bumped from 36 to 37 for graph.revealCommit; stash_test.go's TestContractVersion_Is36 was renamed to Is37 and graphChunkFrame.{bin,json} were regenerated with KIRA_GIT_FIXTURES=write.
graph.revealCommit is host-answered (same class as review.open), so it needs no new internal/bridge/gitstream.go allowlist entry.
Run the fixture regeneration and TestGitrpcDispatch_EveryMethodIsClassified in the same commit as a contract change, because stale artifacts otherwise need a follow-up commit.
The comment compose zone was unclickable because in pinned monaco-editor@0.56.0 .view-zones is appended before .view-lines, which is position:absolute with z-index:auto, so it wins hit tests over the earlier sibling.
Both view zones this repo creates (comment compose zone and the error-banner zone's Retry button) use z-index: 10, VS Code's own value for view-zone widgets.
The compose zone's fixed 120px height is only a starting guess, corrected once via a nextTick-deferred scrollHeight measurement of the mounted .review-thread root plus layoutZone, so long comments scroll in their own box instead of clipping.
user-select: text is set on .review-thread because Monaco's .view-lines layer otherwise wins text selection inside the zone.
The FileTree.vue mark-reviewed control is a real input type=checkbox reusing the package's square-checkbox CSS in app-shell.css (P67c D8), replacing the KuiButton icon toggle.
The indeterminate (partial review) checkbox state has its own codicon-dash glyph (\eacc) and fill, sharing every other declaration with :checked, rather than a third color on a two-state control.
The mark-reviewed checkbox sits at the leading edge of the file-tree row, with a same-width empty slot on directory rows, so the tree keeps one aligned checkbox column instead of shifting with the +N/-N counts' width.
The mark-reviewed checkbox uses click.prevent because ReviewFilesState.mark's server answer is its only state; an optimistic native toggle would contradict it during the round trip.
A fully reviewed file no longer gets the whole-line reviewed tint: paint() in both reviewDecorations.ts (desktop) and reviewMarking.ts (VS Code) skips it when coverage({start:1,end:lineCount}, reviewedRanges) === 'full'. Per-hunk glyphs, the partial-review overview ruler and the file tree's checked box are unchanged.
fakeReviewHost.ts scripts review.files (three none/partial/full entries), editor.openAllChanges and graph.revealCommit responses, each recorded onto its own window.__*Calls array like editor.openDiff.
transport.ts's emitUiAction and localEmittersByCodeRepoId are left in place though no longer called, kept as a general-purpose primitive for pushing ui.action from outside a host handler closure; deleting them would cascade into localEmittersByCodeRepoId's other read site.

### v1.8 P76 Git-blame status-bar widget and right-click worktree creation
RepoFileView.vue's blameable check is gitRepoId !== undefined && rev === null, because blame.line always blames the working tree and revision-pinned repo-file tabs (read via file.read) would otherwise show worktree blame against different bytes.
blameLine.ts owns the blame.line request/debounce/cache/cancellation lifecycle; blameAnnotation.ts keeps only the Monaco decoration/hover/reveal-action rendering, driven by a BlameLineController.
In the blame controller, the same-line early return must precede cancelPending, and an aborted request must never be cached as a miss.
BlameLineController exposes gitRepoId and transport so the reveal action can call graph.revealCommit without a second transport lease.
state/blameStatus.ts is an owner-token store mirroring cacheStats.ts/appMetrics.ts, so workbench/ never imports views/repo/ or repo/git/ directly.
RepoFileView.vue creates the blame controller whenever blameable, regardless of the inlineBlame setting; inlineBlame only attaches/detaches blameAnnotation.ts's renderer, so the status-bar item still works with it off.
StatusBar.vue renders the blame item on the left (data-testid blame-status) with author, relative date and subject, and an absolute-date tooltip; clicking it calls graph.revealCommit, with no new contract method.
The createWorktreeHere row action is a plainItem, not gatedItem, because worktreeAdd is not in GATED_OP_KINDS, so it stays enabled during an in-progress operation.
createWorktreeHere is offered in buildRefMenu's branch/remoteBranch arms and buildRowMenu's commit-row menu, never for tags (the tag's commit row already offers the detached item) and never in read-only builders.
WorktreeCreateSeed (state/worktrees.ts) replaces WorktreeDialog.vue's boolean createOpen with createRequest, and a seeded reset watch applies mode/branch/startPoint defaults on open.
Create-worktree seeds: commit row uses mode detach with startPoint sha; local branch uses existingBranch with branch; remote branch uses newBranch with branch+startPoint via localNameForRemoteBranch. BranchPicker.vue handles ref rows identically, forwarded through AppToolbar.vue/WorktreeList.vue's widened create-worktree emit.
The P76 work touched no Go file and needed no contract change or CONTRACT_VERSION bump.
gitStreamMock.ts takes an optional additive third parameter merged into resultByMethod; it is opt-in per call, so existing callers still hang on every method beyond app.init/repo.list/refs.list.
mockRuntime.ts's inferredBootMode() maps a tabsList boot tab kind through TAB_KIND_MODE, where repo-file/repo-graph map to the sentinel 'repo' (not a real AppMode), so seeding a repo-file active boot tab without an explicit windowsEnsure snapshot crashes WorkbenchShell.vue on first render.
In repo-workspace.spec.ts, seed a repo-file boot tab together with an explicit windowsEnsure snapshot (mode 'studio') to avoid the inferredBootMode sentinel crash.
In the revision-pinned blame test, repo.open and blame.line are deliberately left resolvable so the item's absence proves the rev === null guard rather than passing vacuously.
The revision-pinned repo-file tab in the test is seeded with rev set directly via IPC.tabsList, because real navigation paths need graph.stream/file.goToTarget, which are out of the mock's scope.
blame-line-controller.spec.ts covers: same-line in-flight, later line superseding earlier, aborted request never cached, RPC failure cached as a miss, repo.changed scoped to its own repo, and the all-zero sha resolving to 'uncommitted'.

### v1.8 P77 Redesign the branches/stashes dropdown
BranchPicker.vue folds its seven stacked sections into five KuiSegmented tabs (Branches, Tags, Stashes, Worktrees, Stacks) sharing one filter box and one scrollable body.
pickerModel.ts is a pure fold out of the template, following the convention of refListModel.ts, stashListModel.ts and stackListModel.ts.
refListModel.ts is untouched by the P77 redesign because review/BaseSelector.vue and review/ReviewView.vue still call it directly.
The single filter box matches each tab's own row identity, including stash, worktree and stack rows, and every tab's badge shows a live match count computed for all five tabs on every keystroke regardless of the active tab.
Local branches rank HEAD first, then a branch checked out in another worktree, then the rest by committerDate descending; remote branches and the global stash bucket rank by recency alone.
Tags keep naturalCompare ordering, the stash stack keeps its own index order, and Stacks keeps its forest order; none of these three are reordered by recency.
A pinned row is never capped out (capWithPins), and a per-list "Show more" step (capSteps, threaded from BranchPicker.vue) raises only that list's cap.
Opening the branch picker panel focuses the filter box; ArrowDown from it moves into the first row, and ArrowUp/ArrowDown/Home/End/Enter roam the active tab's rows using enabledNeighbour/firstEnabled from @kira/kira-ui.
enabledNeighbour and firstEnabled take MenuItem[] rather than the {id, disabled}[] that rowIds produces, so BranchPicker.vue pads rows with a local toMenuItems() before calling them.
BranchPicker.vue uses a local lastEnabled() for the End key because kira-ui has no such function and enabledNeighbour(items, undefined, -1) does not walk backward correctly from no selection.
The five row-owning children (TagList, StashList, GlobalStashList, WorktreeList, StackList) have a focusedRowId prop only for :tabindex/:data-row-id binding; roving-focus logic lives in BranchPicker.vue via one native keydown listener on the shared scroll container.
Selecting a stash now sets hasSelection (it also checks selectionIsStash), a stashState.selected watch opens the detail pane at a narrow breakpoint, and StashList/GlobalStashList emit selected, closing the picker via the existing closeForCheckout().
Four new UiActionKind members (openTagPicker, openStashPicker, openWorktreePicker, openStackPicker) each open the branch picker on its own tab, replacing eight MUTATING_COMMANDS entries that previously funneled into plain openBranchPicker.
AppToolbar.vue's openBranchPicker expose forwards an optional tab to BranchPicker.open, and App.vue's runUiAction gained four cases for the new picker actions.
The contract version was bumped from 37 to 38 in both validate.ts and Go's contract.go, kept in lockstep; stash_test.go's TestContractVersion_Is38 asserts the Go constant directly, and graphChunkFrame.{bin,json} were regenerated.
toggle() opens the panel through open() so focus-on-open runs on a plain trigger click; the click path passes no tab and keeps the last active tab.
pickerModel.ts wraps a Stacks-tab entry as PickerList<PickerStackGroup> ({summary, branches}) so a stack's base and needsRestack survive alongside its branches into StackList.vue.
fakeGraphHost.ts has a withPickerData option adding opt-in scripted responses for refs.list, stash.list, globalStash.list, worktree.list and stack.list; existing callers keep hanging on every other method.
pickerModel.test.ts has 15 unit cases, and branch-picker.spec.ts covers tab badges, tab switching, match counts following tab switches, focus-on-open and filter/first-row roving-focus boundaries; rowMenuModel, stashListModel and stackListModel tests are untouched.

### v1.8 P78 Code navigation: Cmd/Ctrl+click fix, Go inheritance matching, reference/implementation wiring
Cmd/Ctrl+click cross-file underline/preview did not render because standalone Monaco's StandaloneTextModelService.createModelReference only resolves an already-open model, and goToDefinitionAtPosition's single-result branch only calls addDecoration inside that promise's .then.
Ctrl+click on macOS is not a bug: Monaco's clickLinkGesture picks metaKey under isMacintosh, never both.
apps/kira-studio/frontend/src/views/repo/textModels.ts is installed once in loadMonaco()'s .then before the first editor.create; it resolves tab-owned URIs via mod.editor.getModel and kira-repo URIs with no open tab by reading the file via codeWorkspaceReadFile and routing through getOrCreateModel.
Preview models in textModels.ts are kept in a capped LRU registry that evicts least-recently-resolved models; a preview promoted to a real tab leaves the registry and is not evicted.
Go method receiver types, interface method names and embedded field/interface names are captured by queries/go/p78_method_sets.scm as ordinary reference/symbol rows, with no schema migration.
internal/codegraph/methodsets.go methodSet walks embedding up to a depth cap of 8 with a cycle guard so mutual embedding terminates.
Go implementation matching is seeded by rarestGoMethodName (the wanted method with the fewest FindSymbolsByName rows) and matches by method name only, never by signature.
Go implementation results always have Scoped confidence, never Exact; rule strings are implementationsOf.goMethodSet for own methods and implementationsOf.goMethodSet.promoted for ones completed via embedding.
The resolver has a seventh tiebreak, sameReceiver, which demotes (does not filter) same-receiver-type Go method candidates ahead of basenameMatches, computed only when the reference site is inside a Go method.
codegraph.Site has a Confidence field: Exact for an IncludeDefinition row, the group's own confidence otherwise, RepoWide for a singleton.
codeworkspace nav.go References never early-returns on an empty Sites list, because Total/Truncated/Unattributed can carry a real reading (all occurrences unattributed) even then.
navigation.ts registers reference and implementation providers on the same scheme-scoped selector as the definition/hover providers, and is the sole writer of the state/navStatus.ts store, which StatusBar.vue renders as the nav-status item.
The nav status shows the 'method set, signatures not compared' caveat only when every target's rule starts with implementationsOf.goMethodSet.
Hover markdown shows a 'Receiver-matched (Go)' line, replacing the blanket 'not type-resolved' disclaimer, only when every target resolved via sameReceiver.
RepoFileView.vue and RepoDiffView.vue set gotoLocation.multipleReferences and multipleImplementations to 'peek' while definitions stay 'goto', because the hover already lists every candidate.
Playwright modifier-click tests must press Meta, never Control, because this tier's WebKit reports a Macintosh UA and a Control press would pass vacuously.
Known gap: rarestGoMethodName's seeding can miss an implementer that owns the seeded method only through embedding/promotion; this is the accepted bounded-cost, no-signature-aware, no-type-inference boundary, not filed as an open item in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md.

### v1.8 P79 Code review
P79 code review was a single round, by SPEC.md's explicit instruction, rather than CLAUDE.md's standard two rounds.
Close every review round with a full-suite verification pass; it is mandatory and not part of any fix batch's own scope.
AdvancedSettings.GitLogLevel and AppearanceSettings.DateFormat existed in the settings schema but were never wired to the storage patch structs, repo read/write or downstream, so they were silently no-op; they are now wired end to end.
internal/logging has SetLevel, called by a SettingsService.Set hook; the level was previously hardcoded to slog.LevelInfo, and "off" silences logging via LevelError + 4.
SettingDef.instanceWide was deleted as a dead flag left over from a removed sentinel-row mechanism.
PrBrowserURL (gitsession/gh.go) and the bridge/github.go validator hardcoded github.com, which broke GitHub Enterprise and made the authenticated-host allowlist not a real allowlist for GHES hosts; both now use repo.Host via the shared IsGitHubHost.
VS Code's proxyHandlers.ts now validates PR URL shape via prUrl.ts, which it previously lacked.
linkify.ts renders commit-body URLs as a button calling the generic link.openExternal wire method, which validates scheme/host before opening, instead of a live anchor href that bypassed external-open capability gates; CONTRACT_VERSION went 38 to 39.
goInterfacesSatisfiedBy's reverse-direction search unions candidates over every method name in have, because seeding from the single rarest method name is only sound in the forward direction.
Never write a truncated depth-cap/cycle-guard result to the memo, because it poisons later unrelated lookups; a truncated bool return gates the memo write.
codeworkspace/nav.go's Definitions, Implementations and References share resolveQueryPoint/resolvePosition helpers, which hold the security-relevant symlink-containment guard.
Anonymous-nested-struct-embed over-promotion in methodsets.go is left as a documented limitation.
Always-on blame RPCs regardless of inlineBlame are a deliberate prior design choice and were not changed.
pr.ts rebuildAncestry excludes ancestors of each PR's baseRef, resolved via the new CommitStore.rowOfBranchTip (local decoration scan, no new RPC); previously it had no base cutoff and tagged nearly all loaded history.
CommitGrid.vue coalesces PR ancestry rebuilds through a shared ancestryRebuildPending flag drained once via nextTick, which also fixes newly-loaded rows never getting PR badges.
Preview-cohort eviction in state/tabs.ts batches evicted tabs into one state mutation and one save, and incognito-off now triggers a save.
The pickerModel Stacks tab count uses the same units as capItems (entries/groups) instead of counting branches.
navigation.ts provideReferences/provideImplementation capture the active tab id before await and check it is unchanged afterward, to avoid publishing a stale nav-status after a fast tab switch.
buildPickerModel's expensive filter is split into filterPickerInput (query-gated) and orderAndCapTab (cheap, per-tab), so tab switches no longer refilter when the query is unchanged.
CommitMeta.vue's PR icon is gated on capabilities.openExternal, like every other external-open surface.
The scheduleResize zero-size guard raced the detailOpen-watcher rebuild, handling the same host resize twice and letting invalidateAllRows() detach a DOM node mid-measurement; fixed with a lastRebuiltHostWidth dedup and keying the KeepAlive skip off the graphVisible signal instead of a 0×0 read.
When rebasing a branch containing merge commits, use git rebase --rebase-merges, because a plain git rebase flattens merges and replays batch commits linearly, causing spurious conflicts with already-integrated content.
Several UI tests are flaky under full-suite parallel load but pass standalone: cell-editor.spec.ts, grpc-request.spec.ts, slick-grid.spec.ts spike test, and commit-meta-clamp.spec.ts; they were filed as P81.

### v1.8 P80 Update main docs
Use git rebase --rebase-merges, because a plain rebase flattens merge commits and reproduces spurious conflicts (recorded in docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md).
P76 shipped the blame status-bar readout as a sibling item, not a LAW-14 violation.
The v1.8 docs update documents only externally visible P79 changes: two settings leaves, GHES host support, link.openExternal, the PR-ancestry base cutoff, preview-model LRU refcounting, and the KeepAlive-visibility pause with its resize-race fix. Internal correctness fixes are left out.
Per CLAUDE.md's repo-map process, the next phase waits for a dedicated fix pass to close an open dogfooding entry. docs/v1.8/mcp-repo-map-issues.md has two open non-trivial entries with no fix-pass phase in v1.8, so they stay open rather than folded into the docs-only P80.
Biome does not check .md files; it reports them as ignored, so markdown docs need a manual read for consistency.
The pre-commit hook runs bun run typecheck across all 5 sub-projects.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md covers incognito tabs and the external-open capability (link.openExternal, IsGitHubHost/GHES support).

### v1.8 P81 Fix flaky UI tests
In cell-editor.spec.ts scenario 11, the hardcoded elapsed < 250ms timing assertion was replaced with a structural check: a marker attribute on the Monaco root proves instance reuse across a cell switch.
In cell-editor.spec.ts the editor-text read is scoped to the encoded pane, not the panel-wide helper, because row 3's base64 fixture opens a second MonacoHost (decoded pane) that makes the panel-wide locator strict-mode-ambiguous.
grpc-request.spec.ts drives the 150ms schema-load debounce with page.clock.runFor() via the support/clock.ts helper, installed after app boot.
page.clock.install() alone does not freeze time, and pauseAt(Date.now()) fails because CDP round-trip staleness races into the past; the support/clock.ts helper uses a Date.now() + 10_000 forward buffer instead.
slick-grid.spec.ts sub-row/cross-row mutation counts wait for DOM-mutation quiescence (N consecutive quiet animation frames, mutationsForScroll() in support/grid.ts) instead of a fixed waitForTimeout(300); the fixed wait reproduced the flake (2/3 failed), quiescence held 3/3.
The commit-meta-clamp.spec.ts flake was caused by concurrent startCommitMetaHarnessServer() calls under fullyParallel: true racing on a shared on-disk dist/, where one worker's emptyOutDir wiped files another worker's page was loading.
The commit-meta harness server now builds with write: false and serves from an in-memory Map, eliminating the shared dist/ resource rather than partitioning or serializing it.
The repo's Vite 8.3.0 is rolldown-based and has no RollupOutput export, so the build result is typed as Rolldown.RolldownOutput.
http-curl.spec.ts (400ms curl-preview debounce) and mode-switch.spec.ts (150ms mode-write debounce) use the same fake-clock treatment as the gRPC test.
Never fix flaky UI tests by loosening assertions or lengthening timeouts; tighten them to a real condition (structural check, fake clock, quiescence polling, or fixture isolation), because P81's plan explicitly forbids loosening.
Known pre-existing unrelated failures in bun run test:ui include api-ui-consistency.spec.ts:484, interaction.spec.ts:1144, slick-grid.spec.ts:1529, and budgets.spec.ts's wall-clock perf tripwire.

### v1.8 P82 Git module repo-list row polish: drop the hover close button, expand to switch worktrees
The hover close button (x, .repo-row-close, onRowClose) on GitPanel.vue repo rows was removed, because a repository is imported once and rarely removed or closed this way.
The repo row right-click menu (onRepoContextMenu) lists "Close" (when open) and "Remove" (danger-styled), so closing or removing a repo is still possible without the hover button.
Each repo row in GitPanel.vue has a disclosure control (twisty) that expands it to list that repository's own worktrees inline, and each worktree can be switched to directly from the list.
Worktree switching from repo rows reuses WorktreeList.vue's existing switch/open machinery, which BranchPicker.vue also uses, rather than adding a second worktree-switching path.
The new repo/state/worktrees.ts is a per-repo expansion store. It fetches via the gitTransportFor/ensureRepoOpen/worktree.list shape that blameLine.ts already uses, and refreshes live on repo.changed.
Lease invariant in worktrees.ts: a lease exists exactly while a row is expanded. On release, the lease's transport subscriptions are disposed, and the shared git transport is also disposed (via disposeGitTransport, as closeRepoWorkspace does) only when no workspace has the repo open.
Disposing the shared transport closes the real socket and clears the repoOpenMemo entry, so a later re-expand performs a fresh repo.open.
state/coderepos.ts gained canonicalPath, recordForRoot and openRepoAtPath: clicking a worktree opens the existing repo row or imports the worktree first, with an E_ALREADY_IMPORTED race fallback.
A Playwright test shows that expanding a repo row doesn't open the workspace (the @click.stop guard). Clicking a linked worktree row imports, opens and activates it with its own pinned graph tab.
The Close menu item collapses the row's worktree lease synchronously (§8.3).
GitPanel.vue applies a height cap on .repo-section.has-workspace and uses token-based CSS for the expanded worktree list.
P82 changed no Go file, no packages/ file, no contract and no dependency. It is independent of P77's dropdown redesign and of P81.
The app is a Wails v3 native app (GTK4/WebKitGTK on Linux, not Electron/CDP) with no remote-debugging hook, and repo import goes through a native folder-picker dialog, so GUI passes cannot be driven by a driver.

### v1.8 P83 Git module: an embedded terminal, opened at the current worktree
internal/terminal is a domain package that must not import internal/bridge; layering_test.go enforces this by automatic scan.
internal/terminal uses creack/pty with a Session/Registry design: one reader goroutine per session.
Terminal sessions start with Setsid: true so a process started inside the terminal (e.g. npm run dev) dies with the tab instead of outliving it.
Closing a terminal Session sends SIGHUP to the whole process group, waits a 2s grace period, then sends SIGKILL.
loginShell() resolves $SHELL, falling back to the seventh field of /etc/passwd, then /bin/sh; sessions spawn the shell with -l -i at the caller's cwd, never $HOME.
internal/bridge/terminal.go is the single place that turns internal/terminal's plain errors and callbacks into ipcerr responses and push-channel events.
The terminal tab kind has reuse: false, is never persisted, allows many per worktree, and is rendered by RepoTerminalView.vue using a lazy-loaded @xterm/xterm and @xterm/addon-fit renderer (terminalRenderer.ts).
state/terminals.ts owns the client-side terminal session registry.
TabStrip.vue has a "+" button opening a dropdown of tab-creation options, currently only "Terminal", designed to leave room for more entries.
GitPanel.vue repo-row and worktree-row context menus have "Open terminal" items rooted at that row's own path; they are deliberately not routed through packages/git-ui/rowMenuModel.ts because this is a host-only surface.
A terminalCountAtPath registry drives the .repo-terminal-indicator on any repo or worktree row with a live terminal open at its path.
A batched Go call (gitclient.ResolveHead/RepoHeads) plus repo/state/repoHeads.ts drives a .repo-head branch label on every repo row, collapsed or expanded.
worktreeEntries() in repo/state/worktrees.ts always sorts the main worktree first, at the rendering layer only; wt.isMain stays positional (IsMain: i==0 in internal/gitsession/worktree.go) and is not redefined.
A tab kind's dropResources must be a safe no-op for an id it doesn't own, because otherwise unrelated tab kinds trigger side effects; closeTerminalSession in state/terminals.ts returns early if the tabId is not in byTabId (it previously called TerminalService.Close for every tab kind and broke unrelated specs).
Playwright terminal tests in repo-workspace.spec.ts are wiring tests using a synthesized kira:terminal:data chunk; real shell I/O is covered by internal/terminal/session_test.go, not the UI tier.
apps/kira-studio's Taskfile defines only darwin:* tasks (sign, notarize, darwin:package:dmg) and no Linux build/dev task.

### v1.8 P84 Git panel: fix the duplicate worktree listing, split Repositories and Files into separate tabs
Before P84, opening a worktree via switchToWorktree → openRepoAtPath (state/coderepos.ts) imported it as its own top-level code_repos record, so it rendered twice: nested under its parent repo and as a flat top-level row.
Each worktree keeps its own workspace (file tree, search, review, graph); P82 §5.3 rejected switching the graph in place.
An opened worktree appears only nested under its parent repo (marked open/active there), never in the top-level list, which is reserved for explicitly imported repositories.
GitPanel.vue is one PanelShell with a two-option SegmentedControl ("Repositories" / "Files") in the #title slot; the repo-name title was removed. Repositories shows the repo/worktree list; Files shows the active workspace's Files/Search/Review body, each at full panel height.
The 50% height cap and bottom border of .repo-section.has-workspace were deleted because nothing stacks below the list once Files has its own tab.
gitclient.WorktreeIdentity was extracted from Identify's two rev-parse calls (--absolute-git-dir, --git-common-dir), and Identify now uses it.
CodeWorkspaceService.RepoWorktreeLinks returns one parentId per code_repos row, grouped by git common dir. The anchor is the row that is not a linked worktree, else the smallest (sortOrder, createdAt, id). It is shaped like RepoHeads (errgroup, errors never fail the batch).
repo/state/repoLinks.ts is a session-scoped Map store of worktree parent links. switchToWorktree writes a click-time hint so a newly imported worktree never flashes at the top level; GitPanel.vue filters the top level with !worktreeParentId(r.id).
A repo row's isOpen is true when that row, or a row parented to it, is open. Both context menus offer Rename/Close/Remove on the nested worktree row.
The Files/Search/Review control and icon buttons live in the Files tab as a .view-strip. local.repoSearch and local.fileSearch are split so a filter typed on one tab does not hide rows on the other.
A local tab ref, driven by an immediate watcher on repoId, switches to Files whenever a workspace opens or activates and back to Repositories when the last workspace closes.
onRowClick (P67b) opens or activates a repo on a single click, so Playwright specs use .click() rather than .dblclick() on repo rows; the second click of a dblclick lands on the Files tab's view-strip after the auto-switch.
Opening a terminal from a worktree row's context menu calls openRepoTerminalTab → openRepoWorkspace(repo.id), which triggers the auto-switch to Files, so tests must click git-panel-tab-repos before reading repo row state.
The flash window and the RepoWorktreeLinks error path have no dedicated Playwright coverage, because installGitStreamMock cannot hold a stream open.
GitPanel.vue is desktop-only; packages/git-* and apps/kira-studio-vscode are unaffected by P84.

### v1.8 P85 Tab-strip "+" button: launch Claude Code or a configured custom script in a terminal
The tab-strip + dropdown offers Terminal, Claude Code, one entry per user-configured custom script, and a Manage scripts… entry; all launches open the same P83 terminal tab kind over the same PTY/worktree-cwd machinery.
Custom scripts are entirely user-configured; the list starts empty and none are predefined.
When terminal.OpenParams.Command is non-empty, the PTY runs it as $SHELL -l -i -c <command>, with the command as a single argv element and no quoting layer; an empty command gives P83's plain login shell.
maxTerminalCommandBytes (64 KiB) bounds the terminal command length well under macOS's ARG_MAX, so an oversized command gives a clear E_INVALID instead of an opaque E2BIG.
The initial command's own exit status reaches onExit, not a wrapper shell's; TestSessionRunsInitialCommand guards this because P86 needs it.
terminalTabStateSchema gains command, label and color fields, each defaulted so older persisted records still parse through duplicateState -> parseState.
A terminal tab's title is its label, falling back to the cwd's basename, and its rail colour comes from state.color.
The Claude Code dropdown entry launches the fixed command claude; it is not configurable and not probed for availability. A user wanting flags writes a custom script instead.
Every dropdown entry launches through the TabStrip.vue helper launchInActiveWorkspace(launch?, cwdOverride?), and openRepoTerminalTab takes an optional TerminalLaunch (command/label/color).
Custom scripts are stored in the custom_scripts table (migration 0024), a named, ordered, immediately-mutated list shaped like code_repos, not a settings leaf.
CustomScriptFields.Validate() is the sole validation authority: name and command are required and trimmed, workingDir is empty or absolute, and colour must be a valid palette value. The shared zod schema is only the dialog's affordance.
Every CustomScriptsService mutation broadcasts the full list on ChannelCustomScriptsChanged using Emit (not EmitTo), so a second window's dropdown stays live.
The Settings sections list and Section type live in state/settings.ts, with openSettingsAt(section) so TabStrip.vue can deep-link into Settings without importing the dialog component (workbench/ -> state/ is the permitted direction).
The Scripts settings section sits between Git and Code intelligence and bypasses draft/Save: row fields commit on blur (empty reverts), the colour swatch commits immediately, remove asks to confirm, and the add row stages locally until Add is clicked.
Configured scripts appear in the dropdown behind a separator, showing name, command as hint, and a colour swatch or a play icon when colourless; Manage scripts… opens Settings at 'Scripts'.
A custom script launches at its own workingDir, not the active repo's root.
Script dropdown rows have test id menu-item-script-<script.id>, so a fixture id 'script-1' renders as menu-item-script-script-1.
onAddScript in SettingsDialog.vue trims the new script's name and command before building CustomScriptFields, matching onScriptFieldBlur for existing scripts.
P85 touched no packages/git-* or apps/kira-studio-vscode files.
TestBroker_QueueBoundedAgainstUnlimitedEnqueue in pairing_test.go deliberately never resolves the maxQueueLen goroutines it parks in Broker.Request, so they leak for the rest of the test binary's run; the test proves the queue stays capped, not that requests complete.
Under high system load, leaked goroutines from TestBroker_QueueBoundedAgainstUnlimitedEnqueue plausibly push the internal/gitsock package's total test runtime past Go's default 10-minute test-binary deadline.
The project's CLAUDE.md sets the bar for re-running measurements as a real, concrete question genuinely at stake, not routine reproduction.
apps/kira-studio is a Wails v3 native app with no remote-debugging hook, and its Taskfile defines no Linux build/dev task, so it cannot be launched under Xvfb for a manual GUI pass.
Plan §15.3 records two deliberate Playwright coverage gaps: no real shell running a real command, and no cross-window broadcast coverage.

### v1.8 P86 Claude Code lifecycle hooks, opt-in and user-configured, plus a running-agent-sessions status-bar widget
P86 wires Claude Code lifecycle hooks (SessionStart/SessionEnd/PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Notification/Stop) into Claude Code terminal tabs launched by P85; it is opt-in: a user must enable it from Settings.
Hook configuration is never written into a project's .claude/settings.json; --settings always points at Kira Studio's own generated temp path, and no project file is written anywhere in the phase.
claude --settings is additive: project .claude/settings.json hooks still fire alongside hooks from a --settings-supplied file (verified with two independent SessionStart hooks both firing).
A StatusBar.vue widget shows an icon plus the number of running Claude Code sessions (P85's launched terminals), counted across every open workspace, and is absent rather than showing zero when nothing runs.
P86 depends on P85's Claude Code launch entry; the widget is P87's click target, so P87 follows P86.
TerminalOpenArgs.LaunchKind ('shell' | 'claude-code' | 'script') is validated and threaded through the tab state as an explicit discriminator, never by checking command === 'claude'.
The internal/agenthooks package uses a per-enable 0700 temp dir holding a generated hooks.json, a 0700 shim script and a 0600 unix socket served by stdlib net/http.
The hook listener decodes only nine hook-payload fields, truncates a Notification message to 200 bytes on a rune boundary, and never retains tool_input, tool_response or transcript_path.
AgentHooksService starts/stops the listener behind the claudeCode.hooksEnabled setting, read fresh at every launch; TerminalService.Open adds the --settings flag and env only for a claude-code launch when the listener is running.
Keep launchFor unexported, because an exported method would be bound to the wire by Wails and leak KIRA_AGENT_HOOK_TOKEN to the webview.
Settings has a Claude Code section with an instant-effect toggle and error/path display, placed between Scripts and Code intelligence.
terminal.Registry is the single place the app-wide agent session count exists, because a per-window map would disagree between windows and P87's keep-awake control needs one shared truth.
ChannelAgentSessions broadcasts the live session list with Emit (not EmitTo) on every Registry.OnChange, and TerminalService.AgentSessions() hydrates a late-opened window.
The status-bar count is based on PTY liveness, never counts a script as an agent (OQ-5), and is deliberately decoupled from the hook stream.
Hook events are broadcast via ChannelAgentEvent using Emit because a hook event has no window to address; the receiving window filters by terminalId.
reduceAgentActivity is a pure (prev, event) => next reducer that pairs PreToolUse/PostToolUse by tool_use_id rather than a depth counter, since each hook runs in its own curl process and PostToolUse can arrive before PreToolUse; SessionEnd drops the activity entry.
The status-bar tooltip shows each session's basename(cwd) plus activity text (waiting for you / running <tool> / working / idle), and TabStrip shows an attention dot on a non-active Claude Code tab in the 'attention' phase, cleared by activating it.
A dismissible banner above RepoTerminalView's xterm host shows only on a Claude Code tab with hooks off and the prompt not dismissed; Enable calls setAgentHooksEnabled, never types into the PTY, and applies to the next Claude Code tab; Not now persists hooksPromptDismissed permanently.
OQ-2 (curl shim vs. re-invoking the app binary) and OQ-6's exact truncation length remain open, reversible with a measurement.

### v1.8 P87 Titlebar keep-awake toggle, plus an agent-aware auto-awake setting (off by default)
The titlebar action row in TitleBar.vue is ordered left-to-right: Connections, Operations, Settings, keep-awake, New window (New window moved from leftmost to rightmost).
The titlebar keep-awake button has exactly two states, on and off, toggled by a single click, with no dropdown, popover or three-way menu; it uses a coffee-cup icon with .is-on and aria-pressed for the states.
A separate Settings toggle, claudeCode.keepAwakeWithAgents (default false), keeps the machine awake whenever at least one Claude Code session is running, regardless of the titlebar button state.
Keep-awake is asserted whenever the titlebar button is on, OR the agent-aware setting is on and at least one agent session is running; the two sources compose additively.
On macOS keep-awake spawns/kills the child process caffeinate -i -s -w <pid> rather than calling IOPMAssertionCreateWithName/IOPMAssertionRelease via cgo, because Wails has no power-management API, caffeinate wraps the same IOKit API, and direct cgo glue would be unmaintainable and untestable in the sandbox.
Keep-awake is macOS-only; every other OS uses a documented no-op driver.
A separate "keep display awake" sub-toggle (caffeinate -d) was deliberately not included because it goes beyond what was asked.
internal/keepawake's Controller holds a set of named reasons (ReasonManual/ReasonAgent), not a refcount; it acquires only on empty→non-empty and releases only on non-empty→empty, so re-asserting an already-held level is a no-op.
NewPlatformDriver() switches on runtime.GOOS without build tags (mirroring internal/gitclient.NewPlatformLocator): noopDriver everywhere except darwin, where caffeinateDriver is used.
Keep-awake self-heals without a timer: when the reaper goroutine's loss callback flips internal state to false, the next Set/Rearm call (any reason) recomputes and re-acquires.
Resume from sleep is detected via shell.AttachSystemWake, modeled on AttachReopen, registering events.Mac.ApplicationDidWake, so the assertion is re-armed because a live caffeinate process doesn't reliably survive sleep/wake.
The agent-aware setting is stored through all three layers: packages/shared/domain/settings.ts, internal/storage/model/settings.go and internal/storage/repos/settings.go.
bridge/keepawake.go's KeepAwakeService composes the manual titlebar state and the agent-aware setting (gated on AgentSessions()'s running count) onto one Controller, and broadcasts every change on ChannelKeepAwake via Emit, never persisted across relaunch.
Frontend state/keepAwake.ts hydrates and subscribes, mirroring the agentHooks.ts/agentSessions.ts pattern; the agent-aware checkbox in SettingsDialog.vue sits beside the hooks toggle in the Claude Code section.
Controller has a Supported() bool forwarding to the driver, so bridge/keepawake.go can report supported: false on non-darwin.
recomputeAgent() in the keep-awake bridge always re-reads Deps.Repos.Settings.GetAll() fresh.
Verifying the keep-awake assertion with pmset -g assertions is a manual macOS-only step and is not faked in tests.
internal/keepawake tests use a fakeDriver that fails on double-acquire or premature release, a -race concurrent test, an argv golden test (caffeinateArgv), and a process-lifecycle test that polls for ESRCH because the sandbox's minimal init reaps slowly.
The root-level layering test TestDomainPackagesDoNotImportBridge covers the internal/keepawake package, so it must not import the bridge.
The internal/keepawake package cross-compiles for darwin/arm64 with CGO_ENABLED=0 (both go build and go vet).

### v1.8 P88 App-wide checkbox glyph alignment
The off-center checkbox checkmark is not a CSS mistake: the @vscode/codicons check and dash glyph artwork is itself off-center within its 16x16 icon tile. check's ink center is at y=7.495 (about 3% high) and dash's at y=8.495 (about 3% low); true center is 8.
Both checkbox implementations (Checkbox.vue's .p-check .glyph and git-ui's input[type=checkbox]::after) center the glyph tile with flexbox, which reproduces the tile's internal bias instead of correcting it.
The glyph fix is an em-based translateY nudge: +0.03125em for codicon-check and -0.03125em for codicon-dash (0.5/16), so it scales with font-size at both packages' glyph sizes (10px/11px).
The fix is applied in two places, apps/kira-studio/frontend/src/theme/primitives.css (.p-check .glyph.codicon-check/.codicon-dash) and packages/git-ui/src/theme/app-shell.css (checkbox :checked::after/:indeterminate::after), because the checkbox styling is not shared code.
git-ui re-implements checkbox styling itself because it cannot reach the host app's CSS (VS Code webview parity, P67c).
In real WebKit at 14px, the check glyph's vertical offset was -0.31px (kira-ui) and -0.34px (git-ui) before the fix, and 0.0px on both axes after for both glyphs and implementations; Chromium showed the same direction.
A fresh worktree lacks the gitignored Wails-generated apps/kira-studio/frontend/bindings/, so typecheck of changes touching apps/kira-studio/frontend needs them generated via wails3 task common:generate:bindings (pinned wails3 v3.0.0-beta.21 from go.mod).
DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md's scoped-typecheck workaround for missing bindings covers only git-only changes, not changes touching apps/kira-studio/frontend.

### v1.8 P89 Studio: FK preview action layout, SQL format-button regression
In the FK preview popover (FkPreviewPopover.vue), the layout is header/actions/body flex, and only the body (.fk-preview-body) scrolls, so "Open in new tab" stays visible under the header regardless of previewed row width.
The FK preview popover has no "Edit this record" action; the cell context menu's "Edit referenced row" item (fkEditItem in menu.ts) is the sole entry point for editing a referenced row.
ConsoleView.vue's localDoc is a shallowRef, and Vue skips the dep trigger when the assigned value equals the current one, so after format-undo-format the second identical result was a silent no-op and Monaco kept showing the unformatted text.
MonacoHost's props.doc watcher body is factored into applyExternalDoc/setDoc (with the doc === model.getValue() guard); ConsoleView's tab.state.text watcher calls it directly and updates lastEmitted there. ConsoleSavedMenu.vue's setText goes through the same watcher.
The "(ClickHouse identifiers)" aside is omitted from the format note on non-ClickHouse consoles.
The Format button's keywordCase 'preserve' (P13 D4) only touches whitespace by design, and the "Already formatted" note fires when result.text equals originalText byte-for-byte.
A regression test in console-format.spec.ts (format→undo→format) fails against the pre-fix source and passes after the fix.
tests/ui/interaction.spec.ts's P67 case 3 drives the cell-menu "Edit referenced row" item instead of the removed popover edit button.

### v1.8 P90 Api module: configurable request settings, a Cookies tab, and a broken multi-line value editor
The Api module has seven request settings, each configurable globally in Settings and per request (overriding the global default): httpVersion (1 or 2, default 2), requestTimeoutMs (default 0/none), maxResponseMb (default 50), sslVerify (default on), followRedirects (default on), maxRedirects (default 10), disableCookieJar (default on).
Go's httpclient.Send takes an Options struct (all pointer fields, nil means default) instead of reading package constants.
resolveSendOptions/orGlobal in bridge/http.go resolve a request's seven null-means-inherit tab-state leaves against the global settings row before every send.
Setting sslVerify to false shows a plain-prose security warning taken verbatim from the plan, per a CLAUDE.md carve-out for that class of text.
Cookies use one process-wide cookiejar.Jar keyed by publicsuffix, and incognito sends use a throwaway per-send jar.
Response has SentCookies and ReceivedCookies fields, populated from the trace's WroteHeaderField hook and every hop's own Set-Cookie.
Both request and response Cookies panes reuse one CookiesPane.vue component. Request mode shows what the jar would send next, debounced on URL change and after every send. Response mode shows sent/received cookies grouped by hop.
Secrets in the new SentCookies and ReceivedCookies response fields are masked by maskSecrets, through two new loops, like every other response field.
Grow-field multi-line bug, first cause: the UA padding of 2px on the textarea was absent from the sizing replica. Fixed as the plan described.
Grow-field second defect: a grid item's normal alignment resolves to start, not stretch, for a replaced box with an intrinsic size in that axis (a textarea counts via its rows attribute). The textarea stayed at 1-row height and scrolled from row 2. Fixed with an explicit align-self: stretch.
The overlay never painted on a grow textarea because the selector .input-wrap input.has-overlay did not match a textarea. It was widened to input.has-overlay, textarea.has-overlay, and line-height: inherit was added so overlay lines do not drift from the textarea's lines past line 2.
The Playwright grow-field test reads line boundaries from the overlay's own Range client rects, not a hard-coded character count.
The container has no display (docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md), so the Playwright ui project is the repo's established substitute for GUI-driven checks.

### v1.8 P91 New module: Terminal — quick-command scripts panel and tabbed terminal sessions
The Terminal module is a fourth top-level module (AppMode 'terminal'), placed last in MODE_ORDER, with async TerminalPanel.vue and TerminalStart.vue; Go validWindowModes includes it.
tabs.ts hydrateTabs skips non-repo workspaces via !isRepoWorkspace(key) instead of an enumerated studio/api skip-list, so a bare 'terminal' workspace survives session restore.
Go DefaultCwd returns the user home directory (os.UserHomeDir), or an empty string on failure so it never fails boot; it is hydrated once at boot into terminalDefaults.cwd and used by the Terminal module's unscoped launches.
openTerminalTab was factored out of openRepoTerminalTab (state/terminalTabs.ts) so a terminal tab can open under the bare 'terminal' workspace without the openRepoWorkspace side effect, which would otherwise silently jump into Git; openRepoTerminalTab delegates to it.
TabStrip.vue uses one wrapper element whose testid switches on tab count, so the Terminal module's empty initial state (no pinned graph tab) still shows the "+".
The Terminal module's "+" menu (terminalModuleMenuItems) deliberately omits Claude Code and per-script entries, since the left panel already covers scripts; it lists Terminal plus one entry per known repository/worktree, each opening a scoped terminal within the Terminal workspace.
Every quick-command and scoped launch from the Terminal module carries workspaceId 'terminal' and never redirects into a matching repo's Git workspace.
TerminalPanel.vue is a second view over P85's existing custom_scripts store, not a separate module-scoped list; quick commands therefore also appear in a repo workspace's "+" menu. It offers search, inline add, run-on-click, and a Run/Edit…/Remove context menu.
The panel's "Edit…" deep-links to the Settings dialog's Scripts section rather than re-implementing four fields in a 180-480px panel.
The terminal-panel testid must sit on an outer wrapper, not in PanelShell's #body slot, because that slot renders only when non-empty and the testid would not mount with zero custom scripts.
The Terminal module reuses P83's PTY/terminal-tab machinery (creack/pty backend, xterm.js frontend) and is deliberately simple: no split panes, no saved session state beyond the existing terminal tab kind, no per-script argument prompts; a quick command runs its exact configured command.
The Terminal module's quick-command list starts empty with no predefined entries, and clicking a script runs it in a new terminal tab.
The http-request-body.spec.ts assertion "every IPC call stays under 500 bytes" fails deterministically, likely because P90's per-request Api-settings payload (7 new override leaves sent with every httpSend) made the threshold stale; not a P91 regression.

### v1.8 P92 Git panel: graph column/scroll/labels, titlebar new-window button, diff tabs, review nav, go-to-file, tab-persistence...
The pinned repo-graph tab's creation path left path unset, tripping the DB's NOT NULL constraint on the first write; it now gets a real (empty-string) path.
When a git graph row's height changes after first render (e.g. a late decoration chunk), every row below must be repositioned via invalidate(), not updateRowCount() + render(), which never repositions already-cached rows.
The git graph column has a drag handle and a capped default width (DEFAULT_GRAPH_LANE_CAP, 6 lanes/95px) instead of growing unbounded with branch count; PersistedViewState is at v7.
Ref/tag badges in the git graph render as an outline (colored border, transparent fill) instead of a filled chip.
General settings include a control for git-graph font size.
The title bar has a "New window" button before the project-panel toggle, which calls WindowsService.OpenNew.
Repos, Files and Review are three peer top-level tabs in GitPanel, not Review nested inside Files' own Files/Search strip.
The diff view has a visible go-to-file action that reuses P74's existing open-and-land-on-line path.
"Open all changes" on a commit opens one repo-multi-diff tab with per-file collapsible sections, and only the expanded section's editor mounts, instead of one tab per changed file.
SlickGrid's _columnDefaults carries a hidden minWidth: 30 merged onto any column declaring none, clamping narrower seeded widths up. graphColumnWidth(0) = 17 at mount was inflated by 13px, causing the graph's horizontal scrollbar sizing bug.
Graph columns in columns.ts declare minWidth: 0 (not undefined) on all four columns, because updateColumnProps() clamps only when m.minWidth is truthy, so only a falsy value disables it.
The loadedRows watcher calls scheduleResize(), deduped via lastRebuiltWidth, as a defensive fix for platforms whose scrollbar consumes layout width.
graphStreamFixture.ts builds a real graph.stream chunk with the actual @kira/git-ipc FlatBuffers codec, and gitStreamMock.ts reassembles the blob-frame wire format in-page, because gitStreamMock.ts previously only answered unary req/res and not streamed binary chunks.

### v1.8 P93 Git graph: collapse non-checked-out branches by default, branch-ordered commit layout
By default the git graph renders fully expanded, commit by commit, only the checked-out branch's own direct-ancestor chain.
Every non-checked-out branch in the git graph collapses to two visible points, where it starts (diverges from what's shown) and where it ends (its own tip); intermediate commits hide behind a single clickable expandable row. Clicking it expands that branch's own intermediate commits inline.
The git graph layout engine (layoutStore.ts/geometry.ts) currently assumes one row per commit unconditionally, so collapsing must be reconciled with lane assignment and graph-line layout.
The git graph currently interleaves commits from different branches by timestamp (standard git log --graph order).
The git graph is to switch to branch-ordered layout: all commits of one branch's own chain render consecutively before the next branch's commits, so the user never sees commits alternating between branches. The checked-out branch's own commits render first at the top, followed by each other branch's own commit group.
Likely default ordering for non-checked-out branch groups is most-recently-active branch first; the planning pass must state it with a reason.
Graph incremental/streamed history loading (graph.stream, chunked) and lane-column rendering currently assume rows arrive in commit-timestamp order; branch-ordered layout interaction must be confirmed in planning.
The collapse and branch-ordering changes (phase v1.8 P93) touch packages/git-ui/src/graph/ (layoutStore.ts, layout.worker.ts, rowSvg.ts, geometry.ts, graphColumn.ts) and CommitGrid.vue's row model.
The collapse and branch-ordering changes land together as one phase because both concern how a non-checked-out branch's commits are grouped and shown.
Phase v1.8 P93 needs its own dedicated planning pass before implementation. Planning must design the collapsed row visuals, expand state scope (per-branch, per-session vs persisted per repo), and collapsing of sub-branches.

### v1.8 P94 Repo-wide code-quality tooling: complexity, dead-code and performance linters, pre-push hook, CI wiring, fix every fi...
Frontend/TS/Vue cognitive-complexity check is Biome's complexity/noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity, built into @biomejs/biome 2.5.13; Go complexity uses golangci-lint's gocognit and gocyclo.
knip is used instead of ts-prune for unused exports/files/dependencies in TS/Vue packages, because ts-prune is unmaintained and its README points at knip; for Go, golangci-lint's unused linter covers dead code.
scripts/install-golangci-lint.sh builds golangci-lint from source against the repo's own Go toolchain with GOTOOLCHAIN pinned, because a prebuilt release binary is built with an older Go than go.mod's go 1.27.1 and refuses to run.
.golangci.yml enables 11 linters: bodyclose, copyloopvar, gocritic (performance tag only), govet, ineffassign, makezero, prealloc, unconvert, unused, plus gocognit and gocyclo at min-complexity 30 with _test.go excluded.
golangci-lint errcheck (220 findings) and staticcheck (49 findings) were declined in P94 by name and opened as P95 rather than silently dropped.
golangci-lint is configured with uniq-by-line: false, because the v2 default (true) hid 28 of gocyclo's 32 findings behind a gocognit finding on the same func line.
Biome performance rules enabled: noAccumulatingSpread, noDelete, noBarrelFile, noReExportAll.
mockStreamBrowser.js's matchKey() sets key = undefined instead of using delete in a loop, since JSON.stringify drops undefined values identically.
knip.json has one workspace block per package/app, and @types/vscode, @vscode/codicons, @vscode/vsce and tailwindcss are in ignoreDependencies because each is resolved through a non-static-import mechanism.
knip treats packages/git-core/src/search/differentialRunner.ts as an entry because it is invoked as a bun run subprocess from apps/kira-studio/internal/gitsearch/differential_test.go.
knip treats scripts/demo-dbs/sqlite/seed.ts as an entry because it is a manual, README-documented script with no package.json wrapper.
git-core's src/index.ts re-exports every port and setting directly from source, so the ports/index.ts and settings/index.ts barrels were redundant and deleted.
knip.json sets rules.duplicates to "warn" rather than the default "error", because most duplicate-export findings are deliberate documented same-value exports across unrelated subsystems; the reason is recorded inline in knip.json.
lint:dead is plain knip with no --include filter, so it also gates exports, types and binaries.
.githooks/pre-push runs go build ./..., bun run lint:go and bun run lint:dead, guarded on node_modules existing; package.json gained scripts lint:go, lint:dead and lint:all.
.github/workflows/pr.yml cannot be pushed from the sandbox, so the CI change is a patch at docs/pending-changes/.github__workflows__pr.yml.patch, per docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md's workaround.
Pass 1's gocritic/unused/prealloc/copyloopvar fixes did not change complexity scores, because they do not add or remove branches.
Shared relational read/mutate helpers were hoisted into internal/adapters so the three SQL adapters consume one copy.
(*Router).ForConn Request dispatch in gitrpc uses a package-level requestHandlers map[string]requestHandler lookup table, because every arm already forwarded to an existing handler, so a table clears the gocyclo score with less code moved.
TestGitrpcDispatch_EveryMethodIsClassified's AST walk recognises both switch method statements and map[string]XxxHandler{...} composite literals, so it does not undercount the dispatched-method set.
A table-driven test for StripSQLComments was added in pass 2, as it had no direct test before.
GOOS=darwin cannot be cross-compiled from the sandbox container: internal/gitclient's unconditional cgo FSEvents file and internal/codeparse's tree-sitter cgo binding fail, and Wails does not cross-compile for main.
main has no test files; its boot proof is test:ui's real backend built with -tags server, the established substitute for a GUI boot check in a display-less sandbox.
http-request-body.spec.ts has a known pre-existing flaky form-data payload threshold assertion (expect(size).toBeLessThan(500), received 657) that reproduces in isolation and is owned by pass 4.
test:ui can fail tests such as tree.spec.ts (120s timeout) under 4-worker parallel runs due to resource contention; they pass alone, so they are flakes, not regressions.
To tell resource contention from regression in test:ui, re-run the failing test alone with --project=ui --workers=1.
Biome complexity/noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity is enabled at maxAllowedComplexity 30; an overrides entry for test globs (**/tests/**, **/test/**, **/*.test.ts, **/*.spec.ts) exempts test-only functions.
The Biome complexity rule fires only on exceeding 30, so a function scoring exactly 30 (rowPlan.ts's projectLayoutInput, a split phase of buildRowPlan) passes; beautify.ts:43's parseJsonString stays under the gate at 29.
The knip baseline after the complexity refactors is 73 exports plus 50 types, 123 findings; the delta from the earlier 122 is InlineBlameAttachParams, a type introduced by the useInlineBlame.ts extraction.
knip.json sets ignoreBinaries: ["go"] because go is the system toolchain invoked by six package.json scripts, not an npm bin, and dropping --include turned on knip's binaries rule.
Knip triage found no test-only-helper exemption and no intentional-public-API category; the fixes were 105 keyword-drops, 15 re-export-list prunes and 3 deletions.
Three unused exports were deleted outright: findVariableSetTab in api/tabs.ts, gutterWidth in graph/rowSvg.ts, and tabsForMode in state/mode.ts. The last two had zero callers anywhere, as Biome's noUnusedVariables flagged once export was removed.
Removing the re-export from curl/parse.ts left tokenize.ts's CURL_WARNING_KINDS unused, so it was fixed in commit 24 with its doc comment pointing at index.ts's real re-export path.
knip's ignoreExportsUsedInFile is declined because it would clear the 105 keyword-drops with one config line but blind future passes to genuine dead public surface.
The 4 remaining knip configuration hints, which ask for .vue/.css compiler registration, stay declined: .vue imports already resolve without it, and honoring them would require converting knip.json to knip.ts for no functional gain.
A table-driven test for lintMongoBrackets was added (commit 7), raising unit tests from 1538 to 1543.

### v1.8 P95 Go error-handling and correctness linters: `errcheck` and `staticcheck`
P94 pass 1 enabled nine golangci-lint linters and declined errcheck (220 findings) and staticcheck (49 findings) by name, because they are error-handling/broad-correctness work outside the complexity/dead-code/performance categories P94 scoped itself to; reason documented in docs/v1.8/plans/P94-code-quality-tooling.md §4.1.
Each of the 269 errcheck/staticcheck findings needs a per-call judgement, not a mechanical rewrite: for errcheck, handle the error, log it, or explicitly discard with _ =; for staticcheck, whatever its per-finding rule calls for.
Phase P95 plans by grouping the findings by package/pattern, enables both errcheck and staticcheck in .golangci.yml, and states the fix judgement for each group before implementing.
A deferred fix becomes its own named phase, not a line in a result section (CLAUDE.md); P94 named errcheck and staticcheck in its declined-linter table instead of silently dropping them.

## v1.9
Source: docs/v1.9/ at bd0c224688d9. 57 phases.
The v1.9 chapter continues the running P numbering from P96 (v1.8 covered P71-P95) rather than taking a fresh letter.
v1.9 drops the repo-map subsystem.
v1.9 adopts a frontend library baseline of Pinia, TanStack Query, VueUse, shadcn-vue and Tailwind, and migrates onto it.
The git module and native code workspace are extracted into a standalone app, Kira Space (P100).

### v1.9 P96 Finish P94's pass 4: full-suite flake sweep
The http-request-body.spec.ts 500-byte bound failure was deterministic, not a flake: openTab's unconditional saveNow() fires when new-request-start opens the tab, and that first tabsSave serializes to 657 bytes, over the stale bound.
The http-request-body.spec.ts fixture is 4 MiB and every logged call is bounded against PICKED_FILE.file.size instead of a magic number, so schema growth (httpRequestTabStateShape outgrew the 500 constant) cannot erode the bound again.
The internal/grpcclient deferred reflection failure is not a port race: v1 negotiation always answers a well-formed Unimplemented in under 1ms, but in the v1alpha fallback (the second RPC) grpc-go's HTTP/2 transport occasionally ends the stream with a bare io.EOF (codes.Unknown) under scheduling pressure.
retryOnTransportGlitch wraps both v1's and v1alpha's reflection round trip and retries a bare transport glitch up to 4 times with a 50ms delay; the delay is load-bearing because an immediate no-delay retry did not lower the failure rate.
A client-side WaitForReady-only fix only roughly halved the grpcclient reflection failure rate (13/8109 vs 26/8226), so the retry approach was chosen instead.
The grpcclient test assertion is correct: a real schema error must still surface as CodeSchema, confirmed by mutation check.
startStubReflectionServer originally had no readiness wait at all, a real gap fixed in fc1d32f regardless of the root-cause finding.
The Biome useImportType finding in RequestSettingsPane.vue is a template-blind false positive: the imported constants are used as values only inside <template>, which Biome does not analyse, so applying its fix would erase the import at runtime and break the v-for/:min/:max bindings.
biome.json's **/*.vue override turns off noUnusedImports, noUnusedVariables and useImportType, because Biome cannot see template usage; this replaced 12 per-line biome-ignore comments across 7 files.
The UncommittedChangesStrip.vue:49 Biome finding was a genuine useShorthandFunctionType hit and was fixed in code (ace58f0), not suppressed.
markdown-reading.spec.ts's pre-toggle scroll snapshot used expect.poll(...).not.toBe('0px'), which checks non-zero rather than settled; it now uses an in-page stability loop of six consecutive quiet frames, the same idiom as mutationsForScroll in support/grid.ts.
http-request-body.spec.ts's XML token-colour check called hasTokenColor() one-shot right after a language switch and text insert, racing Monaco's asynchronous tokenization; it is now wrapped in expect.poll.
Playwright skips ui-timing's 4 tests when a ui test fails because ui-timing depends on ui; they pass in an isolated --no-deps run, so this is a reporting artifact, not a coverage gap.
The chapter continues P numbering (P96+) rather than taking a fresh letter; P96 carries over v1.8's last open item (P94 pass 4, a full-suite flake sweep with no new lint rules).

### v1.9 P97 Drop repo-map/tree-sitter code intelligence; Biome lints Vue files
P97 removed the repo-map MCP server and its tree-sitter code-intelligence engine: internal/repomap, internal/codegraph, internal/codeparse, cmd/kira-repo-map, the frontend nav surface, the Settings Code intelligence tab, RepoMapService, code_repos.mcp_enabled, the codeIntel.mcpServerEnabled setting and migration 0025.
In-app code navigation (go-to-definition, hover, F12) was explicitly dropped by instruction and not replaced.
internal/mcpinstall and internal/mcpauth remain because both are internal/dbmcp's dependencies (bearer-token install flow, auth middleware), never repo-map's.
EnumerateAll moved from internal/codeindex to internal/codeworkspace/enumerate.go because ListFiles and Search still call it for repo-wide file listing.
internal/codeworkspace/textpos.go (LineIndex/utf16Units) was kept because search.go depends on it for column computation and it has a correctness test.
Biome lints .vue files via html.experimentalFullSupportEnabled on, with html.formatter.enabled off to avoid whitespace churn across 126 files from an experimental formatter.
The biome.json **/*.vue override turns off three rules: a11y, useVueHyphenatedAttributes and noNonNullAssertion. The reasons are recorded in commit d64808b's message, because biome.json cannot hold comments.
useVueHyphenatedAttributes is off because Vue does not re-camelize aria-*/data-* attributes to a component's declared prop (e.g. KuiSegmented.ariaLabel), so the rule's fix stops matching the prop and vue-tsc reports a missing required prop.
noNonNullAssertion is off for Vue templates because templates do not narrow types across separate calls of a v-if-guarded expression, so the rule's ?. suggestion yields '| undefined' against a required type and vue-tsc rejects it.
255 lint/a11y findings across 85 files are deferred to row P105, added after P99, because they concentrate in the dialogs, menus and pickers that P99 replaces.
This Biome version's Vue support does not link scope across the plain script block and script setup block of one SFC, so functions declared in one and used in the other are falsely reported as unused. RepoTerminalView.vue's loadTerminalRenderer and TreeHost.vue need a targeted biome-ignore.
In StashList.vue and StashDialog.vue, the stash@{N} label stays string concatenation with a biome-ignore, because a template literal puts two closing braces before the mustache's }} and this Biome Vue parser reads that as its delimiter, causing a parse error.
P97 added four biome-ignores (TreeHost.vue, RepoTerminalView.vue, StashList.vue, StashDialog.vue); SlickGridHost.vue and ConsoleSlickGrid.vue have pre-existing unrelated noExplicitAny ignores.
frontend/bindings/** is entirely gitignored (apps/kira-studio/.gitignore), so regenerating bindings produces no commit.
The 'repository-wide search has no include/exclude filter' Known-open-item stays in the docs because it is still true after P97. CLAUDE.md's standing rule keeps that section only while genuinely open.
After P97, bun run test:ui has 311 tests in total.

### v1.9 P98 Add shadcn-vue, Tailwind CSS, VueUse, Pinia, TanStack Query as standing deps; codify Vue conventions in `CLAUDE.md`
Every future phase's planning pass must adapt its design to lean on shadcn-vue, Tailwind CSS, VueUse, Pinia and TanStack Query rather than hand-rolling equivalents; e.g. new UI styling goes through Tailwind rather than custom CSS even if the spec didn't say so.
Every Vue component must use script setup lang="ts" (Composition API only, no Options API); this is codified in CLAUDE.md.
Pinia stores follow single-responsibility: one store, one concern, never a grab-bag app-wide store.
P98 is prep only: it adds the libraries and minimal bootstrap plumbing, migrating no existing .vue file, store or styling; P99 does the migration onto the already-wired libraries.
state/pinia.ts exports the single createPinia() instance, and main.ts registers Pinia and the TanStack Query plugin before directive/mount.
state/queryClient.ts exports one QueryClient with retry: false and refetchOnWindowFocus: false, because every query resolves over the Wails bridge to the local Go process, never the network.
The @ alias maps to ./src in both vite.config.ts resolve.alias and tsconfig.json compilerOptions.paths; existing source keeps relative imports, and @/ is for shadcn-vue's generated import specifiers.
shadcn-vue is configured with reka-ui base, nova style, neutral base color, lucide icons and CSS variables; files are components.json, src/lib/utils.ts and src/theme/shadcn-bridge.css.
shadcn-bridge.css maps every shadcn-vue variable to an existing --kira-* token, and base.css imports it after ./vscode-bridge.css.
There is no .dark block in the shadcn bridge because tokens.css is a single :root and index.html hardcodes class="dark".
The icon dependency resolved by shadcn-vue's lucide registry is @lucide/vue 1.47.0, not lucide-vue-next.
src/lib/utils.ts uses a single combined clsx import in the repo's style, with an explicit : string return type on cn().
knip.json has ignoreDependencies entries for @vueuse/core, reka-ui, class-variance-authority, tw-animate-css, @lucide/vue, clsx and tailwind-merge, plus a workspace ignore for src/lib/utils.ts.
Pinia 4.0.3, @tanstack/vue-query 5.103.2 and @vueuse/core 15.0.0 were added to root package.json; VueUse needs no bootstrap.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md Stack table records the frontend library baseline (Vue/Vite/Tailwind v4/shadcn-vue on Reka/Pinia/TanStack Query/VueUse); P99 will migrate the app's 39 reactive() modules and 200 .vue files onto them.
shadcn-vue CLI 2.8.2 does not support --dry-run, --diff or --view.
Never unset HTTPS_PROXY to work around shadcn-vue 2.8.2 init failing with TypeError: fetch failed (it passes undici's ProxyAgent into ofetch calling the runtime's global fetch); see docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md.

### v1.9 P99 Part 1 shadcn-vue component set; every store on Pinia; every ad hoc fetch on TanStack Query
The P99 plan is docs/v1.9/plans/P99-vue-migration.md; scope is the 143 .vue files under apps/kira-studio/frontend/src plus the 45 reactive state modules beside them, not packages/git-ui (46 files) or packages/kira-ui (11).
packages/git-ui and packages/kira-ui are excluded from P99 because they have no Tailwind build, are git-ui's own primitive layer (0 .vue usages from the studio app), and P100 extracts them wholesale.
P99 follows one touch per file: every .vue file is opened for judgment in exactly one part; work runs as one sequential subagent per part, in order, with no parallel fan-out.
Part 1 runs first so Parts 2-4 open every file with its state layer already final.
shadcn-vue add cannot run in the sandbox (root-caused in P98), so shadcn-vue components are fetched via a verified direct-registry curl procedure.
