17 shadcn-vue primitives live under frontend/src/components/ui/: alert, button, checkbox, command, context-menu, dialog, dropdown-menu, input, input-group, label, popover, scroll-area, separator, textarea, toggle, toggle-group, tooltip. No app code imports them yet; Part 2 is the first consumer.
knip.json's 7 bootstrap ignoreDependencies entries (from P98) must stay until Part 2 imports the first shadcn-vue primitive; removing them now makes lint:dead flag all 7 as unused.
All module-level reactive()/ref() state modules are now Pinia setup stores: 71 defineStore() calls across 65 files, more than the plan's estimate of 45 modules to 41 stores.
state/connections.ts, state/tabs.ts, api/state/curl.ts, api/state/variables.ts and project/state/tree.ts were each split into two Pinia stores for a real two-concern reason (e.g. connection records vs. the connect dialog's UI state).
views/grpcrequest/state.ts's two createRuntimeStore runtimes (gRPC call and schema-browser) were merged into one useGrpcRequestViewStore because they share one registerTabRuntimeCleanup teardown and are torn down as a coupled unit.
state/schemas.ts was split so DDL fetch/save moved to TanStack Query while the dialog's draft state stayed a Pinia store, rather than the two-Pinia-store split guessed in plan §1.3.
The only remaining reactive()/ref() call sites are the generic factory definitions (createRuntimeStore in views/shared/viewOp.ts, createHistoryStore in api/state/history.ts) and composable factories (createPageSearch, createPageStore, useEditBuffer, useDiffEditor), which produce fresh state per call and are out of scope.
Plain Map/Set registries (state/tabRuntime.ts, state/viewCommands.ts, state/repoOpenHold.ts, maskRules.ts's correlationKeys) were left alone because they were never reactive() and are out of scope.
Exactly 10 modules subscribe to backend broadcasts via control.on*Changed(): state/connections.ts, customScripts.ts, dbmcp.ts, gitClients.ts, keepAwake.ts, layout.ts, schemas.ts, settings.ts, tabs.ts and project/state/tree.ts; the plan's count of 13 was inaccurate.
The broadcast subscriptions are registered inside the store body on first use and write the same reactive field, except schemas.ts's onSchemaChanged, which calls queryClient.invalidateQueries instead.
state/schemas.ts's ensureDdl/saveDdl, state/maskRules.ts and views/grid/PreviewCommandPanel.vue were migrated onto TanStack Query (PreviewCommandPanel uses useQuery in place of the ref([])/loading/error triple).
maskRules.ts's upsertMaskRule/removeMaskRule re-call loadMaskRules synchronously instead of using useMutation with invalidateQueries, because invalidateQueries only refetches an active useQuery observer and some callers (menu.ts existingMaskRule scan, ConnectionDialog.vue Privacy tab) have none; this matches schemas.ts's saveDdl.
Never hoist a Pinia store call such as useConfirmDialogStore() to true module scope without an explicit pinia argument in files reachable from main.ts's static imports, because it runs before app.use(pinia) and throws at boot (no active Pinia).
views/browse/menu.ts, views/documents/menu.ts and api/reveal.ts call useConfirmDialogStore() inline at their single call site instead of hoisting it; repo/git/transport.ts's module-top-level useGitCredentialStore(pinia) correctly passes the explicit pinia singleton.
After regex call-site sweeps, run grep '// .*storeName\.' to catch comments wrongly re-qualified, and check for storeName.storeName double-prefixes, because blanket regexes rewrite function names mentioned in comments.
test:ui timing flakiness (budgets.spec.ts scroll-delta percentile, slick-grid.spec.ts pacing-histogram invariant) is a known ui-timing/pacing-budget class, flaky under cross-file worker contention and documented in the tests' own comments.
test:visual has a pre-existing baseline of 5 failures (connection-dialog, console, data-view, schema-dialog, workbench), unrelated to P99.
After Part 1, baselines are: test:unit 1535 passed, test:webview 55 passed, and knip with 6 duplicate exports plus 4 config hints, all pre-existing.
SearchResults.vue in packages/git-ui has a dual script block that P99 fixes (§2.3), the one deliberate crossing of the scope boundary.

### v1.9 P99 Part 2 shell, chrome and the primitive layer (48 files)
Each theme/primitives/* wrapper keeps its public prop/emit/slot API while its internals move onto shadcn-vue, so Parts 3-4's 95 files need zero edits from Part 2.
DialogFrame.vue builds on reka-ui's DialogRoot/DialogPortal/DialogOverlay/DialogContent directly, not through the registry wrapper in src/components/ui.
AutocompleteField.vue declines reka-ui/shadcn combobox because its Listbox+Popover shape assumes single-select, which doesn't fit the field's free-text-plus-suggestions model.
workbench/ContextMenu.vue stays hand-rolled; DropdownMenuRoot and ContextMenuRoot were declined because reka-ui's item slot lacks props for swatch/checked/danger/shortcut/hint content, its trigger model assumes one owning trigger versus this point-anchored singleton with many call sites, and forking the tuned keyboard model risked a behavior regression.
The tooltip system keeps its directive-plus-singleton architecture (AppTooltip.vue and workbench/state/tooltip.ts), per the plan's own recommendation.
initTooltips() must keep returning an explicit manual-teardown () => void, because leaks.spec.ts and App.vue rely on it rather than implicit unmount cleanup.
SettingsDialog.vue's 375-line scoped style block was converted to @apply utilities under @reference "@/theme/base.css" rather than inlining 50+ template class usages.
The primitives.css .p-btn/.p-dlgbtn/.p-iconbtn cleanup is out of scope for Part 2, because those classes are still used in terminal/, views/, api/ and repo/.
TreeRow.vue must keep the selector name .spin (body @apply animate-spin;), because tests/ui/tree.spec.ts and tests/ui/support/tree.ts select .twisty .spin directly.
TreeRow.vue's local @keyframes pulse was renamed tree-row-pulse, because Vue scoped CSS doesn't scope @keyframes names and Tailwind v4 defines a global pulse at a different timing.
ErrorPopover.vue attaches its document listeners via useEventListener at setup time (not in onMounted), preserving immediate-attach/auto-detach timing.
SchemaDialog.vue must keep its explicit onBeforeUnmount cancel of the useDebounceFn debounce, because VueUse's debounceFilter has no built-in tryOnScopeDispose.
App.vue's onMounted/onUnmounted pairs are control.on*() IPC subscriptions, not DOM listeners or timers, so they are left without VueUse equivalents.
tw-animate-css, @lucide/vue, clsx and tailwind-merge stay in knip ignoreDependencies because their only importers are src/lib/utils.ts and src/components/ui/**, both in the workspace ignore list.

### v1.9 P99 Part 3 the API client surface (39 files)
FieldRowsTable.vue keeps its script setup lang=ts generic=... unchanged, per the plan's named note.
GrpcRequestView.vue replaced its manual setTimeout/clearTimeout schema-load debounce with useDebounceFn plus explicit .cancel() calls, before rescheduling and on unmount.
The classes .request-pane, .request-splitter, .url-field, .grpc-target-field and .grpc-method-field keep their names because api-ui-consistency.spec.ts, http-request.spec.ts and grpc-request.spec.ts select them directly; their CSS is still converted to @apply.
Very small style blocks (one or two rules) in ApiStart.vue, CopyAsCurlDialog.vue, SaveRequestDialog.vue and BinaryBodyPicker.vue are inlined into template classes and the style block dropped, rather than using @apply.
EditRawRequestDialog.vue uses useDebounceFn with an explicit setter and onBeforeUnmount cancel (same as SchemaDialog.vue) because its dialog-open handler must write both live and debounced values immediately, which refDebounced cannot do.
ImportCurlDialog.vue uses refDebounced directly because it has no requirement for an external immediate write.
The classes .twisty and .node-icon in CollectionRow.vue keep their names as test markers in mutations.spec.ts, fake-data.spec.ts and tree.spec.ts.
The grid-template-columns literal in VariableRow.vue and VariableSetView.vue stays plain CSS beside the @apply line, because Tailwind has no named utility for an explicit column template.
Component-local clipboard writes (copyUrl in CollectionsTree.vue, onCopy in VariablesOverviewPanel.vue, onCopyRequest/onCopyResponse in RawExchangePane.vue) keep their copyText() calls rather than useClipboard(), following Part 2's precedent.
CollectionRow.vue's .rename-input border uses border-[var(--kira-accent)] instead of border-accent, because shadcn-bridge.css maps --color-accent to --kira-hover (grey), not --kira-accent (brand blue).
In the compiled CSS, .bg-accent compiles to background-color: var(--accent) (grey), and .text-accent-fg compiles to no rule at all.
AppButton.vue and IconButton.vue use bg-accent/text-accent-fg for the primary variant without a rendering bug, because unscoped, unlayered .p-btn.primary/.p-dlgbtn.primary rules in primitives.css supply the real --kira-accent background, and unlayered author CSS wins over Tailwind v4 layers.
The .response-body and .response-status-row classes in the http response pane keep their names because api-ui-consistency.spec.ts and three http-*.spec.ts files select them directly.
The grpc ResponsePane.vue keeps the .response-status-row class name for consistency with the http component, though no grpc test selects it.
The test:visual suite has 5 failing specs as a pre-existing baseline (connection-dialog, console, data-view, schema-dialog, workbench).
The ui-timing/pacing-budget UI tests are flaky under cross-file worker contention.

### v1.9 P99 Part 4 data, repo, terminal and editor views, plus the phase-closing audit (56 files)
SlickGridHost.vue and ConsoleSlickGrid.vue carry zero style lines, so their migration work is VueUse only.
RepoTerminalView.vue's dual script block was fixed by moving lazy-loaded terminal-renderer state into sibling module terminalRendererLoader.ts, which retired the P97 biome-ignore.
The P97 biome-ignore (lint/correctness/noUnusedVariables) was needed because Biome's Vue support can't link scope across a plain script and a script setup block in one SFC.
BrowseView.vue's keyTypesDebounceTimer setTimeout/clearTimeout pair was replaced with useDebounceFn, with ensureKeyTypesDebounced.cancel() in onBeforeUnmount, following the SchemaDialog.vue precedent.
Hover styles needing the accent color use text-[var(--kira-accent)] instead of the text-accent utility, as a workaround for the accent/--kira-hover cascade-layer problem documented in CollectionRow.vue's rename-input.
SlickGridHost.vue's viewportEl scroll listeners and ResizeObserver stay hand-rolled because their registration order relative to SlickGrid's internal scroll listener is load-bearing for the velocity sampler, and onUnmounted's hand-ordered teardown (§6 D3) can't take a composable's onScopeDispose cleanup.
SlickGridHost.vue's header-cell zone/sortIndicator click listeners stay hand-rolled because SlickGrid creates and destroys those nodes per header-cell render, after setup()'s effect-scope capture, so they are not stable Vue-tracked refs.
SlickGridHost.vue's onPaste keeps navigator.clipboard.readText() instead of useClipboard, because useClipboard's read() is not documented to preserve the promise-rejects-on-permission-denial contract the existing try/catch depends on.
SlickGridHost.vue's scrollSaveTimer became a useDebounceFn (persistScroll) with an explicit .cancel() in onUnmounted.
ConsoleSlickGrid.vue mirrors SlickGridHost.vue's three declined patterns for the same reasons.
FkPreviewPopover.vue's document keydown Escape listener uses useEventListener, mirroring PopoverPanel.vue.
RepoTerminalView.vue's ResizeObserver, constructed in mount() after await loadTerminalRenderer(), stays hand-rolled because Vue's synchronous current-instance tracking is lost across the await, and converting would need a behavior change that §9.4 forbids.
GitPanel.vue's two raw navigator.clipboard.writeText() calls (Copy path on repo and worktree rows) now use the app's shared copyText() wrapper, as TabStrip.vue and OperationsPanel.vue do.
Existing copyText() calls are left unconverted to useClipboard.
GitPanel.vue's .prompt-scrim keeps literal z-30 rather than var(--kira-z-dialog), because the prompt raises directly from the panel, unlike ConsoleSavedMenu.vue's prompt-scrim, which is raised from inside a popover with its own full-viewport backdrop (P28 D17(c)).
MonacoHost.vue converts only host-wrapper rules (.monaco-host, .monaco-host-pending, .monaco-host--single-line.monaco-host-pending) to @apply; rules reaching Monaco's own DOM (:deep(.monaco-editor), :global(.monaco-hover), :global(.suggest-widget)) or Monaco tokenizer classes (:deep(.kira-ed-*)) stay plain CSS.
DateTimePicker.vue keeps its custom calendar; replacing it with the shadcn calendar component was declined, and only CSS/VueUse conversion applied.
Never let behavior changes ride along with a conversion (plan §9.4).
CLAUDE.md's git policy amends commits only on an explicit user request.
The §12.3 phase-closing audit runs whole-tree over apps/kira-studio/frontend/src, so it surfaced pre-existing hand-rolled patterns in files no part had been assigned.
The search-debounce watch+setTimeout pairs in api/state/collections.ts and project/state/tree.ts were converted to refDebounced.
state/mode.ts, state/layout.ts and views/repo/blameLine.ts use useDebounceFn, with .cancel() replacing the old clearTimeout.
state/tabs.ts saveDebounced uses useDebounceFn with .cancel() replacing clearTimeout; saveNow and flushPendingTabState still flush immediately by calling the underlying save function directly.
The repo/git/hostHandlers.ts clipboard.write RPC handler uses the copyText() wrapper, the same one GitPanel.vue uses.
In project/state/tree.ts the trim/lowercase step lives in activeSearchQuery's computed, so searchResult must read activeSearchQuery.value, not debouncedQuery.value. This keeps TreeRow.vue highlighting and row matching identical to pre-conversion behavior.
views/httprequest/cookies.ts keeps its per-tabId debounce map, because useDebounceFn debounces one function identity. A per-key cache of debounced instances would be a new abstraction for one call site, which §9.4 forbids outside a genuine multi-site finding.
views/shared/slick/scrollTrace.ts keeps its raw clipboard write. Its optional-chained navigator.clipboard? guard and outer synchronous try/catch handle a context where the Clipboard API may be absent (a dev-tool console hook), which copyText()/useClipboard() don't account for.
SlickGridHost.vue's insert-region el.addEventListener listeners stay hand-wired. They share the hand-ordered onUnmounted teardown sequence with the viewportEl listeners, and a declined-with-reasoning comment was added.
Remaining raw addEventListener and ResizeObserver uses are intentional. They are non-component .ts modules with no active Vue effect scope (kiraSlickGrid.ts, reviewDecorations.ts, views/grid/slick/editor.ts, repo/git/transport.ts) or named declines (order-dependent teardown listeners in SlickGridHost.vue/ConsoleSlickGrid.vue, header-cell zone listeners, RepoTerminalView.vue's async-boundary construction).
The §12.3 audit found zero reactive()/module-level ref() uses outside a store and zero manual loading/error/isLoading fetch triples.
Every .vue component has exactly one script block, confirmed across all 269 .vue files in apps/kira-studio/frontend/src and packages/git-ui/src. RepoGraphView.vue's second grep hit is a comment mentioning script setup.
Scoped-CSS lines fell from a 7,125-line baseline to 5,260 (26% reduction). Remaining plain CSS covers :deep() reaching third-party DOM, :global(), @keyframes, grid-template, calc() mixing a literal 14px with --kira-s-* tokens, and a deliberately preserved literal z-index: 30 in GitPanel.vue.
Several Part 2/3 files (SettingsDialog.vue, ConnectionDialog.vue, OperationsPanel.vue, TitleBar.vue, TabStrip.vue, FiltersDialog.vue) still use plain-CSS var(--kira-*) declarations where a Tailwind utility exists (e.g. text-muted). They were left alone, since fixing them means re-verifying shipped files outside Part 4's 56-file assignment, which CLAUDE.md's carve-out treats as outside the phase's scope.
Four of P98's seven knip bootstrap ignores remain (tw-animate-css, @lucide/vue, clsx, tailwind-merge), because no app file outside src/components/ui/** or lib/utils.ts imports any of them directly. The other three (@vueuse/core, reka-ui, class-variance-authority) were removed in Part 2.
bun run lint:dead is clean apart from the baseline of 6 pre-existing duplicate exports and 4 config hints.
No theme/primitives wrapper was deleted anywhere in v1.9, so there is no orphaned primitive.
With biome.json's **/*.vue a11y: off override removed, biome check over the frontend src, packages/git-ui/src and packages/kira-ui/src reports 255 errors across 86 files (P97 baseline: 255 across 85). P99 fixes none of it; it is P105's starting point.
The budgets.spec.ts scroll-response p50 assertion is contention-sensitive and tuned against a specific dev machine; it fails under full-suite cross-file worker contention (13ms measured against a 12ms budget) and is a pre-existing environmental flake, not a regression.
The ui-timing Playwright project has dependencies: ['ui'], so its tests are skipped ("did not run") when any ui test fails; run with --no-deps to check them directly.
cell-editor.spec.ts can fail with 'locator.click: Target page, context or browser has been closed' under the full suite; this is a known pre-existing timeout signature (documented in a comment in SlickGridHost.vue) and passes when isolated with --workers=1.
bun run test:visual has a documented baseline of 5 failing specs: connection-dialog, console, data-view, schema-dialog, workbench.
Baselines after P99 Part 4: test:unit 1535 passed, test:webview 55 passed, and knip (lint:dead) reports 6 duplicate exports plus 4 config hints, all pre-existing; build has only a pre-existing chunk-size warning.
P99 migrated every .vue file in apps/kira-studio/frontend/src (plus packages/git-ui's one crossing) to Tailwind, shadcn-vue, VueUse, Pinia and TanStack Query; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's Stack row was changed from "P99 migrates" to "P99 migrated".
P99's phase-closing audit runs exactly once, at the end of the last part, over the whole tree rather than only that part's files.

### v1.9 P100 Part 1 the Go extraction
apps/kira-space cannot import anything from apps/kira-studio/internal/ because of Go's internal/ visibility rule, so shared packages were hoisted to a repo-root internal/ first.
Eight packages were hoisted to repo-root internal/: ipcerr, notify, pathsafe, rpcstream, startupfail, logging, plus new kirapaths and sqlitex (generalized from Studio's config/storage path-join and migration-runner logic).
startupfail and logging were parameterized when hoisted (startupfail.Info/Reporter dependency injection, logging.Init taking a logs-dir argument) because neither could stay a process-wide singleton once two apps link it.
The extraction moved 15 git packages to apps/kira-space: the 13 originally planned plus internal/ghclient (importers gitrpc, gitsession) and internal/codeworkspace (importers bridge, main.go); 272 Go files, 62,066 lines.
Kira Space has its own home ~/.kira-space (KIRA_SPACE_HOME), its own kira.db, review.db and git.sock; a shared git socket is impossible because gitsock.Server.Start's flock makes the loser silently not listen.
The git packages were moved by copy-forward/delete-backward: each was copied to apps/kira-space leaving Studio's copy intact, and one final commit deleted all old Studio copies, because moving gitwire first would break Studio's still-resident gitsock, which imports it.
The plan's §4.6 suggested move order was not a valid topological order (gitstore imports gitclient/porcelain); the real order was computed with Kahn's algorithm.
apps/kira-space builds without a frontend/; frontend/dist/index.html is a force-added placeholder so //go:embed compiles. Root package.json scripts dev:space/build:space/package:space/typecheck:space-web are deferred to Part 2.
apps/kira-space's 0001_init.sql has no layout/tabs tables, since nothing in Part 1 uses them; model.Settings is trimmed to Appearance/Advanced.GitLogLevel/Git, and repos.Repos to 5 fields (Settings, Windows, GitClients, CodeRepos, GitRepoSettings).
Kira Space's bridge uses a trimmed appcore.Deps (Repos, Events, GitRegistry) and local StreamSession and Browser interfaces with only the methods gitstream.go/github.go call, to avoid importing adapterhost/appupdate.
apps/kira-space/internal/bridge/events.go declares only the channel constants ChannelCodeSearch, ChannelGitPairing and ChannelGitClientsChanged, which are not yet subscribed since there is no Events.Attach wiring in Part 1.
Kira Space's shell is simpler than Studio's: quit has no per-window flush handshake, menus are minimal (3 sections, no ItemEmit kind), and app.go has no Dialogs or AttachSystemWake; wireGit is lifted wholesale from Studio's.
Kira Space's main.go is now the only askpass entry point; Studio's askpass shim was removed from its main.go.
kira-space's gitreview DefaultPath() uses config.KiraSpaceHome() instead of config.KiraHome(), which is undefined in kira-space's config package.
layering_test.go exists in both apps with its own modulePrefix and exemption set; Kira Space's exempts only internal, internal/bridge and internal/shell. TestDomainPackagesDoNotImportBridge checks domain packages do not import bridge.
Studio's model.Settings kept advanced.gitLogLevel and GitSettings because they are mirrored in packages/shared/domain/settings.ts and SettingsDialog.vue; removing them Go-only would break the settings round-trip, and removal needs a frontend change outside the Go-only phase.
In Studio, ValidLogLevel was moved into settings.go (its only remaining caller) and LinkService's fakeBrowser test double moved into link_test.go, after their original files were deleted with the git code.
The git-related wiring was removed from Kira Studio: wireGit/gitWired, git-threaded parameters in wireEmbeddedServices/wireLifecycle/postAppDeps, the 3 git Services entries, and shell/app.go's RegisterGitStream.
The pairing-notification block (wirePairingNotifications and its helpers) was removed because gitsock.OnPairingChanged was its only trigger; the notifications package and its notifier variable were removed too, since Studio has no other caller.
bridge/events.go dropped the Git Sources field and the two pairing channels; a comment points at their new home.
model/window.go's validWindowModes no longer includes "git"; a stored git row degrades to studio through NormalizeMode, as for any unrecognised mode, so no migration is needed for the window-mode leaf.
internal/ipcfixture/harness.go never referenced the git bridge services, so it needed no edit.
Migration 0026_p100_drop_git_tables.sql drops the git_repo_settings, git_clients and code_repos tables.
tabs.workspace_id was kept in migration 0026 because every remaining tab already parses as workspace_id IS NULL, and dropping the column would rewrite the whole table for no behavioural gain.
The one-time import of pairings and repo list (§4.5) was scoped out of Part 1 per the plan's named fallback. It needs a settings read/write path in kira-space's boot, a first-boot ordering step after the DB opens and before any repo/pairing read, and handling of a second database (review.db) without damaging either file.
Part 1 has no SettingsService bridge in kira-space; nothing reads settings beyond Advanced.GitLogLevel at logging.SetLevel time. The deferred import is left for the orchestrating session to schedule as a new phase or in Part 2.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md Known open items is Part 4's to edit, so the deferred first-boot import is not recorded there.
Kira Space creates ~/.kira-space/git.sock at mode 0600 at startup.
gitsock.Start() fails with 'bind: invalid argument' if the KIRA_SPACE_HOME socket path exceeds Linux's ~108-byte sun_path limit, so a short path is needed.

### v1.9 P100 Part 2 the frontend extraction
P100 Part 2 moves 53 files (8,238 lines) to Kira Space: all of repo/ (21), all of views/repo/ (23), seven state/ stores (now Pinia stores since P99 Part 1), and two workbench/ dialogs.
monacoEntry.ts (app-wide Monaco bootstrap that editor/monaco.ts dynamically imports) and monarch/{mongo,redis,decorators}.ts under views/repo/ are not git-related and stay in Kira Studio, relocated to editor/ as a P60a layering leftover.
apps/kira-space/frontend is a workbench built on Tailwind/shadcn-vue/VueUse/Pinia/TanStack Query per CLAUDE.md, with a ported shell subset, because the moved files import 17 Studio stores and 10 theme/primitives components.
Hoisting a shared packages/workbench and adopting packages/kira-ui as the primitive layer were both weighed and declined (plan §5.2); Kira Space ports its workbench instead.
Kira Studio drops AppMode's 'git', WorkspaceKey collapses to AppMode, and packages/shared/domain/workspace.ts is deleted rather than stubbed.
packages/theme is a single shared package holding tokens.css, base.css, 18 shadcn-vue components/ui components with lib/utils.ts, 12 verbatim-identical primitives ports, stickyBand.ts, connColor.ts, wrapSelection.ts and CodiconIcon.vue; both Kira Studio and Kira Space import it via '@theme/...'.
Kira Studio adopted packages/theme by deleting its own components/ui, theme/primitives and theme css/ts copies outright rather than keeping them alongside; vite.config.ts, tsconfig.json and components.json in both frontends point at the shared package.
ColorPicker and completion.ts were dead before the P103 dedup and are absent from packages/theme; connColor.ts and wrapSelection.ts were added because they were found identical during the pass.
Tailwind v4's automatic content scan roots at the Vite project root and does not reach sibling monorepo packages, so packages/theme/src/base.css needs an explicit @source "./"; directive, placed after every @import per CSS at-rule ordering.
Never use Tailwind v4 bracket-integer arbitrary values like z-[1]/z-[2] in this setup, because they generate no CSS; use the bare-integer form z-1/z-2.
The z-[1] bug caused the tests/ui/tree.spec.ts sticky-band stacking-order assertion failure, fixed in VirtualList.vue, ProjectTree.vue, StreamView.vue and RepoFileTree.vue.
Running sh scripts/setup.sh (wails3 task common:generate:bindings -clean=true) deletes and rebuilds the gitignored frontend/bindings/ from current Go source, so stale binding files that keep bun run build green are removed and hidden breaks surface.
Kira Studio's bridge/index.ts no longer imports gitclientsservice, githubservice and codeworkspaceservice bindings (services moved to Kira Space in Part 1); the dead git/codeWorkspace bridge methods were removed, and the unused @kira/git-ipc dependency was dropped from Kira Studio's package.json.
ColorPicker.vue, completion.ts, repo/QuickOpen.vue, repo/state/quickOpen.ts and the quick-open-index-truncated-reactive unit test were left out of Kira Space because they were dead or have no quick-open surface there.
apps/kira-studio-vscode/src/commands.test.ts fixture paths OPS_GO/REMOTE_GO/STACK_GO were updated because gitsession moved from kira-studio/internal to kira-space in Part 1.
Fix a failing or stale reference when found, not in a later phase, per CLAUDE.md's standing rule.
Root wiring added dev:space/build:space/package:space/typecheck:space-*/test:ui:space scripts, knip.json workspace blocks for apps/kira-space/frontend and packages/theme, check-tokens.sh resolving --kira-*/--kui-* references inside packages/theme/src, and sign-bundle.sh support for the second bundle.
The fuzzysort dependency was dropped from root package.json and go-toast/v2 from go.mod via go mod tidy, since both became unused after P100 Part 1 moved their consumers.
apps/kira-space/frontend/dist/index.html is untracked (git rm --cached), matching Kira Studio which tracks no files under frontend/dist; it was a placeholder so Go's //go:embed all:frontend/dist compiled before a real frontend existed.
Kira Space has no WindowsService, a design simplification documented in main.ts's comment, so a plain ?window=main request returns 400 "unknown window: main"; a real Startup-created window UUID is required.
Kira Studio's WindowsService.Ensure() auto-provisions the main window row, so the plain fallback ?window=main boots with zero console errors.
The real native shell in Kira Space (internal/shell/window.go) always navigates with URL "/?window=" + w.Key against a row Startup already created, so the missing plain-URL window is only an ad hoc diagnostic limitation, not a product defect.
On a fresh KIRA_SPACE_HOME with no repos imported and no git-client config, Kira Space logs first-run console errors (422 resource loads and "codeworkspace: git is unavailable: notFound" rejections); this is a config probe finding nothing, not a missing git binary.
Part 2 added typecheck:space-web, typecheck:space-unit and typecheck:space-tests, so bun run typecheck now covers eight projects.
The new knip.json workspace blocks for apps/kira-space/frontend and packages/theme each add a ".vue extension not registered as compiler" configuration hint, raising knip hints from 4 to 6 (exit 0, not unused-code findings).
The VS Code extension is still named kira-studio-vscode; renaming it is Part 3's job.
Under concurrent heavy load, tests/ui/sql-schema.spec.ts can fail spuriously; this resource-contention pattern is documented in the repo's sandbox notes and is not a real bug.

### v1.9 P100 Part 3 the extension rename, retarget and packaging
The VS Code extension was renamed from apps/kira-studio-vscode to apps/kira-space-vscode, with package name kira-space-vscode and displayName "Kira Space"; identifiers moved to the kiraSpace./kira-space prefix.
The 11 kiraVersion.* settings keys are RepoSettingsSnapshot's key space in packages/git-ipc/src/contract.ts, so renaming them is a contract change (CONTRACT_VERSION 39 → 40 on both sides, plus its pinning test).
All 11 kiraVersion.* wire keys (graph.pageSize, graph.scope, stash.showInGraph, stash.includeUntracked, review.baseCandidates, pull.strategy, log.level, github.enabled, worktree.prepareScript, worktree.basePath, checkout.autoStash) were renamed to kiraSpace.*, in both RepoSettingsSnapshot and *Patch json tags (BREAKING CHANGE).
CONTRACT_VERSION/ContractVersion was bumped from 39 to 40 on both TS and Go sides, and gitrpc/stash_test.go's TestContractVersion_Is39 became TestContractVersion_Is40.
No data migration is needed for the settings-key rename: the git_repo_settings.key column stores short undotted leaf names (e.g. graphPageSize, logLevel, advanced.gitLogLevel), decoupled from the wire namespace by an earlier P72 redesign, so a kiraVersion.% migration would be a no-op.
The kiraVersion.* keys were never VS Code settings.json keys; apps/kira-space-vscode/package.json has no "configuration" section, and they were only wire-protocol JSON field names.
packages/git-ipc/testdata/graphChunkFrame.{bin,json} bake in the contract version and must be regenerated after a version bump, because unwrapVersioned rejects the old version. Regenerate with KIRA_GIT_FIXTURES=write go test ./apps/kira-space/internal/gitsock/... -run TestFixtures_CaptureGraphChunkFrame, then run bunx biome check --write.
Sweep the rename per-file, never with a repo-wide replace, because that would rewrite the DB client's own name in docs/, scripts/demo-dbs/ and NOTICES.md.
"Kira Studio" was replaced with "Kira Space" only where the string named the backend app, never where it named the DB client.
Some references were deliberately left unchanged: DB-client citations in theme CSS/readTokens.ts, the @kira-version/git upstream npm package citation in packages/git-ipc/src/rpc.ts (a real external dependency), and the immutable migration 0017_g18_git_repo_settings.sql.
The manifest has 47 command ids, not the plan's 62; the pre-rename manifest already had 47. All were renamed kiraVersion.<verb> → kiraSpace.<verb> and the command category became "Kira Space".
The manifest has 10 colour ids, not the plan's 14: kiraSpace.graphLane0-7 plus kiraSpace.reviewedLineBackground and kiraSpace.reviewedLineOverviewRuler.
Container and view ids were renamed: panel container kiraVersion → kiraSpace (title stays "Kira"), activitybar container kiraVersionReview → kiraSpaceReview (title "Kira Space"), views kiraSpace.graph and kiraSpace.review.
The comment controller is now kiraSpace.reviewComments, the menu group is kiraSpace@1, and the virtual-document URI scheme is kira-space (when clauses use resourceScheme == kira-space).
The extension's socketPath() in connection.ts now uses KIRA_SPACE_HOME and ~/.kira-space instead of KIRA_HOME and ~/.kira-studio.
The search conformance fixture packages/git-core/testdata/searchConformance.json is shared by the TS matcher test and the Go test apps/kira-space/internal/gitsearch/conformance_test.go; its sample addresses became fixture@kira-space.test.
The .vsix ships inside the app bundle and installs from the Connected editors pane, so packaging reused the existing five-link chain, retargeted at apps/kira-space/bin/kira-space.vsix → Kira Space.app/Contents/Resources/.
The build:vsix task now lives in apps/kira-space/build/Taskfile.yml. Its sources are apps/kira-space-vscode/**, packages/git-{core,ipc,ui}/src/** and packages/kira-ui/src/** (a real build dependency the plan omitted), and it generates apps/kira-space/bin/kira-space.vsix.
The build:vsix task and the vsix-copy blocks were removed from the apps/kira-studio Taskfiles (build and darwin); the Kira Space darwin Taskfile mirrors the copy-and-fail-loudly block with kira-space.vsix naming.
apps/kira-space/internal/gitvsix/install.go sets vsixFileName to "kira-space.vsix".
bun run package:vscode writes apps/kira-space/bin/kira-space.vsix, and the extension bundles are built under apps/kira-space-vscode/dist/.
scripts/verify-packaging.sh: S9 targets apps/kira-space-vscode/package.json and apps/kira-space/build/config.yml; the vsix check (A6) moved from the Kira Studio artifact block to a new Kira Space block with A1/A3/A5/A6/N2 plus A4/N3 for the .dmg. The Kira Space bundle id is com.kirathecat.kira-space.
bun run verify:packaging skips the A1/A3/A5/A6/N2/A4/N3 checks when no built .app/.dmg exists, while the S1/S2/S5/S9 static checks still run for both apps.
scripts/sign-bundle.sh needs no change because it takes the app dir and name as $1/$2, and package:space already calls it with apps/kira-space "Kira Space".
Route changes to .github/workflows/ through docs/pending-changes/ when the session cannot push workflow files (e.g. the Linux sandbox, per docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md).
The release.yml patch, staged at docs/pending-changes/.github__workflows__release.yml.patch, drops the release job's stale apps/kira-studio-vscode/package.json version-stamp step (path no longer exists after the directory rename) and adds a release-space job.
The release-space job needs [test-matrix, db-compat, release] so it uploads onto the same draft release the release job creates instead of racing to create a second one. It has its own Wails bindings cache, version-stamp step (kira-space/build/config.yml and kira-space-vscode/package.json), runs package:space and verify:packaging, and asserts bundle id com.kirathecat.kira-space.
pr.yml needs no change for the rename, since it never references the vscode extension path and never builds a packaged app or dmg.
README.md and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md still describe apps/kira-studio-vscode / "Kira Version" in several places; the documentation update is deliberately deferred to §7.3, Part 4 of the plan.
The gitignored Wails-generated apps/kira-space/frontend/bindings models.ts can carry stale kiraVersion.log.level doc comments; it self-corrects on the next wails3 task common:generate:bindings -clean=true.

### v1.9 P100 Part 4 the icon, the docs, and the phase-closing audit
Kira Space's app icon SVG (apps/kira-space/build/appicon.icon/Assets/kira_icon_vector.svg) uses the repo's blues (#3B9BE8/#0A5FA8) for its #bg gradient stops instead of tan (#D2A97C/#A3794C).
Kira Space's icon replaces all four client glyphs (database stack, SQL cell grid, document braces, message queue) with the commit-graph mark copied verbatim from apps/kira-space-vscode/resources/icon.svg, so app and extension share one drawing; JSON-document and Kafka/SQS marks say nothing on a git icon.
In the Kira Space icon SVG, the commit-graph mark is placed with translate(232 700) scale(8.5) rotate(-6) translate(-12 -12) in the open bottom-left gap, because at dead centre it sat behind the cat's opaque #FDFDFB body fill, which paints later, and was invisible.
Kira Space's icon SVG must contain no database-client glyph and no tan colors (#D2A97C, #A3794C); Kira Studio's SVG at apps/kira-studio/build/appicon.icon/ must stay unchanged.
The icon PNGs are rendered through Playwright's headless Chromium because the container has no system SVG rasterizer.
apps/kira-space/build/appicon.png is 1024×1024 and apps/kira-space-vscode/resources/icon.png is 128×128; both are rasterized from the full-colour kira_icon_vector.svg, not the small currentColor activity-bar glyph, matching the pre-P100 precedent.
Docs cite gitrpc's 51 request methods and ContractVersion 40, no longer list startupfail as a git package, and give the command count as 47 (len of package.json's contributes.commands).
Kira Studio's main.go still binds LinkService although it has no live caller since git-ui's commit-body link left with the git module; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md records it as a Known open item rather than removing it.
The review.db storage path in docs uses ${KIRA_SPACE_HOME} instead of ${KIRA_HOME}.
apps/kira-studio/tests/ui/support/gitStreamMock.ts and graphStreamFixture.ts were removed as dead test-support files with no importers.
docs/PACKAGING.md has a section "§8 Kira Space packaging"; the .vsix/Connected-editors description was removed from Kira Studio's sections; Kira Space has its own apps/kira-space/README.md, with git sections moved out of the root README.
scripts/setup.sh regenerates Wails bindings for both Kira Studio and Kira Space, because otherwise a fresh clone's first bun run typecheck fails on missing apps/kira-space/frontend/src/bridge/*.ts.
package.json has predev:space and prepackage:space hooks so dev:space and package:space do not run against a stale or absent wails3 CLI on a fresh clone; bun honours pre<name> hooks for colon-containing script names.
Kira Space's gitrpc contract version is 40 in both Go (gitrpc.ContractVersion in contract.go) and TypeScript (CONTRACT_VERSION in validate.ts), and TestContractVersion_Is40 guards it.
The one-time cross-DB settings import (~/.kira-studio/kira.db to ~/.kira-space/kira.db) was scoped out in Part 1 per the plan's named fallback, so no kiraVersion.*-seeded settings row is ever migrated.
GitRepoSettingsRepo reads and writes settings under the kiraSpace.* keys, covered by TestRepoSettings_GetSetRoundTrip and five sibling tests.
TestDomainPackagesDoNotImportBridge (layering_test) enforces that domain packages do not import bridge, in both apps (44 non-exempt packages combined).
Kira Studio contains no git Go code or git frontend code after P100; remaining hits are historical comments, and no Git/Repo/CodeWorkspace routes remain in bridge/index.ts or internal/bridge/*.go.
Never import another app's internal/ package from Kira Space code.
The identifier kiraVersion was renamed; no live identifiers remain in extension/app source, and historical docs/SPEC mentions are left uncorrected by convention.
Give every Vue component exactly one <script> block.
Kira Space has no build/test coverage in .github/workflows/pr.yml; this is blocked by the .github/workflows/ push-scope limitation, and a patch would need to go through docs/pending-changes/.
bun run lint:dead (knip) has a baseline of 6 duplicate-export and 6 configuration-hint findings, unchanged since P100 Parts 2-3.
bun run verify:packaging skips checks A1/A3/A4/A5/A6/N2/N3 for both apps when no built .app/.dmg exists, the documented precedent in every P100 part.
P99 and P100, both four-part phases, close with no separate whole-phase result rollup section.

### v1.9 P102 Reclassify root `package.json`'s `dependencies`/`devDependencies` split by actual import reachability; add the xterm...
Vite bundles by import reachability, not by which package.json list a package sits in, so a wrong dependencies/devDependencies placement is a doc-accuracy bug, not a build bug.
Root package.json: packages imported by shipped app code (apps/kira-studio/frontend/src/**, excluding tests) belong in dependencies; build, lint, test and type tooling stays in devDependencies.
P102 moved 11 packages from devDependencies to dependencies in root package.json: vue, vite, @vitejs/plugin-vue, tailwindcss, @tailwindcss/vite, vue-tsc, sql-formatter, simple-icons, @wailsio/runtime, @faker-js/faker and @vscode/codicons.
vite, @vitejs/plugin-vue, tailwindcss and @tailwindcss/vite are not imported by .ts/.vue files; they are reachable via apps/*/frontend/vite.config.ts and packages/theme/src/base.css's @import "tailwindcss".
vue-tsc was moved to dependencies without import-reachability justification: no runtime or build-script import exists, and the frontend build script is plain vite build with no type-check step.
sql-formatter is imported in views/console/sqlFormatterEntry.ts, which format.ts loads via dynamic import().
@wailsio/runtime is used type-only via bridge/port.ts, with tsconfig.web.json path-mapping onto /wails/runtime.js.
@faker-js/faker is imported from '@faker-js/faker/locale/en' in dynamic-import entry files fakerEntry.ts (kira-studio grid fakeData and packages/api-core http/dynamic), used by grid Generate Data and HTTP/gRPC dynamic request values.
@vscode/codicons has no JS/TS import but is a runtime dependency because packages/theme/src/base.css and packages/git-ui/src/icons/codicon.css CSS-@import its codicon.css into the shipped bundle.
mariadb and pg stay in devDependencies because they are imported only by test fixture support (packages/db-fixtures/support/{mariadb,postgres}.ts), not shipped app code.
typescript, @biomejs/biome, knip, @playwright/test, @types/*, testcontainers, @testcontainers/*, @typescript/native-preview, @vscode/vsce and bun-types remain in devDependencies as build/lint/test/type tooling.
@xterm/addon-web-links is pinned exactly at 0.12.0, following the exact-pin convention of @xterm/addon-fit@0.11.0; no peerDependencies entry pins it below @xterm/xterm@6.0.0.
The xterm Terminal/FitAddon setup lives in views/terminal/terminalRenderer.ts's getOrCreateTerminal, not in TerminalPanel.vue (tab UI chrome); WebLinksAddon is loaded there via term.loadAddon(new WebLinksAddon()) beside the fit addon.
apps/kira-space/frontend/src/views/repo/terminalRenderer.ts is a byte-identical duplicate of kira-studio's terminalRenderer.ts, so edits must be applied to both copies until P103 hoists them to packages/workbench.

### v1.9 P103 Part 1 `packages/workbench` and the verbatim tier
packages/workbench uses the alias @workbench/*, following packages/theme's vite-alias/tsconfig-paths precedent rather than packages/git-ui's exports map, because its files import @theme/*, which only resolves through the consuming app's own alias table.
P100 split Kira Studio and Kira Space but left their bases duplicated rather than shared, because P100's scope was extraction, not dedup.
The byte-identical theme tier (tokens.css, base.css, 18 components/ui shadcn-vue components, lib/utils.ts, 12 identical theme/primitives ports, stickyBand.ts, connColor.ts, wrapSelection.ts, CodiconIcon.vue) was already hoisted to packages/theme in P100 Part 2 (122 files).
ContextMenu.vue and AppTooltip.vue moved to packages/workbench with their hand-rolled internals untouched, because P104 reworks one shared copy after P103, which is why P103 is sequenced first.
P103 Part 1 moved no Go files; the Go shell hoist, including internal/terminal, belongs to Part 3 of P103.
packages/workbench holds 28 files (~2,350 lines) across components, state, editor, terminal, bridge, shortcuts, util and testing/unit, and is wired into both knip.json workspace blocks, check-tokens.sh's kira- usage scan, the root workspace list, both apps' vite.config.ts/tsconfig.json and their unit/e2e test project configs.
workbench.css carries only an @source "./"; directive because Tailwind v4 does not content-scan a sibling package by default; without it ConfirmDialog.vue's classes such as m-0 and whitespace-pre-wrap compile away silently.
monacoTheme.ts in packages/workbench holds MonacoModule, KIRA_EDITOR_THEME and the cssVar/normalizeColor/rgbaToHex8 apparatus extracted from each app's editor/monaco.ts; monaco.ts stays per-app (deferred to Part 2) and re-exports MonacoModule and KIRA_EDITOR_THEME.
cssVar has no consumer outside monacoTheme.ts in either app, so it is not re-exported from the apps' monaco.ts; bun run lint:dead flags such a re-export as an unused export.
The shared terminalRenderer.ts takes a TerminalRendererDeps parameter (appearance(), onTerminalOutput(), writeTerminal()) on getOrCreateTerminal/applyTerminalAppearance, so it never depends on either app's private state/settings or state/terminals store shape; TerminalView.vue (Studio) and RepoTerminalView.vue (Space) build and pass it.
state/contextMenu.ts moved to packages/workbench in Part 1 rather than Part 2, because ContextMenu.vue and contextMenuKeys.ts depend on it, and plan §5.3 specifies moving it whole with no parameterization.
Kira Space keeps terminalRenderer.ts and terminalRendererLoader.ts under views/repo/ while Kira Studio keeps them under views/terminal/, so a same-path sweep missed them.
Never hoist state/pinia.ts or state/queryClient.ts into the shared workbench package; keep them duplicated per app, because bun test runs both apps' unit specs in one process with one module cache and Pinia keys stores by id string alone, so hoisting made kira-space's 'tabs' store resolve to kira-studio's and broke 9 kira-space unit tests.
In production the two apps are separate Vite builds with no shared JS runtime, so the singleton collision only affects the shared-process bun test run.
queryClient.ts was un-hoisted pre-emptively because it has the same module-level singleton shape and shared-process hazard (QueryClient cache key collision).
Duplicating singleton modules per app rather than hoisting follows the established precedent of state/terminals.ts and editor/monaco.ts.
state/tabRuntime.ts stays hoisted despite its singleton shape, because it is a self-gated callback fan-out (each cleanup no-ops on a tabId it doesn't own), not an identity registry, and shared terminal/terminalRenderer.ts imports it directly.
Deferred to Part 2: tests/ui/support/{ipcChannels,mockRuntime}.ts (real per-app divergence), state/terminals.ts, state/terminalTabs.ts, editor/monaco.ts, and pinia.ts/queryClient.ts; Part 2 should consider a factory-function redesign for pinia/queryClient.
packages/workbench/src/testing/unit/*.ts imports bun:test, so it is excluded via a narrow exclude in both apps' tsconfig.tests.json, whose broad packages/workbench/src/**/*.ts include glob would otherwise pull it into the Playwright project (types: node).
monacoTheme.ts cannot be split further because KIRA_EDITOR_THEME must stay synchronously available (MonacoHost.vue and ResponseDiffDialog.vue read it as a plain prop value); this causes an informational Rolldown INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT warning costing a few KB.
The knip lint:dead run shows a 'vue extension not registered' configuration hint for packages/workbench, the same hint other .vue-bearing knip workspaces carry.

### v1.9 P103 Part 2 the parameterized frontend
Each app declares its own tab-kind union (Kira Studio 12, Kira Space 5, with terminal shared) instead of one shared 16-member tabKindSchema, which forced each app to stub the other's kinds.
packages/workbench owns only the generic TabKindDef/TabViewMap contract for tab kinds.
Kira Studio's Go RenderableTabKinds (apps/kira-studio/internal/storage/model/tabs.go) no longer lists the four repo-* kinds Studio cannot produce.
go-ts-vocabulary-parity.spec.ts is two checks, one per app, each passing on its own vocabulary rather than passing only because both sides were equally wrong.
packages/shared knip.json treats every export as public API, so domain logic moved from packages/shared into app-internal files becomes subject to lint:dead, which then flags unused private consts.
createTabsStore<K, R, E>(host) in packages/workbench/src/state/createTabsStore.ts owns the shared tabs-store skeleton (persistableTabs, saveIfChanged, openTab, closeTab, activateTab, moveTab, stepTab, patchTabState) that both apps' state/tabs.ts used to duplicate.
createTabsStore exposes a hook surface for per-app divergence: persistable, onCleanup, seedWorkspaceKeys, onHydrated, onOpened, onClosed, onDuplicated, fallbackWorkspaceKey, extend.
The extend(actions): E hook of createTabsStore merges extra named actions onto the store's return object, so Studio call sites such as tabsStore.findDataTab(id) work as store methods unchanged.
createOpenTerminalTab<K>() in createTerminalTabs.ts is the shared terminal-tab opener hoisted from the near-identical per-app versions.
packages/workbench/tsconfig.json exists only so bun test can resolve real (non-type-only) cross-package @shared imports from packages/workbench/src.
createLayoutStore<E>, createSettingsStore<S>() and createTerminalsStore are the shared factories for the layout, settings and terminals stores.
contextMenu.ts needs no store factory because it is identical between the two apps with no per-app divergence.
Both apps' state/terminals.ts are byte-identical, and createTerminalsStore takes no extend.
Kira Space's state/coderepos.ts local canonicalPath duplicate was folded into @shared/domain/path, which has been the single definition since P100 Part 2.
Never make a store factory's extend hook optional with a defaulted type parameter, because a call leaving it at the Record<string, never> default (by omission or by giving another type parameter explicitly) makes Pinia's setup-store return type collapse every property to never.
The extend/never collapse error surfaces far from its cause, for example as useLayoutStore(pinia).hydrateLayout not callable at a main.ts call site.
An inferred empty object type for extend works, but an explicit Record<string, never> or Record<string, unknown> type argument breaks Pinia's store typing.
extend is required in every store factory, including createTabsStore; createSettingsStore is curried on S (the app's Section union) so the inner call carries no explicit type argument and E is inferred from the real extend argument.
The P103 Part 2 plan drops a settings search feature because no search affordance exists in either settings dialog, so building one would be a feature rather than dedup.
SettingsDialog.vue was split into a shared SettingsShell.vue in packages/workbench/src/components/, generic over T extends Record<string, Record<string, unknown>>, owning the frame, nav and the baseline/draft/diffSection/pendingPatch/isDirty/isAtDefault/resetLeaf/validate engine.
Each app's settings panes live in workbench/settings/*.vue (Kira Studio 8 panes, Kira Space 4), extracted verbatim with markup, classes and data-testids unchanged, because about 331 class-based Playwright selectors depend on them.
Shared workbench components (WorkbenchShell, TitleBar, StatusBar, TabStrip, MainView) live in packages/workbench/src/components/, generic over a provided typed WorkbenchHost<K> plus named slots; each app's workbench/ files are thin wrappers that provide the host and fill the slots. No new hand-rolled primitive is added; each app's own button markup stays in the app for P104 to swap once.
createCoreControl<S, L, T>(bindings) in packages/workbench/src/bridge/createCoreControl.ts covers 20 bound-call methods across 7 services that were byte-for-byte identical between Kira Studio's studioControl and Kira Space's control objects.
The 20 shared methods are settings/layout get-all and set-plus-changed, the quit and close-window flush handshakes, the folder picker, tabsList/tabsSave, the five terminal methods, and linkOpenExternal.
CoreBindings is a structural interface, not an adapter: each app's generated @bindings/* service modules (Settings, Layout, Tabs, Lifecycle, Terminal, Files, Link) satisfy it by shape, with no per-app glue code and no change to Wails binding generation.
After the move, Kira Studio's studioControl has 94 methods left (114 total minus 20 shared) and Kira Space's spaceControl has 24 left (44 total minus 20 shared).
Kira Studio's bridge/index.ts method-count comment was stale (said 67, actually 114), so verify method counts by enumerating the real file.
In CoreBindings, the tabs.Save tabs field is typed as scalar unknown rather than unknown[], because unknown[] failed the structural check against each app's TabsService.Save taking TabRecord[] (a large discriminated union).
Typing problems in shared bindings only surface when bun run typecheck runs, not by inspection; typecheck covers 8 parallel projects.
P103 Part 2 is sequenced before P104 because ContextMenu.vue and AppTooltip.vue sit in both scopes, so deduping first means P104 reworks one shared copy once.
state/pinia.ts and state/queryClient.ts stay duplicated per app; hoisting them into a shared package was declined.
Each of state/pinia.ts and state/queryClient.ts is 5-20 lines of pure library construction with no per-app option divergence (both queryClient.ts are byte-identical), so a factory would save almost nothing.
Hoisting the Pinia singleton (export const pinia = createPinia()) into shared code collided both apps' stores onto one Pinia instance within bun test's single shared module cache, breaking 9 of Kira Space's unit tests.
A per-app factory such as createAppPinia() would avoid the collision but was rejected: the saving is marginal and a later edit could turn the function back into a shared constant and reintroduce the bug.
state/terminals.ts and editor/monaco.ts follow a pre-P103 'duplicate, don't hoist' precedent for low-value shared surfaces.
Scope left out of a phase stays out entirely and is never half-implemented (CLAUDE.md).
Part 3 is a distinct, Go-only workstream (the shell/terminal/event hoist), independent of Parts 1-2.
A wrong class or never-typed prop in a shared component fails silently in test:visual/test:ui rather than at typecheck, and test:visual alone takes minutes per iteration.
TabStrip's '+' button menu differs per app (Kira Studio: data connection/request/console; Kira Space: repo/terminal), so it is kept as a slot and never unified.
StatusBar's keep-awake toggle is Kira Studio-only, because Kira Space has no adapter connections to keep awake; it sits behind a slot only the Kira Studio wrapper fills.
The shared field-level style vocabulary (.field, .field-head, .helper-text, .field-error, .sec-label, and .settings-pane { display: contents }) lives in packages/workbench/src/workbench.css.
.section-subhead has no style rule anywhere in the repo and was deliberately left unstyled, since adding one would be new styling outside the phase's 'move code, don't add it' scope.
Keep settings panes mounted for the dialog's lifetime (v-show, not v-if), because a v-if-gated pane destroys its validator and field error on tab switch, silently re-enabling Save on an invalid draft in a hidden section.
Neither app's real settings dialog onDismiss has a discard-confirm guard; both are a plain emit('close'), unlike the plan's sketch, and it was not changed.
The settings dialog footer differs between apps: Kira Space's action wrapper has class="end" and style gap var(--kira-s-2), Kira Studio's doesn't; it is kept as an app-supplied #footer scoped slot.
Implement the whole phase first, then test once and fix what is found (CLAUDE.md).
bun run test:visual has a pre-existing baseline of 5 failing specs: connection-dialog, console, data-view, schema-dialog, workbench; they are not a regression and not recorded as a Known open item.
bun run lint:dead reports a pre-existing baseline of 6 'Duplicate exports' findings and 7 configuration hints.
Builds (bun run build and build:space) emit pre-existing advisories: INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT on monacoTheme.ts and chunk-size warnings.
The settings classes moved to the shared stylesheet (section-pane, field-error, helper-text, sec-label) are present in each app's built dist/assets/index-*.css.
Test baselines after P103 Part 2: unit tests 1535 pass across 156 files, test:ui 279 passed, test:ui:space 20 passed.

### v1.9 P103 Part 3 the Go shell, terminal and event hoist
Kira Space's internal/shell has the full per-window flush handshake; shell/quit.go and shell/closeflush.go differ between the two apps only by the bridge import path.
Kira Studio's Dialogs covers three panels versus Kira Space's one, AttachSystemWake is Studio-only, and Studio's menu is fuller.
internal/buildinfo is not hoisted because its Version is an -ldflags -X target on each app's own import path, so one shared symbol could not carry two versions.
storage/migrations/embed.go is not hoisted because //go:embed resolves only inside its own package directory.
Bound Wails services are not hoisted, because a bound service's types drive binding generation and therefore the frontend's @bindings/* paths; their shared dependencies move instead. internal/bridge/{lifecycle.go,link.go,tabs.go,layout.go} stay for this reason.
config/paths.go is left untouched because it is already parameterized through internal/kirapaths and only three literals remain.
Each app's shell residue lives in apps/<app>/internal/appshell, a name distinct from repo-root internal/shell so one main.go can import both without an alias.
Repo-root internal/shell holds the generic shell behind four seams: Config, Signaller, WindowStore, and WindowRecord/WindowBounds.
internal/terminal is hoisted to repo root; the app-specific TERM_PROGRAM strings became package vars TermProgram and TermProgramVersion, which each app's main.go must set before the first Registry.Open.
internal/appevent holds the Emitter interface, the Events core (Signal/SignalTo/Broadcast, NewEvents) and the six channel constants; each app's appcore.Emitter is a type alias to appevent.Emitter and each bridge.Events embeds *appevent.Events.
Kira Studio's bridge.Events keeps its own emit appcore.Emitter field for Attach's five producer subscriptions and SettingsChanged, which the shared core does not expose.
AttachCloseFlush no longer takes a separate emit parameter, since NewCloseFlushCoordinator takes the Signaller directly.
Both apps' main.go use a windowStore/toShellWindowRecord adapter to convert each app's storage/model types into shell's shared seams.
Both apps' layering_test.go exemption was renamed from internal/shell to internal/appshell; internal/appshell is the sole remaining bridge-import exemption in TestDomainPackagesDoNotImportBridge in both apps.
storage/model/time.go moved to repo-root internal/kiratime, and all model.NowISO/FormatISO/ParseISO call sites were retargeted to kiratime across 28 files in both apps.
config env.go, prod.go and prod_default.go moved to repo-root internal/kirapaths; each app's config package keeps one-line wrappers such as IsDev() calling kirapaths.IsDev().
The 4-commit Part 3 sequence is not bisect-safe: 99b713e, 85a57e7 and 2fc80da leave the tree not fully building until f94c5e0.
internal/storage/db.go, storage/migrate.go, storage/model/window.go and bridge/settings.go are not shared in Part 3 because of real per-app differences: config.KiraHome() vs KiraSpaceHome(), Studio-only Mode/NormalizeMode, Studio-only PushCacheConfig.
Each app keeps its own Events/Deps residue after the shared core of internal/bridge/events.go and the Emitter alias in internal/appcore/deps.go were hoisted.
In the shell package's quit test, the test must wait for flushThenQuit's async goroutine to populate the pending map before calling q.Flushed, otherwise the ack races a nil map and is swallowed as an unknown key.
docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md documents a --no-verify exception to the pre-commit hook for a fresh worktree, where typecheck fails only on missing Wails-generated @bindings/* modules until scripts/setup.sh codegen runs for both apps.
The .githooks/pre-push hook runs go build ./..., bun run lint:go, then bun run lint:dead; lint:dead fails in a fresh worktree on unresolved @bindings/* imports, and the hook header documents --no-verify for that case.
Server-mode binaries (go build -tags server) need a frontend/dist/index.html to satisfy //go:embed all:frontend/dist; a gitignored placeholder works. They run with KIRA_HOME/KIRA_SPACE_HOME, KIRA_INSECURE_SECRETS=1 and WAILS_SERVER_HOST set, and answer GET /health.
Implement the whole phase, then test once, and fix pre-existing failures unless genuinely outside the phase's scope, in which case name them (CLAUDE.md).

### v1.9 P103 Part 4 `model.Settings` composition, the settings schema split, and the audit
Repo-root package internal/appsettings holds the shared settings section types (Appearance, Git, AdvancedCore and Patch variants), defaults, validators and SQL helpers (Leaf/LeafValid, Upsert*/Read*); each app composes its own Settings and its own GetAll/Set.
Both apps embed appsettings.AdvancedCore/AdvancedCorePatch into AdvancedSettings/AdvancedPatch; regenerated Wails bindings were unchanged (gitLogLevel stays a flat string/string|null field in generated TS), so the embedding was kept.
Bindings are regenerated with wails3 task common:generate:bindings -f; the Taskfile's generate:bindings target already passes -clean=true, and -clean is not a valid flag at the wails3 task level.
The frontend never imports generated binding models directly; bridge/index.ts reads Settings/SettingsPatch from each app's own state/settingsDomain.ts, narrowed with trust<T>.
packages/shared/domain/settings.ts holds only shared settings pieces: row density, FONT_SIZE_RANGE, FETCH_AUTO_INTERVAL_MINUTES_RANGE, appearance and git schemas, gitLogLevel schema, and HTTP_VERSIONS/httpVersionSchema/HttpVersion.
HTTP_VERSIONS/httpVersionSchema/HttpVersion stay in packages/shared because packages/shared/domain/http.ts depends on them, and a shared package may not import apps/*/frontend/src/state/*.
Each app owns frontend/src/state/settingsDomain.ts composing its own settingsSchema, settingsPatchSchema and defaultSettings: Kira Studio has 8 sections (appearance, data, cache, advanced, git, api, dbMcp, claudeCode), Kira Space has 3 (appearance, advanced, git).
Kira Space has no data, cache, api, dbMcp or claudeCode settings sections and its frontend does not reference them, since its Go backend never sends them.
Knip treats packages/shared as an always-exempt entry tier (entry: **/*.ts), while app-local files are usage-traced, so moving schemas into app settingsDomain.ts exposes unused exports; raw zod schema consts there are not exported.
The lint:dead baseline is 6 duplicate-export pairs and 7 configuration hints with zero unused-export findings.
Kira Studio's Git settings section is vestigial and could not be removed Go-side in P100 Part 1; the schema split makes removing it a one-commit change.
Fix a failing lint finding on the spot, per CLAUDE.md.
A shared package never imports apps/*/internal/..., per CLAUDE.md.
Each app (Kira Studio and Kira Space) keeps its own state/pinia.ts, because a single shared createPinia() instance made both apps' same-named stores ('tabs', 'settings', ...) share one Pinia registry in the shared bun test process.
bun run test:unit runs both apps' spec files in one Bun process, so state shared across the apps leaks between their tests, even though each app's real runtime is a separate Vite/Wails bundle with its own module graph.
state/queryClient.ts was hoisted to packages/workbench, as it was byte-identical in both apps.
state/terminals.ts is intentionally code-identical in both apps as a by-design host-function shim.
internal/bridge/{lifecycle,link}.go and internal/buildinfo/buildinfo.go remain duplicated across the apps as declined moves in the §2.3 decline table; internal/config/env.go stays duplicated as a one-line wrapper.
The layering test TestDomainPackagesDoNotImportBridge runs in both apps (27 subtests each); its exemption sets were not grown.
biome.json has a noRestrictedImports block forbidding packages/workbench/** from importing **/apps/** or @/*, so the workbench package imports nothing app-local.
Every component has exactly one <script> block across both frontends and packages workbench, theme, git-ui and kira-ui.
Never change Pinia store ids 'tabs', 'settings', 'layout', 'contextMenu', 'terminals', 'confirmDialog', 'tooltip' (CLAUDE.md store-id-stability rule).
The P103 refactor cut net 19,252 lines across both apps' frontend/src and internal/ (6,076 insertions, 25,328 deletions), well past the ~3,800 target.
The ui-timing project of test:ui (Kira Studio) intermittently misses perf.spec.ts's p95 scroll-frame gate (<80) and slick-grid.spec.ts's 150ms select-all gate under sandbox load; the thresholds are documented as sandbox-cadence-tolerant.
The apps/kira-space/internal/gitsock package has load-sensitive flaky tests (fsnotify timing, e.g. TestMatrix_M3_FullIndependence and TestRevoke_DoesNotDisturbAnotherClient); whole-package reruns can hang to Go's 10-minute timeout.
bun run test:visual has 5 known pre-existing failures (~1% pixel ratio each); a sixth failure would be a regression.
The pre-commit hook runs bun run lint and bun run typecheck; the pre-push hook runs go build ./..., golangci-lint run and bun run lint:dead.

### v1.9 P104 Delete every hand-rolled UI primitive for shadcn-vue's own components, and normalize spacing/sizing onto Tailwind's d...
P104 deletes every hand-rolled UI primitive (theme/primitives/*.vue wrappers) and repoints call sites directly at shadcn-vue components in components/ui/*, with no wrapper layer and no hand-rolled fallback for any component.
The no-fallback rule is the user's explicit override of P99's precedent, not a default judgment call of the chapter.
P104 merges the primitive swap and the spacing/sizing normalization onto Tailwind's default scale into one pass so each file is touched once, not by two phases.
P99 fetched 18 shadcn-vue components into components/ui/ but never called them from app-level files, and it preserved 425 arbitrary-bracket [...var(--kira-*)...] spacing/sizing utilities across 115 files verbatim under its pixel-identical gate.
theme/base.css's @theme block maps only --color-* and --radius-kira-* tokens, not spacing, so P104 extends it to a coherent set as needed.
P104 re-opens three cases P99 declined (Checkbox, workbench/ContextMenu.vue, AppTooltip.vue); each must be solved by composing or modifying reka-ui/shadcn-vue primitives, not kept hand-rolled.
CheckboxRoot renders <button role="checkbox"> rather than a real <input>, which breaks Playwright .check() calls and native form semantics; P104 accepts this and updates about 331 class-based Playwright selectors.
The point-anchored context menu is built by layering a positioning wrapper over PopoverAnchor/DropdownMenuContent, because reka-ui's model is trigger-anchored while the app's context menu is a point-anchored singleton.
The tooltip's rearm-delay/singleton behavior is built on TooltipRoot/TooltipProvider instead of the former directive-plus-singleton AppTooltip.
P104 allows styling and DOM shape to change where the swap requires it, as long as functionality is preserved. New test:visual baselines are recorded per changed surface with the reason stated per baseline, not chased back to zero diff.
ConfirmDialog.vue's Dialog was bound only by :open, so it claimed an early fixed DOM position from app boot and stayed under any dialog opened later, because reka's DialogPortal teleports content to the end of body at open time. Gating the Dialog itself on v-if fixes this, mirroring DbMcpApprovalDialog.vue.
Reka's TooltipTrigger opens on pointermove, not pointerenter/mouseenter, so a .hover() at the pointer's already-current position dispatches no real pointer event and does not open the tooltip.
The shared tooltip test helper (tests/ui/support/tooltip.ts, assertTooltipShows/tooltipContent) uses a move-to-a-neutral-corner-then-hover retry loop bounded by an overall deadline, because a single hover or a wait held past 600ms did not reliably open reka's tooltip.
The live data-kira-tip attribute remains only for the two tooltipAttrs() writers (SlickGridHost.vue, ConsoleSlickGrid.vue), the grid-header bridge (AttributeTooltip.vue, state/tooltip.ts), and tooltips.spec.ts's geometry test; there is no other live consumer.
After P104, 15 components/ui/* sets survive, each with outside importers; scroll-area and context-menu were deleted because they had no honest consumer. ARCHITECTURE.md line 35 states 17 fetched, 15 surviving.
primitives.css has 152 selector rules covering 53 classes, each named in ARCHITECTURE.md line 35.
The bun run lint:dead baseline is 6 duplicate-export pairs and 7 configuration hints, which existed before P104.
The P104 plan is at docs/v1.9/plans/P104-primitive-swap.md.
The Studio UI Playwright config uses fullyParallel: true and workers: '100%', which causes cross-file worker-contention flakes in this sandbox; the failing tests pass when re-run in isolation.
Visual baselines captured outside CI's ubuntu-latest image show glyph-rendering noise (about 0.01 diff ratio), documented in ARCHITECTURE.md; it is not chased to zero.
A --color-muted custom-property collision exists between base.css (foreground-gray) and shadcn-bridge.css (shadcn's semantic background token). It predates P104 and is tracked as follow-up P110 in docs/v1.9/SPEC.md.
P104 re-recorded all 5 Studio visual baselines (workbench, console, data-view, connection-dialog, schema-dialog) because the primitive swap changed their rendering. The command is bun run test:visual:update:studio.
The data-view checkbox is now CheckboxRoot's <button role="checkbox">, not a native <input>. The workbench splitter is now reka's Splitter*, and schema-dialog tree rows use @tanstack/vue-virtual.
With the a11y: off override for **/*.vue removed, biome check reports 254 errors across 13 rules, against P99's 255 findings across 86 files. P105 starts from this 254/13-rule breakdown. The largest class is noNoninteractiveTabindex (81), then noStaticElementInteractions (62) and useKeyWithClickEvents (46).
Every components/ui component ships type="button" and correct roles, which sharply reduced useButtonType and useFocusableInteractive findings. Adopting ui/label reduced noLabelWithoutControl to 4.
noNoninteractiveTabindex is the largest a11y finding class because of the <span tabindex="0"> disabled-hover wrappers described in §6.3, which exist so tooltips work on disabled controls.
The pre-commit hook runs bun run lint and bun run typecheck. bun run lint consists of Biome plus check-tokens.sh.

### v1.9 P105 Fix the remaining `.vue` accessibility findings and delete the `a11y: off` override
P105 is sequenced last, after P104's full primitive swap to shadcn-vue components, because fixing a11y findings earlier would mean hand-fixing markup that P104 deletes and rewrites.
P97 turned lint/a11y/* off for **/*.vue via a biome.json override rather than hand-fixing 255 findings across 85 files that P99's shadcn-vue migration was expected to rewrite.
P99 only wired reka-ui into the existing hand-rolled wrapper layer rather than swapping to shadcn-vue's own components, so the a11y finding count stayed effectively unmoved (255 across 86 files).
Deleting the a11y: off entry from the biome.json **/*.vue override is P105's own acceptance test; the override keeps only its unrelated useVueHyphenatedAttributes and noNonNullAssertion entries.
P105 was originally landed as P101 and renumbered to P105 to keep table position and number in lockstep, per CLAUDE.md's renumbering rule.
P105 landed as 11 commits merged as 6febddb, fixing 254 findings across 13 rules and 95 files to 0, done sequentially by one subagent because findings share files across rules.
The 79 duplicate span tabindex="0" disabled-tooltip wrappers now use the shared component packages/theme/src/components/ui/tooltip/TooltipDisabledTrigger.vue, which carries a single noNoninteractiveTabindex suppression.
Container-level listeners (keydown, contextmenu, drag-surface) on layout-only divs were moved off templates onto VueUse's useEventListener to clear noStaticElementInteractions.
packages/kira-ui/src/KuiColumnResizeHandle.vue, extracted from CommitGrid.vue's role="separator"/arrow-key pattern, is used by StreamView.vue's ten column-resize handles and CommitGrid.vue's three; StreamView gained keyboard column resizing.
KuiColumnResizeHandle.vue carries a useSemanticElements suppression because an hr cannot carry aria-valuenow or focus.
Dismiss scrims/backdrops (GitPanel, ConsoleSavedMenu, FilterHistoryMenu, FkPreviewPopover, CommandPalette, KuiDialog) use VueUse's onClickOutside instead of click handlers and are aria-hidden="true".
List/tree/menu rows became button type="button" where nothing nests inside them; otherwise they get the implied role (option/treeitem/menuitem), tabindex and keydown enter/space handlers, with data-testid preserved verbatim.
Tab-close controls in ConsoleView.vue, ExplainResultView.vue and TabStrip.vue are now sibling button type="button" elements instead of focusable children nested inside the tab button.
All autofocus attributes were removed; focus relies on KuiDialog's useModalFocus first-focusable-element behavior, with explicit VueUse useFocus/watch where the control is not first.
The InputGroup role="group" was converted to a fieldset, so tests select InputGroup as fieldset rather than div.
InputGroupAddon.vue's click listener is attached via useTemplateRef and VueUse's useEventListener instead of a template @click, avoiding a biome-ignore suppression and following CLAUDE.md's VueUse rule.
Only 2 biome-ignore lint/a11y suppressions exist in apps and packages (TooltipDisabledTrigger.vue noNoninteractiveTabindex and KuiColumnResizeHandle.vue useSemanticElements), under the plan's §17 budget of 3, and the forbidden bound :tabindex="0" evasion never landed.

### v1.9 P106 Normalize every dual-app npm script's naming to explicit `:studio`/`:space` scoping
Root package.json dual-app scripts use explicit scoping: Kira Studio-only scripts carry a :studio suffix, because unscoped names misleadingly read as the default when there are two apps.
The Studio script renames were full renames with no compatibility alias, per CLAUDE.md's no-backwards-compat-shim rule.
Fifteen root scripts got a :studio suffix: dev, predev, build, build:test, package, prepackage, typecheck:tests, typecheck:web, typecheck:unit, typecheck:api-core, test:ui, test:visual, test:visual:update, test:ipc:fe, test:e2e-real (e.g. test:ui→test:ui:studio). Studio-only scripts with no Space counterpart also get the suffix for consistency.
packages/api-core is imported only by apps/kira-studio/frontend, so typecheck:api-core is Studio-only and was renamed typecheck:api-core:studio.
Composite scripts that call a renamed script via bun run must be updated with the rename; e.g. test:ui:studio calls build:test:studio, test:e2e-real:studio calls build:studio, and typecheck calls the four Studio typecheck sub-scripts by their new names.
Genuinely repo-wide scripts stay unscoped: test:go, test:unit, test:compat, test:matrix, lint, lint:go, lint:dead, lint:all, typecheck, format, verify:packaging, generate:wire, because each spans both apps and scoping them would be false naming in reverse.
verify:packaging checks both apps/kira-studio and apps/kira-space bundles; generate:wire regenerates both packages/shared/protocol/wire.fbs and packages/git-ipc/schema/gitwire.fbs.
Space-only scripts build:vscode, package:vscode, test:webview and typecheck:git deliberately remain without a :space suffix, because the rename scope covered only Studio-only scripts.
Frontend-local bun run dev/build lines in both apps' build/Taskfile.yml and build/darwin/Taskfile.yml (dir: frontend) refer to the frontend's own package.json scripts, not the renamed root scripts, so they stay unchanged.
The .github/workflows/ files cannot be pushed per CLAUDE.md, so workflow changes are delivered as patches in docs/pending-changes/ (pr.yml and release.yml), not applied directly.
P106 rename hunks for workflow files were merged into the existing pending-changes patch for each file rather than adding a second conflicting patch: pr.yml (build, test:ui, test:ipc:fe, test:visual) and release.yml (package→package:studio).
Historical SPEC.md and plans/*.md prose is not required to be rewritten for renamed scripts, since it documents what a past phase ran under its own name at the time.
Per CLAUDE.md, fast checks are cheap and fine per-commit, while an expensive suite runs once near phase end.

### v1.9 P107 Fable-audited duplication findings and their consolidation: near-duplicate logic and flow-level duplication across th...
P107 audit and consolidation run as one phase by explicit one-time user instruction: a Fable agent performs the audit and writes the findings document, which is the phase's plan, with no separate methodology plan.
P107 completes only once both the findings document and the consolidation are committed; the original row stopped at findings, and the user redirected it mid-phase before any consolidation phase was opened.
P107 scope is the whole implementation (both apps' Go and TypeScript/Vue, every packages/*), covering tier 1 (small helpers or near-identical functions mergeable by parameters) and tier 2 (flows or chains mirroring another chain, extractable into a shared composable, component or package function).
Each P107 finding must carry file:line for every instance, what is duplicated, what differs, and one concrete named consolidation shape; declines must name the requirement that keeps the copies apart.
P107 excludes generated code (page/wire, gitwire, shared/protocol/wire, git-ipc/src/generated) and cross-language mirrors (mask.go/mask.ts, page/encode.go/encodeFrame.ts, queryplan Go vs planParsers TS) from duplication counting.
P107 consolidation is implemented by one sequential Sonnet subagent in the findings doc's §4 order, with one commit per finding, fast checks per commit, full suites once near the end, and a closing re-run of the exact-body sweep requiring every remaining group to be a named decline.
P107 is sequenced last so it surveys a tree already carrying P103's cross-app hoist and P104's primitive consolidation, and it is independent of P106.
P107 duplicate detection uses CodeGraph index sweeps over .codegraph/codegraph.db (S1 body hash, S2 name collisions, S3 callee fingerprint LCS ratio ≥0.7 with ≥6 callees, S4 file-pair diff), verified per candidate with codegraph_explore and comment-blind diff.
Iteration 2 of P107 added S5 (K=6 normalized-line shingles, maximal repeated blocks ≥8 lines, Vue templates included) and S6 (MinHash/LSH, identifier-blind 4-gram token shingles, functions ≥8 lines, Jaccard ≥0.5) because S1 sees only exact bodies and S3 only call order, missing near-identical blocks inside larger functions and repeated Vue template regions.
P107 iteration 1 (findings in docs/v1.9/plans/P107-duplication-findings.md, tree at c2ce9969) found 25 tier-2 findings, 28 tier-1 findings and 12 declines, and was implemented as ~62 per-finding refactor/fix commits merged into v1.9-integration.
