P107 iteration 2 (docs/v1.9/plans/P107-duplication-findings-iter2.md, at ad5c4959) found 28 tier-2, 14 tier-1 findings and 13 declines; 10 were residue of narrowly landed iteration-1 findings, 2 were thin wrappers around iteration-1 helpers, 30 were new, mostly via S5/S6.
In P107 iteration 2 the disabled-tooltip wrapper and the column/pane resize handle were excluded as in-flight because P105 was concurrently extracting them, and were picked up after P105 merged (I2-18, I2-21).
P107 closing-sweep addendum found five candidates (I2-43 to I2-47); four were fixed outright (openTrackedTab, resolveReadGated, sumChunkBytes, sequencerArgs), and in I2-47 WalkFor/ReviewWalkFor were merged into walkForSlot while alreadyHeld was declined because it uses a different map with a different value type.
Every P107 decline names the concrete requirement keeping near-duplicates apart, such as a differing type set, differing error contract, or a generated-code or cross-language boundary, never mere similarity.
Iteration 1 declined typeClassFor ×4 as engine-specific type tables, and declined bound Wails bridge service struct types because P103 §2.3 already covers their binding generation.

### v1.9 P108 Part 1 Whole-codebase code review pre-plan — chunking and stream assignment (review itself: one Opus reviewer per chunk acro...
P108 review uses one single Opus reviewer agent per chunk doing freeform 'any kind of issue or bug' review with care for edge cases, not CLAUDE.md's three-parallel-agent Code review recipe; this is a one-time user-instructed deviation not to be generalized to later phases.
The P108 pre-plan and each chunk's review plan are written by the standing Opus planner, which is CLAUDE.md's default.
Each P108 review chunk is a cohesive subsystem's own files plus its direct callers and callees (one hop out on both edges of the call graph), so the review confirms correctness across the chunk's immediate boundaries.
P108 runs 2 parallel streams, matching CLAUDE.md's P103/P104 2-stream default. A chunk whose one-hop callers/callees overlap a chunk still open in the other stream stays in the same stream or waits, so both streams never edit the same files at once.
Within each P108 stream, chunks run one at a time: an Opus agent writes the chunk review plan, a single Opus agent reviews and reports findings without fixing, then a Sonnet subagent fixes every finding and commits. The next chunk starts only after that fix pass is committed.
P108 is split into the pre-plan (Part 1) plus 19 chunks as Parts 2-20. Stream A (Kira Studio, opened by the shared Go base) is Parts 2-12; stream B (Kira Space, opened by the shared frontend base) is Parts 13-20.
P108 has two cross-stream gates: Part 14 waits for Part 2, and Part 6 (the first Studio chunk holding TS) waits for Part 13.
Edit scope for P108: after a shared base closes, only its owning stream edits it; a fix the other stream needs there is handed to the orchestrator as a fix-only step in the owning stream. The same rule applies to the Go appsettings against shared/domain/settings.ts mirror.
P108 is appended last, after P107, so it reviews the tree in the shape the other phases in the chapter leave it; reviewing code P99-P107 were still changing would re-find what they already fix.
apps/kira-studio/tests/support/encodeFrame.ts imports flatbuffers, which root package.json never declared. Bun 1.3's isolated linker resolves declared dependencies only, so the pre-commit typecheck failed with TS2307 on a fresh install.
flatbuffers is now a root devDependency pinned to packages/shared's version, with a knip.json ignoreDependencies entry because no knip workspace scans apps/kira-studio/tests.
The lint:dead baseline is 6 duplicate exports and 7 hints.
The P108 review is split into 19 chunks rather than about 10 because the tree is about 370k lines of Go/TS/Vue with tests (2,061 files), and ten chunks would average about 37k lines each, too much for one reviewer's edge-case pass. The chunks follow dense call-graph clusters and cut none.
Kira Studio and Kira Space have zero real imports between them, in Go or TS. Every shared edge runs through Go internal/*, the TS workbench/theme/kira-ui packages, ten packages/shared/domain files and protocol/events.ts; the rest of packages/shared is Studio's alone.
CodeGraph's TS name resolution over-links, so cross-app dependency checks must be verified against import lines.
Streams are split by app rather than by language because a Go/TS split would make every IPC boundary cross streams. Load is uneven (about 217k lines against 152k), and rebalancing would put a Studio chunk beside its own one-hop neighbors in the other stream, which is forbidden.
Each app's cross-language mirrors (page wire, mask, git wire, git RPC contract) stay in one stream, and the two-sided ones in one chunk.
Chunk order is bottom-up within each stream (storage, adapters, engine, bridge; git process layer, ops/review, session, RPC, UI logic, components, hosts). The exception is Studio's state/**, a callee of every view, which lands with the shell last and is re-reviewed there.
Two merges were taken in the chunking: the Space desktop host with the VS Code extension, and the console with the per-kind data views. Four further merges were declined on size (about 32-41k lines each).
The codegraph MCP server exposes no codegraph_node tool.

### v1.9 P108 Part 2 Shared Go base and repo tooling
terminal.Session.Close is bounded after SIGKILL by a second wait (closeKillWait): it waits, force-closes ptmx, waits once more, then logs and returns instead of blocking, because a job-control shell's child can sit outside the signalled process group or ignore SIGHUP/SIGKILL and leave readLoop's ptmx.Read blocked.
Force-closing ptmx hangs up the slave but is not guaranteed to unblock Read on darwin, so Session.Close stays bounded regardless.
Registry.CloseAll closes every terminal session concurrently, so quit costs about one grace period total instead of one per open terminal.
In closeflush.go, the flushing flag is reset with flushing.Store(false) right after win.Hide() on the last-window path; this is safe because Hide() never emits WindowClosing. Without the reset, a Dock re-show followed by a second close dropped the pending flush.
CI workflow fixes cannot be edited directly in this setup; they land as patches in docs/pending-changes/ (e.g. .github__workflows__pr.yml.patch).
The pr.yml checks and container-tests jobs run bun run build:space beside every build:studio step, because Kira Space's frontend was never built in CI and both apps' //go:embed all:frontend/dist therefore never compiled there.
The gitclient darwin test path in the pr.yml patch is apps/kira-space/internal/gitclient, since P100 moved it from kira-studio.
verify-packaging.sh check S5 reads both package:studio and package:space script keys, since P106 split the old "package" key.
generate-wire.sh writes gitwire Go output to apps/kira-space/internal/gitwire (namespace gitwire), since P100 moved the package from kira-studio.
notify.OrderedEmitter.Emit holds a dedicated private emit mutex across the whole check-and-deliver, so a later higher-sequence call cannot deliver before an earlier one lands stale. The mutex is not shared with callers' state locks. Its subscribers include dbmcp.ApprovalBroker and gitsock.Broker.
The production subscriber of OrderedEmitter (bridge/events.go Attach) only forwards to Wails EventsEmit and never calls Emit synchronously, so the emit mutex cannot self-deadlock.
rpcstream.Session activeWork stores an activeEntry pointer (wrapping the CancelFunc) and creditGates compares its *creditGate pointer before delete, so cleanup is keyed by identity, not id alone; this prevents a late completion deleting a later request's entry after id reuse.
The one real RPC client, packages/git-ipc/src/rpc.ts, issues request ids monotonically.
sqlitex.BuildDSN escapes the bytes ?, # and % in the path explicitly, because modernc opens with SQLITE_OPEN_URI and would treat them as URI syntax in a KIRA_HOME/KIRA_SPACE_HOME-derived path. net/url's URL.String() is not used since it adds a "//" authority marker that breaks relative paths.
pathsafe.ValidateRelPath's not-exist fallback does an Lstat check and rejects a symlink leaf outright, so a dangling symlink is not accepted like a genuinely missing leaf (TOCTOU risk for codeworkspace's read-only callers).
biome.json's noRestrictedImports override targets apps/kira-space/frontend/src/repo, since P100 moved it from apps/kira-studio.
ipcerr.Error.Error() retries json.Marshal with Details: nil before falling back to the bare Message, so the code the renderer's control.ts wrapper branches on is kept. Marshal can fail because Details is a json.RawMessage that may hold invalid JSON.
startupfail.Report's stderr headline uses r.info.AppName instead of a hardcoded kira-studio-shell, because Reporter is per-app since P100 Part 1.
In this repo the .githooks/pre-commit hook runs bun run lint and bun run typecheck, and the pre-push lint uses golangci-lint built via scripts/install-golangci-lint.sh.
Part 2 of P108 owns internal/** (all 20 packages), most scripts/*.sh, .githooks, .claude/hooks, root package.json scripts, biome.json, knip.json, .golangci.yml, root tsconfig files and .github/workflows (fixes via docs/pending-changes/); every Go chunk in both apps one-hops into internal/*, so it closes first.

### v1.9 P108 Part 3 Studio persistence, secrets and connection lifecycle
preconnect.Supervisor.Start sets cmd.Stderr to a plain io.Writer feeding the tail tracker (not StderrPipe) and cmd.WaitDelay = killGrace, so Wait() is bounded even if a backgrounded helper outside the process group (setsid, daemon(3)) keeps stderr open.
killEntry's wait after SIGKILL is bounded: it gives up after killGrace and logs, because SIGTERM/SIGKILL to -pid never reaches a process outside the process group.
VariablesRepo.Upsert takes value as *string; nil leaves the stored value untouched, mirroring connections.Input.Password. Needed because a secret's list projection is always "", so renaming an unrevealed secret would otherwise wipe it.
VariablesUpsertArgs.Value is a pointer and returns BadRequest when absent on create.
VariableSetView.vue keeps a per-draft valueTouched flag so only a real edit sends a value to Upsert.
connections validateMode rejects URIs whose password contains an unencoded /, ? or # (uriHasAmbiguousPassword) with a BadRequest asking for percent-encoding, because findAuthority ends the authority at the first such character and the password would be stored unencrypted.
Each connections Connect(id) attempt carries its own cancellable context; Disconnect and Remove cancel it first, and attemptConnect checks ctx.Err() after each racing step and unwinds instead of finalizing connected.
preconnect.Supervisor.Start takes a ctx parameter; cancellation during the 2s settle wait kills the just-spawned process directly, since Stop cannot see entries not yet tracked in s.entries.
destinationUnchanged uses zeroExemptFields to clear all exempt ConnectionFields members on copies of both sides, then reflect.DeepEqual, so any newly added field is gated by default.
redactURLCredentials, once a real :// is found, redacts userinfo unconditionally up to the next @; without one (e.g. jdbc:sqlite:/path) it keeps the conservative check so a coincidental path @ is left alone.
redactQueryCredentials masks any URL query param whose key contains pass, pwd, secret or token, case-insensitively.
keyring_darwin.go's duplicate-item re-query path must apply the same len(Data) == keyBytes check as the main path, otherwise a wrong-length key panics in aes.NewCipher at startup instead of reporting storage unavailable.
keyring_darwin.go is built only with darwin && cgo, so it cannot be built or tested in the Linux sandbox.
MaskRules CopyForConnection lists and inserts rules inside one transaction, calling mintID once per row, to avoid ids/rules count mismatch under concurrent rule changes.
connections Duplicate calls emitListChanged before returning either post-row-insert failure (mask-rule copy or McpEnabled write), since the new connection row is already committed.
capFilterText truncates where_text on a UTF-8 rune boundary (truncateUTF8ToBoundary, mirroring internal/page/scratch.go) and drops an oversized order_by_json to nil rather than truncating, because truncated JSON fails List's decode guard and drops the whole row.
Part 3 of v1.9 P108 owns apps/kira-studio/internal/{storage,secrets,localauth,connections,preconnect,datagrip}; it is pure Go and the bottom of Studio's Go call graph (adapters to storage is 679 edges, mostly model), so it is reviewed before its callers.
In a shared checkout, stage commits by explicit file path and never with git add -A or git add ., because other sessions' uncommitted files would be swept into the commit.
In a shared checkout, scope git stash by pathspec (git stash push -- <paths>), because a full-tree stash captures other sessions' dirty files.
Never bypass .githooks/pre-commit (bun run lint and bun run typecheck) with --no-verify.
bun run lint:go runs golangci-lint, built via scripts/install-golangci-lint.sh, the same way as the pre-push hook.

### v1.9 P108 Part 4 Studio DB adapters I — adapter core and SQL engines
The shared comment/quote scanner (scanQuote) treats backslash as an escape only inside Postgres E'...'/e'...' strings (isEStringOpen, word-boundary-checked); previously a backslash-escaped quote let trailing string content be read as a real -- comment, smuggling a second statement past ClassifySQL and AssertNoTransactionEscalation.
ClassifySQL and classifyClickHouseSQL run the embedded-semicolon guard against the raw statement, never the comment-stripped text, because comment-stripping can be fooled by backslash-quote ambiguity; worst case is a false ClassUnknown, never a false-safe read.
AssertNoTransactionEscalation has a fail-closed backstop (AssertNoHiddenStatement/quoteHasBackslash) that rejects any quoted run containing a raw backslash, because standard_conforming_strings=off makes even plain '...' strings honour backslash escapes and this cannot be detected at parse time.
For read-only Postgres connections, Read and Count run assertReadOnlyFilterSortSafe over the grid filter and text-sort clause.
RunWithAbortRace returns on Stop/cancel before its background goroutine stops touching the connection, so Acquire's release and the detached ROLLBACK/COMMIT cleanup must wait for it; each connEntry (postgres trackedConn, mysqlfamily Entry) has its own inFlight WaitGroup, separate from the adapter-wide tracker Disconnect uses.
Acquire's release Waits on the per-connection inFlight before unlocking the per-connection mutex, and mutate's rollback and console's read-only-wrap COMMIT Wait before issuing cleanup; otherwise cleanup races the still-running goroutine on Postgres's *pgx.Conn or queues behind it on MySQL/MariaDB and blows its 5s deadline.
adapters.ConnSet has a closed flag under its own mu so a dial in flight when CloseAll runs cannot land in the conns map afterward; ConnSet.Current(key) is re-checked after locking entry.mu in Acquire, retrying on mismatch, to handle LRU eviction closing an entry between Get and Lock.
The relational, clickhouse and sqlite adapters guard their connection handle fields (connSet/cfg/primaryDatabase/readOnly; clickhouse handle; sqlite db/file/readOnly) with locked get/set/clear accessors, because Connect/Disconnect previously wrote them unlocked against in-flight ops.
QueryTracker.TrackerFor does inFlight.Add(1) under the same lock that checks/sets a draining flag, to avoid WaitGroup misuse against a concurrent Drain.
Disconnect in postgres, mysqlfamily and clickhouse takes a QueryTracker.Snapshot() of tracked opIDs and cancels each server-side before calling the ctx-bounded Drain(ctx), so Disconnect no longer blocks for the longest running query; Drain's background goroutine keeps running so the tracker still clears. Sqlite mirrors this with local sqlite3_interrupt via ctx.
ClickHouse's http.Client has no overall Timeout (a 60s one failed long queries client-side without sending KILL QUERY); connection setup is bounded by http.Transport DialContext and TLSHandshakeTimeout (10s each), and cancellation is ctx-driven plus KILL QUERY on cancel.
adapterhost's reconnect ordering (Router.Connect's existing.Disconnect outside RunOp, Router.Disconnect serialization against other ops) is out of scope for Part 4 and deferred to Part 6, which reviews that package.
Postgres buildConfig sets connConfig.Fallbacks = nil whenever it overrides Host/Port or TLSConfig, because environment-derived (PG*) fallbacks would let pgconn silently connect to another host or downgrade to plaintext regardless of the user's explicit sslmode.
In MySQL/MariaDB, BIT columns are classified as binaryType (not numberType) in typeClassFor, so BinaryColumnsOf recognizes them and NewParamRenderer decodes the 0x<hex> display text back to raw bytes on edit.
SQLite PRIMARY KEY does not imply NOT NULL (composite or non-INTEGER single-column PK on a rowid table can hold NULL), so getReadTarget checks the PK with pkIsKeysetEligible and falls back to a unique key or rowid; WITHOUT ROWID PKs and a single-column INTEGER PRIMARY KEY (rowid alias) are exempt because table_xinfo's notnull flag misreports them.
SQLite catalog queries must be schema-qualified because default resolution lets an ATTACHed/TEMP table shadow a main-schema one: pragmas table_xinfo/index_list/index_info/foreign_key_list take schema as second argument and sqlite_master is schema-quoted.
pragma_table_list takes only a single table-name argument with no schema-scoping form, so getReadTarget filters on its schema output column via WHERE.
WhereClause puts the closing paren of the filter on its own line, so a filter ending in a -- line comment cannot comment out the paren or keyset text appended after it.
Sort direction must be exactly lowercase asc or desc: ComputeEffectiveOrder (via validateRequestedTerms) and ClickHouse computeOrderBySql reject anything else with CodeQuery, because BuildKeysetPredicate compares the lowercase literal while BuildOrderBy uppercases, so an upper-case direction would silently mispage.
rejectDSNMetacharacters rejects ?, # and % in a SQLite DSN path, because SQLite URI parsing percent-decodes the path and could open a different file than the one assertFileExists confirmed.
scripts/demo-dbs/docker-compose.yml binds every demo database port mapping to 127.0.0.1, since the demo databases use trivial passwords.
Keyset boundary arithmetic is covered by table-driven unit tests, which is CLAUDE.md's test-bar exception.
In a shared checkout with concurrent sessions, verify each commit with git log and git show --stat against the intended file list right after committing, because another session's concurrent commit can sweep in or block your changes.
Confirm regression tests for parser-with-interacting-rules and keyset-boundary-arithmetic logic fail against the pre-fix code before landing, per CLAUDE.md's test bar.

### v1.9 P108 Part 5 Studio DB adapters II — document, key-value, stream and object-store engines
v1.9 P108 Part 5 owns apps/kira-studio/internal/adapters/{mongo,redis,kafka,sqs,s3,awscfg}/**, is pure Go, and runs after Part 4 so the shared adapter core is settled first.
Part 5 was split from Part 4 on size (about 38k lines together).
The Redis console must deny connection-state commands (SELECT, MULTI/EXEC/DISCARD/WATCH/UNWATCH, SUBSCRIBE and siblings, MONITOR, HELLO/AUTH/RESET, QUIT, CLIENT REPLY OFF|SKIP) before they reach conn.Do, because the shared pooled client does not track state raw commands change.
Redis rejectConnectionStateCommand matches CLIENT REPLY as its own two-token command so ordinary CLIENT subcommands (GETNAME/INFO/LIST) stay usable, and points SELECT at the app's own database selector.
Kafka advanceWindows only clamps a partition to Next = End if the round was not page-capped, the partition's watermark reached End, and this round delivered or skipped zero records for that partition, because franz-go caps each partition's fetch at 1 MiB per round (maxPartBytes in config.go) independent of the page budget.
Kafka true exhaustion of a partition falls back to the exhaustedByEmptyPolls latch.
In all five adapters (Mongo, Redis, Kafka, SQS, S3), handle fields written by Connect/Disconnect are guarded by locked get/set/clear accessors, because ops run on whatever goroutine adapterhost dispatches them on.
SQS cacheQueueURL is a no-op once Disconnect has nilled the map, to avoid a nil-map write panic from an op that passed requireClient before a concurrent Disconnect.
Mongo tracks in-flight queries with adapters.QueryTracker[uint64], registered at each RunWithAbortRace call site via a TrackQuery hook; a package-level queryTokenSeq disambiguates overlapping registrations under the same opID.
Mongo Disconnect snapshots and cancels/killOps every tracked opID, then Drains bounded by ctx, then gives client.Disconnect its own deadline (disconnectTimeout) so in-use connections are force-closed regardless of the ctx passed.
Router.Connect's reconnect path passes context.Background() unbounded to Disconnect; this is adapterhost's file (Part 6), left as an explicit hand-off to Part 6, with Mongo's disconnectTimeout containing the exposure.
S3 applyPreservedAttributes copies ServerSideEncryption, SSEKMSKeyId, BucketKeyEnabled, Expires, WebsiteRedirectLocation and the three object-lock fields on edit, because otherwise an SSE-KMS object was silently re-encrypted under the bucket default.
S3 applyUpdate refuses an edit when head.TagCount > 0 rather than doing a tagging round trip, so it never silently drops tags.
S3 applyUpdate sends IfMatch with head.ETag and applyInsert sends IfNoneMatch "*"; both fall back (logged) to unconditional behavior for S3-compatible endpoints that reject the conditional header with NotImplemented.
Mongo keysetIDCondition widens the _id boundary with an $or across every BSON type sorting on the correct side (bsonSortTiers, numeric aliases collapsed into one tier), because $gt/$lt only match the boundary's own BSON type and collections can mix _id types (applyInsert mints an ObjectId when _id is omitted).
Mongo buildReadPage uses id="" for documents lacking _id (e.g. a $project {_id:0} view) and forces offset pagination with no keyset tokens when any document in the result lacks _id.
Mongo console parseStatement runs ResolveEJSONWrappers on parsed arguments before the isWriteStatement check, so {$oid}/{$date}/{$numberLong} wrappers copied from the grid work; aggregate's $out/$merge pipeline-stage detection is unaffected since resolving a stage bson.D preserves its type.
AWS requires MessageGroupId on every .fifo queue and MessageDeduplicationId unless content-based dedup is enabled; SQS SendMessage previously set neither, while caps.CanInsert reports true for all queues including FIFO.
SQS FIFO inserts: resolveFIFOInsertFields derives MessageGroupId and MessageDeduplicationId from $messageGroupId/$messageDeduplicationId sentinel fields on the insert body for a .fifo queue name.
For SQS FIFO queues, ContentBasedDeduplication is checked via GetQueueAttributes only when a dedup id is missing, and a clear E_QUERY naming what is required is returned.
SQS receiptHandles records receivedAt and the queue's visibility timeout (default 30s when unreadable) with each handle, and forDelete refuses a delete past that window (exactly time.Since == visibilityTimeout counts as expired), because AWS may accept a stale handle without deleting and the adapter would falsely report AffectedRows:1.
Mongo and Redis console execute parse/tokenize and read-only-check every statement up front before executing any, because a typo later in a batch previously left earlier writes committed with no rollback and re-running double-applied them.
Redis console scanQuotedToken implements redis-cli sdssplitargs escaping: \n, \r, \t, \b, \a and \xHH inside double quotes; only \' special inside single quotes; a closing quote followed by a non-space character is rejected.
The Redis tokenizer is split into scanQuotedToken/scanBareToken to keep gocognit under its limit.
Mongo fields-mode buildURIFromFields builds userinfo via url.UserPassword/url.User, not url.QueryEscape, because the driver decodes with url.PathUnescape and the two diverge on spaces, breaking auth for passwords containing a space.
Mongo buildURIFromFields and Redis dial use net.JoinHostPort for host:port, because a bare %s:%d is invalid for an IPv6 literal; this matches Kafka's adapter.
Docker-backed conformance suites (SA/*/*_test.go real-container tests) require Docker.

### v1.9 P108 Part 6 Studio engine data plane and page wire protocol
Router.Connect's reconnect branch and Router.Disconnect previously ran the old adapter's Disconnect outside RunOp, unbounded under context.Background(), so a dead-network TCP retransmit timeout or an in-flight op holding CloseAll's entry mutex could hang them for minutes.
Host.CancelOpsForConnection locally cancels every other running op for a connection id before the old adapter is disconnected; the old adapter's Disconnect now runs inside RunOp (kind "disconnect"), bounded to the caller's ctx plus a fixed 10s (disconnectTimeout), at every call site.
QueryTracker.Drain and ConnSet.CloseAll have no time bound of their own.
Because every op on the old adapter is locally cancelled before a reconnect or disconnect replaces or removes it, CancelOp no longer sends Cancel to a replacement adapter; a later redundant CancelOp for a cancelled opID is a harmless no-op.
adapters.DeleteLiveAdapterIf is a real compare-and-delete; Router.takeLiveAdapterForTeardown wraps it, serialized per connection id by Router.teardown (a keyedMutex), and removes the registry entry before the slow Disconnect, so a racing reconnect and Disconnect cannot both tear down the same adapter and a concurrent reconnect's newer adapter is never deleted.
Router.SchemaColumns and KeyTypes pass "schemaColumns" and "keyTypes" to RunOp, so both were added to model.opKinds (Go), opKindSchema (packages/shared/domain/ops.ts) and throttledKinds; oplog.handleOpStart rejects unrecognized kinds, which previously produced phantom "test"-kind op:end records.
tests/unit/go-ts-vocabulary-parity.spec.ts asserts that the Go and TS op-kind lists match, guarding against drift.
enginecache uses a generationTracker (enginecache/generation.go) with a monotonic generation per (connectionID, path) target, per connection, and global, guarded by Cache's mutex; Dispatcher.Read/Count capture Cache.CurrentGeneration before issuing the op and use StorePageIfCurrent/StoreCountIfCurrent so a result racing an invalidation is not cached.
The enginecache generation is bumped by DropTarget, DropPagesOnly, InvalidateAfterMutation (pages drop and counts stale-mark), DropConnection and Clear; StorePage/StoreCount remain plain unconditional stores for callers with no race.
Host's eventSub queues events unboundedly; a single drain goroutine is the only sender on and closer of the consumer channel, and on unsubscribe it delivers all queued events before closing, so no accepted event is lost. This replaced a fixed 32-slot non-blocking buffer that silently dropped events.
oplog is the only production Host.Subscribe consumer (via main.go's oplog.New(router.Host(), ...)), and it does a synchronous SQLite write plus a Wails emit per event; a dropped op:end leaves an op_log row stuck "running" forever.
A session's in-flight op cap is 64 concurrent ops (session.go).
In port.ts, onmessage wraps the whole decode call in try/catch and drops any frame it cannot decode, with no id to reject a specific pending call with.
decodeFrame wraps only the ok:true payload decode in its own try/catch and, on failure, returns an ordinary {ok:false} response using the already-extracted id, so handleMessage rejects that pending call instead of leaving it hanging.
A structurally corrupt frame (bad "KIF1" identifier, missing envelope field) still throws from decodeFrame and is dropped by port.ts, because there is no id to recover.
A data op has no client-side timeout of its own (timeoutMs: null), so cancellation is the only escape hatch; a res frame whose payload failed to decode previously left its pending call hanging forever.
Children/Describe/Definition/SchemaColumns capture the connection's Since via sinceEpoch before each backend call, and putIfSinceUnchanged stores the result in metadata_cache only if Since is unchanged, skipping the write (not the response) otherwise.
An unconditional Put stamped fetchedAt after the new freshness floor set by a reconnect (freshnessFloor reads the connection Since), so a listing fetched against the old target was wrongly treated as fresh.
Go's truncateUTF8ToBoundary must mirror page.ts's truncateUtf8ToBoundary: back off only while the byte at the cut boundary is a continuation byte (utf8.RuneStart check), capped at 3 bytes (utf8.UTFMax-1). The earlier utf8.DecodeLastRune version walked back through runs of Latin-1 0xE0-0xFF bytes without bound, clipping far below the 64 KiB page-scratch limit.
The ipcfixture harness tracks connections made via Recorder.ConnectionsConnect (App.trackConnected) and disconnects them automatically via disconnectTracked in a cleanup registered last so it runs first (t.Cleanup is LIFO), tearing down live adapters before connectionsSvc.Shutdown and before the DB/repos close.
Previously the clickhouse/kafka/mariadb/mysql/redis/sqs tests never disconnected, so real adapters accumulated in the process-global registry (adapters.live) across test runs.
The e2e real-build lock file (.e2e-real-build.lock) carries its owner's pid; isLockStale reclaims it when older than 10 minutes or when the pid is no longer a live process (zero-signal process.kill probe), and acquireBuildLock fails loudly after 15 minutes instead of looping forever.
.e2e-real-build.lock is gitignored.
After each commit in a shared checkout, diff git show --stat HEAD against the intended file list, because concurrent sessions' commits can sweep in your staged files.
F9's regression tests drive the ipcfixture harness's cleanup mechanism directly rather than through a real Docker-gated connect, so they run without a Docker daemon.
The Docker-backed conformance suites (ipcfixture's six *_test.go files and e2e-real) need a reachable Docker daemon to run.

### v1.9 P108 Part 7 Studio Go app shell, Wails bridge and agent integrations
queryplan.StatementsFor composes ClickHouse statements starting with EXPLAIN PLAN or EXPLAIN ESTIMATE, so classifyClickHouseSQL must skip the optional EXPLAIN kind keyword (AST/SYNTAX/QUERY TREE/PLAN/PIPELINE/ESTIMATE/TABLE OVERRIDE) and its optional 'k = v, …' settings list before classifying the real target.
Without stripping the EXPLAIN kind keyword, ClickHouse SQL was classified 'unknown', so every ClickHouse explain_query errored and run_query's auto-explain heavy-query gate never fired.
ClickHouse classifier tests are built from queryplan.StatementsFor("clickhouse", …) so the composer and classifier stay in lockstep.
On a connection with an active mask set, explain_query masks plan output via maskPlanForMaskedConnection/maskPlanNode: it recursively blanks Detail, drops metrics whose name ends with ' condition', and blanks Raw, because MySQL/MariaDB attached_condition exposes real const-folded values.
explain_query's prompt-approval path re-resolves resolveEnabled and the read verdict after ApprovalApproved before executing, mirroring run_query's awaitApproval, because read-mode or MCP exposure can change during the up-to-2-minute approval dialog.
All six MCP tool handlers are wrapped by a generic withPanicRecovery[In any] around every mcp.AddTool call, because go-sdk v1.8.0 has no recover() and a handler panic would crash the desktop app; it logs the stack and returns a generic 'internal error' IsError result.
Never return panic text to MCP clients, because on a masked connection it could carry row data.
The DB MCP Command/Install UI is gated on the on-disk helper file existing and matching the current token record (TokenRecord/LoadHelperToken/helperTokenMatchesRecord/helperTokenValid), not on the in-memory minted flag, so it survives restarts within the token's 7-day TTL.
A missing or mismatched helper token file makes the server write the record and helper together (reminting) rather than serve a record no client can match; SetToken no longer takes a plaintext.
In DatabaseMcpPane.vue, Regenerate renders whenever the MCP server is running, regardless of whether a command is shown, so an expired token always has a remedy in the UI.
The Claude Code headersHelper path must be single-quoted (shellSingleQuote) in both the add-json payload and the displayed Command, because Claude Code runs it through a shell and a path with a space silently sends no Authorization header, so every call 401s.
Install refuses outright when the helper path is not absolute, because an os.UserHomeDir failure could yield a relative path resolved against Claude Code's cwd instead of Kira's.
If the DB MCP server cannot bind DefaultPort (8766), bindHTTP returns the bind error instead of falling back to an ephemeral port; the error surfaces via DbMcpStatus.Error and the boot-time warn log.
Ephemeral-port fallback was rejected because existing registrations would point at 8766 while the live server and its bearer token sat on a different port that any local uid could have bound first.
keepawake's caffeinate driver reap reports a lost assertion (onLost) only if its own cmd is still current (d.cmd == cmd), so Release followed immediately by Acquire (Rearm) does not turn an intentional kill into a false onLost.
TestCaffeinateDriverReapIgnoresSupersededChild deterministically forces the Rearm race by holding d.mu across the kill-then-replace window, because the probabilistic timing did not reproduce.
main.go teardown stops DB MCP and agenthooks before connectionsSvc.Shutdown(), each blocking on its own in-flight handlers, so a mid-quit run_query cannot dial a preconnect target already being torn down.
The connections Service has a closed flag; Shutdown refuses new Connects, cancels all in-flight attempts, waits for each to unwind, then calls Preconnect.StopAll, because StopAll only kills tracked entries and an attempt inside Preconnect.Start's 2s settle window would otherwise orphan its Setpgid sidecar (e.g. ssh tunnel).
CLAUDE.md has a rule that known-open ARCHITECTURE.md items are deleted once resolved.
ARCHITECTURE.md documents a known-open item that DB MCP over-refuses JSON/array columns.
The repo's .githooks/pre-commit runs biome, check-tokens.sh and the full parallel typecheck:* split.

### v1.9 P108 Part 8 Studio API client backend
httpclient compares each redirect hop against the origin and strips secret headers on either a host change or an https-to-http same-host scheme downgrade.
gRPC Describe resolution is bound by a default timeout, so a hung reflection server cannot hang the call forever.
The reflection linker is guarded against cyclic proto dependencies, which previously crashed the whole app.
An undecryptable or NULL environment secret shadows as unresolved instead of falling through to a different scope's secret of the same name.
A plain (non-secret) value containing literal {{secretName}} text is reported as deferred by stage 2 rather than expanded as a secret reference.
Postman import applies the object-form {{$alias}} rewrite before the 'unchanged from origin' comparison, as export does, so untouched requests with alias references keep their verbatim origin.
Postman import strips plaintext auth blocks and secret-typed folder/item variables from origin_json, matching export-time blanking (D16), so the origin copy cannot re-export secrets verbatim.
curl import of -u user:pass uses a UTF-8-correct btoa helper and keeps a credential containing a {{var}} reference unencoded with a warning, because bare btoa throws on non-Latin1 and destroys {{ }} references.
curl import treats --json as --data-binary plus two headers, routing @file arguments through the same file-reference path as -d/--data-binary.
The gRPC descriptor cache uses a generation counter (Put checked against the generation at resolve start, bumped on invalidate) and singleflight so stale Puts cannot resurrect invalidated entries and concurrent identical resolutions share one reflection RPC.
In httpclient the wire pane's secret masking runs before the D4 truncation cap, via a *strings.Replacer passed through Options/renderRequest/renderFormDataBody; an opaque stdlib type keeps httpclient from importing apivars.
sanitizeUnmarshalError keeps only the line:column position for protojson syntax errors so substituted secrets are not echoed to the renderer; semantic errors such as unknown field pass through untouched.
Go forgivingBase64Decode matches the WHATWG forgiving-base64 algorithm (as TS atob): it strips up to 2 trailing '=' only when length is a multiple of 4, rejects leftover '=', and decodes unpadded, so "YQ=" is rejected in both stages.
The history per-scope trim is 30, and the secret replacer masks both Wire.Request and Wire.ResponseHead.
Response bodies, gRPC response messages and jar cookies reach kira.sqlite history unmasked, accepted by design (P8 OQ-6, P11) and listed in ARCHITECTURE.md's Known open items.
The Go/TS parity extractors strip // comments before scanning, close a map body with a string-aware depth count, and assert the extracted set/map is non-empty.
writeFileAtomically resolves the destination via filepath.EvalSymlinks, writes through symlinks, mirrors an existing target's mode (0600 default for fresh export), and re-asserts it with Chmod because OpenFile's perm is subject to umask.
If ImportVariables fails after ImportTree committed, the returned error names both failures (including a failed compensating Delete) and states the collection remains and needs manual removal.
Commit with an explicit -- <pathspec> on every git commit (not just git add) in a shared non-worktree checkout, because the slow full-monorepo pre-commit hook creates a race window where another session's staged changes get absorbed into the commit.
.githooks/pre-commit runs biome plus a full monorepo typecheck, and its run time is long enough to create a race window between concurrent sessions sharing one checkout.
Regression tests exist for stale-generation/singleflight, mask-before-cap/rune-boundary, syntax-error-token stripping, base64 truth table, and mode preservation/symlinks; roundtrip_test.go fixture/assertions were extended.
Per CLAUDE.md, doc and error-message fixes get no dedicated test; dedicated tests are required only for complex or hard-to-get-right logic.
Never bypass the pre-commit hook with --no-verify.

### v1.9 P108 Part 9 Studio API client UI
The history store's load must separate 'superseded by a newer load' (discard, no retry) from 'marked stale during this load' (retry only if no newer load exists), because concurrent loads each bumping latestSeq superseded each other and retried forever with loading stuck true.
ensureVariablesLoaded uses a per-key in-flight promise map plus a per-key generation counter, and a load only commits if its generation is still current, so a slow concurrent call cannot overwrite fresher loadVariableSetRows data with stale rows.
syncDrafts seeds a secret row's draft from revealedValues when present, because reloading rows otherwise blanked a revealed secret's draft to '' while revealedValues stayed set, so re-revealing never fired the mirror watch.
Request and gRPC views call ensureSavedRequestLoaded/ensureSavedGrpcRequestLoaded on mount and on itemId change, with an orphan vs not-loaded-yet distinction gating Save, because restored tabs otherwise never loaded their saved side, so the dirty dot never lit and Save created a duplicate row.
Request views call initCollections() on mount, because collectionIdFor read '' until CollectionsPanel mounted, silently dropping collection-scoped plain values and secrets from resolution.
The Cookies pane and its badge use the same stage-1-resolved URL that send uses, with a note when the host still carries a deferred secret, because the unresolved tab.state.url showed zero cookies for templated hosts like {{baseUrl}}/login.
The cookies store uses a per-tab request sequence (only the latest reply is written), clears the debounce timer on tab cleanup, and refetches every open tab after clearCookies, fixing out-of-order replies, timers recreating a closed tab's runtime, and single-tab refetch.
HTTP tab cleanup calls stopOp first, and send's post-await path and noteRecorded return early once the tab is gone, because closing a tab mid-send otherwise leaked the op and kept recording history, recreating the runtime.
Deleting an environment drops matching incognitoEnvByTab overrides and evicts listCache[environment:<id>]; deleting a collection evicts listCache[collection:<id>], so incognito tabs do not keep substituting a deleted environment's stale values.
useCollectionsStore and useVariablesStore expose state.error and dismissError (the variable-set half reuses variableSetError), every mutation is wrapped, and importCollection reloads the tree in finally so a partial import is visible, because bridge errors previously vanished.
Postman export's exportWarning includes both the secret-count and skipped-gRPC-count notes, because ExportReport.skippedGrpc was ignored and gRPC requests were silently omitted.
F12 (no cross-window invalidation; hand-rolled server-state caches) was deferred to follow-up phase P112: listCache, environment list/active environment, and collections tree/saved-request caches would move onto TanStack Query, invalidated by a new Go-broadcast api-data-changed event.
openHistoryMenu captures variableId and writes only if it still matches, and tab cleanup resets historyMenuState, preventing one row's entries showing in another row's popover.
The gRPC call() function itself errors clearly when findMethod hasn't resolved, instead of defaulting streaming to false, because the button disable is bypassed by Enter and the command palette; the view's methodResolved computed disables the Call button as a secondary UX fix.
The shared shadcn Alert in packages/theme has first-class warn and note variants, mirroring the destructive variant's *:data-[slot=alert-description]: targeting, replacing duplicated .strip-warn/.strip-note CSS, per CLAUDE.md's Tailwind rule.
Save-dialog UI state (open/tabId/suggestedName/payload, openSaveDialog/openSaveGrpcDialog/closeSaveDialog) lives in useSaveRequestDialogStore, per CLAUDE.md's one-store-one-concern rule; submitSaveDialog stays in useCollectionsStore and closes through the new store.
The gRPC history view carries each stored message's truncated flag (messages over the 64 KiB per-message cap, applied only to what History stores) and shows a warning-icon tooltip on truncated message headers; live messages map in as false.
The gRPC history pane shows requestMessageTruncated as a note next to the 'viewing a history call' band, because the pane does not render the stored request message body.
P108 Part 9 owns apps/kira-studio/frontend/src/api/**, views/httprequest/**, views/grpcrequest/** and related unit/UI tests; it is Stream A position 8 and its backend (Part 8) is settled first.
The repo's .githooks/pre-commit runs biome, check-tokens.sh and the full parallel typecheck:* split.
Per CLAUDE.md's pre-existing-failure rule, pre-existing lint warnings in files a phase never touched are out of scope for that phase.
CLAUDE.md has a CRUD-round-trip exclusion bar: repetitive try/catch error-surfacing patterns across many call sites get no dedicated regression test (applied to F10).
Commit with an explicit pathspec (git commit -- <pathspec>) in a shared, non-worktree checkout, because concurrent fixers work in the same checkout on disjoint files and a bare commit could pick up their changes.
Never use --no-verify on commits in this repo; the pre-commit hook must run.

### v1.9 P108 Part 10 Studio grid and shared view machinery
In Studio grid navigation, goNext and goPrev return immediately if a load is in flight (rt.opId !== null), before any optimistic pageIndex patch, because a second call reused the same token and could double-advance pageIndex or revert to the wrong previous index.
fake-data's datatype.boolean generator emits '1'/'0' for MySQL and SQLite and true/false for Postgres and ClickHouse, because text 'true'/'false' fails MySQL strict-mode TINYINT bind and lands as text in SQLite's NUMERIC-affinity BOOLEAN column.
ejson.ts gates its $numberLong branch on Number.isSafeInteger rather than Number.isFinite, so values beyond Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER stay wrapped instead of being re-encoded as a rounded plain number.
Never put aria-hidden="true" on FkPreviewPopover.vue's backdrop, because it is the popover's own interactive backdrop and the attribute hides it from assistive tech.
quoteIdent for ClickHouse escapes a literal backslash as a double backslash before doubling backticks, and strips embedded NUL bytes via name.split('\u0000').join('') because a regex literal containing \u0000 trips biome's noControlCharactersInRegex rule.
reloadAfterMutation steps back one page and reloads again when the reloaded page has rowCount === 0 and pageIndex > 0 (e.g. the last row of the last page was deleted).
CountRuntime has a countError (string | null) field, set by runPagedCount's catch path via classifyLoadError and implemented by the grid, documents, keyvalue and stream runtimes. DataToolbar.vue shows it in error-red with a tooltip, ahead of the stale-amber state, so a failed count refresh is distinguishable from count not yet requested.
createImmediateMutator isolates each post-write step (opts.reload, reloadTabsForTarget, after) in its own try/catch and throws one combined 'saved, but refresh failed: ...' error only if any failed, so a successful write is not reported as a failure.
parseTimestamp's epoch branches accept only strictly formatted decimals matching /^-?\d+(?:\.(\d+))?$/, rejecting hex, exponent and blank text. encodeTimestamp's epochSeconds branch reuses the original fraction text when the value is unchanged, so re-encoding is exact.
fractionDigits and fractionRaw apply to both the iso8601 and epochSeconds timestamp formats.
eachMatch takes an optional maxLength that truncates scanned cell text, with REGEX_SCAN_TEXT_CAP = 10_000, to bound ReDoS exposure from user-authored regexes. It is applied via regexTextCap only in grid/search.ts and shared/keyvalue/search.ts when q.regex is true, not in console/search.ts or documents/search.ts.
When the edit buffer is dirty and the previous cell is still editable, the selectedCell watch stages the edit via prevCell.onEdit(doc.value) before reseeding for the new cell, following the file's 'stage on leave' rule, so unsaved edits are not discarded.
PreviewCommandPanel.vue's preview-SQL query uses gcTime: 0, because DataView.vue mounts the panel with v-if="previewOpen" (full unmount on close) and the query key has no pending-set version, so a stale cached entry would show on reopen.
finance.amount allows wholeDigits = 0 and then uses max = 1 - 10 ** -dec, so a numeric(p,p) column such as numeric(2,2) (max 0.99) does not overflow.
stream/state.ts is not a caller of runPagedLoad (only documents, grid and keyvalue are), although its hand-written load() has the same shape. It is an unadopted candidate left to Part 11, which owns that file.
@tanstack/vue-virtual is still used in this app: views/shared/keyvalue/KeyValuePane.vue and views/grpcrequest/ResponsePane.vue import useVirtualizer from it. Only the grid no longer uses it, since SlickGrid virtualizes there.
Navigation that discards pending changes without a confirm (pager, sort, filter, projection, refresh) is intended per decision D3; only background and failed loads are covered by findings F1 and F2.
The grid's edit actions (canEditTable, canDeleteRows in SlickGridHost.vue and DataToolbar isWritable) all include !maskPreview, and toggling preview is refused while pending changes exist.
Go's AssertKeyIsPrimaryKey (sqlmutate.go) rejects any mutation key that is not exactly the primary key, so a partial PK from a projection hiding PK columns cannot cause a wrong write; the affected-exactly-one assertion also rejects keys matching zero rows.
primaryKeyOf reads the committed page value via cell(), not the staged value, when edits touch a PK column.
The inline editor skips unchanged values, but paste can stage a same-value UPDATE; this is intended because paste is an explicit write.
Mutate is one transaction per adapter contract, and commitPending clears pending changes only on success; DataView.onCommit shows the error on failure.
applyLoadFailure with a stale opId returns before touching status, since a superseding op owns the status, so status cannot get stuck at loading.
Range paste spilling into insert rows past the filtered rows is intended behavior per the pasteTargetRows doc.
useConnectionGate.onReconnectAndLoad marks hydrated after a connect that lands in an error state, but needsReconnect also checks connectionStatus !== 'connected' so the gate stays up, and the failed load makes applyLoadFailure unmark hydrated (self-correcting).
ejsonToPlain loses precision for Long/Decimal values; this is a documented accepted tradeoff (ejson.ts).
rowsToInsert writes a truncated column as NULL with a leading comment naming the column.
Tables and views cannot expose duplicate column names in any supported engine (SQLite renames view duplicates), so the data grid never sees duplicates and pageColumnIndexFor's last-wins behavior is safe there.
Tab-close cleanup is registered via registerTabRuntimeCleanup for grid runtime, keyvalue runtime, the ::preview page, document rows, search and visible-rows state; pending changes clear in state/tabs.ts.
The TanStack Query client is configured app-wide with retry: false and no refetch on window focus (packages/workbench/src/state/queryClient.ts).
Every :disabled button in views/grid and views/shared sits inside TooltipDisabledTrigger.
AutocompleteField.vue escapePlain escapes & < > for text content; double quotes matter only in attributes, which paintOverlayHtml builds from static class names.
validate.ts accepts an empty value for every format; an empty string on a NOT NULL numeric column fails server-side with the engine's message.
The F7 fix required rewriting view-state.spec.ts test 9, which had encoded the race being fixed as correct behavior; F9, F15 and F16 each added a dedicated regression test.
The repo's .githooks/pre-commit runs biome, check-tokens.sh and the full parallel typecheck:* split.
Write dedicated tests for hard-to-get-right logic, and fix a pre-existing test that encodes a bug on the spot, per CLAUDE.md.

### v1.9 P108 Part 11 Studio query console, SQL editor and per-kind data views
Part 11 (plan §5.10, Stream A position 10) owns the Studio query console, SQL editor and per-kind data views under apps/kira-studio/frontend/src (views/console, editor, documents, keyvalue, stream, browse, definition), plus beautify.ts, the SQL shared/domain files and tests/fixtures.
The console and the per-kind views both sit on views/shared (Part 10) and share result rendering, so they are one chunk.
Closing a console tab cancels the in-flight run and auto-explain, because run()/explain() otherwise kept a reference to the detached runtime object, never saw supersession, and the statement kept running on an unseen connection.
isExplainable in console/explain.ts rejects a SELECT/WITH statement containing an embedded ; before the trailing one, so a merged multi-statement blob is not sent through EXPLAIN as one statement; guarded on both the TS and Go sides.
Run statement treats the span after a ; or on a blank line before the next statement's first non-space character as belonging to the preceding statement. It is implemented by statementAtOffset in packages/shared/domain/sql-split.ts, used by statementAtCursor and ConsoleView.vue's caret lookup.
scanSqlSpan (sql-lex.ts) is dialect-aware through SqlLexOptions: hashComments, slashSlashComments, nestedBlockComments, bracketIdentifiers and postgresEscapeStrings. These cover MySQL/ClickHouse # comments, Postgres E'' strings and nested block comments, and [bracket] identifiers. A $ inside a Postgres identifier is not read as a dollar-quote tag. sqlIdent.ts's dialect-keyed builders build the options.
Operation history records the originating tab's path in OpRecord.Path (migration 0027_p108part11_op_log_path.sql). Re-run reopens at that path, refuses when it is missing, and reconnects via ensureConnectedForRun. Otherwise unqualified DML could hit a same-named table in a different database or schema.
All adapters (postgres, sqlite, mysqlfamily, mongo, clickhouse) join console statements with the shared adapters.JoinConsoleStatements instead of a bare strings.Join(statements, ";\n"), mirroring the TS joinFormattedStatements, so a trailing line comment does not swallow the separator.
ProjectionMenu.vue does not infer 'everything' from selected.size === fieldNames.length on unmount, because fieldNames is the already-projected page's field set and an untouched close cleared an active projection. The shared helpers setsEqual and nextDocumentProjectionOnClose in projection.ts decide this.
Console row/range copy disambiguates duplicate column names (e.g. self-join) via disambiguateNames in clipboardFormats.ts, so TSV/CSV/JSON output does not overwrite same-named columns. It is scoped to console; grid's name-keyed column widths are excluded.
Escape database-sourced text in Monaco hover content: plain-text lines go through escapeMarkdownSyntaxTokens (VS Code's convention), and the single value entry goes through fenceMarkdownValue, a CommonMark fence one backtick longer than the longest backtick run in the value.
formatConsoleText's joinFormattedStatements appends an extra newline before the ;\n separator when a statement's last line contains a -- or # comment, so the separator is not absorbed into the comment. formatMongoStatement refuses trailing content with 'unexpected trailing content after statement', matching the Go adapter.
The shared KuiColumnResizeHandle in packages/kira-ui emits cancel when a drag is cancelled (Escape or lost pointer capture) and only responds to the left button, so StreamView resets liveResizeWidth. CommitGrid.vue ignores cancel because it holds no live-resize preview state.
Console Run, Run all and Explain are guarded by a starting ref, checked alongside running and used in the disabled bindings. It covers the await of ensureConnectedForRun() so a second click during a slow reconnect cannot start an overlapping run.
Regex-search text is capped per cell/body/document by REGEX_SCAN_TEXT_CAP in console search, documents search, the editor find bar (findRanges.ts), and grid/keyvalue search, to avoid pathological regexes like (a+)+$ blocking the main thread.
onFormat() discards the formatted result and shows a note if the tab's live text changed during the await of formatConsoleText (a dynamic import of sql-formatter on first use), instead of overwriting a keystroke typed meanwhile.
stream/state.ts load() uses the shared runPagedLoad from shared/page/load.ts, like documents, grid and keyvalue, for the supersede, tab-closed, page-kind-check and applyLoadFailure handling.
applyStreamFilter clears rt.countError along with rt.count and rt.countOpId, and the stream and document toolbars' count buttons tint and show the count failure in their tooltip, as grid/DataToolbar.vue does. runPagedCount sets countError on failure for all four views.
Wrap toolbar buttons with a dynamic :disabled in TooltipDisabledTrigger, because a disabled native button dispatches no pointer or focus events and its tooltip never fires.
Console, Stream and Document Refresh/Stop buttons are wrapped in TooltipDisabledTrigger. Browse's and Definition's Stop buttons, permanently disabled with nothing to cancel, were removed.
The document context menu's Delete item is gated by a deleteGate parameter of rowMenu(), the same way editGate gates Edit. DocumentView.vue passes { deletable: canDelete.value, label: deleteTitle.value }, the same values the row's Delete button uses (caps.canDelete combined with the connection's readOnly flag), so it does not offer a Delete that fails server-side.
In a shared checkout, commit with an explicit pathspec (git commit -m msg -- <paths>), because a plain commit sweeps in files another concurrent session has staged; a pathspec commit builds its tree only from the named paths.
The container's preinstalled golangci-lint binary refuses this repo's go 1.27.1, so golangci-lint must be installed via scripts/install-golangci-lint.sh as described in docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md.
Pre-existing failure: bridge-unwrap.spec.ts (1 test) fails because bridge/control.ts does not meet its wrap contract (every promise-returning method should surface { code: 'E_QUERY' }, not a raw rejection); it is unrelated to console/editor.
Per CLAUDE.md, a pre-existing failure may be left unfixed when fixing it needs work genuinely outside the phase's scope, such as a different subsystem or a real design decision.

### v1.9 P108 Part 12 Studio shell, project tree, state stores and UI-test harness
In Kira Studio, applyRemote writes the pushed DDL from a remote onSchemaChanged event straight into the query cache instead of only calling invalidateQueries, because the refetch always hit schemaQueryOptions' prefer-cached-value guard and never moved the cache.
Schema in-flight-save race protection uses a per-connection write generation bumped on every direct cache write (saveDdl and applyRemote, via a shared commitDdl helper), rather than always preferring the cache.
The DB MCP approval dialog is keyed by requestId and its onApprove/onDeny are bound to the id rendered in the template, so a queue swap mid-click cannot land the click on a different request; focus moves to Deny whenever requestId changes.
approveQuery/denyQuery rely solely on the approval broker's own broadcast, not the RPC's returned snapshot, because the snapshot could race a second window's concurrent answer.
initTreeSync, initSchemaSync and initSchemaColumnsSync run from main.ts's bootstrap() beside initApiDataSync(), not from ProjectTree.vue's onMounted, so windows with the project panel hidden or zero connections still get them.
Credential reveal is guarded against a draft swap while its async round trip is outstanding, so one connection's password cannot be written into another draft.
ensureSchemaColumns does not cache a failed fetch as an empty list, and has a generation guard so a stale in-flight fetch cannot overwrite a newer result after a drop.
Mounted console completion is re-warmed after a schema-columns drop (reconnect or tree Refresh).
hydrateOps subscribes to onOpUpdate before taking its opsRecent snapshot, so updates landing in between are not lost.
Tree loadChildren results that resolve after the connection's disconnect are dropped, and a collapse issued during an in-progress expand() is honored when expand() resolves.
Tab saves are serialized: enqueueSave chains every save through one persistent promise, coalescing intervening changes to the latest snapshot, and flushPendingTabState awaits the full chain. This is needed because Wails dispatches each bound call on its own goroutine and nothing serializes tabsSave calls DB-side.
Studio boot has an error path: BootFailure.vue (adapted from apps/kira-space) and a bootstrap() wrapper with Retry replace a blank window on hydrate rejection.
The mode store's debounced write is flushed via the native onFlushBeforeClose/onWindowFlushBeforeClose handshake (as createTabsStore.ts does), not browser beforeunload, which never fires when closing the last window because that path calls Hide(), not Close().
dataTabStateSchema fields all have .default(...) like sibling schemas, and hydrateTabs resets to the kind's defaultState() on a genuine parse failure rather than keeping raw state, avoiding NaN from pageIndex * pageSize.
CommandPalette is built on the vendored shadcn-vue CommandDialog.vue (reka-ui), which provides role=dialog, focus trap, focus restore, Escape and click-outside close, replacing a hand-rolled backdrop with aria-hidden that hid the palette from assistive tech.
Go's ConnectionsService.Connect never rejects for a bad connection; it resolves with an error status, so callers must check the status before running dependent SQL.
OperationsPanel's one-shot context-menu re-run uses the non-reactive ensureConnectedOnce, because a fresh useConnectionGate() there has no owning effect scope to dispose its computed()s. onReconnectAndLoad returns success/failure.
ConnectionDialog's Test result check uses a JSON.stringify content snapshot in addition to identity/editingId, because draft is mutated in place by v-model and identity alone misses in-flight field edits.
Mask rule changes (Upsert/Remove/RegenerateKey) are broadcast across windows via ChannelMaskRulesChanged (Go internal/bridge/{events,maskrules}.go, per-connection payload like schemaChanged), consumed by onMaskRulesChanged and state/maskRules.ts's initMaskRulesSync wired in main.ts.
initMaskRulesSync writes pushed rules/counts straight into the query cache with no writeGeneration guard, because mask rules take effect immediately with no local draft to race; a RegenerateKey payload makes receiving windows drop their cached correlation key.

### v1.9 P108 Part 13 Shared frontend base
Part 13 (shared frontend base, Stream B position 1 of 8) owns packages/{workbench,theme,kira-ui}/**, packages/shared/caps.ts, packages/shared/protocol/events.ts, and packages/shared/domain/{base64,color,connection,git,layout,path,repo,settings,shortcuts,tabs}.ts.
Part 13 closes first so Part 6 (gate G2) and every later TS chunk review against a settled base, because every TS chunk in both apps one-hops into it.
useVirtualRows in virtualRows.ts watches rowHeight()/rowHeights() and calls virtualizer.value.measure(), because estimateSize is not one of virtual-core's memo deps and measure was not re-triggered when heights changed with the same item count.
monacoTheme.ts normalizeColor draws a 1×1 fillRect and reads the pixel via getImageData, emitting #rrggbbaa, because Chromium and WebKit serialize color-mix() tokens as color() strings that the old rgba regex missed, resolving silently to Color.red in Monaco.
Monaco's 8-digit #rrggbbaa form is valid for token rules foregrounds as well as colors widget entries, per ColorMap.getId's regex in tokenization.js.
DateFormatField, FontSizeField and GitLogLevelField.vue tie their Label to the control via for+useId instead of wrapping the Reset button inside the label.
KuiDialog.vue and KuiPopoverPanel.vue must not put aria-hidden="true" on the backdrop when it is an ancestor of the panel, because it hides the whole dialog/popover subtree from assistive tech.
modalFocus.ts's watch is immediate with an onScopeDispose backstop, so a dialog that mounts already open still gets initial focus, Tab-trap and Escape handling.
TabStrip.vue no longer reassigns dragId to the hovered tab mid-drag, and createTabsStore's moveTab looks up the target's pre-removal index.
hydrateTabs syncs the defaulted tab's own active flag, not just activeIdByWorkspace, and closeOthers goes through setActiveTabId instead of setting keep.active directly.
confirmDialog.ts and useTextPrompt.ts settle a still-pending promise before a second call overwrites it.
createLayoutStore's applyRemote reapplies the still-pending local patch on top of a same-window layoutChanged echo.
createTerminalsStore only buffers output into the drain when byTabId still has the tab; routeTerminalData and applyTerminalStatus helpers were extracted from the onTerminal callback to stay under the lint cognitive-complexity ceiling.
shortcuts/keys.ts matches letter/digit chords against e.code instead of e.key, so Option-modified macOS shortcuts (e.g. tree.copyUri ⌥⌘C) match the physical key; this is unconfirmed on real macOS hardware.
shared/domain/path.ts canonicalPath keeps a lone separator instead of stripping the filesystem root down to an empty string.
KuiColumnResizeHandle.vue cleans up on pointercancel and lostpointercapture as well as pointerup, so an interrupted drag cannot leak window-level listeners or fire a stale change.
The --color-input Tailwind @theme collision: packages/theme/src/base.css:31 (var(--kira-bg-input), wins) shadows shadcn-bridge.css:74 (var(--input)); both resolve to #313131 so nothing is visibly broken. To be folded into P110 with --color-muted.
CommandPalette.vue (shortcuts/) and FkPreviewPopover.vue (views/grid/) in apps/kira-studio each hand-roll the backdrop-ancestor aria-hidden="true" shape outside the shared Kui components. They belong to Part 12 and Part 10 respectively.

### v1.9 P108 Part 14 Space git process layer
Part 14 owns apps/kira-space/internal/{gitclient,ghclient,gitpath,gitaskpass}/** (porcelain/testdata included).
gitclient is the bottom of Space's Go call graph (gitsession to gitclient has 265 edges); porcelain parsing and askpass are edge-case and security heavy.
parseDecorationToken ignores unrecognised bare %D tokens (such as a shallow clone's "grafted" marker or a replace ref's "replaced" token) as non-ref annotations instead of hard-erroring in ParseLogRecord.
On a split/parse error the log session's consumeChunkLocked calls failLocked, which kills the child git log process and marks the Session permanently failed; every later ReadPage returns the same error immediately.
gitsession/walk.go readPageLocked resets the walk on a log-session error, so a permanently failed Session does not wedge every future loadMore; the failing read still surfaces its own error.
The 0x1f field separator can appear inside non-last fields (hostile author/committer/tagger names or emails), so log, scan and tags formats use NUL as field delimiter, and the stash format puts its message field (%gs) last.
porcelain.FieldGrouper groups a flat NUL-field stream into fixed-size records; ParseLogRecord and ParseScanRecord take pre-split fields, and ParseLogRecordFromRaw handles one-shot show -s -z. logsession.Session and gitsearch.Scan each use a FieldGrouper alongside their RecordSplitter.
gitclient forces -c diff.suppressBlankEmpty=false in configOverrides because a user's diff.suppressBlankEmpty=true makes git print a bare empty line for blank context lines; parseOneHunk also tolerates a bare empty line.
Run/reap use a buffered path (Spec.buffered/startBuffered) with direct io.Writers instead of StdoutPipe/StderrPipe, because os/exec pipes have no copy goroutine so cmd.WaitDelay cannot force-close them and a hook's backgrounded process holding stdout/stderr would block Run and Repo.Write indefinitely.
startStreaming (used by logsession, gitsearch, catfile) remains pipe-based; Process.Wait() now carries Stdout directly.
catfile requestPipelined calls fail() before waiting on writeErrCh on a response error, to avoid deadlock when the writer goroutine is stuck on a full stdin pipe; stdin is captured to a local before the writer goroutine starts.
catfile Read does a second size-gate check inside the same closure that holds the size, since a mutable rev can resolve differently if HEAD moves; an oversize blob is still fully drained and not counted as a failure.
catfile readHeader treats an " ambiguous" reply (short-OID collision) by suffix like " missing", not as a protocol error that would permanently trip the circuit breaker.
catfile Check/Read/CheckMany and persistentProcess request/requestPipelined take a ctx; a watchCtx helper closes the process on cancellation.
catfile ReadOneShot runs cat-file -s before the unbounded read so the size gate is exact before any content is read.
buildEnv strips GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE, GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY, GIT_COMMON_DIR and GIT_NAMESPACE from the parent environment before spawning git, since passing them through would silently retarget every spawn.
Repo uses a pendingWriters counter so that once a Write is pending, new Read admissions are refused (already-admitted reads are unaffected), preventing readers from starving a Write.
Stash header detection (isStackStashHeader/isGlobalStashHeader) consumes a rename's two numstat path records structurally via collectNumstatRecs/isRenameNumstatRecord, so header-shaped renamed filenames do not split into phantom entries.
MergeTreeArgs uses -z (requires git 2.38+) because default LF framing C-quotes conflicting paths containing quote/backslash/tab/LF; ParseMergeTreeOutput parses NUL framing.
ResolveHead reads plain symbolic-ref -q HEAD and strips refs/heads/ itself, because --short yields "heads/<b>" when a tag shares the checked-out branch's name.
Never assume SHA-1 in code, because SHA-1-only literals break every SHA-256 repository: get the empty tree hash from a real hash-object -t tree /dev/null spawn (EmptyTreeHashArgs/ParseEmptyTreeHash), accept 40 or 64 hex in stash isHexObjectID, and keep IsUncommittedBlameSHA width-agnostic.
UncommittedBlameSHA is kept for SHA-1 back-compat.
gitsession/working.go WorkingDetail spawns EmptyTreeHashArgs() itself on an unborn branch, replacing the removed EmptyTreeSHA constant.
gitaskpass buildShim single-quotes each helper-command element (shellQuoteSingle, '\'' idiom) so the shell cannot expand $var, $(...) or backticks; double-quoting allowed command injection.
The gitaskpass helper reads SSH_ASKPASS_PROMPT: "none" exits 0 without contacting the broker; "confirm" sends Request.Confirm (Masked false), never prints the answer, and conveys the decision by exit code alone.
fseventsBackend.run()'s inner per-event select has a case for b.stopping so it abandons a blocked batch send and returns to the outer drain loop; Close() closes stopping before es.Stop(). The file is darwin+cgo only.

### v1.9 P108 Part 15 Space git preflight, ops, review, search and graph store
RunRemote's protected-branch gate and PushPreflight's ClassifyPushInput.Branch use the resolved upstream remote branch name (resolveUpstreamRemoteBranch), not local params.Branch, because a force-push lands on the upstream name, which can differ (e.g. local feat tracking origin/main).
deleteRemoteBranch is unaffected by the upstream-resolution fix because its params.Branch already names the remote branch directly.
ClassifyInProgress uses SequencerTodoKind, read from the first pending command in sequencer/todo (mirroring git's wt-status.c), to re-derive revert/cherryPick when every *_HEAD file is gone, e.g. after a paused multi-commit revert/cherry-pick was finished with plain git commit.
A plain git am shares rebase-apply/ with an apply-backend git rebase, distinguished only by the applying marker file; it is reported only, with every Can* flag false, and no new am InProgressKind is added because that would change the git-ipc wire contract (Part 17's boundary).
head-name and onto are also read from rebase-apply/, not only rebase-merge/, so HeadName/OtherSha are populated there too; the operation classification changes are mirrored in the TS twin packages/git-core/src/model/operation.ts.
classifyOpErrorRules is an ordered table of whole-blob strings.Contains checks, so user-controlled text (commit subjects, file paths) could misclassify errors; a line-anchored Conflict check now runs first, matching only lines starting with 'error: could not apply ', 'error: could not revert ' or 'conflict ('.
PullConfigArgs applies regexp.QuoteMeta to the branch name in its --get-regexp pattern, as BranchConfigRegexpArgs does, because unescaped names like feat+x match the wrong key and a(b makes git config exit 6, failing PullPreflight.
MapRebaseValue (exported from gitpreflight) accepts all of git's case-insensitive boolean synonyms (yes/on/1, no/off/0) and short forms i/m, so e.g. pull.rebase=yes no longer falls through to the ff-only default.
pull.rebase=merges passes --rebase-merges via a rebaseMerges bool in gitops.RebaseArgs; gitpreflight.WantsRebaseMerges is asked by the executor re-reading the same config spawn PullPreflight made, avoiding a PullStrategy wire-union widening (git-ipc contract change, Part 17's boundary).
Known limitation: an explicit pull strategy override is indistinguishable, from the config re-read, from one the config ladder produced; closing it needs a git-ipc contract change.
No TS twin exists for pull preflight logic: packages/git-core/src/preflight/ holds only reset.ts and tag.ts.
When a prepare script exits 0 but a background process keeps stdout/stderr open, cmd.Wait returns a wrapped exec.ErrWaitDelay; Run treats it as a normal result, taking the exit code from cmd.ProcessState, and sets the new additive Result.Incomplete field to mark a possibly incomplete transcript. gitsession/worktree.go does not yet read it.
On prepare-script timeout/cancel, SIGKILL is sent to the whole process group immediately and unconditionally after Wait returns (ESRCH tolerated), so SIGTERM-trapping descendants cannot outlive the documented 15-minute hard timeout; this is safe from pid reuse because Linux does not free a PGID while any member is alive.
The delayed escalation SIGKILL timer must never separately fire after Wait returns; the G31 round-2 #11 regression test expects exactly one prompt SIGKILL.
resolveStackBase checks that the parent ref exists in the parentIsStacked branch too, so a branch on a parent whose ref was removed (git update-ref -d leaves kirastack config) becomes a visible orphan, preserving BuildStacks' contract of never dropping a branch.
In a shared checkout, run git add and git commit in one shell invocation and check git show --stat HEAD against the intended file list, because a concurrent session's staged files can land in the same index and be swept into the commit.

### v1.9 P108 Part 16 Space git session
Part 16 (Space git session) owns apps/kira-space/internal/gitsession/**; one-hop neighbours are gitrpc (153 edges), gitsock, gitaskpass, the Space bridge and main.go wireGit; callees are Parts 14-15, codeworkspace and storage.
Preflight RPC entry points (preflight.checkout target, revertMergeParents shas, preflight.cherryPick sha, preflight.stashPop targetSha) are guarded by validOpArg in gitsession, with a second validRefArg layer in gitrpc handlers (refs.go, reset.go, stash.go), because a client value with a leading '-' reaches git argv as a flag (e.g. --output=<path> on git show/git diff writes or truncates an arbitrary file).
PushPreflight carries a ResolvedBranch field (Go, @kira/git-ipc, git-core mirrored type) holding the resolved upstream branch name, because the backend requires ConfirmToken to equal the resolved upstream branch name while ForcePushDialog.vue could only use the local name.
ForcePushDialog.vue (packages/git-ui/src/components/dialogs/, Part 19's file) was minimally changed to gate, display and send the resolved branch name instead of the local one; flagged for Part 19's reviewer.
RunRemote resolves the upstream branch name once per call and threads it through the gate, lease check and runPushFamily spawn, instead of three separate resolveUpstreamRemoteBranch reads.
RepoEntry subscriptions (e.subs) are keyed by a per-call monotonic subscriptionID (counter under e.mu, never reused) instead of ConnID, because concurrent repo.open calls on one connection overwrote each other's subscriber and could leave zero subscribers, so no repo.changed delivery.
logsession.Session exposes Failed() bool (apps/kira-space/internal/gitclient/logsession/session.go, Part 14's file, flagged for Part 14's reviewer); walk.go readPageLocked resets the walk only when w.log.Failed(), so a client cancel or transient read error no longer discards the loaded store.
runPullOp checks whether the pulled branch is still checked out inside the Repo.Write closure using a fresh gitclient.ResolveHead (never cached), immediately before the merge/rebase spawns, because the cached e.Head check before acquiring Write left a race window.
wantsRebaseMerges re-runs ResolvePullStrategy with a fresh cfg and adds --rebase-merges only when the result's source is config-derived (SourceBranchConfig/SourcePullConfig), and surfaces config-read errors instead of swallowing them.
Open item: an explicit pull strategy override is indistinguishable from a config-produced one in wantsRebaseMerges; a follow-up needs a @kira/git-ipc wire-contract change threading the resolved PullStrategySource (or a plain rebaseMerges bool) from remote.pullPreflight's result into remote.run's request params. It belongs in Part 17's boundary (gitrpc and the wire contract), like Part 15's F3 (am InProgressKind widening), and is to be added to SPEC.md.
Conn.Walk swaps the walk slot under c.mu and disposes the old walk only after unlocking, because dispose() takes the walk's lock, which a running Stream holds across page reads and emit backpressure; this keeps subscribers from blocking behind a page read. CloseRepo/Close use the same shape.
A disposed Walk has a disposed flag set in dispose(); ReadPage, Stream, Status, Search (and resetLocked) check it first and refuse with ErrRepoNotHeld, so stale handler references cannot spawn a git log process that lingers until the 5-minute idle reclaim.
RunOp and RunRestack clear e.undo once before the first write, so a prior op's undo record does not survive a later op that partially wrote then failed; CancelRestack racing the Repo.Write gate wait in runRestackSpawn maps ErrCancelled to the Cancelled OpResult.
RepoEntry has a cacheGen counter, bumped by invalidateAfterWrite and note's refsChanged branch alongside every cache drop; Refs/CommitDetail/Stacks/mergeBase/statusAndInProgress capture it before spawning and write back to cache only if it still matches, preventing stale reads from clobbering invalidated caches.
The repo-wide open-PR snapshot matches a branch only when ghclient.PR.HeadRepoOwner equals repo.Owner (empty when head.repo is null), falling through to the per-branch query on mismatch, so fork PRs from commonly named branches are not badged onto same-named local branches.
RunOp runs a whole argvList (e.g. auto-stash push then switch for checkout) under ONE Repo.Write acquisition via runWriteArgvList; if the switch fails after the stash succeeded, the error message names the exact stash ref (fresh rev-parse refs/stash).
Registry.release calls closeCatFile after releasing reg.mu (refcount and linger timer bookkeeping stay under the lock), because closeCatFile can block on an in-flight request such as a slow lazy-object fetch in a partial clone and would block other repos' Acquire/release/IsOpen/ReconcileAutoFetch. closeCatFile is idempotent and guarded by entry-local catfileMu.
Compute under the lock and run slow or blocking work (dispose, closeCatFile) after releasing it, to avoid stalling other lock users.
When working concurrently with other sessions in a shared checkout, git add only your own files and check git status and git show --stat HEAD immediately after every commit, because the shared index leaks other sessions' staged changes into commits.
Concurrency-sensitive regression tests in gitsession (F3, F7, F9's restack-cancellation case) are run under -race.

### v1.9 P108 Part 17 Space git RPC, socket server and `git-ipc` contract
verifyClientToken in gitsock is tri-state (tokenVerified/tokenInvalid/tokenLookupFailed); on a trust-store lookup failure runHandshake closes the connection with no frame instead of sending tokenRejected.
The extension treats a tokenRejected frame as authoritative and deletes its stored token, forcing a re-pair; so a transient trust-store error (SQLite busy/locked, DB closing) must never be answered with tokenRejected.
