The dummy verifyToken call is kept on the lookup-failed path so handshake timing stays uniform (D6).
The pairing Broker returns PairingAborted (not PairingDenied) for non-decisions: closed guard, queue-cap (200 requests), and Shutdown/Request after Shutdown. runHandshake answers it with the no-frame close so the client backs off and redials.
A pairingDenied frame sets a terminal state in the extension that only a manual retry() clears, so pairingDenied must be sent only for a real user Deny (the Broker cooldown short-circuit in Request stays PairingDenied).
Broker.Cancel(requestID) removes a still-queued pairing entry and resolves it PairingAborted; it is a no-op if already resolved.
During runHandshake's row-7 wait (up to 120s for pairing approval), a watcher goroutine does a 1-byte Peek on the connection's reader while the request is queued; a read error cancels the entry, so an Approve cannot mint a trusted git_clients row for a disconnected requester.
The watcher stop sequence sets a past read deadline to unblock Peek, waits for the watcher goroutine to exit, and only then clears the deadline, before the connection is handed to Serve, so nothing reads it concurrently.
The handshake's first Receive() has a 10s read deadline, because readFrame allocates the declared body (up to the 8 MiB cap) before reading it and a silent or trickling local client would otherwise hold a goroutine, fd and memory until Server.Close.
hello.Client.ID is capped at 256 bytes; an oversized ID is rejected as row 1 (silent close).
In the VS Code extension connection.ts, #disconnectHandledFor = dialToken is set synchronously before awaiting secrets.delete in the tokenRejected branch, and #dial() clears #reconnectTimer at its top, so a server-side close after tokenRejected does not cause a double dial.
ConnectionManager in kira-space-vscode has no test harness mocking vscode.ExtensionContext/net.Socket, so the F14 reconnect-ordering fix has no dedicated test.
remote.pullPreflight and remote.run strategy fields are not validated against the TS literal set; this is an open item handed off to P111.

### v1.9 P108 Part 18 `git-core` and `git-ui` logic
GraphViewState.reset() aborts #loadController and bumps #loadGeneration; #runLoad's post-request resync checks that generation, so a repo switch mid-load cannot reopen the old repo's graph stream over the new one.
BridgeClient re-establishes a held repo on host reconnect, so *State classes are not left pointed at a repo the host no longer considers open.
StackState.runRestack reconciles undo/inProgress from the restack result and re-checks repo identity before applying, matching other op-running methods' post-write step.
OpsState refreshes refreshUndo() on every repo.changed event, not only setRepoId, because the undo slot is shared per RepoEntry and another surface's write must be observed.
Reload-shaped methods in ops.ts, stack.ts, refs.ts, worktrees.ts and stash.ts route through createLatestRequest so an older reply arriving after a newer one is dropped; repoSettings.ts uses a shared #generation counter instead because its own set() must never drop its own result as stale.
PendingSlot.abandon() shares resolve()'s settle closure; OpsState.setRepoId/dispose use it to settle every pending confirm dialog and pull prompt with its cancel value, so a repo switch mid-dialog does not leave busy stuck true.
worktreePrepareOutput uses a private #worktreePrepareBuffer appended and trimmed in place with triggerRef, capped at WORKTREE_PREPARE_OUTPUT_LIMIT (500 lines, mirroring the server's retained-transcript bound), instead of re-spreading the array per ~100ms batch (O(n²)).
ReviewSessionState uses a #sessionGeneration counter (bumped by setTarget/setBase/dispose) and a dedicated #checkController, checked before #checkForChange applies its result, so a stale background re-resolve cannot overwrite a newer selection.
PrState uses a #clearGeneration counter bumped by #clear() to gate ensureSnapshot's worker pool and resolveBranch's stale-reply guard; #branchRequests cleanup is conditional so it never deletes a newer request's in-flight marker.
Fire-and-forget reload()/refreshX() calls in ops.ts, stack.ts, refs.ts, worktrees.ts, stash.ts and repoSettings.ts route through a per-class #logBackgroundError (graphView.ts #runAutoRefresh does so inline) to avoid unhandled rejections; the underlying methods still throw for direct callers.
GraphViewState #applyChunk folds a chunk into the store and returns without awaiting relayout, which unblocks rpc.ts's per-chunk credit gate; #drainLayoutRebuilds reruns #rebuildLayout only while a newer range landed, coalescing relayouts for large loads (e.g. 200k-row rehydration, 400 wire chunks).
git-core structural type copies must be kept in sync with contract.ts by hand, because tests/unit/ipc/wireConformance.test.ts does not exist in this repo; contract.ts's doc comment documents this.
git-core copies were synced to contract.ts: OpErrorKind gained 'BranchChanged', StashEntry gained scope/ref, CheckoutPreflight.routes gained 'autoStash'/'detachHere', HostKind gained 'kira'.
HostKind and CheckoutPreflight in git-core have no git-core consumer today.
The shared full-monorepo pre-commit hook runs long enough to create a race window in a shared checkout, where one fixer's git commit can absorb another's staged-but-uncommitted changes and index.lock contention can occur.

### v1.9 P108 Part 19 `git-ui` components
CommitGrid row conversion is guarded against a row plan that no longer matches the current chunk state on a cold-boot scrollRow restore.
Context-menu targets in git-ui are captured at open time, and the menu closes on a graph.refresh or repo switch instead of trusting a live row lookup, hooked into App.vue's handleRepoOpened/applyRepoIdToStates lifecycle point.
On a repo switch or reconnect, App.vue also clears the repo-settings dialog and any open menu refs, so destructive actions (reset, stash drop) cannot fire against the wrong repo's state.
RepoState.open sequences overlapping opens with an openSequence token, mirroring Part 18's GraphViewState#loadGeneration pattern, so a stale open cannot clobber a newer one.
Selection and scroll position are preserved across a reconnect instead of resetting to a cold-boot default.
ReviewView uses a targetSequence counter so a review.target push arriving during repo.list is not overwritten by the workspace default that resolves afterward.
bootstrap() in App.vue and ReviewView.vue disposes/unsubscribes prior state at entry before recreating it, so retries do not leak subscriptions.
The shared retryBootstrap helper in state/bootstrap.ts takes an optional onSuccess callback so App.vue's cold-bootstrap pendingUiAction fires exactly once, whether the first attempt or a retry succeeds.
The boot-error banner renders above every repoState sub-branch, and NoRepositoryPanel.vue has its own refreshError/Retry so a failed refreshList() reads distinctly from no folder being a Git repository.
ReviewCommitRow.vue builds menuSections/onMenuSelect from props.actions (always available) rather than props.expansion?.actions, which is undefined until the row is first expanded.
Fire-and-forget async sites in App.vue, ReviewView.vue, ReviewCommitRow.vue and NoRepositoryPanel.vue route rejections through reportAsyncError or a local try-catch that ignores TransportError('transport-closed') and otherwise surfaces the message on the announcement/live-region surface.
App.vue's startDetailResize uses pointer events with setPointerCapture and VueUse's useEventListener for window-level listeners, fixing a stuck-drag bug that predates P105; KuiColumnResizeHandle.vue uses the same shape.
App.vue's onDocumentKeydown acts only if the event landed inside this instance's root, or falls back to this instance's own graphVisible flag (useGraphVisible()) when focus is elsewhere.
App.vue's onClickOutside targets a template ref scoped to its own overlay aside rather than document.querySelector, which could resolve to another mount's identically-testid'd element.
ReviewView.vue's onDocumentKeydown is scoped via a rootEl template ref; root containment alone suffices because it has no KeepAlive/visibility concept.
main.ts provides GRAPH_VISIBLE_KEY unconditionally for every mount (view 'graph' or 'review'), so the graphVisible fallback behaves identically regardless of host.
Skip unit tests for simple prop or scoping fixes; per CLAUDE.md's unit-test bar, write regression tests only for complex or hard-to-get-right logic such as races and sequencing.

### v1.9 P108 Part 20 Kira Space hosts — desktop app and VS Code extension
GitClientsService.AttachPush in apps/kira-space/internal/bridge/gitclients.go subscribes to gitsock.Server's OnPairingChanged/OnClientsChanged and emits the kira:git:pairing and kira:git:clients push events; main.go wires it and detaches it in teardown.
Frontend main.ts mounts a BootFailure.vue island with Retry on any hydrate rejection; essential hydrates run in Promise.all, non-essential (gitClients/terminals) in Promise.allSettled so one optional store cannot block the shell.
In transport.ts, onClose deletes the shared-client map entry only when it still points at the transport that fired it, so a late async onClose cannot evict a newer shared client.
codeworkspace.Session has a closed flag under its own mutex and ErrSessionClosed; catfileSession() returns the error on a closed session instead of spawning a new cat-file pair, and BeginSearch() returns an already-cancelled context.
In main.go teardown, CodeWorkspaceService.Shutdown must run before repositories.Close(), because a running search still reads settings through Deps.Repos.
main.go teardown calls gitRegistry.Close() unconditionally after gitSock.Close(), because gitsock.Server.Close() can early-return; Registry.Close and gitreview.Store.Close are idempotent.
Kira Space enforces a single instance: main.go acquires an app.lock file via exported gitsock.AcquireLock before storage.Open and exits with os.Exit(0) if another instance owns the home, so a second launch cannot silently share kira.db.
The app lock must be a different file from git.sock.lock, because flock is scoped to the open file description, so reusing the path would make gitsock.Server.Start's own AcquireLock in the same process see its own first instance as another instance.
startupfail.StepInstanceLock is the startup-failure step for the real-error path of instance locking, following the existing Step/Classify pattern.
The VS Code extension's .vscodeignore excludes tests/**, playwright.config.ts, test-results/** and playwright-report/**, so the .vsix contains only package.json, README.md, LICENSE, resources/** and dist/**.
The build:vsix Taskfile sources list includes packages/git-ui/vite.config.ts, apps/kira-space-vscode/README.md and bun.lock as real build inputs.
In the VS Code extension, ConnectionManager checks vscode.env.remoteName at construction; in Remote-SSH workspaces it skips the dial loop and goes to a denied reason 'remote' with a distinct message, and retry() is a no-op for that reason.
The VS Code extension uses connection.ts's exported socketPath() for the git socket path in the status bar and commands instead of hardcoding ~/.kira-space/git.sock.
fakeReviewHost.ts exports a FakeReviewHostWindow interface naming the three window globals, which review-target-race.spec.ts uses to avoid biome noExplicitAny warnings.
ReadFile and scanFile check Mode().IsRegular() on the pre-open os.Stat before os.Open, then read through io.LimitReader, because os.Open (not os.Stat) blocks on a FIFO with no writer, so open-then-stat would not close the hang.
Regression tests are written only for concurrency/race/hang-shaped fixes, per CLAUDE.md's unit-test bar; wiring, build-list, type-only and os.Exit changes get no dedicated tests.

### v1.9 P109 True up `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md`, every README and `CLAUDE.md`'s own stale pointers against...
P109 is the closing documentation phase of v1.9: it trues up docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md, CLAUDE.md and the READMEs against the repo state after P108, with no app behavior changes and no .vue/.ts/.go source edits.
When trueing up docs, verify claims against the actual repo state, not earlier phases' result-section prose, because that prose goes stale (e.g. startupfail listed as a git package, a stale command count, a stale ContractVersion).
In CLAUDE.md's "prune as you go" rule, remove only dead pointers (references to deleted files/subsystems or already-answered questions); process rules stay.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's Quick open (C9) subsection was removed outright, along with its four Known open items entries.
In docs/ARCHITECTURE.md the "The native code workspace (C5-C7, P67c)" section sits inside "## Git module", immediately before "Git graph in the native workspace (C10)".
The P72/P79 KeepAlive graph tab persistence is dead code in both Kira Space and Studio: neither app's shared MainView.vue wraps its dynamic component in a KeepAlive any more. Graph tab continuity comes from TabViewStateStore persist/restore.
The dead KeepAlive graph tab persistence is recorded as a Known open item in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (Kira Space block) rather than fixed, because fixing it is a source change outside a docs-only phase.
Kira Space has no e2e-real test tier for git pairing; this is recorded as a Known open item in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md.
docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md's DB MCP port note no longer says "OS-assigned", and the doc still contains the sentence "no `bun run mcp:*` script exists".
docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md has shadcn-vue and CodeGraph sections.
packages/api-ui and packages/ui-kit do not exist; the docs mention them only to say so.
Verified counts at the end of v1.9: 29 bound services in Studio and 10 in Space (counted via application.NewService); gitrpc has 56 requests plus 1 stream; gitstream.go's allowlist is 53+1=54.
ContractVersion is 41 on both the Go and TypeScript sides; the VS Code extension (kira-space-vscode) has 47 commands in contributes.commands.
The Studio frontend has 20 components/ui sets, 70 defineStore calls and 8 settings sections.
The storage migration high-water mark is 0027 for Studio; Kira Space has 2 migrations.
Test inventory at end of v1.9: tests/ui has 283 tests in 53 spec files; visual has 6 specs and 13 snapshots; e2e-real has 4 specs and 6 tests; db-fixtures/support has 7 modules.
docs/v1.9/README.md was added in P109; the plan lives at docs/v1.9/plans/P109-docs-trueup.md.
The P113 row in SPEC.md still claims P113 runs "last … after P112", which is stale because P114 now follows it in the phasing table.
Stale source comments remain for a later phase to decide on: connections.go:172 nativeKinds mention, two quickOpen.ts references, internal/datagrip/jdbc.go comment about CLAUDE.md's per-adapter port literals, two comments naming deleted IconButton/PopoverPanel, and RepoGraphView.vue:10 "(Studio only)".

### v1.9 P110 Fix the `--color-muted`/`--color-input`/`--color-border` `@theme` collision, folded into the full CSS-to-Tailwind mig...
Before P110, base.css's @theme re-declared --color-muted, --color-input and --color-border, which shadcn-bridge.css's later @theme inline already owns; under Tailwind v4's last-definition-wins rule the legacy gray/border values won app-wide and shadcn bg-muted/border-input consumers rendered the wrong fill.
P110 fixed the collision by renaming legacy consumers (bg-input to bg-field, text-muted to text-muted-foreground) and deleting base.css's duplicate @theme lines, so each of the three names is defined once, resolving to shadcn's value.
Give each @theme name exactly one definition, because under Tailwind v4 the last definition wins and silently overrides shadcn values; the rule and the escape-hatch rule are in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, and check-theme-classes.sh guards it in bun run lint.
P110 was a single phase, not split into CLAUDE.md Part N parts, because one grep set over one end state is the acceptance and the coordinator asked for two parallel implementers; it ran as two file-disjoint streams: Stream A (git-ui, kira-ui, kira-space-vscode) and Stream B (theme, workbench, both apps' frontends).
P110 widened by explicit user request from the narrow collision fix to the full CSS-to-Tailwind migration audit (~250 files, ~8,000 CSS lines), so primitives.css, workbench.css and scoped style files are not reopened in a later phase.
Iteration 2 included 4 user-approved amendments: one 1s animate-spin, Alert err variant collapsed onto destructive, one canonical colour/radius name per value repo-wide, and all reka SplitterGroup/SplitterPanel call sites converted to the shadcn-vue Resizable wrappers.
git-ui has its own prefixed, kv:-scoped Tailwind v4 root, and kira-ui gets cn() and a theme partial; packages/kira-ui/src/theme/controls.css was deleted and Kui* components moved onto cva variants or kv: utilities.
Dialog widths became utilities and DialogContent no longer has shadcn's sm:max-w-sm cap.
Keep kv-* marker classes that specs and scripts query when converting to utilities, because Stream A's conversion removed them and A-fix had to restore them.
B34's shared @utility virtual-row/sticky-row lost to an unlayered scoped position: relative, which collapsed virtualized rows.
NativeSelect's own v-model overrode a raw :value at 9 call sites, a real bug fixed in P110.
scripts/check-class-conflicts.ts runs inside bun run lint; missing tailwind-merge groups are registered so cn() merges kira scales.
Shared components replaced pasted class strings in iteration 2: Empty, TreeTwisty, RunState, NumberStepperInput, ViewToolbar, SwatchRadio, and settings fields on Field/Label.
P110 end state at 0491dae: packages/theme/src/base.css holds exactly 4 @utility blocks, and only 5 .vue files keep a style block (MonacoHost, OperationsPanel, ResponseDiffDialog, RepoFileView, CommitGrid), down from 149.
primitives.css went from 1013 lines to 75, and workbench.css is down to 2 non-comment lines.

### v1.9 P111 Thread the pull-strategy ladder's resolution source through the `git-ipc` wire contract, so a config-derived `--rebas...
Before P111, the pull-strategy result reaching remote.pullPreflight could not distinguish an explicit kiraSpace.pull.strategy override of "rebase" from a git-config-derived one, so a config-derived --rebase-merges could wrongly apply to an explicit override.
An explicit Kira Space pull.strategy override of "rebase" never adds --rebase-merges even when the repo's git config says pull.rebase=merges; a git-config-derived "merges" resolution still does.
The fix was a git-ipc wire-contract change deferred out of P108 Part 16 into a named follow-up phase, because CLAUDE.md's exception reserves non-mechanical contract changes with design decisions for such phases.
remote.pullPreflight's strategySetting is validated against model.ValidPullStrategy and remote.run's strategy against a closed validPullStrategy vocabulary at the gitrpc layer, both refusing with ipcerr.BadRequest before any spawn.
The pure function gitpreflight.ResolveRebaseMerges replaced gitsession.wantsRebaseMerges, which was deleted; runPullOp now reads params.RebaseMerges instead of re-deriving it.
The wire contract carries rebaseMerges as PullPreflight.RebaseMerges and RemoteOpParams.RebaseMerges (Go) with TS mirrors in contract.ts and git-core, and remote.run has a rebaseMerges guard.
In git-ui, runPull sends preflight.rebaseMerges for the preflight-resolved strategy and false for an explicit strategy override.
The git-ipc contract version was bumped 40 → 41 (ContractVersion in Go, CONTRACT_VERSION in TS) as a breaking change, kept in lockstep with history comments and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md.
A git-ipc contract version bump must update every literal version in lockstep, including test assertions such as TestContractVersion_Is41 in gitrpc/stash_test.go and the embedded "version" in packages/git-ipc/testdata/graphChunkFrame.bin.
Do not regenerate graphChunkFrame.bin with TestFixtures_CaptureGraphChunkFrame (KIRA_GIT_FIXTURES=write) for a version bump, because it re-derives from a non-deterministic test repo (fresh commit SHAs, timestamps, repoId, JSON array reformatting) and adds unrelated noise. Patch the version bytes directly and revert any regenerator side effects.
Tests added: TestResolveRebaseMerges (8-case table of strategy x source x two config keys), an integration test proving RebaseMerges is false end to end when pull.rebase=merges is set with stored "rebase" strategy, and ops.test.ts cases for runPull rebaseMerges true (resolved) vs false (explicit rebase).
P111's change to the git-ipc contract required no changes to apps/kira-space-vscode/src/proxyHandlers.ts or apps/kira-space/internal/bridge/gitstream.go, and nothing under apps/kira-studio/ was touched.
Never use git commit --amend in a shared checkout, because it always amends whatever HEAD currently is (possibly another session's commit), not a specific commit this session made.
In a shared checkout, commit with a pathspec-scoped git commit -m message -- <files> instead of a plain git commit, because the shared index may hold another session's staged files that would otherwise land in the commit.
After each commit in a shared checkout, check its file list against the intended set with git show --stat, to catch commits that picked up foreign files or amended foreign commits.
The packages/git-ipc/testdata/graphChunkFrame.bin fixture is regenerated by TestFixtures_CaptureGraphChunkFrame, which only runs when KIRA_GIT_FIXTURES=write.
SPEC.md holds one row per phase and is updated as phases land or split.
The plans/ directory holds one implementation plan per phase, committed before that phase's implementation starts, written by an Opus subagent per CLAUDE.md's process, and never edited afterward.

### v1.9 P112 Move Studio API-client server state (collections tree, saved requests, variable rows, environments, active environmen...
Studio API-client server state (collections tree, saved requests, variable rows, environments, active environment) moves onto TanStack Query, invalidated by a Go-broadcast scoped api-data-changed event rather than a hand-rolled generation counter.
Before P112, the variables listCache, environment list, active environment and collections tree/saved-request caches did not invalidate across windows: edits in one window never reached another, and listCache never refetched once filled, so a stale entry could substitute stale plain values into every send.
The api-data-changed event payload is scoped and names what changed: a collection id, an environment id, or "environments list changed"; Go broadcasts it whenever a mutation lands.
P108 Part 9's F2/F9 fixes (per-key in-flight dedupe and generation guard on ensureVariablesLoaded, evicting a deleted owner's listCache entry) stay as landed; P112 replaces only the caching mechanism underneath them.
P112 was created as a follow-up phase (from P108 Part 9 finding F12) because the migration was too large for the review-fix chunk, using the same CLAUDE.md exception P110/P111 use.
Go emits api-data-changed through the emitApiData helper with the ApiDataChange scoped payload (bridge/apidata.go), wired into 19 mutation call sites in variables.go and collections.go under apps/kira-studio/internal/bridge/ (definition at apidata.go:30).
On the TS side, a boot-time listener turns an api-data-changed broadcast into queryClient.invalidateQueries; query keys and options live in apiQueries.ts.
Environments list state in variables.ts uses apiEnvironmentsQueryOptions with useQuery, and active-environment mutations use useMutation.
Variable rows use apiVariablesQueryOptions; listCache, ensureVariablesLoaded, cachedVariables and evictListCache were removed and replaced by pure functions mergeVariableRows and overviewRowsOf and async helpers variablesForSend and apiIdsForTab.
The collections tree, orphanRequests and orphanGrpcRequests use apiCollectionsTreeQueryOptions, with create/rename/delete as useMutation.
Saved requests use apiSavedRequestQueryOptions and apiSavedGrpcRequestQueryOptions, read reactively through useSavedRequest and useSavedGrpcRequest in both request views; useSavedRequest and useVariableRows are used by both HttpRequestView.vue and GrpcRequestView.vue.
loadCollectionsTree and loadEnvironments live in apiQueries.ts as queryClient.query-backed imperative loaders; the names are reused but the mechanism is new TanStack Query, not the old hand-rolled cache.
P112 removed the old cache identifiers state.requests, state.grpcRequests, listCacheGen, ensureInFlight and initInFlight; none remain outside comments.
useQuery/useMutation callers: collections.ts (1 query, 5 mutations), variables.ts (1 query, 8 mutations), and apiQueries.ts (3 useQuerys for saved request, grpc request and variable rows).
An unsaved in-progress draft in the active window must not be clobbered by a refetch triggered by a save in another window.
apiCollectionsTreeQueryOptions, apiSavedRequestQueryOptions and apiSavedGrpcRequestQueryOptions are not exported, since only apiQueries.ts uses them (knip flagged them).
grpc-schema-supersession.spec.ts hung after loadSchema began awaiting apiIdsForTab; P112 fixed it, resolving the deferral recorded in P108 Part 11.
Restore control.collectionsList and variablesListEnvironments after each spec file, because otherwise the control mock leaks across files.
api-cross-window-sync.spec.ts (3 cases) covers a variable edit reaching another window's next send, an active-environment switch reaching another window's selector, and a save not re-dirtying after its own event-triggered refetch; it stands in for the plan's §9.6 manual check.
In a shared checkout, check each commit's file list against its intended set, because a broad git commit sweeps in another session's staged files from the shared index.
CLAUDE.md requires implementing the whole plan first and then testing once at phase end.
The v1.9 chapter's lint:dead (knip) baseline is 6 duplicate-export pairs and 8 configuration hints.
build:studio emits a pre-existing INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT warning on monacoTheme.ts, which is not a failure.
GrpcRequestView.vue's schema-load watch returns early for descriptorMode 'reflection' with an empty target, so grpcDescribe never fires, methodResolved never resolves and the Call button stays disabled.
gRPC tab UI tests must set a target (e.g. demo.example.com:443) and a grpcDescribe snapshot mock when restoring a tab via grpcTab, or the Call button stays disabled.
The ui-timing Playwright project's tests are skipped via project dependency when the ui project has failures.
No automated spec covers a rename/delete in one window reaching another window's collections tree with the open tab reading orphan; it is unverified.
F2/F9 guarantee specs are api-variables-duplicate-names, api-variables-delete-eviction, api-variables-ensure-load-race and api-secret-reveal-expiry-round2.

### v1.9 P113 Second duplication sweep: CodeGraph re-run + jscpd/dupl tooling
P113 is a second duplication sweep that re-runs P107's method (S1 body hash, S2 name-collision, S3 callee-fingerprint LCS, S4 file-pair diff) only after P108-P112 all close, because earlier re-sweeping would re-find duplication those phases are mid-removing or mid-introducing.
P113 widens S3's threshold (previously LCS ratio ≥0.7 and ≥6 callees) to catch shorter/looser duplicates, rather than adding a third round of new sweep types.
jscpd (TS/Vue) and dupl (Go) are permanent, independently-installed devDependencies in the repo, not one-off scripts, per the user's explicit instruction, with dedup:ts and dedup:go package.json scripts.
jscpd and dupl are both MIT-licensed (dupl is MIT, not BSD-3-Clause as commonly assumed), so both pass CLAUDE.md's OSS-only license check.
P113 follows P107's loop: an Opus review pass writes findings only to a committed plans/ doc, then one sequential Sonnet fixer implements with a commit per finding, fast checks per commit, and full suites once near the end.
P113's planning pass re-checks each of P107 iteration 1's 12 explicit declines against the widened thresholds and the new tools, since declines made under a 0.7/≥6-callee or CodeGraph-only sweep may not hold.
The second sweep is its own phase (P113) rather than reopening closed P107, because CLAUDE.md's numbering rule gives a new phase the next sequential P number.
P113 added jscpd@5.3.2 with a root .jscpd.json and dedup:ts script, and dupl v1.1.0 as a Go tool dependency (go get -tool) with a dedup:go script; both are report-only and gate neither commits nor CI.
ipcerr gained InternalResult[T] and InternalErr helpers, replacing ipcerr.Internal(err.Error()) pass-throughs in bridge files; the one documented exception is apps/kira-space/internal/bridge/codeworkspace.go, since session()'s 3-value return fits neither helper.
adapters.Guarded was introduced across all 9 adapters (redis, sqs, postgres, mysqlfamily, sqlite, kafka, clickhouse, mongo, s3) for connection-state guards, and the getX/getReadOnly wrapper methods were removed.
relational.ConnState and Disconnect are shared between the postgres and mysqlfamily adapters; MarkPrimaryKey and BeginReadOnlyConsole are also shared helpers.
RequirePath is a shared fixed-depth path validator, migrated into the mysqlfamily, mongo, sqlite, clickhouse, kafka, sqs, redis and s3 adapters.
queryplan shares tableLabel and pushIndexIssues in queryplan/issues.go for MySQL/MariaDB, while raw schemas stay per-engine.
appstorage.ListWindowTabs and CheckWindow are shared across both apps; each app's TabsService is a thin bound-type delegate.
appstorage.QueryLeaves and UpsertLeafList are shared helpers for the layout repo.
The connections repo uses a private insertTx shared by Insert, InsertWithSecret and InsertDuplicateWithSecret; G11 landed before G12 per the plan's same-file ordering.
sqlitex.QueryOne[T] and NextSortOrder are shared helpers for sqlite repo Get-by-id and next-sort-order call sites.
The tree service uses a generic cacheAside[T] for Describe, Definition and SchemaColumns; Children keeps its own paging/invalidation logic.
gitsession detailCache and mergeBaseCache use github.com/hashicorp/golang-lru/v2 (v2.0.7, a direct require in go.mod); refsCache and stackCache use a generic valueCache[T].
Process-group kill and graceful cancel logic lives in the new internal/procgroup package; each of the 4 callers (gitclient, ghclient, gitprepare, toolexec) keeps its own killGroup/gracefulStopDelay test seams.
G13 deduplicated gitsession opslot.claim, gitrpc validReviewScope and postman importKeyValueRows.
GitPairingDialog's Deny focus and countdown re-arm per request id, because watching pending !== null stays true across a queue advance from request A to B and silently skipped the reset for B.
The shared usePendingDecision composable is used by both GitPairingDialog and DbMcpApprovalDialog.
TooltipIconButton is the shared tooltip-wrapped icon button component, migrated across about 150 sites in the initial sweep plus completion passes; 66 .vue files reference it.
RowActionsButton in git-ui builds on TooltipIconButton (F2), so F7 had to land after F2.
SlickGrid host shared helpers (gutterColumn, computeCellFillHash, renderedPageRowBand, subscribeRangeSelecting, subscribeRowHeight) live in views/shared/slick/gridHostShared.ts.
PagedViewRuntime, defaultPagedRuntime and applyPagePosition live in views/shared/viewOp.ts, and the stream view uses runPagedLoad.
SearchOptionToggles.vue in packages/workbench is shared by three search sites; a testidPrefix prop keeps each site's testids.
BootFailure.vue, bootstrapShell, terminalTabKind and defineAppViteConfig are shared cross-app frontend shell code in packages/workbench.
git-ui RepoScopedReload and FileListCursor are composed into the existing state classes, with latest-request-wins ordering unchanged.
pushIndexIssues is shared in views/console/planIssues.ts for MySQL/MariaDB plan parsers; raw schemas and MySQL's cost_info expansion stay per engine.
Putting ref on TooltipIconButton and reading x.value.$el doesn't reliably yield the real button DOM node, because Tooltip's teleported TooltipContent sibling breaks the single-root $el. This broke refocus-after-close and a popover anchor in 7 call sites. The fix exposes $el off the real template ref explicitly.
Wails requires bound types in each app's own package, so the ~29-line bridge tabs.go shell (struct, arg types, delegate calls to appstorage.ListWindowTabs/SaveWindowTabs) stays per-app in kira-space and kira-studio and still shows up in dedup:go.
No bound Wails method signature (parameters or return type) changed in P113 (acceptance criterion 5).
dedup:ts and dedup:go are report-only tools and are not expected to reach zero; residue such as tagRenderedRows in the SlickGrid hosts and the wrap helper in mariadb.ts/mysql.ts remains.
Per-commit rule in CLAUDE.md: each commit must pass fast checks (build, typecheck, lint, plus the owning package's go test); the expensive UI suites (test:ui:studio, test:ui:space, test:webview) take several minutes each.

### v1.9 P114 Investigate and fix the git-blame status-bar item's unreliable appearance in Kira Space's `test:ui:space` harness
The P76 test in repo-workspace.spec.ts (revision-pinned half) failed because it seeds an active restored repo-file tab whose boot opens the git transport (main.ts post-hydrate fall-forward mounts it before app.mount), but its git-stream mock was installed via page.evaluate after relaunch() resolved, too late.
Since commit cac7093b (react to the git transport's own stream closing, F3), an unmocked, closed git transport rejects file.read instead of hanging; this is correct app behavior, so RepoFileView never reaches state 'found' and the editor container does not render.
Before cac7093b the P76 revision-pinned half passed vacuously off a forever-loading empty host and never moved the cursor, so the blame.line guard it exists to prove was never exercised.
gitStreamMock.ts provides installGitStreamMockOnInit (page.addInitScript) for launches whose boot itself opens the git transport, while installGitStreamMock (page.evaluate) remains the per-spec default for other callers.
The git stream mock's browser-side installInBrowser logs every method seen in a t:'req' frame to window.__kiraGitRequests, read back via gitStreamRequests().
fixtures.ts RelaunchOptions has an optional gitStream field wired into installMocks, installed before relaunch()'s single navigation.
Git stream mock overrides passed via addInitScript must use null, not undefined, for 'repo.open', because addInitScript JSON-serializes its args and undefined would drop the key and hang repo.open forever.
The fixed P76 revision-pinned half asserts file content 'export const a = 1;' renders in .view-lines (proving RepoFileView's blameable guard ran), then after a click plus ArrowDown and ~500ms wait (>3x blameLine.ts's 150ms DEBOUNCE_MS) asserts file.read was requested, blame.line was not, and blame-status count is 0.
The P114 mutation check (dropping '&& rev === null' from the blameable guard at RepoFileView.vue:204) made the P76 test fail 3/3 at expect(requests).not.toContain('blame.line'), confirming the revision-pinned half is no longer vacuous.
The original P114 reported symptom (60s timeout on status-bar visibility, app never launching) did not reproduce; the actual deterministic failure was repo-workspace.spec.ts:531 expect(editor).toBeVisible() on repo-file-editor timing out at 5s with 'the transport was disposed' shown.

### v1.9 P115 Third duplication pass: fingerprint dimensions beyond call-sequence LCS
P113's S3 widening (LCS ratio ≥0.6, ≥4 callees counting resolved plus unresolved refs) found 835 of its 891 triaged pairs invisible to the original 0.7/≥6-resolved-callee cutoff, by adding unresolved callees to the callee-sequence fingerprint.
P113 declined to widen S3's threshold below 0.6/4 only because of hand-triage cost, not because callee-sequence LCS was the only fingerprint the graph data could support.
The duplication scans S1-S4 are custom scripts querying the .codegraph/*.db graph (nodes, calls edges, unresolved_refs); they are not a built-in CodeGraph feature.
P115 is a third duplication pass testing fingerprint dimensions other than callee-sequence LCS, to find real duplication that S1-S4 structurally can't see.
P115 candidate scan: type/interface-signature grouping, bucketing by normalized parameter/return types before diffing bodies, to catch same-interface-implementation duplication that S3 misses when callees differ per adapter.
P115 candidate scan: control-flow-shape fingerprint (branch/loop/switch/defer count and nesting, order-normalized) to catch copy-pasted dispatch/validation scaffolding whose branches call different things.
P115 candidate scan: literal/format-string fingerprint with identifiers blind but literal shapes kept, to catch error-message templates and SQL fragments, including drifted wording, like what G2 found by hand.
P115 candidate scan: struct/type field-shape fingerprint (same field names and types, different struct names) to catch data-shape duplication, which S1-S4 don't touch because they cover function/method bodies only.
P115 candidate scan: import-set pre-filter that buckets candidates by shared unusual import combinations before running LCS, so a lower threshold is affordable without the hand-triage volume that going below 0.6/4 would have caused in P113.
P115 is an Opus planning pass only, same split as P107/P113: build and run each new scan against the tree, triage by hand, and write findings plus a fix plan in the shape of docs/v1.9/plans/P113-duplication-sweep.md, with no fix implementation in this phase.
P115 is numbered as the next sequential P number per CLAUDE.md's numbering rule (one running sequence), and positioned last because it depends on P113's closed findings.

### v1.9 P116 Kira Space parity with Kira Studio on generic window chrome (menu bar, status bar, window lifecycle)
Kira Space and Kira Studio share packages/workbench and repo-root internal/shell; since P100 each app has its own main.go, menutemplate.go and App.vue.
Hoist Kira-Studio-only primitives to repo-root or packages/workbench so both apps bind the same implementation; never write a Space-only reimplementation.
P116 hoisted menu/keep-awake/metrics channel constants to internal/appevent, plus internal/keepawake, internal/metrics and AttachSystemWake (internal/shell) to repo-root; apps/kira-studio/internal/(keepawake|metrics|appshell/wake.go) are gone and Studio delegates.
internal/metrics provides NewAppTicker(appName), so the anchor needle is each app's own shipping executable name rather than a hardcoded AnchorNeedles.
AttachSystemWake lives in internal/shell and both apps' main.go call it; previously only Studio did.
createKeepAwakeStore and createAppMetricsStore Pinia store factories live in packages/workbench/src/state, and both apps' state/keepAwake.ts and state/appMetrics.ts call them.
TitleBarWindowActions.vue and AppMetricsItem.vue live in packages/workbench/src/components; both apps' TitleBar.vue and StatusBar.vue are thin callers.
Kira Space's menu has Settings… (⌘,), View › Toggle Project Panel (⌘B), dev-only Reload/Open DevTools, and Window-menu tab navigation, using the shared appevent channel constants (including ChannelOpenSettings, ChannelTabClose).
In Kira Space, Close Window is ⇧⌘W and ⌘W closes the active tab, matching Kira Studio's scheme; Studio's menu.go was not changed.
Kira Space keep-awake holds only the titlebar toggle reason, with no agent-hooks/Settings leaf (P100's design), unlike Studio's two reasons (toggle plus claudeCode.keepAwakeWithAgents).
Kira Space has a KeepAwakeService, WindowsService.OpenNewWindow wired to shell.OpenNewWindow in main.go (which also sets OnEmit and IsDev), and an app CPU/memory metrics ticker built via NewAppTicker.
Kira Space's state/tabs.ts has activateNextTab, activatePrevTab and closeActiveTab keyed on useWorkspaceStore().active, the analogue of Studio's useModeStore().active.
In Kira Space's main.ts, initAppMetrics() is in the critical boot Promise.all but initKeepAwake() is in the optional Promise.allSettled group, so a stuck OS power-assertion call cannot block boot; this deliberately differs from Studio.
state/tabs.ts, state/workspace.ts and state/repoTabs.ts form a circular import that is safe only via one existing entry point's ordering, as documented in tabs.ts's header comment.
In Kira Space, never import useTabsStore directly into App.vue; import it via the re-export in workbench/host.ts, because biome's organizeImports reorders it before ./workbench/host, adding a second cycle entry that left useTabsStore unbound on repo-row clicks.
The UI-spec keepAwakeStatus mock default lives in mockRuntime.ts WILDCARD_DEFAULTS with supported: true, not in bootSnapshots.ts, so the titlebar button renders by default in every spec (the UI suite runs against a static server, not a real Go build).
P119 (Kira Space release feed and update notifier) is a SPEC row appended after P118 and stays unimplemented, blocked on a release job and per-app tag scheme that are design decisions outside P116's scope.
budgets.spec.ts documents flakiness of its scroll-frame p50 timing as "cross-file worker contention" under a shared, CPU-constrained sandbox runner; it is not a deterministic regression.
The pr.yml CI patch names ./internal/metrics/... .
Space has a window-chrome.spec.ts UI test with 8 tests, bringing the test:ui:space suite to 28.

### v1.9 P117 Post-P110 Tailwind-migration visual misalignment sweep, Settings dialog and Api module named
P117 is a visual-QA sweep for misalignment after P110's CSS-to-Tailwind migration; it must launch running instances of the apps and screenshot surfaces, because lint, typecheck and existing test:visual snapshots only catch drift in states they already capture.
For each visual finding, name the offending Tailwind class by file:line and search with codegraph_explore for other call sites sharing the pattern, because a fix may need to land everywhere the class combination was copied during the migration.
P117 was appended as its own phase after P116 under CLAUDE.md's standing exception for a cross-cutting regression discovered outside any single in-flight phase's scope.
P117 ran as one Opus planning pass and one sequential Sonnet implementer with no stream split, because S1/S6/S7 share settings-pane files and A1/A2 share UI/toggle and UI/input primitives with Settings' steppers and selects.
S2 fix: DateFormatField.vue and GitLogLevelField.vue switched NativeSelect from :value/@change to :model-value/@update:model-value (as ApiPane does) because the select rendered blank.
S3 fix: the static hover:bg-hover in SettingsShell.vue and in DateTimePicker.vue's day/month/year cell grids moved into the inactive branch so the active item keeps its fill on hover, following GitPanel's precedent.
S1 fix: items-center was dropped from vertical Field layouts at 19 sites across 7 files (GitPane, RequestSettingsPane, AdvancedPane, ApiPane, AppearancePane, CachePane, DataPane), and self-start was added to selects that must stay narrow; the plan estimated 21 sites across 8 files.
S6 fix: text-kira-sm was added to the labels in FontSizeField.vue, DateFormatField.vue and GitLogLevelField.vue (WordWrapField.vue's precedent) to fix 14px settings labels, leaving the Label primitive untouched.
S7 fix: for checkbox rows with descriptions, the Label and FieldDescription are wrapped in FieldContent (shadcn's recipe) with items-start on the Field.
S8 fix: NumberStepperInput.vue's inner input uses AutocompleteField's allowlisted spin-button-hiding utilities to hide the native spinner inside steppers.
S4 fix: steppers stay w-full (as before P110), and FontSizeField.vue passes group-class="w-24" because it is the one caller needing a narrow stepper.
A1/S5: a kira size token was added to toggleVariants (Button's precedent) and applied as size="kira" to pane/tab ToggleGroup roots and RowDensityField.vue to fix stock ToggleGroup sizing.
A2: Input gained a kira/kira-lg size axis in inputVariants, and the default size stays byte-identical per P110 B25's InputGroup rule.
EnvironmentsView and VariableSetView filter InputGroups use bounded width and the kira variant, and their trailing action group has shrink-0 so the "New environment" button no longer clips.
A2 also sized inputs in VariableRow, SchemaBrowser, RequestSettingsPane timeout/limit fields, ResponseFindBar, TimestampPane, DateTimePicker hour/minute/second, and KeyValuePane's add/edit popover inputs (kira-lg).
PagerControls and SearchToolbar are already asserted at --kira-control-h by control-sizing.spec.ts, and FieldRowsTable's auto-height textarea rows are deliberate, so P117 left them alone.
P117 added UI-tier guards: control-sizing.spec.ts asserts the HTTP request pane's toggle-group-item height and environments-filter input-group height at --kira-control-h and that New-environment never clips; settings-apply-on-save.spec.ts and Space's window-chrome.spec.ts assert the date-format and git-log-level selects report a real value on open.
http-request-view.spec.ts is the Api module's first test:visual coverage, covering the method/URL bar, pane-switcher ToggleGroup, and response headers/body chrome on a sent 200 response.
ConnectionDialog's Port field shares NumberStepperInput with S8's spin-button fix, so S8 legitimately altered Studio's connection-dialog visual baseline (re-recorded though the plan expected it unchanged); a re-record commit for such a ripple must name S8 as the cause.
P117 touched no .go files, no bound Wails method signatures, and nothing outside apps/kira-studio/frontend/src, apps/kira-space/frontend/src, packages/{theme,workbench}/src and the two apps' tests/ui and tests/visual trees.
This repo never rewrites history, so P117's first 12 commits, which lack Co-Authored-By/Claude-Session trailers, stay as committed rather than amended.

### v1.9 P118 Fix P115's third-duplication-pass findings (H1-H10)
P118 fixes the 17 items across P115's findings H1-H10 (fingerprint-dimension duplication findings), using docs/v1.9/plans/P115-fingerprint-dimensions.md as its plan with no separate planning pass.
P118 fix work takes its own sequential P number rather than reopening P115, because P115 was investigation-only ("No fix implementation in this phase"), the same investigation-then-fix split as P107→P113, per CLAUDE.md's numbering rule.
P118 acceptance: each finding lands as its own refactor:/fix: commit (H4 is fix:) with fast checks; error text and behavior stay byte-identical unless a finding says otherwise; no bound Wails method signature changes; the plan's §4 closing run is green.
P118 must run only after P113 lands on the chapter branch, because H1-H3 sit on P113's G1/G2 shape and H4/H6/H9 sit near its F1/F4/F6/F8; the implementer rebases and re-reads every site since line numbers drift.
P118 is split into two file-disjoint streams: Stream A (Go: H1, H2, H3, H7, H8, H10) touching only *.go (plus 5 regenerated .fixture.ts mocks and docs), and Stream B (TS/Vue: H4, H5, H6, H9) touching only *.ts/*.vue, with zero overlap.
H2: redis, kafka, mongo, clickhouse and s3 depth-0 Children path-kind errors route through adapters.UnexpectedPathKind with byte-identical error text; variable-depth loop checks in redis/s3 stay hand-written because threading a depth would change their text.
H3: clickhouse and mongo readReq are aliases of adapters.ReadReq (as postgres/mysqlfamily/sqlite already are), and ClickHouse computeOrderBySql uses the newly exported adapters.ValidateRequestedTerms.
H1: adapters.ParseSSLMode(options, engine, accepted...) is the shared sslmode parser used by postgres, mysql-family, redis, mongo, kafka and clickhouse, keeping the refusal text '<engine>: unknown sslmode "x"'.
internal/terminal.OpenArgs fields are ordered to match both apps' TerminalOpenArgs exactly, because a Go struct conversion requires identical field order; both apps' Terminal-Open bridges pass terminal.OpenArgs(args) directly.
H7: toWireAgentEvent uses the conversion AgentEventWire(ev), and ApprovalPlanIssue/DbMcpApprovalPlanIssue element copies in dbmcp/explain.go and bridge/dbmcp.go are conversions; no wire shape, JSON tag or error text changed.
H8: gitpreflight's HeadStateFromBranch is exported and shared; gitsession converts at its own boundary with gitclient.HeadState(...), replacing its duplicate headStateFromStatusBranch.
H10: logBaseArgs takes the format string as a parameter (logBaseArgs(format string)) and LogScanArgs reuses it; argv order and content are unchanged.
Five frontend mock .fixture.ts files (clickhouse, kafka, mariadb, mysql, sqs) were regenerated because they were missing caps.keyTypes.
TestFixture_Redis in the on-demand ipcfixture suite fails because P113 G2 (689b6eea) narrowed redis/mutate.go resolveDatabaseSegment from any database-rooted path to RequirePath's exact-one-segment match, so the fixture's namespaced-key delete fails path validation.
Recommended fix for the redis regression: revert to the pre-G2 rooted-path check, or add a rooted-path variant to RequirePath; documented in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md and docs/v1.9/plans/P118-stream-a-findings.md.
App.vue had a hand-rolled copy of the detail-pane resize drag/keyboard logic that lacked the shared KuiColumnResizeHandle's primary-button guard, so right-clicking the handle started a drag.
KuiColumnResizeHandle has a direction prop ('normal' | 'reverse') so the right-docked detail pane, which widens as the handle moves left, can reuse it; the component also cleans up on unmount mid-drag.
buildReadOnlyRowMenu is a plain alias of buildReviewRowMenu, since they were verbatim duplicate copy-sha/copy-message-only menus; both call sites keep their own name.
ReviewSessionState#createRowActions uses createDetailActions, whose announce step is parametrized as (text) => void so both call sites share it.
SQL lex option types are consolidated on sql-lex.ts's SqlLexOptions: LintSqlOptions and SplitSqlOptions alias a new SqlScanOptions, SqlTokenOptions extends SqlLexOptions, and sql-split.ts and sql-lint.ts share one resolved-options resolver.
simpleObjectMenu was removed because it equaled copyNameItems(row, true); connectionMenu, containerMenu and callers use copyNameItems from menuItems.ts directly.
crockfordBase32 in mask.ts is exported and shared with celleditor/generate.ts, replacing its identical toCrockford; encodeUlidTime stays local because its left-padding is a different operation.
The workspace-tab-to-repo-id ternary and no-repository message are centralized as repoIdOfTab and NO_REPOSITORY_MESSAGE in state/workspace.ts, used by RepoGraphView, RepoDiffView, RepoFileView and RepoMultiDiffView.
ServerAppInitResult is exported once from git-ipc's contract.ts instead of being declared locally in extension.ts, proxyHandlers.ts and hostHandlers.ts.
A generic SettingsPaneProps<S> lives beside SettingsShell.vue, and each app instantiates it with its own SettingsSections, replacing identical per-app interfaces in workbench/settings/types.ts.
The JSON and shell scanner cursor, whitespace skipping, parse and beautify code is shared in rawTree.ts as Cursor, skipWs, tryParse, BeautifyMode, BeautifyResult and beautifyWith, replacing copies in beautify.ts and ejson.ts.
Fix fresh lint:dead (knip) findings on the spot, not later, per the repo's standing rule; e.g. make unused exports such as qualifiedNameFor, renderIndented and renderCompact module-private.
After P118, lint:dead shows 7 duplicate-export findings (6 before); the extra one is the intentional buildReviewRowMenu/buildReadOnlyRowMenu alias, matching the plan's "one body" verification grep.

### v1.9 P119 Kira Space release feed and update notifier
Kira Space has no update-available status-bar notifier, unlike Kira Studio's UpdateService/useAppUpdateStore; wiring it before Space is released would ship dead code.
The release job in .github/workflows/release.yml originally built, stamped and published only Studio's DMG on v*.*.* tags, so Space's packaging checks in scripts/verify-packaging.sh never ran.
apps/kira-space/build/config.yml keeps version "0.0.0" as the dev sentinel, so appupdate.isReleaseBuild is always false for Space.
appupdate.Checker hard-coded Studio's own releases/latest URL even for a stamped build.
Decision: one shared v*.*.* tag builds and releases both Kira Studio and Kira Space together under one release, with identical versions; there is no per-app tag scheme.
The release job's version-stamping step must also rewrite apps/kira-space/build/config.yml's version from the tag, alongside the existing stamps of apps/kira-studio/build/config.yml and apps/kira-space-vscode/package.json.
Changes to .github/workflows/release.yml are delivered via docs/pending-changes/, the same .github/workflows/ push-scope workaround P116 used.
The release job must run bun run package:space after stamping, then verify, codesign and mount-check apps/kira-space/bin/Kira Space.app and its .dmg the way Studio's are asserted.
scripts/verify-packaging.sh already carries Space-side A1/A3/A5/A6/N2/A4/N3 checks, gated on the bundle existing.
Space's disk image is named kira-space-macos-arm64.dmg (following Studio's naming convention) and is added as a second asset on the same gh release create call, so one draft release under the shared tag carries both DMGs.
internal/appupdate lives at the repo root (hoisted from Studio-only) and is parameterized only by app display name (User-Agent token) and the app's own running version.
The update checker does no asset matching or download-link validation; it only determines whether the shared release's tag is a strictly newer semver than the app's own stamped version, with identical logic for both apps.
scripts/install.sh in this public repo downloads the chosen app's DMG from the latest release, mounts it, and replaces the app in /Applications; install and update are the same command: curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vlad-cirstean/kira-studio/main/scripts/install.sh | sh -s -- --app=<studio|space>.
Each app's UpdateService shows an explicit update dialog on detecting a newer version (available vs. running version, Update and Later buttons); nothing installs without an explicit Update click.
Clicking Update spawns the install script detached (so it survives the app quitting) with the app's own name, then the app quits so the script can replace the running bundle, and the script relaunches the new version.
Dev builds (config.yml "0.0.0" sentinel) show no update dialog or status-bar affordance, as with Studio's dev builds.
Opening a direct DMG download link was rejected because a browser-downloaded DMG carries the com.apple.quarantine flag and Gatekeeper blocks it as unnotarized.
Homebrew was dropped entirely (no tap, no cask formulas, vlad-cirstean/homebrew-kira abandoned) because Cask quarantines downloads by default and its --no-quarantine option is deprecated as of Homebrew 4.7.0/5.0.0 with no replacement.
A self-hosted curl | sh install script is used because plain curl never sets the quarantine flag; release notes name this script as the only supported install method, and manual DMG-drag/Gatekeeper-workaround instructions are dropped as the primary path.
The Installer (internal/appupdate/install.go) spawns scripts/install.sh detached (Setsid: true) and waits for a 'staged <tag>' line over fd 3 before the app quits, so the script can safely replace the running bundle.
The installer script is fetched from raw.githubusercontent.com/vlad-cirstean/kira-studio/main/scripts/install.sh, the only sanctioned occurrence of that host across apps/, packages/, internal/, enforced by verify-packaging check S11.
scripts/install.sh is POSIX sh with contract marker '# kira-install-contract: 1' on line 2, kept in sync with the Go side by S11's grep.
Both Kira apps' UpdateService (Status/InstallUpdate/CancelInstall) share one createAppUpdateStore (TanStack Query for polling and install mutation, VueUse useLocalStorage for per-version dismissal shared across windows) and one UpdateDialog/UpdateAvailableItem pair.
Kira Studio's OpenReleasePage/Browser/release-URL-validation path was removed entirely, replaced by the click-to-install dialog.
The release workflow patch docs/pending-changes/.github__workflows__release.yml.patch stages one release job that builds, stamps and releases both apps' DMGs under one shared tag, with release notes naming the curl installer as the supported install path.
scripts/verify-packaging.sh added check S11 (installer/contract pin) and rescoped S10 from apps/ packages/ to apps/ packages/ internal/.
The P119 plan chose a single sequential implementer because the install script and Installer share a live hand-off contract, an ordering dependency rather than independent work.
Whether an ad-hoc-signed self-replacing install triggers an 'App Management' TCC prompt on macOS is unverified.

### v1.9 P120 Audit and remove leftover git-related surface from Kira Studio
Kira Studio is a database/API client with no git module; it had accreted git-shaped surface from the shared app base it split from (P100 per-app split, P103 shared packages/workbench and internal/shell).
Git settings (inlineBlame, dateFormat, gitLogLevel, protectedBranches, fetchAutoIntervalMinutes, gitPath, graphFontSize) and LinkService live only in Kira Space, not Kira Studio; Space keys, defaults, UI and wire shape are byte-identical to before. They moved from internal/appsettings to apps/kira-space/internal/storage/model/settings.go with identical JSON tags.
internal/appsettings.AdvancedCore and its Read/UpsertGit methods are deleted, as are GitSettings, InlineBlame and DateFormat from the shared layer, because AdvancedCore forced Studio's log-level leaf to share a JSON key with Space's git log level; each app now owns a plain LogLevel field directly.
Studio's settings leaf was renamed advanced.gitLogLevel to advanced.logLevel via migration 0028_p120_drop_git_settings.sql, which also deletes leftover git.* and appearance.inlineBlame/appearance.dateFormat rows from pre-P120 installs and carries the log-level value through.
GitLogLevelField.vue was generalized to LogLevelField.vue, parameterized by leaf key, because the two apps' markup was already byte-identical.
Dead IPC surface was removed from Studio: ChannelCodeSearch, CodeWorkspaceService mocks, codeSearch/quickOpen channels, and the vsix tombstone in the darwin Taskfile; the LinkService/OpenExternal binding had no real Studio caller.
linkOpenExternal appears 0 times in packages and apps/kira-studio and 3 times in apps/kira-space/frontend/src: the definition, the hostHandlers.ts caller, and a comment in bridge/index.ts explaining the P120 move.
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md no longer lists LinkService under Studio and drops the git-settings known-open-item.
Remaining git-word comments in apps/kira-studio are intentional: they explain coincidental substrings (buildinfo.go's github.com module path, datagrip's "DataGrip" mention).
TerminalTabState's shared shape (@shared/domain/tabs, also used by Kira Space) keeps codeRepoId even though Studio never passes it.
P120's settings-schema moves are order-dependent, so it used one sequential implementer: commit 2 compiles only once commit 1 removed Studio's Git consumer, and commit 3 is breaking for Studio's wire shape only.
P120 acceptance requires a committed inventory (file:line, verdict, disposition) of git-related hits in Studio, every real leftover removed, and both apps still building, typechecking and passing existing suites; plan is docs/v1.9/plans/P120-studio-git-audit.md.
The test:ui:studio 'ui-timing' project is skipped whenever the 'ui' project has a failure, because playwright.config.ts declares dependencies: ['ui'].

### v1.9 P121 Tab-strip elements: sizing, corners and spacing inconsistent with the rest of the UI, both apps
ToggleGroupItem corner/border rules must key on reka's data-orientation attribute, never Base UI's data-horizontal/data-vertical, because reka never emits those and such selectors are dead.
The Toggle component uses the control-tier radius rounded-kira-sm (not rounded-kira) and has a kira-lg size (26px height, 11px text) matching tab-strip density.
A default-variant ToggleGroup spaces its chips 2px apart; an outline-variant group stays connected with 4px outer corners and one divider.
ConnectionDialog's Fields/URI toggle and its three MCP read/write/DDL toggles use the kira-lg size, matching the dialog's control density.
All tab chips must call tabChipVariants, the single tab-chip class definition shipped by the UI/tabs shadcn-vue set (Tabs, TabsList, TabsTrigger, TabsContent), instead of pasting hand-rolled classes.
ConnectionDialog's detail tabs use real Tabs/TabsTrigger/TabsContent, gaining hover and arrow-key roving focus from reka's Tabs.
TabStrip.vue (both apps), ConsoleView result chips and TitleBar mode tabs call tabChipVariants directly rather than using Tabs, because they are closable/draggable/panel-less and not a Tabs fit.
TabStrip.vue's pt-0.5 top offset was the root cause of every document tab sitting 2px off-centre in its 34px bar; it was removed so the active tab centres exactly.
Kira Space's GitPanel header must not apply its own text styling to its nested top-level tabs.
scripts/check-theme-classes.sh guards against toggle data-horizontal/vertical selectors, hand-rolled tab-chip class prefixes, and rounded-kira in UI/toggle* files.
P121 tab-strip sizing, corners and spacing were likely a P110 Tailwind-migration-era gap, and Studio and Space may each have their own tab-strip copy after P100's split.
The orchestrating session must sequence P121, P122 and P123 rather than run them concurrently, because they have real file-overlap risk in shared theme tokens (packages/workbench or packages/theme).
P121 changed chip and toggle radius from 6px to 4px, and the default-group chip gap from 0 to 2px.
P121 changed dialog toggles (S5/S6) from height 22px to 26px and font-size 10px to 11px.
P121 fixed document-tab vertical centring offset from 2px to 0px (T1, both apps) and GitPanel tab letter-spacing from 0.55px to normal (S1).
Every hand-rolled tab/toggle instance funnels through the same one or two shared primitives (tabChipVariants, and the ToggleGroup/Toggle fix), so a representative instance per primitive proves the other callers moved the same way.
Studio never renders a tab-strip-new button, because WorkbenchShell.vue gates it to Terminal mode via showNewTab; in Kira Space the "New terminal" button is always visible.
budgets.spec.ts:356 is a strict wall-clock latency budget test (e.g. p50 scroll budget ≤12ms); its source comment says its flakiness is cross-file worker contention, which no in-file serialization mode addresses.
Visual baseline diffs after P121 were confined to named S/T instances (connection-dialog, console, data-view, http-request-view, settings).

### v1.9 P122 Focus-ring border too thick on every element that shows one, both apps
The shared focus ring is defined once as @utility focus-ring in packages/theme/src/base.css: a 1px --kira-focus outline with -1px offset. The SQL-view filter input is the reference for this value, and P122 left that value unchanged.
A base-layer :focus-visible { @apply focus-ring; } rule applies the shared focus ring to every natively focused element.
P122 changed the focus ring from the old ring-3 / ring-focus/50 halo to a 1px outline. Input, Textarea, Button, Toggle and Checkbox no longer draw their own halo and inherit the base rule. Button's destructive variant uses focus-visible:outline-error instead of a ring.
InputGroupInput and InputGroupTextarea opt out of the focus ring with outline-none because their ancestor InputGroup draws it via has-[...:focus-visible]:focus-ring. NativeSelect and InputGroup's kira variant use the same focus-ring utility.
In packages/theme/src, focus-ring is used in input-group/index.ts twice: the default variant's has-[...]:focus-ring and the kira variant's focus-within:focus-ring.
Studio's five hand-rolled filter/search boxes now use InputGroup variant="kira" with InputGroupInput: ConnectionDialog.vue engine search (1), BrowseView.vue (1), StreamView.vue offset and since fields (2), OperationsPanel.vue filter (1). They render the same reference ring as the SQL data-view filter, where before they had no ring.
SwatchRadio.vue keeps its 2px white focus ring by design, and the focus-width guard excludes it.
scripts/check-theme-classes.sh has a check_focus_width guard that fails on any focus*:ring-* or focus*:outline-[2-9] class. A (?<![-\w]) lookbehind excludes SwatchRadio's peer-focus-visible:outline-2, with 0 false positives at HEAD.
The check_focus_width guard's pre-phase hit count is 14 (input 2, textarea 2, input-group 2, button 4, toggle 2, checkbox 2), not the plan's estimate of 17; the plan's estimate was off, not the guard or fix.
The red aria-invalid focus halo (also 3px) was left out of P122's scope and deferred to a follow-up phase. No aria-invalid token was touched: every removed aria-invalid:* token reappears the same number of times on an added line.
Kira Space's VS Code-hosted git-ui focus styling is treated as VS Code-owned chrome, outside "both apps" for the focus-ring work, so no kv: root change was made.
In control-sizing.spec.ts, the expectFocusRing helper polls on outlineColor before asserting the rest of the style. Tailwind transition-colors/transition-all utilities on the primitives transition outline-color/-width/-offset, so a one-shot read after focus was flaky.
The 5 new control-sizing.spec.ts tests pass at HEAD; per P117's "confirm once" rule, the 4 non-reference shapes were confirmed to fail at 8f78d94e before being kept.
StreamView.vue (Kafka stream view) has no Playwright fixture anywhere in the test tree, so its filter changes could only be verified by class-string diff.
BrowseView.vue has no ui-project fixture; its filter box is covered by redis.frontend.spec.ts (ipc-frontend project, 'browse tab: filter and Up').
docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md describes sandbox font drift as a uniform, whole-page signature that would show on every visual spec, so a clean full visual pass proves sandbox/baseline parity.

### v1.9 P123 Font sizes inconsistent across both apps — normalize UI chrome to a small fixed set of sizes
P123 scope is UI chrome only (labels, buttons, menus, panel headers, status bar, dialogs, tree/list rows) in both apps; data-view surfaces (SQL results grid, document/JSON viewers, diff/blame views) stay user-customizable and out of scope.
Chrome font sizes in both apps use one four-value scale: text-kira-sm/md/lg/xl (host), kv:text-sm/base/lg (git-ui/kv root), kv:text-kui-sm/base (kira-ui), driven by --kira-t-sm/md/lg/xl in packages/theme/src/tokens.css.
Scale sizes: sm for secondary/caption/status-bar text, md for default/control/body text, lg for headings, xl for hero titles only.
The md size uses the same --kira-font-size variable that Data font size's own default already used, by pre-existing P16/P22 design.
The retired xs step (10px) folds into sm everywhere in chrome.
Tailwind's stock text-xs…text-9xl scale is reset with --text-*: initial in both @theme roots: packages/theme/src/base.css and packages/git-ui/src/theme/tailwind.css.
scripts/check-theme-classes.sh has a check_font_scale check that fails if the stock Tailwind text scale or a stray xs chrome class returns.
Data views (SQL/document/git grids, Monaco, status-bar/tab dirty-dot glyphs) keep their own fixed or Data-font-size-tracking sizes, untouched by design; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md records this in the Styling row.
text-kira-xs remains only in the @theme entry in packages/theme/src/base.css, AppearancePane.vue (2), StreamView.vue (2) and KeyValuePane.vue (1).
Changing chrome font sizes changes visual baselines for Studio specs connection-dialog, console, data-view, http-request-view, schema-dialog, settings and workbench, and for the Space settings spec.
ConnectionDialog.vue's fixed height was bumped from h-136 (544px) to h-138 (552px) because the MCP tab content overflowed by 6px (scrollHeight 462 vs clientHeight 456) once its labels moved from sm/xs to md.
connection-dialog-tabs.spec.ts has a "the dialog's box never moves" test that catches ConnectionDialog height overflow.
The earlier P104 change also bumped the ConnectionDialog MCP tab height, from 520 to 544, which is the precedent for the h-138 bump.
Fix a failing test on the spot, even when it is outside the current scope (CLAUDE.md rule); the ConnectionDialog height regression was fixed in an extra commit.
P123 visual re-recordings are committed one per diffing spec (7 Studio + 1 Space), not as a single commit, so each spec's diff review stays legible in history, following P122's per-shape commit granularity.
The P123 runtime probe's key/value and stream grid coverage is source-level proof only (anchor script plus token-chain diff) because no existing test harness exposes their redis fixture protocol.
Git conflict resolution during landing/rebase is orchestration-level work, not a subagent's.
When rebasing P123 onto P121, keep P121's structure and apply P123's §1.2 text-size fix to the resulting class string.
packages/theme/src/components/ui/tabs/index.ts (tabChipVariants) is a P121-only file that P123 never touches, so a rebase carries its text-kira-sm through without conflict; it was fixed to text-kira-md (control-text role), raising every tab chip from 11px to 12px.
After rebasing P123, run rg -n 'text-kira-(xs|sm)' packages/theme/src/components/ui/tabs to catch leftover small text classes that cause no merge conflict.
Tab chips render at 12px, so the control-sizing.spec.ts assertions for P121's S5/S6 and T4 tests expect 12px instead of 11px.
Tab chips appear in five visual baselines: connection-dialog, console, data-view, schema-dialog and workbench.
Resolve binary snapshot conflicts during rebase by keeping one side as a placeholder and regenerating with test:visual:update:studio / :space against the fully merged tree, not by hand-merging.

### v1.9 P124 Kira Space commit-details "Show more" button non-functional
The commit-details "Show more" bug in Kira Space came from the Go server's commit.detail sending null instead of [] for decoration (commit with no ref), parents (root commit) and trailers (no trailers).
porcelain.parseDecoration, parseParents and ParseTrailerBlock return nil for "none", and encoding/json marshals a nil slice as null, which violates the wire contract that declares decoration, parents and trailers as non-null arrays.
In CommitMeta.vue, hasDetails reads props.detail?.decoration.length only once expanded flips, so a null decoration threw a TypeError on click and Vue aborted the patch, leaving the label at "Show more"; commits with a ref expanded fine.
A root commit's parents: null also threw in FileTree.vue, so its file tree never rendered.
The fix is server-side: gitsession/queries.go wraps Parents, Trailers and Decoration in a generic nonNil helper (incremental.go), which replaced the package's per-type nonNilRanges and nonNilUpdates, so both hosts (desktop and VS Code extension) are covered.
The regression test is a raw-JSON assertion in TestIntegration_CommitDetailAndFileTree (gitsock/detail_test.go) that a root commit's parents, trailers and decoration are [] rather than null.
Only a raw-JSON check at the server layer can catch the null-vs-[] bug, because a Go struct decode cannot tell null from [] and mocked UI payloads cannot exercise the server; so no UI spec was added.
Hand-written UI fixtures did not reproduce the bug because every existing fixture types decoration: []; reproduction required replaying the real server's commit.detail JSON captured over a real socket (gitsock detail fixture) through tests/ui's mock.

### v1.9 P125 Rewrite root `README.md` as user-facing entry point: features, status, install
The root README.md is a user-facing entry point (what Kira Studio is, features, status, install/run) and points to apps/kira-space/README.md for the sibling app.
Deep reference material (full per-engine matrix, every script, chapter history) is not duplicated in the root README; it links out to docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/DEV_ENVIRONMENT.md, docs/PACKAGING.md and docs/v1.9/.
Check every feature, status or version claim in the README against current source; never copy it.
The README stays normal prose under CLAUDE.md's exemption.
Requirements and Development sections are kept in the root README because apps/kira-space/README.md cites both.
The README dropped the claim "SSH tunneling (planned for v2)" because no spec backs it.
